“What is it?” she asked, still with bowed head.
“Listen, till I tell you of the Golden Fleece.”

===Chinese===
他看着她，这般认真地蜷在那里干着她那费解的活计，一阵对这个比无母之子更可怜的孩子的怜悯涌上他心头。他紧张地、热切地俯下身去，她便放下针线，静静地、顺从地坐着，一双黑眼睛灼灼发亮。
「佐拉，」他说，「我要你做这一切——为我。」
「我愿意，只要你要我这么做。」她平静地说，可嗓音里有一种什么东西，使他半是惊愕地望进她那双美丽的眼睛，感到脸上莫名地一热。他伸出手，握住她的手，轻轻地握着，直到她一阵颤栗，抽回手去，重新埋首做她的针线。
接着，一种无名的高扬在他心头升起。
「佐拉，」他低语，「我有个打算。」
「什么打算？」她仍低着头问。
「听着，我来讲金羊毛的故事给你听。」

===Original===
Then she too heard the story of Jason. Breathless she listened, dropping her sewing and leaning forward, eager-eyed. Then her face clouded.
“Do you s’pose mammy’s the witch?” she asked dubiously.
“No; she wouldn’t give her own flesh and blood to help the thieving Jason.”
She looked at him searchingly.
“Yes, she would, too,” affirmed the girl, and then she paused, still intently watching him. She was troubled, and again a question eagerly hovered on her lips. But he continued:
“Then we must escape her,” he said gayly. “See! yonder lies the Silver Fleece spread across the brown back of the world; let’s get a bit of it, and hide it here in the swamp, and comb it, and tend it, and make it the beautifullest bit of all. Then we can sell it, and send you to school.”

===Chinese===
于是她也听见了伊阿宋的故事。她屏息听着，放下了针线，身子前倾，眼里满是急切。然后她的脸阴了下来。
「你觉得我娘就是那个女巫吗？」她满腹狐疑地问。
「不；她不会把自己亲生的骨肉交出去，去帮那个偷东西的伊阿宋。」
她审视地看他。
「不，她会交出去的。」姑娘一口咬定，然后顿住，仍目不转睛地盯着他。她心里不安，又一个问题急切地在她唇边徘徊。可他继续说下去：
「那我们就得躲开她，」他欢快地说，「瞧！那边铺在世界棕色脊背上的，就是银羊毛；咱们去弄它一缕，藏在这沼泽里，梳理它、照看它，把它弄成所有羊毛里最美的一缕。然后咱们就能把它卖掉，送你去上学。」

===Original===
She sat silently bent forward, turning the picture in her mind. Suddenly forgetting her trouble, she bubbled with laughter, and leaping up clapped her hands.
“And I knows just the place!” she cried eagerly, looking at him with a flash of the old teasing mischief﻿—“down in the heart of the swamp﻿—where dreams and devils lives.”

===Chinese===
她默默地前倾着身子坐着，在脑海里反复转着这幅图景。忽然，她把烦恼抛到了脑后，笑出一串泡泡，一跃而起，拍着手。
「我还正好知道地方！」她急切地嚷道，眼里闪过昔日那促狭的调皮——「就在沼泽的心窝里——梦和恶魔住的地方。」

===Original===
---
Up at the schoolhouse Miss Taylor was musing. She had been invited to spend the summer with Mrs. Grey at Lake George, and such a summer!﻿—silken clothes and dainty food, motoring and golf, well-groomed men and elegant women. She would not have put it in just that way, but the vision came very close to spelling heaven to her mind. Not that she would come to it vacant-minded, but rather as a trained woman, starved for companionship and wanting something of the beauty and ease of life. She sat dreaming of it here with rows of dark faces before her, and the singsong wail of a little black reader with his head aslant and his patched kneepants.

===Chinese===
---
山上学校里，泰勒小姐正在出神。人家请她去乔治湖（Lake George）跟格雷太太（Mrs. Grey）一起过夏，那是何等的一个夏天啊！——丝绸衣裳、精美吃食、汽车和高尔夫、衣冠楚楚的男人和优雅的女人。她不至于就这么形容它，可这幅景象在她心里几乎就是天堂的同义词。倒不是说她要空着脑袋去赴会，而是以一个训练有素的女子之身，饿坏了人际往来、渴慕着些许生活的美好与悠闲。她坐在这里做着这个梦，面前是一排排黝黑的脸，还有一个黑人小读者那吟哦般的哭腔，他歪着脑袋，膝盖上打着补丁。

===Original===
The day was warm and languorous, and the last pale mist of the Silver Fleece peeped in at the windows. She tried to follow the third-reader lesson with her finger, but persistently off she went, dreaming, to some exquisite little parlor with its green and gold, the clink of dainty china and hum of low voices, and the blue lake in the window; she would glance up, the door would open softly and﻿—
Just here she did glance up, and all the school glanced with her. The drone of the reader hushed. The door opened softly, and upon the threshold stood Zora. Her small feet and slender ankles were black and bare; her dark, round, and broad-browed head and strangely beautiful face were poised almost defiantly, crowned with a misty mass of waveless hair, and lit by the velvet radiance of two wonderful eyes. And hanging from shoulder to ankle, in formless, clinging folds, blazed the scarlet gown.

===Chinese===
这一天温暖而慵懒，银羊毛最后一缕淡淡的雾气从窗口探进头来。她想用手指跟着第三册读本的课文走，可一个劲儿地走神，做着梦，飘到某间精致的小客厅去——绿与金的装饰、精致瓷器相碰的叮当声和低低的絮语、窗外的蓝色湖水；她会一抬头，门便轻轻地开了，于是——
就在这里，她当真抬起了头，全校也跟着她抬起了头。那读者嗡嗡的念诵声静了下去。门轻轻地开了，门槛上站着佐拉。她的小脚和纤细的脚踝，黑黑的、赤裸着；她那深色的、圆圆的、宽阔额头的头颅和那张奇异地美丽的脸，几乎是挑战似地昂着，顶着一团雾蒙蒙的、不起波浪的头发，被那双奇妙眼睛的天鹅绒般的光辉照亮。而从肩头到脚踝，一身鲜红的衣裳，以无形的、贴身的褶皱，熊熊燃烧。

## VI Cotton / VI 棉花

===Original===
The cry of the naked was sweeping the world. From the peasant toiling in Russia, the lady lolling in London, the chieftain burning in Africa, and the Eskimo freezing in Alaska; from long lines of hungry men, from patient sad-eyed women, from old folk and creeping children went up the cry, “Clothes, clothes!” Far away the wide black land that belts the South, where Miss Smith worked and Miss Taylor drudged and Bles and Zora dreamed, the dense black land sensed the cry and heard the bound of answering life within the vast dark breast. All that dark earth heaved in mighty travail with the bursting bolls of the cotton while black attendant earth spirits swarmed above, sweating and crooning to its birth pains.

===Chinese===
赤身露体之人的呼喊正席卷世界。从俄国劳作的农人、伦敦闲倚的贵妇、非洲烈火中的酋长、阿拉斯加冻僵的爱斯基摩人；从一排排饥饿的汉子、逆来顺受眼神悲苦的妇人、老人和蹒跚的孩子——都发出同一声呼喊：「衣服，衣服！」在遥远的地方，那条环抱南方的辽阔黑土地——史密斯小姐在那儿工作、泰勒小姐在那儿苦熬、布莱斯和佐拉在那儿做梦——那片浓黑的土地感应到了这呼喊，听见了它那广袤幽暗的胸膛里应答的生命搏动。那一整片暗色的大地，因棉铃的迸裂而剧烈阵痛、隆起，黑色的侍奉地灵们在上方成群飞舞，流着汗，哼着歌，为它的分娩之痛低吟。

===Original===
After the miracle of the bursting bolls, when the land was brightest with the piled mist of the Fleece, and when the cry of the naked was loudest in the mouths of men, a sudden cloud of workers swarmed between the Cotton and the Naked, spinning and weaving and sewing and carrying the Fleece and mining and minting and bringing the Silver till the Song of Service filled the world and the poetry of Toil was in the souls of the laborers. Yet ever and always there were tense silent white-faced men moving in that swarm who felt no poetry and heard no song, and one of these was John Taylor.
He was tall, thin, cold, and tireless and he moved among the Watchers of this World of Trade. In the rich Wall Street offices of Grey and Easterly, Brokers, Mr. Taylor, as chief and confidential clerk surveyed the world’s nakedness and the supply of cotton to clothe it. The object of his watching was frankly stated to himself and to his world. He purposed going into business neither for his own health nor for the healing or clothing of the peoples but to apply his knowledge of the world’s nakedness and of black men’s toil in such a way as to bring himself wealth. In this he was but following the teaching of his highest ideal, lately deceased, Mr. Job Grey.

===Chinese===
棉铃迸裂的奇迹发生之后，当大地因堆积如雾的羊毛而最为明亮、当赤身露体之人的呼喊在人们嘴中最为响亮之际，一团突如其来的工蜂般的劳动者涌到了棉花与赤身者之间，纺纱、织布、缝纫、运送羊毛，开矿、铸造、搬运白银，直到「服务的歌」充满世界，「劳作的诗」住进了劳动者的灵魂。然而在这一群里，永远有那么一些神情紧张、默不作声、脸色惨白的男人穿行其间，他们感觉不到诗、听不见歌——约翰·泰勒便是其中之一。
他高挑、消瘦、冷漠、不知疲倦，周旋于这贸易世界的「守望者」之间。在格雷与伊斯特利经纪行（Grey and Easterly, Brokers）那气派的华尔街办公室里，身为首席机要职员的泰勒先生，俯瞰着世人的赤裸与用以蔽体的棉花供给。他这番守望的目的，对自己、对世人都不加掩饰：他投身商界，既非为自身康健，也非为疗救或衣被万民，而是要把自己对世人的赤裸、对黑人之劳作的知识，用一种能给自己带来财富的方式施展出来。这一点上，他不过是秉承了他那位刚去世的最高典范——约伯·格雷先生（Mr. Job Grey）的教诲。

===Original===
Mr. Grey had so successfully manipulated the cotton market that while black men who made the cotton starved in Alabama and white men who bought it froze in Siberia, he himself sat﻿—

> “High on a throne of royal state
 That far outshone the wealth
 Of Ormuz or of Ind.”

Notwithstanding this he died eventually, leaving the burden of his wealth to his bewildered wife, and his business to the astute Mr. Easterly; not simply to Mr. Easterly, but in a sense to his spiritual heir, John Taylor.

===Chinese===
格雷先生操纵棉市操纵得如此成功，以至于当种棉花的黑人在阿拉巴马挨饿、买棉花的白人在西伯利亚挨冻之时，他自己却端坐——

> 「高踞于王者威仪的宝座，
 远胜奥尔穆兹与印度
 之富庶。」（注：引弥尔顿《失乐园》卷二，喻撒旦之奢靡）

尽管如此，他终究还是死了，把财富的重担留给不知所措的妻子，把生意留给精明的伊斯特利先生（Mr. Easterly）；不只是留给伊斯特利先生，在某种意义上，也是留给他精神上的继承人——约翰·泰勒。

===Original===
To be sure Mr. Taylor had but a modest salary and no financial interest in the business, but he had knowledge and business daring﻿—effrontery even﻿—and the determination was fixed in his mind to be a millionaire at no distant date. Some cautious fliers on the market gave him enough surplus to send his sister Mary through the high school of his country home in New Hampshire, and afterward through Wellesley College; although just why a woman should want to go through college was inexplicable to John Taylor, and he was still uncertain as to the wisdom of his charity.
When she had an offer to teach in the South, John Taylor hurried her off for two reasons: he was profoundly interested in the cotton-belt, and there she might be of service to him; and secondly, he had spent all the money on her that he intended to at present, and he wanted her to go to work. As an investment he did not consider Mary a success. Her letters intimated very strongly her intention not to return to Miss Smith’s School; but they also brought information﻿—disjointed and incomplete, to be sure﻿—which mightily interested Mr. Taylor and sent him to atlases, encyclopædias, and census-reports. When he went to that little lunch with old Mrs. Grey he was not sure that he wanted his sister to leave the cotton-belt just yet. After lunch he was sure that he did not want her to leave.

===Chinese===
诚然，泰勒先生不过领着一份不算丰厚的薪水，在这桩生意里也没有丝毫股份，可他有知识、有商界的胆识——甚至可以说是胆大妄为——而且下定决心，要在不久的将来成为一名百万富翁。他在市场上谨慎地试了几手，赚得了一点余钱，足够送妹妹玛丽上完新罕布什尔（New Hampshire）老家的高中，之后又读完了韦尔斯利学院；尽管女人究竟为什么要上大学，对约翰·泰勒来说始终是个谜，而他也仍拿不准自己这番慈善之举是否明智。
当她得到去南方教书的差事时，约翰·泰勒催她快去，原因有二：他对产棉区兴趣浓厚，她在那儿或许能为他效劳；其次，他在她身上已经花光了目前打算花的钱，他要她去自食其力。作为一笔投资，他不认为玛丽算成功。她的来信强烈暗示她无意再回史密斯小姐的学校；可这些信也带来了情报——诚然是零散而不完整的——却令泰勒先生极感兴趣，驱使他去翻地图册、百科全书和人口普查报告。他去赴老格雷太太那顿便饭午餐时，他还拿不准是否想让妹妹眼下就离开产棉区。午饭之后，他拿准了：他不想让她走。

===Original===
The rich Mrs. Grey was at the crisis of her fortunes. She was an elderly lady, in those uncertain years beyond fifty, and had been left suddenly with more millions than she could easily count. Personally she was inclined to spend her money in bettering the world right off, in such ways as might from time to time seem attractive. This course, to her husband’s former partner and present executor, Mr. Edward Easterly, was not only foolish but wicked, and, incidentally, distinctly unprofitable to him. He had expressed himself strongly to Mrs. Grey last night at dinner and had reinforced his argument by a pointed letter written this morning.
To John Taylor Mrs. Grey’s disposal of the income was unbelievable blasphemy against the memory of a mighty man. He did not put this in words to Mrs. Grey﻿—he was only head clerk in her late husband’s office﻿—but he became watchful and thoughtful. He ate his soup in silence when she descanted on various benevolent schemes.

===Chinese===
阔绰的格雷太太正处于她命运的关口。她是位上了年纪的夫人，五十开外那几个说不准的年头，忽然被留下多得自己都数不清的百万家财。就她本人而言，她倾向于立刻就把钱花在改善世道上，用什么法子，全看什么法子当时合她心意。这条路子，在她丈夫昔日的合伙人、如今的遗嘱执行人爱德华·伊斯特利先生（Mr. Edward Easterly）看来，不仅愚蠢，简直是作孽，而且——顺带一提——对他本人明显不利。昨晚席间，他已经向格雷太太强烈地表明了立场，今早又写了一封信，把话挑得更明。
对约翰·泰勒而言，格雷太太这样支配那笔收益，简直是对一位伟人之名的难以置信的亵渎。这话他没对格雷太太说出口——他不过是她亡夫办公室里的首席职员罢了——但他变得警觉而深思。当她滔滔不绝地讲起种种慈善计划时，他只是默默喝着他的汤。

===Original===
“Now, what do you know,” she asked finally, “about Negroes﻿—about educating them?” Mr. Taylor over his fish was about to deny all knowledge of any sort on the subject, but all at once he recollected his sister, and a sudden gleam of light radiated his mental gloom.
“Have a sister who is﻿—er﻿—devoting herself to teaching them,” he said.
“Is that so!” cried Mrs. Grey, joyfully. “Where is she?”
“In Tooms County, Alabama﻿—in﻿—” Mr. Taylor consulted a remote mental pocket﻿—“in Miss Sara Smith’s school.”
“Why, how fortunate! I’m so glad I mentioned the matter. You see, Miss Smith is a sister of a friend of ours, Congressman Smith of New Jersey, and she has just written to me for help; a very touching letter, too, about the poor blacks. My father set great store by blacks and was a leading abolitionist before he died.”

===Chinese===
「那么，关于黑人——关于教育他们这件事，你知道多少？」她终于问道。泰勒先生对着鱼盘，正打算一口否认自己对这个题目有任何了解，可他忽然想起了妹妹，一道光照亮了他心里的阴霾。
「我有个妹妹——呃——正专心致志地教他们呢。」他说。
「当真！」格雷太太喜出望外地叫起来，「她在哪儿？」
「在阿拉巴马的图姆斯县——在——」泰勒先生在脑子里一个偏僻的口袋里翻了翻——「在萨拉·史密斯小姐的学校里。」
「哎呀，太巧了！幸亏我提起了这事儿。您要知道，史密斯小姐是我们一位朋友的姐姐——新泽西（New Jersey）的史密斯众议员（Congressman Smith），她刚给我写了信求助；信写得很动人，说的是那些可怜的黑人。家父生前极看重黑人，是位有名的废奴主义者呢。」

===Original===
Mr. Taylor was thinking fast. Yes, the name of Congressman Peter Smith was quite familiar. Mr. Easterly, as chairman of the Republican State Committee of New Jersey, had been compelled to discipline Mr. Smith pretty severely for certain socialistic votes in the House, and consequently his future career was uncertain. It was important that such a man should not have too much to do with Mrs. Grey’s philanthropies﻿—at least, in his present position.
“Should like to have you meet and talk with my sister, Mrs. Grey; she’s a Wellesley graduate,” said Taylor, finally.
Mrs. Grey was delighted. It was a combination which she felt she needed. Here was a college-girl who could direct her philanthropies and her etiquette during the summer. Forthwith Mary Taylor received an intimation from her brother that vast interests depended on her summer vacation.

===Chinese===
泰勒先生脑筋转得飞快。是的，彼得·史密斯众议员（Congressman Peter Smith）这名字他熟得很。伊斯特利先生身为新泽西共和党州委员会主席，曾因史密斯先生在众议院投了几次「社会主义」的票而不得不狠狠地整饬过他，因此此人的前程未卜。这样一个人，不宜与格雷太太的慈善事业牵扯太深——至少在他眼下这个位子上不宜。
「格雷太太，很想让我妹妹跟您见见面、聊一聊；她是韦尔斯利毕业的。」泰勒终于说。
格雷太太大喜。这正是她觉得自己需要的那种搭配。眼前来了一位女大学毕业生，整个夏天既能替她打理慈善、又能指点她的礼数。玛丽·泰勒随即收到哥哥的暗示：本年度暑假，关系重大。

===Original===
Thus it had happened that Miss Taylor came to Lake George for her vacation after the first year at the Smith School, and she and Miss Smith had silently agreed as she left that it would be better for her not to return. But the gods of lower Broadway thought otherwise. Not that Mary Taylor did not believe in Miss Smith’s work, she was too honest not to believe in education; but she was sure that this was not her work, and she had not as yet perfected in her own mind any theory of the world into which black folk fitted. She was rather taken back, therefore, to be regarded as an expert on the problem. First her brother attacked her, not simply on cotton, but, to her great surprise, on Negro education; and after listening to her halting uncertain remarks, he suggested to her certain matters which it would be better for her to believe when Mrs. Grey talked to her.
“Interested in darkies, you see,” he concluded, “and looks to you to tell things. Better go easy and suggest a waiting-game before she goes in heavy.”
“But Miss Smith needs money﻿—” the New England conscience prompted. John Taylor cut in sharply:
“We all need money, and I know people who need Mrs. Grey’s more than Miss Smith does at present.”

===Chinese===
就这样，泰勒小姐在史密斯学校教完第一年之后，便来到乔治湖度假；她和史密斯小姐在她临行前心照不宣地达成默契——她还是不再回来的好。可下百老汇的神明们另有安排。倒不是说玛丽·泰勒不信史密斯小姐的事业——她为人太诚实，不会不信教育；可她确信那不是她的事业，而她脑子里也还没成形一套黑人在其中各得其所的世界观。因此，当自己被人当作这一问题的专家，她颇感意外。先是哥哥来攻她，不光攻棉花，还——这更出乎她意料——攻黑人教育；听完了她结结巴巴、含糊其辞的话之后，他给她提了几点，让她在格雷太太跟她谈话时最好照此相信。
「她对黑鬼们（注：原文 darkies，系对黑人的轻慢称呼）挺上心的，你瞧，」他总结道，「指望你给她说道说道呢。她要下重注之前，你最好悠着点，劝她先按兵不动。」
「可史密斯小姐需要钱啊——」新英格兰的良知提示道。约翰·泰利尖刻地打断：
「我们都需要钱，我知道有些人，眼下比史密斯小姐更需要格雷太太的钱。」

===Original===
Miss Taylor found the Lake George colony charming. It was not ultra-fashionable, but it had wealth and leisure and some breeding. Especially was this true of a circumscribed, rather exclusive, set which centred around the Vanderpools of New York and Boston. They, or rather Mr. Vanderpool’s connections, were of Old Dutch New York stock; his father it was who had built the Lake George cottage.
Mrs. Vanderpool was a Wells of Boston, and endured Lake George now and then during the summer for her husband’s sake, although she regarded it all as rather a joke. This summer promised to be unusually lonesome for her, and she was meditating a retreat to the Massachusetts north shore when she chanced to meet Mary Taylor, at a miscellaneous dinner, and found her interesting. She discovered that this young woman knew things, that she could talk books, and that she was rather pretty. To be sure she knew no people, but Mrs. Vanderpool knew enough to even things.

===Chinese===
泰勒小姐觉得乔治湖这一帮人挺可爱。算不上顶时髦，却有财富、有闲暇、也有几分教养。一个范围不大、相当排外的小圈子尤其如此，它以纽约和波士顿的范德普尔家为中心。他们——更确切说是范德普尔先生的人脉——是老荷兰裔纽约望族出身；乔治湖这所别墅，便是他父亲盖的。
范德普尔太太是波士顿的韦尔斯家（Wells）的小姐，夏天为了丈夫的缘故，偶尔忍受一下乔治湖，尽管她把这一切都当作一个笑话。今夏她预料会格外冷清，正盘算着退守马萨诸塞北岸，恰好在一次乱点鸳鸯的宴席上遇见了玛丽·泰勒，觉得她颇有意思。她发现这个年轻女子懂得多、能谈书、人又生得相当俏。她固然不认识什么人，可范德普尔太太自己认识的人足可把这缺憾补上。

===Original===
“By the by, I met some charming Alabama people last winter, in Montgomery﻿—the Cresswells; do you know them?” she asked one day, as they were lounging in wicker chairs on the Vanderpool porch. Then she answered the query herself: “No, of course you could not. It is too bad that your work deprives you of the society of people of your class. Now my ideal is a set of Negro schools where the white teachers *could* know the Cresswells.”
“Why, yes﻿—” faltered Miss Taylor; “but﻿—wouldn’t that be difficult?”
“Why should it be?”
“I mean, would the Cresswells approve of educating Negroes?”
“Oh, ‘educating’! The word conceals so much. Now, I take it the Cresswells would object to instructing them in French and in dinner etiquette and tea-gowns, and so, in fact, would I; but teach them how to handle a hoe and to sew and cook. I have reason to know that people like the Cresswells would be delighted.”
“And with the teachers of it?”
“Why not?﻿—provided, of course, they were﻿—well, gentlefolk and associated accordingly.”

===Chinese===
「对了，去年冬天我在蒙哥马利（Montgomery）认识了几位很可爱的阿拉巴马人——克雷斯韦尔家；你认识他们吗？」有一天，她们在范德普尔家的门廊藤椅上歪着时，她这样问。随即自己回答了这个问题：「不，你当然不可能认识。你的工作剥夺了你与同阶层人交往的机会，这太可惜了。我理想中的一套黑人学校，是白人教师*能够*结识克雷斯韦尔家的那种。」
「哎呀，是——」泰勒小姐嗫嚅道，「可是——那不会很难吗？」
「怎么会呢？」
「我是说，克雷斯韦尔家会赞成教育黑人吗？」
「哦，『教育』！这个词藏着的东西可多了。依我看，克雷斯韦尔家会反对教他们法语、教他们赴宴的礼数和茶会长袍，这一点其实我也反对；可教他们怎么使锄头、怎么缝补做饭，我有理由相信，克雷斯韦尔家这样的人会乐意的。」
「那教这些的老师呢？」
「有何不可？——当然啦，得是——嗯，体面人，并按体面人的方式交往。」

===Original===
“But one must associate with one’s pupils.”
“Oh, certainly, certainly; just as one must associate with one’s maids and chauffeurs and dressmakers﻿—cordially and kindly, but with a difference.”
“But﻿—but, dear Mrs. Vanderpool, you wouldn’t want your children trained that way, would you?”
“Certainly not, my dear. But these are not my children, they are the children of Negroes; we can’t quite forget that, can we?”
“No, I suppose not,” Miss Taylor admitted, a little helplessly. “But﻿—it seems to me﻿—that’s the modern idea of taking culture to the masses.”
“Frankly, then, the modern idea is not my idea; it is too socialistic. And as for culture applied to the masses, you utter a paradox. The masses and work is the truth one must face.”
“And culture and work?”
“Quite incompatible, I assure you, my dear.” She stretched her silken limbs, lazily, while Miss Taylor sat silently staring at the waters.
Just then Mrs. Grey drove up in her new red motor.

===Chinese===
「可总得跟自己的学生交往吧。」
「哦，那是，那是；就像总得跟自家的女佣、司机、裁缝交往一样——客客气气、和和气气，但终究有个分别。」
「可是——可是，亲爱的范德普尔太太，您不会愿意自己的孩子受那样的训练吧，会吗？」
「当然不愿意，亲爱的。可这些又不是我的孩子，他们是黑人的孩子；这一点我们总不能忘了吧，不是吗？」
「是，我想是不能。」泰勒小姐有点无力地承认。「可是——依我看——这不就是把文化带给大众的现代理念吗？」
「那么坦白说，现代理念不是我的理念；它太社会主义了。至于把文化施加于大众，您说的是个悖论。大众与劳作，这才是人必须面对的真相。」
「那文化与劳作呢？」
「水火不容，我向您保证，亲爱的。」她慵懒地舒展了一下她那丝绸裹着的四肢，泰勒小姐则默默坐着，凝视湖水。
正这时，格雷太太驾着她的新红色汽车驶了过来。

===Original===
Up to the time of Mary Taylor’s arrival the acquaintance of the Vanderpools and Mrs. Grey had been a matter chiefly of smiling bows. After Miss Taylor came there had been calls and casual intercourse, to Mrs. Grey’s great gratification and Mrs. Vanderpool’s mingled amusement and annoyance. Mrs. Grey announced the arrival of the Easterlys and John Taylor for the weekend. As Mrs. Vanderpool could think of nothing less boring, she consented to dine.
The atmosphere of Mrs. Grey’s ornate cottage was different from that of the Vanderpools. The display of wealth and splendor had a touch of the barbaric. Mary Taylor liked it, although she found the Vanderpool atmosphere more subtly satisfying. There was a certain grim power beneath the Greys’ mahogany and velvets that thrilled while it appalled. Precisely that side of the thing appealed to her brother. He would have seen little or nothing in the plain elegance yonder, while here he saw a Japanese vase that cost no cent less than a thousand dollars. He meant to be able to duplicate it some day. He knew that Grey was poor and less knowing than he sixty years ago.

===Chinese===
在玛丽·泰勒到来之前，范德普尔家与格雷太太的交情，主要不过是含笑颔首而已。泰勒小姐来了之后，便有了互访和泛泛的往来——格雷太太大喜过望，范德普尔太太则半是好笑、半是懊恼。格雷太太宣布，伊斯特利一家和约翰·泰勒周末就到。范德普尔太太想不出比这更不无聊的事，便答应赴宴。
格雷太太那装潢华丽的别墅，气氛与范德普尔家不同。财富与奢华的炫耀，带着一丝蛮荒之气。玛丽·泰勒喜欢，尽管她觉得范德普尔家的氛围更微妙地令人满足。格雷家的红木与天鹅绒底下，有一种阴沉的力量，令人心惊又令人战栗。正是事物这一面吸引了她哥哥。对面那种朴素的雅致，他怕是看不出什么名堂；而在这里，他看见一只日本花瓶，少一毛都卖不了一千块。他下定决心，有朝一日也要有能力置办一只。他知道六十年前的格雷又穷、又不如他精明。

===Original===
The dead millionaire had begun his fortune by buying and selling cotton﻿—travelling in the South in reconstruction times, and sending his agents. In this way he made his thousands. Then he took a step forward, and instead of following the prices induced the prices to follow him. Two or three small cotton corners brought him his tens of thousands. About this time Easterly joined him and pointed out a new road﻿—the buying and selling of stock in various cotton-mills and other industrial enterprises. Grey hesitated, but Easterly pushed him on and he made his hundreds of thousands. Then Easterly proposed buying controlling interests in certain large mills and gradually consolidating them. The plan grew and succeeded, and Grey made his millions.
Then Grey stopped; he had money enough, and he would venture no farther. He “was going to retire and eat peanuts,” he said with a chuckle.

===Chinese===
这位已故的百万富翁是靠买卖棉花起家的——重建时期他到南方跑生意，再派他的代理人去。就这样他赚到了他的几千块。接着他往前迈了一步，不再跟着行情走，而是让行情跟着他走。两三次小小的棉花囤积，给他赚来了几万块。大约这时，伊斯特利加入进来，给他指了一条新路——买卖各棉纺厂和其他工业企业的股票。格雷犹豫了一下，伊斯特利推着他往前走，于是他赚来了几十万。然后伊斯特利提议，买下某些大厂的控股权，逐步把它们合并。计划越滚越大、大获成功，格雷赚来了他的几百万。
这时格雷停手了；他的钱够多了，不愿再冒险。他「打算告老，吃花生去了」，他嘿嘿笑着说。

===Original===
Easterly was disgusted. He, too, had made millions﻿—not as many as Grey, but a few. It was not, however, simply money that he wanted, but power. The lust of financial dominion had gripped his soul, and he had a vision of a vast trust of cotton manufacturing covering the land. He talked this incessantly into Grey, but Grey continued to shake his head; the thing was too big for his imagination. He was bent on retiring, and just as he had set the date a year hence he inadvertently died. On the whole, Mr. Easterly was glad of his partner’s definite withdrawal, since he left his capital behind him, until he found his vast plans about to be circumvented by Mrs. Grey withdrawing this capital from his control. “To give to the niggers and Chinamen,” he snorted to John Taylor, and strode up and down the veranda.

===Chinese===
伊斯特利大失所望。他也赚了几百万——不及格雷多，但也算不少了。然而他想要的不仅仅是钱，更是权力。金融统治的欲念攫住了他的灵魂，他眼前浮现出一幅图景：一个庞大的棉纺托拉斯，覆盖整个国土。他不住地向格雷灌输这个想法，可格雷只是一个劲儿摇头；这东西对他而言大得难以想象。他铁了心要退休，正当他定下一年后的日子时，却一不留神死了。总的来说，伊斯特利先生对合伙人这番彻底退出倒也乐见——既然他把自己的资本留了下来；直到他发现自己宏大的计划眼看要被格雷太太从他的掌控下抽走这笔资本而功亏一篑。「拿去给黑鬼（注：原文 niggers，蔑称）和中国佬！」他冲约翰·泰利嗤了一声，在门廊里来回踱步。约翰·泰利脱了外套，点上一支黑雪茄，把脚翘了起来。太太们都在客厅里，那几位伊斯特利家的女眷正向范德普尔太太顶礼膜拜。

===Original===
John Taylor removed his coat, lighted a black cigar, and elevated his heels. The ladies were in the parlor, where the female Easterlys were prostrating themselves before Mrs. Vanderpool.
“Just what is your plan?” asked Taylor, quite as if he did not know.
“Why, man, the transfer of a hundred millions of stock would give me control of the cotton-mills of America. Think of it!﻿—the biggest trust next to steel.”
“Why not bigger?” asked Taylor, imperturbably puffing away. Mr. Easterly eyed him. He had regarded Taylor hitherto as a very valuable asset to the business﻿—had relied on his knowledge of routine, his judgment and his honesty; but he detected tonight a new tone in his clerk, something almost authoritative and self-reliant. He paused and smiled at him.
“Bigger?”
But John Taylor was dead in earnest. He did not smile.
“First, there’s England﻿—and all Europe; why not bring them into the trust?”

===Chinese===
「你的计划究竟是什么？」泰利问，一副毫不知情的样子。
「哎呀，老兄，只要一亿股份过到手，我就能掌控全美国的棉纺厂。你想想！——仅次于钢铁托拉斯的第二大托拉斯。」
「为何不搞得更大些？」泰利喷着烟，不动声色地问。伊斯特利先生打量着他。他一向视泰利为这桩生意里极有价值的一枚棋子——倚仗他对例行公事的熟稔、他的判断力和他的诚实；可今夜他从这位职员身上察觉出一种新的腔调，一种近乎颐指气使、胸有成竹的东西。他顿了顿，朝他一笑。
「更大？」
可约翰·泰利是认真的。他没有笑。
「首先，还有英国——还有全欧洲；何不把他们一并纳入托拉斯？」

===Original===
“Possibly, later; but first, America. Of course, I’ve got my eyes on the European situation and feelers out; but such matters are more difficult and slower of adjustment over there﻿—so damned much law and gospel.”
“But there’s another side.”
“What’s that?”
“You are planning to combine and control the manufacture of cotton﻿—”
“Yes.”
“But how about your raw material? The steel trust owns its iron mines.”
“Of course﻿—mines could be monopolized and hold the trust up; but our raw material is perfectly safe﻿—farms growing smaller, farms isolated, and we fixing the price. It’s a cinch.”
“Are you sure?” Taylor surveyed him with a narrowed look.

===Chinese===
「也许以后可以；可先得拿下美国。当然，欧洲那边的局势我盯着呢，触角也伸出去了；可那边的事更难办、更慢——该死的，法律和福音太多了。」
「可事情还有另一面。」
「哪一面？」
「你盘算的是联合并控制棉花的制造——」
「对。」
「可你的原料呢？钢铁托拉斯可是拥有自己的铁矿的。」
「当然——矿是可以被垄断、进而要挟托拉斯的；可我们的原料万无一失——农场越分越小、农场彼此隔绝、价格由我们定。这是板上钉钉的事。」
「你确定？」泰利眯着眼端详他。

===Original===
“Certain.”
“I’m not. I’ve been looking up things, and there are three points you’d better study: First, cotton farms are not getting smaller; they’re getting bigger almighty fast, and there’s a big cotton-land monopoly in sight. Second, the banks and wholesale houses in the South *can* control the cotton output if they work together. Third, watch the Southern ‘Farmers’ League’ of big landlords.”
Mr. Easterly threw away his cigar and sat down. Taylor straightened up, switched on the porch light, and took a bundle of papers from his coat pocket.
“Here are census figures,” he said, “commercial reports and letters.” They pored over them a half hour. Then Easterly arose.
“There’s something in it,” he admitted, “but what can we do? What do you propose?”
“Monopolize the growth as well as the manufacture of cotton, and use the first to club European manufacturers into submission.”

===Chinese===
「确定。」
「我不确定。我查了一些东西，有三点你最好研究研究：第一，棉花农场不是越分越小，而是正飞快地越变越大，眼看就要出现一个大的棉地垄断了。第二，南方的银行和批发商行只要联手，*是能*控制棉花产量的。第三，盯着南方大地主们的那个『农民同盟』（Farmers' League）。」
伊斯特利先生把雪茄一扔，坐了下来。泰利直起身，开了门廊的灯，从外套口袋里掏出一沓文件。
「这是人口普查数字，」他说，「商业报告和信件。」他们埋头研究了半个钟头。伊斯特利站起身来。
「这里头有点名堂，」他承认，「可我们能做什么？你有何高见？」
「既垄断棉花的制造，也垄断棉花的种植，并用后者把欧洲的制造商打到屈膝。」

===Original===
Easterly stared at him.
“Good Lord!” he ejaculated; “you’re crazy!”
But Taylor smiled a slow, thin smile, and put away his papers. Easterly continued to stare at his subordinate with a sort of fascination, with the awe that one feels when genius unexpectedly reveals itself from a source hitherto regarded as entirely ordinary. At last he drew a long breath, remarking indefinitely:
“I’ll think it over.”
A stir in the parlor indicated departure.
“Well, you watch the Farmers’ League, and note its success and methods,” counselled John Taylor, his tone and manner unchanged. “Then figure what it might do in the hands of﻿—let us say, friends.”

===Chinese===
伊斯特利瞪着他。
「老天爷！」他脱口而出，「你疯了！」
可泰利慢慢、薄薄地一笑，把文件收了起来。伊斯特利继续盯着他的下属，带着一种着了魔的神情，带着人在天才出乎意料地从一向被视为平庸之辈身上显露时所感到的那种敬畏。末了他长长吐了口气，含含糊糊地说：
「我想想。」
客厅里一阵动静，是要走的意思。
「那好，你盯着农民同盟，留意它的成效和手法，」约翰·泰利劝道，语气和神态丝毫未变。「然后盘算盘算，它到了——比方说，朋友——的手里，能干出什么名堂。」

===Original===
“Who’s running it?”
“A Colonel Cresswell is its head, and happens also to be the force behind it. Aristocratic family﻿—big planter﻿—near where my sister teaches.”
“H’m﻿—well, we’ll watch *him*.”
“And say,” as Easterly was turning away, “you know Congressman Smith?”
“I should say I did.”
“Well, Mrs. Grey seems to be depending on him for advice in distributing some of her charity funds.”
Easterly appeared startled.
“She is, is she!” he exclaimed. “But here come the ladies.” He went forward at once, but John Taylor drew back. He noted Mrs. Vanderpool, and thought her too thin and pale. The dashing young Miss Easterly was more to his taste. He intended to have a wife like that one of these days.

===Chinese===
「谁在牵头？」
「一位克雷斯韦尔上校是头儿，恰好也是它背后的力量。望族——大种植园主——就在我妹妹教书的地方附近。」
「嗯——好，我们盯着*他*。」
「还有，」伊斯特利正要转身走，他又说，「你认识史密斯众议员吧？」
「那还用说。」
「这个，格雷太太分发她一部分慈善款时，好像在听他的主意。」
伊斯特利似乎吃了一惊。
「她是这样，是吗！」他嚷道。「可太太们来了。」他立刻迎了上去，约翰·泰利却退到后面。他留意到范德普尔太太，觉得她太瘦太白了。那位张扬年轻的伊斯特利小姐更合他的口味。他打算有朝一日娶一个那样的太太。

===Original===
“Mary,” said he to his sister as he finally rose to go, “tell me about the Cresswells.”
Mary explained to him at length the impossibility of her knowing much about the local white aristocracy of Tooms County, and then told him all she had heard.
“Mrs. Grey talked to you much?”
“Yes.”
“About darky schools?”
“Yes.”
“What does she intend to do?”
“I think she will aid Miss Smith first.”
“Did you suggest anything?”
“Well, I told her what I thought about cooperating with the local white people.”
“The Cresswells?”
“Yes﻿—you see Mrs. Vanderpool knows the Cresswells.”
“Does, eh? Good! Say, that’s a good point. You just bear heavy on it﻿—cooperate with the Cresswells.”
“Why, yes. But﻿—you see, John, I don’t just know whether one *could* cooperate with the Cresswells or not﻿—one hears such contradictory stories of them. But there must be some other white people﻿—”
“Stuff! It’s the Cresswells we want.”
“Well,” Mary was very dubious, “they are﻿—the most important.”

===Chinese===
「玛丽，」他终于起身要走时，对妹妹说，「跟我说说克雷斯韦尔家吧。」
玛丽向他细细解释，她这个身份是断不可能了解图姆斯县当地白人望族多少情况的，然后把她所听到的都告诉了他。
「格雷太太跟你谈得多吗？」
「多。」
「谈黑鬼学校的事？（注：原文 darky schools，darky 系对黑人的轻慢称呼）」
「谈。」
「她打算怎么做？」
「我想她会先资助史密斯小姐。」
「你提过什么建议吗？」
「嗯，我把我对与当地白人合作的想法告诉她了。」
「克雷斯韦尔家？」
「是——你瞧，范德普尔太太认识克雷斯韦尔家。」
「是吗？好！嘿，这一点好。你就往这方面多着力——与克雷斯韦尔家合作。」
「哎呀，是。可是——你瞧，约翰，我也说不准一个人*能不能*跟克雷斯韦尔家合作——关于他们的说法实在太矛盾了。不过总有别的一些白人吧——」
「胡说！我们要的就是克雷斯韦尔家。」
「好吧，」玛丽满腹狐疑，「他们确实是——最重要的。」

## VII The Place of Dreams / VII 梦居之地

===Original===
When she went South late in September, Mary Taylor had two definite but allied objects: she was to get all possible business information concerning the Cresswells, and she was to induce Miss Smith to prepare for Mrs. Grey’s benevolence by interesting the local whites in her work. The programme attracted Miss Taylor. She felt in touch, even if dimly and slightly, with great industrial movements, and she felt, too, like a discerning pioneer in philanthropy. Both roles she liked. Besides, they held, each, certain promises of social prestige; and society, Miss Taylor argued, one must have even in Alabama.
Bles Alwyn met her at the train. He was growing to be a big fine bronze giant, and Mary was glad to see him. She especially tried, in the first few weeks of opening school, to glean as much information as possible concerning the community, and particularly the Cresswells. She found the Negro youth quicker, surer, and more intelligent in his answers than those she questioned elsewhere, and she gained real enjoyment from her long talks with him.

===Chinese===
九月底她南下时，玛丽·泰勒有两个明确而相互关联的目标：一是尽可能打听到关于克雷斯韦尔家的一切商业情报；二是说服史密斯小姐为格雷太太的善举做好准备——办法是让当地白人对她的事业产生兴趣。这个计划很合泰勒小姐的心意。她感到自己与那些宏大的工业运动有了联系——哪怕只是隐约的、轻微的联系；她又觉得自己像个有眼光的慈善先驱。这两个角色她都喜欢。况且，两者各自都许诺着某种社会声望；而社交，泰勒小姐主张，即便在阿拉巴马也是不可或缺的。
布莱斯·阿尔文到火车站接她。他已长成一个高大英俊的古铜色巨人，玛丽见到他很高兴。开学头几个星期，她格外用心，想尽可能多地摸清这一带社区的情况，尤其是克雷斯韦尔家。她发现这个黑人小伙子回答问题比她在别处问的那些人更敏捷、更笃定、更聪明，她从与他长谈中获得了真切的乐趣。

===Original===
“Isn’t Bles developing splendidly?” she said to Miss Smith one afternoon. There was an unmistakable note of enthusiasm in her voice. Miss Smith slowly closed her letter-file but did not look up.
“Yes,” she said crisply. “He’s eighteen now﻿—quite a man.”
“And most interesting to talk with.”
“H’m﻿—very”﻿—drily. Mary was busy with her own thoughts, and she did not notice the other woman’s manner.
“Do you know,” she pursued, “I’m a little afraid of one thing.”
“So am I.”
“Oh, you’ve noted it, too?﻿—his friendship for that impossible girl, Zora?”
Miss Smith gave her a searching look.
“What of it?” she demanded.
“She is so far beneath him.”

===Chinese===
「布莱斯长得真出色，是不是？」一天下午她对史密斯小姐说。她的声音里有一种不容置疑的热切。史密斯小姐慢慢合上公文夹，却没有抬头。
「是啊，」她干脆地说。「他十八了——算是个大人了。」
「跟他谈话也最有意思。」
「嗯——是挺——」干巴巴的。玛丽正忙着想自己的心事，没留意对方的神态。
「您知不知道，」她接着说，「有件事我有点担心。」
「我也担心。」
「哦，您也注意到了？——他跟那个不可救药的姑娘佐拉的交情？」
史密斯小姐审视地看了她一眼。
「那又怎样？」她问。
「她配不上他。」

===Original===
“How so?”
“She is a bold, godless thing; I don’t understand her.”
“The two are not quite the same.”
“Of course not; but she is unnaturally forward.”
“Too bright,” Miss Smith amplified.
“Yes; she knows quite too much. You surely remember that awful scarlet dress? Well, all her clothes have arrived, or remained, at a simplicity and vividness that is﻿—well﻿—immodest.”
“Does she think them immodest?”
“What she thinks is a problem.”
“*The* problem, you mean?”
“Well, yes.”

===Chinese===
「怎么配不上？」
「她是个放肆的、不信神的东西；我弄不懂她。」
「放肆和信神，这两样可不是一回事。」
「当然不是；可她未免太早熟了。」
「太聪明。」史密斯小姐补了一句。
「对；她懂得实在太多。您总还记得那件吓人的猩红裙子吧？嗯，她所有的衣裳，都保持着一种简朴和鲜艳，到了——嗯——不知廉耻的地步。」
「她自己觉得不知廉耻吗？」
「她怎么想，是个问题。」
「您是说的*那个*问题吧？」
「嗯，是。」

===Original===
They paused a moment. Then Miss Smith said slowly: “What I don’t understand, I don’t judge.”
“No, but you can’t always help seeing and meeting it,” laughed Miss Taylor.
“Certainly not. I don’t try; I court the meeting and seeing. It is the only way.”
“Well, perhaps, for us﻿—but not for a boy like Bles, and a girl like Zora.”
“True; men and women must exercise judgment in their intercourse and”﻿—she glanced sharply at Miss Taylor﻿—“my dear, you yourself must not forget that Bles Alwyn is a man.”
Far up the road came a low, long, musical shouting; then with creaking and straining of wagons, four great black mules dashed into sight with twelve bursting bales of yellowish cotton looming and swaying behind. The drivers and helpers were lolling and laughing and singing, but Miss Taylor did not hear nor see. She had sat suddenly upright; her face had flamed crimson, and then went dead white.

===Chinese===
两人停顿了一下。然后史密斯小姐缓缓说道：「我不懂的，我不评判。」
「是，可你总免不了看见它、撞见它。」泰勒小姐笑道。
「当然免不了。我也不想免；我倒是求之不得地要去撞、要去见。这是唯一的法子。」
「好吧，也许，对咱们而言是这样——可对一个像布莱斯这样的男孩、一个像佐拉这样的女孩，就不行了。」
「不错；男人和女人在交往中必须有所判断，而且——」她锐利地瞥了泰勒小姐一眼——「亲爱的，你自己也别忘了，布莱斯·阿尔文是个男人。」
远远的大路那头传来一声低低的、悠长的、如歌的吆喝；接着，伴随着大车的吱呀与吃力，四头黑黝黝的大骡子冲进视野，身后拖着十二包鼓鼓囊囊、摇摇晃晃的黄白色棉花。车夫和帮工们东倒西歪地笑着唱着，可泰勒小姐既没听见也没看见。她猛地坐直了身子；脸腾地烧成绯红，随即又变得煞白。

===Original===
“Miss﻿—Miss Smith!” she gasped, overwhelmed with dismay, a picture of wounded pride and consternation.
Miss Smith turned around very methodically and took her hand; but while she spoke the girl merely stared at her in stony silence.
“Now, dear, don’t mean more than I do. I’m an old woman, and I’ve seen many things. This is but a little corner of the world, and yet many people pass here in thirty years. The trouble with new teachers who come is, that like you, they cannot see black folk as human. All to them are either impossible Zoras, or else lovable Blessings. They forget that Zora is not to be annihilated, but studied and understood, and that Bles is a young man of eighteen and not a clod.”

===Chinese===
「小——史密斯小姐！」她倒抽一口气，又是懊丧又是惊惶，一副自尊受创、方寸大乱的样子。
史密斯小姐不慌不忙地转过身，握住她的手；可她说话时，姑娘只是石头似的沉默瞪着她。
「好啦，亲爱的，别把我的话往重里想。我是个老太婆了，见过许多事。这只是世界一个小小的角落，可三十年里也有许多人打这儿过。新来的老师的通病是——跟你一样——他们看不见黑人也是人。在他们眼里，黑人要么是不可救药的佐拉，要么是招人疼的布莱斯。他们忘了，佐拉不是要被消灭的，而是要被研究、被理解；也忘了布莱斯是个十八岁的小伙子，不是一块木头。」

===Original===
“But that he should dare﻿—” Mary began breathlessly.
“He hasn’t dared,” Miss Smith went gently on. “No thought of you but as a teacher has yet entered his dear, simple head. But, my point is simply this: he’s a man, and a human one, and if you keep on making much over him, and talking to him and petting him, he’ll have the right to interpret your manner in his own way﻿—the same that any young man would.”
“But﻿—but, he’s a﻿—a﻿—”
“A Negro. To be sure, he is; and a man in addition. Now, dear, don’t take this too much to heart; this is not a rebuke, but a clumsy warning. I am simply trying to make clear to you *why* you should be careful. Treat poor Zora a little more lovingly, and Bles a little less warmly. They are just human﻿—but, oh! so human.”

===Chinese===
「可他竟敢——」玛丽上气不接下气地开口。
「他没敢什么。」史密斯小姐温和地接下去。「除了把你当老师，再没别的念头进过他那可爱的、单纯的脑袋。可我的意思只是这样：他是个男人，是个活生生的人，你要是老这么抬举他、跟他说话、宠着他，他就有权按自己的方式来解读你的态度——任何一个年轻男子都会那样解读。」
「可——可他是个——是个——」
「黑人。一点不错，他是；可除此之外还是个男人。好了，亲爱的，别把这事太往心里去；这不是训斥，不过是个笨拙的提醒。我无非是想让你明白*为什么*你该谨慎些。待可怜的佐拉多疼一点，待布莱斯少热络一点。他们都只是人——可，唉！太是人了。」

===Original===
Mary Taylor rose up stiffly and mumbled a brief good night. She went to her room, and sat down in the dark. The mere mention of the thing was to her so preposterous﻿—no, loathsome, she kept repeating.
She slowly undressed in the dark, and heard the rumbling of the cotton wagons as they swayed toward town. The cry of the Naked was sweeping the world, and yonder in the night black men were answering the call. They knew not what or why they answered, but obeyed the irresistible call, with hearts light and song upon their lips﻿—the Song of Service. They lashed their mules and drank their whiskey, and all night the piled fleece swept by Mary Taylor’s window, flying﻿—flying to that far cry. Miss Taylor turned uneasily in her bed and jerked the bedclothes about her ears.

===Chinese===
玛丽·泰勒僵硬地站起来，含糊地道了声晚安便走了。她回到自己房间，在黑暗里坐下。光是提起这事，在她看来就已荒谬至极——不，是可憎，她不住地重复。
她在黑暗里慢慢脱衣，听见运棉大车摇摇晃晃驶向镇上去的辘辘声。赤身露体之人的呼喊正席卷世界，而那头，在夜色里，黑人们正应着这召唤。他们不知自己应的是什么、为何而应，只是听从那不可抗拒的召唤，心头轻快，嘴上唱着歌——唱的是「服务的歌」。他们鞭打着骡子，灌着威士忌，整夜整夜，堆积如山的羊毛从玛丽·泰勒的窗前掠过，飞着——向着那遥远的呼喊飞去。泰勒小姐在床上不安地翻来覆去，把被子拽到耳朵上。

===Original===
“Mrs. Vanderpool is right,” she confided to the night, with something of the awe with which one suddenly comprehends a hidden oracle; “there must be a difference, always, always! That impudent Negro!”
All night she dreamed, and all day﻿—especially when trim and immaculate she sat in her chair and looked down upon fifty dark faces﻿—and upon Zora.
Zora sat thinking. She saw neither Miss Taylor nor the long straight rows of desks and faces. She heard neither the drone of the spellers nor did she hear Miss Taylor say, “Zora!” She heard and saw none of this. She only heard the prattle of the birds in the wood, far down where the Silver Fleece would be planted.

===Chinese===
「范德普尔太太说得对，」她向黑夜倾诉，带着人骤然领悟一道隐秘的神谕时那种敬畏——「必须有个分别，永远、永远要有！那个放肆的黑人！」
她整夜做梦，整日也做梦——尤其是当她一身整洁、纤尘不染地坐在讲台上，俯视着五十张黝黑的脸——以及佐拉的时候。
佐拉坐着出神。她既看不见泰勒小姐，也看不见那一排排笔直的课桌和脸庞。她既听不见拼读者们嗡嗡的念诵，也没听见泰勒小姐喊：「佐拉！」这一切她既没听见也没看见。她只听见林中鸟儿的啁啾，来自很远很远的地方，那片将要种下银羊毛的地方。

===Original===
For the time of cotton-planting was coming; the gray and drizzle of December was past and the hesitation, of January. Already a certain warmth and glow had stolen into the air, and the Swamp was calling its child with low, seductive voice. She knew where the first leaves were bursting, where tiny flowers nestled, and where young living things looked upward to the light and cried and crawled. A wistful longing was stealing into her heart. She wanted to be free. She wanted to run and dance and sing, but Bles wanted﻿—
“Zora!”
This time she heard the call, but did not heed it. Miss Taylor was very tiresome, and was forever doing and saying silly things. So Zora paid no attention, but sat still and thought. Yes, she would show Bles the place that very night; she had kept it secret from him until now, out of perverseness, out of her love of mystery and secrets. But tonight, after school, when he met her on the big road with the clothes, she would take him and show him the chosen spot.

===Chinese===
因为种棉花的时节就要到了；十二月那灰蒙蒙、湿漉漉的日子过去了，一月的犹豫也过去了。空气里已悄悄渗进一丝暖意和光辉，沼泽正用低低的、诱人的嗓音呼唤它的孩子。她知道头一批叶子在哪里绽放，小小的花在哪里依偎，初生的活物在哪里仰头向着光、哭叫着、爬动着。一种惆怅的渴望正悄悄潜入她心里。她想要自由。她想要奔跑、起舞、歌唱，可布莱斯想要——
「佐拉！」
这回她听见了呼唤，却没有理会。泰勒小姐烦透了，老是做着、说着些蠢事。于是佐拉不理不睬，只是静静地坐着想。是的，今夜她就要把那个地方指给布莱斯看；她一直瞒着他，是出于任性，出于她对神秘和秘密的偏爱。可今夜，放学之后，当他在大路上拿着衣裳来会她，她就要领他去，把那选定的地点指给他看。

===Original===
Soon she was aware that school had been dismissed, and she leisurely gathered up her books and rose. Mary Taylor regarded her in perplexed despair. Oh, these people! Mrs. Vanderpool was right: culture and﻿—some masses, at least﻿—were not to be linked; and, too, culture and work﻿—were they incompatible? At any rate, culture and *this* work were.
Now, there was Mrs. Vanderpool﻿—she toiled not, neither did she spin, and yet! If all these folk were like poor, stupid, docile Jennie it would be simpler, but what earthly sense was there in trying do to anything with a girl like Zora, so stupid in some matters, so startlingly bright in others, and so stubborn in everything? Here, she was doing some work twice as well and twice as fast as the class, and other work she would not touch because she “didn’t like it.” Her classification in school was nearly as difficult as her classification in the world, and Miss Taylor reached up impatiently and removed the gold pin from her stock to adjust it more comfortably when Zora sauntered past unseeing, unheeding, with that curious gliding walk which Miss Taylor called stealthy. She laid the pin on the desk and on sudden impulse spoke again to the girl as she arranged her neck trimmings.

===Chinese===
不久她察觉到放学了，便不慌不忙地收起书本站起身。玛丽·泰勒带着既困惑又绝望的神情看着她。唉，这些人啊！范德普尔太太说得对：文化与——至少某些——大众，是联结不到一块儿的；再者，文化与劳作——它们水火不容吗？无论如何，文化与*这桩*劳作，是水火不容的。
你瞧人家范德普尔太太——她既不劳作，也不纺织，然而！倘若这些人都像可怜、愚笨、驯顺的珍妮，事情倒简单了；可像佐拉这样一个姑娘，某些事上如此蠢笨、另一些事上又如此惊人地聪明、而事事又如此倔强，想拿她怎么办才有点道理呢？这边，她有的事做得比全班快一倍、好一倍；那边，另一些事她碰都不肯碰，因为「不喜欢」。在学校里给她分班几乎跟在世界上给她归类一样难。泰勒小姐不耐烦地抬手，把领饰上的金别针取下来想弄舒服点，正好佐拉目中无人、充耳不闻地溜达过去，迈着那种泰勒小姐称之为「鬼鬼祟祟」的古怪滑行步子。她把别针放在桌上，一时心血来潮，在整理领口装饰时又对那姑娘开了口。

===Original===
“Zora,” she said evenly, “why didn’t you come to class when I called?”
“I didn’t hear you,” said Zora, looking at her full-eyed and telling the half-truth easily.
Miss Taylor was sure Zora was lying, and she knew that she had lied to her on other occasions. Indeed, she had found lying customary in this community, and she had a New England horror of it. She looked at Zora disapprovingly, while Zora looked at her quite impersonally, but steadily. Then Miss Taylor braced herself, mentally, and took the war into Africa.
“Do you ever tell lies, Zora?”
“Yes.”
“Don’t you know that is a wicked, bad habit?”
“Why?”
“Because God hates them.”
“How does *you* know He does?” Zora’s tone was still impersonal.

===Chinese===
「佐拉，」她不动声色地说，「我叫你的时候，你为什么不来上课？」
「我没听见。」佐拉说，睁着一双大眼直视她，轻轻松松地吐出这半真半假的话。
泰勒小姐笃定佐拉在撒谎，她也知道佐拉从前对她撒过谎。事实上，她发现撒谎在这一带是司空见惯的，而她身为新英格兰人，对此深恶痛绝。她不以为然地看着佐拉，佐拉却十分超然、却也目不转睛地看着她。于是泰勒小姐在心里打起精神，主动出击。
「佐拉，你撒过谎吗？」
「撒过。」
「你不知道那是一种恶劣的坏习惯吗？」
「为啥？」
「因为上帝厌恶谎言。」
「*你*怎么知道他厌恶？」佐拉的语气依旧超然。

===Original===
“He hates all evil.”
“But why is lies evil?”
“Because they make us deceive each other.”
“Is that wrong?”
“Yes.”
Zora bent forward and looked squarely into Miss Taylor’s blue eyes. Miss Taylor looked into the velvet blackness of hers and wondered what they veiled.
“Is it wrong,” asked Zora, “to make believe you likes people when you don’t, when you’se afeared of them and thinks they may rub off and dirty you?”
“Why﻿—why﻿—yes, if you﻿—if you, deceive.”
“Then you lies sometimes, don’t you?”
Miss Taylor stared helplessly at the solemn eyes that seemed to look so deeply into her.
“Perhaps﻿—I do, Zora; I’m sure I don’t mean to, and﻿—I hope God will forgive me.”

===Chinese===
「他厌恶一切恶。」
「可谎言为啥是恶呢？」
「因为谎言叫我们彼此欺骗。」
「那是错的吗？」
「是。」
佐拉探身向前，直直地望进泰勒小姐那双蓝眼睛。泰勒小姐望进她那双天鹅绒般的黑眼睛，纳闷那后面遮着些什么。
「一个人明明不喜欢别人，却假装喜欢，因为他怕他们、觉得他们会蹭过来弄脏他——这样做，是错的吗？」佐拉问。
「这个——这个——是的，要是你——要是你，欺骗了。」
「那你有时候也撒谎，对吧？」
泰勒小姐无可奈何地瞪着那双庄严的、仿佛把她看个透的眼睛。
「也许——我是撒过的，佐拉；我肯定不是有意的，而且——我希望上帝宽恕我。」

===Original===
Zora softened.
“Oh, I reckon He will if He’s a good God, because He’d know that lies like that are heaps better than blabbing the truth right out. Only,” she added severely, “you mustn’t keep saying it’s wicked to lie ’cause it ain’t. Sometimes I lies,” she reflected pensively, “and sometimes I don’t﻿—it depends.”
Miss Taylor forgot her collar, and fingered the pin on the desk. She felt at once a desperate desire to know this girl better and to establish her own authority. Yet how should she do it? She kept toying with the pin, and Zora watched her. Then Miss Taylor said, absently:
“Zora, what do you propose to do when you grow up?”
Zora considered.
“Think and walk﻿—and rest,” she concluded.

===Chinese===
佐拉软了下来。
「哦，他要是个好上帝，我估摸他会宽恕的，因为他该明白，那样的谎言可比把真相一古脑儿倒出来强多了。只是，」她严厉地补充，「你可不能老说撒谎是作恶，因为不是。有时候我撒谎，」她若有所思地回味，「有时候我不撒——看情况。」
泰勒小姐忘了自己的领子，用手指拨弄着桌上的别针。她一方面迫切地想多了解这个姑娘，另一方面又想树立自己的权威。可她该怎么办呢？她不停地摆弄那枚别针，佐拉看着她。然后泰勒小姐心不在焉地说：
「佐拉，你长大了打算做什么？」
佐拉想了想。
「想事情，走路——还有歇着。」她下了结论。

===Original===
“I mean, what work?”
“Work? Oh, I shan’t work. I don’t like work﻿—do you?”
Miss Taylor winced, wondering if the girl were lying again. She said quickly:
“Why, yes﻿—that is, I like some kinds of work.”
“What kinds?”
But Miss Taylor refused to have the matter made personal, as Zora had a disconcerting way of pointing all their discussions.
“Everybody likes some kinds of work,” she insisted.
“If you likes it, it ain’t work,” declared Zora; but Mary Taylor proceeded around her circumscribed circle:
“You might make a good cook, or a maid.”
“I hate cooking. What’s a maid?”
“Why, a woman who helps others.”
“Helps folks that they love? I’d like that.”

===Chinese===
「我是说，做什么工作？」
「工作？哦，我不工作。我不喜欢干活——你喜欢吗？」
泰勒小姐一缩，心想这姑娘是不是又在撒谎了。她赶忙说：
「哎呀，喜欢——也就是说，我喜欢某些工作。」
「哪一些？」
可泰勒小姐不愿把话头扯到自己身上——佐拉有一种令人难堪的本事，能把她们所有的讨论都这样指向人身。
「人人都喜欢某些工作的。」她坚持。
「你要是喜欢，那就不是工作。」佐拉断言；可玛丽·泰勒仍绕着她那个狭小的圈子打转：
「你可以当个好厨子，或者女佣。」
「我恨做饭。女佣是什么？」
「就是一个帮助别人的人。」
「帮助自己爱的人吗？那我愿意。」

===Original===
“It is not a question of affection,” said Miss Taylor, firmly: “one is paid for it.”
“I wouldn’t work for pay.”
“But you’ll have to, child; you’ll have to earn a living.”
“Do you work for pay?”
“I work to earn a living.”
“Same thing, I reckon, and it ain’t true. Living just comes free, like﻿—like sunshine.”
“Stuff! Zora, your people must learn to work and work steadily and work hard﻿—” She stopped, for she was sure Zora was not listening; the far away look was in her eyes and they were shining. She was beautiful as she stood there﻿—strangely, almost uncannily, but startlingly beautiful with her rich dark skin, softly moulded features, and wonderful eyes.

===Chinese===
「这不是爱不爱的问题，」泰勒小姐坚定地说：「干这个是拿工钱的。」
「我不为工钱干活。」
「可你必须，孩子；你得自食其力。」
「你为工钱干活吗？」
「我为自食其力而干活。」
「一回事，我估摸着，可这话不真。活着本来就是白来的，就像——就像阳光。」
「胡说！佐拉，你们的人必须学着干活，稳定地干活，卖力地干活——」她停住了，因为她确信佐拉根本没在听；那双眼睛里又有了那种遥远的神情，亮闪闪的。她站在那里，是美的——一种奇异的、近乎可怖的、却令人吃惊的美：丰润的深色肌肤、柔和的五官轮廓、那双奇妙的眼睛。

===Original===
“My people?﻿—my people?” she murmured, half to herself. “Do you know my people? They don’t never work; they plays. They is all little, funny dark people. They flies and creeps and crawls, slippery-like; and they cries and calls. Ah, my people! my poor little people! they misses me these days, because they is shadowy things that sing and smell and bloom in dark and terrible nights﻿—”
Miss Taylor started up. “Zora, I believe you’re crazy!” she cried. But Zora was looking at her calmly again.
“We’se both crazy, ain’t we?” she returned, with a simplicity that left the teacher helpless.
Miss Taylor hurried out, forgetting her pin. Zora looked it over leisurely, and tried it on. She decided that she liked it, and putting it in her pocket, went out too.

===Chinese===
「我的人？——我的人？」她喃喃道，半是自语。「你认识我的人吗？他们从来不干活；他们玩耍。他们都是些小小的、好玩的、黑黑的人。他们飞，他们爬，他们蠕动，滑溜溜的；他们哭，他们叫唤。啊，我的人！我可怜的小小人！这些日子他们想我呢，因为他们是些影影绰绰的东西，在又黑又可怕的夜里唱歌、散发气味、开花——」
泰勒小姐一跃而起。「佐拉，我看你是疯了！」她喊道。可佐拉又平静地看着她了。
「咱俩都疯了，对吧？」她回道，那份坦然弄得老师哑口无言。
泰勒小姐匆匆出去了，忘了她的别针。佐拉慢条斯理地把别针翻来覆去地看，试了试。她断定自己喜欢它，便把它揣进口袋，也出去了。

===Original===
School was out but the sun was still high, as Bles hurried from the barn up the big road beside the soft shadows of the swamp. His head was busy with new thoughts and his lips were whistling merrily, for today Zora was to show him the long dreamed of spot for the planting of the Silver Fleece. He hastened toward the Cresswell mansion, and glanced anxiously up the road. At last he saw her coming, swinging down the road, lithe and dark, with the big white basket of clothes poised on her head.
“Zora,” he yodled, and she waved her apron.
He eased her burden to the ground and they sat down together, he nervous and eager; she silent, passive, but her eyes restless. Bles was full of his plans.
“Zora,” he said, “we’ll make it the finest bale ever raised in Tooms; we’ll just work it to the inch﻿—just love it into life.”

===Chinese===
放学了，可日头还高。布莱斯从谷仓匆匆出来，顺着沼泽那柔软阴影旁的大路走去。他脑子里忙着新念头，嘴唇欢快地吹着口哨，因为今天佐拉要带他去看那梦寐以求的、种银羊毛的地方。他朝克雷斯韦尔宅邸的方向赶去，焦急地朝大路上张望。终于，他看见她来了——柔软而黝黑的身姿，一路晃荡着走来，头上顶着一大篮白衣服。
「佐拉！」他高声一喊，她挥了挥围裙。
他把她的负担卸到地上，两人一道坐下。他紧张又急切；她沉默、被动，可那双眼不安分。布莱斯满脑子都是他的计划。
「佐拉，」他说，「咱们要种出图姆斯县有史以来最好的一包棉花；咱们要把它伺候到极致——用爱把它催生出生命。」

===Original===
She considered the matter intently.
“But,”﻿—presently﻿—“how can we sell it without the Cresswells knowing?”
“We won’t try; we’ll just take it to them and give them half, like the other tenants.”
“But the swamp is mortal thick and hard to clear.”
“We can do it.”
Zora had sat still, listening; but now, suddenly, she leapt to her feet.
“Come,” she said, “I’ll take the clothes home, then we’ll go”﻿—she glanced at him﻿—“down where the dreams are.” And laughing, they hurried on.
Elspeth stood in the path that wound down to the cottage, and without a word Zora dropped the basket at her feet. She turned back; but Bles, struck by a thought, paused. The old woman was short, broad, black and wrinkled, with yellow fangs, red hanging lips, and wicked eyes. She leered at them; the boy shrank before it, but stood his ground.

===Chinese===
她专注地琢磨这事。
「可是，」——过了一会儿——「咱们怎么卖了不让克雷斯韦尔家知道呢？」
「我们不试那个；我们就把它送过去，分他们一半，跟别的佃户一样。」
「可沼泽太密了，开垦起来太难。」
「我们办得到。」
佐拉一直静静地坐着听；可这时，她猛地一跃而起。
「走吧，」她说，「我把衣裳送回家，然后咱们去——」她瞟了他一眼——「梦待的地方。」于是他们笑着，匆匆上路。
埃尔斯贝思站在那条蜿蜒通向小屋的小径上，佐拉一言不发，把篮子往她脚边一搁。她转身要走；可布莱斯忽然想到什么，停住了。那老妇人又矮又宽，黑黑的、皱巴巴的，长着黄獠牙、红红的耷拉嘴唇、一双恶毒的眼睛。她朝他们淫邪地一笑；男孩在那笑容前退缩了一下，却仍站稳了脚跟。

===Original===
“Aunt Elspeth,” he began, “Zora and I are going to plant and tend some cotton to pay for her schooling﻿—just the very best cotton we can find﻿—and I heard”﻿—he hesitated﻿—“I heard you had some wonderful seed.”
“Yes,” she mumbled, “I’se got the seed﻿—I’se got it﻿—wonder seed, sowed wid the three spells of Obi in the old land ten tousand moons ago. But you couldn’t plant it,” with a sudden shrillness, “it would kill you.”
“But﻿—” Bles tried to object, but she waved him away.
“Git the ground﻿—git the ground; dig it﻿—pet it, and we’ll see what we’ll see.” And she disappeared.
Zora was not sure that it had been wise to tell their secret.
“I was going to steal the seed,” she said. “I knows where it is, and I don’t fear conjure.”
“You mustn’t steal, Zora,” said Bles, gravely.

===Chinese===
「埃尔斯贝思大婶，」他开口，「佐拉和我要种些棉花、照看它们，好挣她上学的钱——就种我们能找到的最好的棉花——我听说——」他犹豫了一下——「我听说您有些了不起的种子。」
「有，」她咕哝，「俺有种子——俺有——神奇的种子，是上古时候、一万个月亮之前，在老家那边用奥比（Obi，西非巫术）的三道咒语种下的。可你不能种，」她忽地拔尖了嗓子，「它会要你的命。」
「可是——」布莱斯想反驳，她却挥手把他赶开了。
「先把地弄好——先把地弄好；翻它——宠它，到时候看咱们能看见啥。」说完她便不见了。
佐拉拿不准，把他们的秘密告诉她是否明智。
「我本来打算偷那种子的，」她说，「我知道在哪儿，我也不怕巫术。」
「你不能偷东西，佐拉。」布莱斯严肃地说。

===Original===
“Why?” Zora quickly asked.
But before he answered, they both forgot; for their faces were turned toward the wonder of the swamp. The golden sun was pouring floods of glory through the slim black trees, and the mystic sombre pools caught and tossed back the glow in darker, duller crimson. Long echoing cries leapt to and fro; silent footsteps crept hither and yonder; and the girl’s eyes gleamed with a wild new joy.
“The dreams!” she cried. “The dreams!” And leaping ahead, she danced along the shadowed path. He hastened after her, but she flew fast and faster; he followed, laughing, calling, pleading. He saw her twinkling limbs a-dancing as once he saw them dance in a halo of firelight; but now the fire was the fire of the world. Her garments twined and flew in shadowy drapings about the perfect moulding of her young and dark half-naked figure. Her heavy hair had burst its fastenings and lay in stiffened, straggling masses, bending reluctantly to the breeze, like curled smoke; while all about, the mad, wild singing rose and fell and trembled, till his head whirled. He paused uncertainly at a parting of the paths, crying:
“Zora! Zora!” as for some lost soul. “Zora! Zora!” echoed the cry, faintly.

===Chinese===
「为啥？」佐拉赶忙问。
可他还没答上来，两人就都把这事忘了；因为他们的脸都转向了沼泽的奇观。金色的日头正把一道道辉煌倾泻过那些纤细的黑树，幽秘的暗潭接住那光辉、又把它以更深、更暗的绯红抛了回来。悠长的回声此起彼伏；无声的脚步向四处爬行；姑娘的眼里闪烁着一种狂野的新的欢欣。
「梦！」她喊道。「梦！」她一跃超前，沿着那阴影的小径舞了起来。他急忙追上去，可她越飞越快；他追着，笑着，喊着，求着。他看见她那闪动的四肢舞着，一如他曾在火光的光晕里看见它们舞过；只是此刻的火，是世界的火。她的衣裳如幽暗的帷幔般盘旋飞舞，绕着她那年轻、黝黑、半裸的身躯的完美轮廓。她那浓密的头发挣脱了束缚，僵直地、凌乱地一绺绺垂着，不情愿地随风弯折，像卷曲的烟；而四周，那疯狂的、野性的歌唱起伏着、颤栗着，直到他的头发晕。他在岔路口迟疑地站住，喊道：
「佐拉！佐拉！」仿佛在唤一个迷失的灵魂。「佐拉！佐拉！」那喊声隐隐地回响。

===Original===
Abruptly the music fell; there came a long slow-growing silence; and then, with a flutter, she was beside him again, laughing in his ears and crying with mocking voice:
“Is you afeared, honey?”
He saw in her eyes sweet yearnings, but could speak nothing. He could only clasp her hand tightly, and again down they raced through the wood.
All at once the swamp changed and chilled to a dull grayness; tall, dull trees started down upon the murky waters; and long pendent streamings of moss-like tears dripped from tree to earth. Slowly and warily they threaded their way.
“Are you sure of the path, Zora?” he once inquired anxiously.
“I could find it asleep,” she answered, skipping surefooted onward. He continued to hold her hand tightly, and his own pace never slackened. Around them the gray and deathlike wilderness darkened. They felt and saw the cold white mist rising slowly from the ground, and waters growing blacker and broader.

===Chinese===
乐声骤然落下；接着是一段漫长的、渐渐浓起来的寂静；然后，一阵扑棱，她又到了他身边，在他耳边笑着，用嘲弄的嗓音喊：
「怕了吗，宝贝儿？」
他从她眼里看见了甜蜜的渴望，却一句话也说不出。他只能紧紧握住她的手，于是他们又飞奔着穿过树林。
突然，沼泽变了，冷成一片死气沉沉的灰；高大的、阴沉的树朝那阴暗的水面压下来；一缕缕长长的、如泪一般的苔藓从树上垂落到地上。他们小心翼翼地、缓缓地穿行。
「你认得路吗，佐拉？」他焦虑地问过一回。
「我睡着了都找得到。」她答，脚步稳当地向前跳着。他继续紧紧握着她的手，自己的步子也一刻不缓。他们四周，那灰白的、死一般的荒野暗了下来。他们感到、也看见，冰冷的白雾正从地面缓缓升起，水也越变越黑、越变越宽。

===Original===
At last they came to what seemed the end. Silently and dismally the half-dead forest, with its ghostly moss, lowered and darkened, and the black waters spread into a great silent lake of slimy ooze. The dead trunk of a fallen tree lay straight in front, torn and twisted, its top hidden yonder and mingled with impenetrable undergrowth.
“Where now, Zora?” he cried.
In a moment she had slipped her hand away and was scrambling upon the tree trunk. The waters yawned murkily below.
“Careful! careful!” he warned, struggling after her until she disappeared amid the leaves. He followed eagerly, but cautiously; and all at once found himself confronting a paradise.
Before them lay a long island, opening to the south, on the black lake, but sheltered north and east by the dense undergrowth of the black swamp and the rampart of dead and living trees. The soil was virgin and black, thickly covered over with a tangle of bushes, vines, and smaller growth all brilliant with early leaves and wild flowers.
“A pretty tough proposition for clearing and ploughing,” said Bles, with practised eye. But Zora eagerly surveyed the prospect.
“It’s where the Dreams lives,” she whispered.

===Chinese===
他们终于来到一个像是尽头的地方。那半死不活的林子连同它鬼魅般的苔藓，阴沉地、凄惨地俯压、暗了下去，黑色的水则铺展成一大片寂静的、满是黏泥的湖。一截倒伏的枯树干横在正前方，撕裂了、扭曲着，树顶隐没在那头，与密不透风的灌木丛纠缠在一起。
「这下往哪儿走，佐拉？」他喊。
转眼间她已挣脱了他的手，爬上了那截树干。下面，黑水阴森森地张着大口。
「小心！小心！」他警告着，挣扎着跟上去，直到她消失在树叶间。他急切地、却也小心地跟着；猛地，他发现自己面对着一座乐园。
他们面前横着一座狭长的岛，朝南敞开在黑色的湖上，北面和东面则有黑沼泽茂密的灌木丛和枯树活树筑成的屏障遮蔽。泥土是处女地，黑油油的，密密地覆盖着一团乱糟糟的灌木、藤蔓和矮小的植物，全都因早生的嫩叶和野花而光彩夺目。
「要开垦、要犁地，这可是个硬骨头。」布莱斯以内行的眼光说。可佐拉热切地打量着眼前这片地方。
「梦就住在这儿。」她悄声说。

===Original===
Meantime Miss Taylor had missed her brooch and searched for it in vain. In the midst of this pursuit the truth occurred to her﻿—Zora had stolen it. Negroes would steal, everybody said. Well, she must and would have the pin, and she started for Elspeth’s cabin.
On the way she met the old woman in the path, but got little satisfaction. Elspeth merely grunted ungraciously while eyeing the white woman with suspicion.
Mary Taylor, again alone, sat down at a turn in the path, just out of sight of the house, and waited. Soon she saw, with a certain grim satisfaction, Zora and Bles emerging from the swamp engaged in earnest conversation. Here was an opportunity to overwhelm both with an unforgettable reprimand. She rose before them like a spectral vengeance.
“Zora, I want my pin.”

===Chinese===
与此同时，泰勒小姐发现别针不见了，四处找也没找着。找着找着，她忽然想到一个事实——是佐拉偷了。黑人爱偷东西，人人都这么说。好吧，她非要那枚别针不可，便朝埃尔斯贝思的小屋走去。
路上她在小径里遇见了那老妇人，却没得到什么满意的答复。埃尔斯贝思只是没好气地哼哼，满腹狐疑地打量着这个白人女子。
玛丽·泰勒又落了单，便在房子看不见的一个拐弯处坐下，等着。很快，她带着某种冷酷的满足，看见佐拉和布莱斯从沼泽里出来，正谈得投入。这是个机会，可以给他们两个一个永生难忘的训斥。她像个幽灵般的复仇者，在他们面前站了起来。
「佐拉，我要我的别针。」

===Original===
Bles started and stared; but Zora eyed her calmly with something like disdain.
“What pin?” she returned, unmoved.
“Zora, don’t deny that you took my pin from the desk this afternoon,” the teacher commanded severely.
“I didn’t say I didn’t take no pin.”
“Persons who will lie and steal will do anything.”
“Why shouldn’t people do anything they wants to?”
“And you knew the pin was mine.”
“I saw you a-wearing of it,” admitted Zora easily.
“Then you have stolen it, and you are a thief.”

===Chinese===
布莱斯一惊，瞪着眼；佐拉却平静地看着她，带着一丝近乎鄙夷的神色。
「什么别针？」她不为所动地反问。
「佐拉，别否认你今天下午从桌上拿了我的别针。」老师严厉地命令。
「我可没说我没拿别针。」
「会撒谎、会偷东西的人，什么事都干得出来。」
「人凭什么不能干自己想干的事？」
「而且你知道那别针是我的。」
「我看见你戴过。」佐拉轻描淡写地承认。
「那你就是偷了它，你是个贼。」

===Original===
Still Zora appeared to be unimpressed with the heinousness of her fault.
“Did you make that pin?” she asked.
“No, but it is mine.”
“Why is it yours?”
“Because it was given to me.”
“But you don’t need it; you’ve got four other prettier ones﻿—I counted.”
“That makes no difference.”
“Yes it does﻿—folks ain’t got no right to things they don’t need.”
“That makes no difference, Zora, and you know it. The pin is mine. You stole it. If you had wanted a pin and asked me I might have given you﻿—”
The girl blazed.
“I don’t want your old gifts,” she almost hissed. “You don’t own what you don’t need and can’t use. God owns it and I’m going to send it back to Him.”

===Chinese===
佐拉却似乎仍不为自己的罪大恶极所动。
「那别针是你做的吗？」她问。
「不是，可它是我的。」
「凭什么是你的？」
「因为是人家给我的。」
「可你用不着它；你还有四枚更漂亮的——我数过。」
「那有什么分别。」
「有分别——人对自己用不着的东西没权利。」
「那没有分别，佐拉，这你知道。别针是我的。你偷了它。你要是想要一枚别针、开口问我要，我说不定会给你——」
姑娘怒火中烧。
「我才不要你那破施舍，」她几乎是嘶声说道。「你用不着、也使不上的东西，就不是你的。它是上帝的，我要把它还给上帝。」

===Original===
With a swift motion she whipped the pin from her pocket and raised her arm to hurl it into the swamp. Bles caught her hand. He caught it lightly and smiled sorrowfully into her eyes. She wavered a moment, then the answering light sprang to her face. Dropping the brooch into his hand, she wheeled and fled toward the cabin.
Bles handed it silently to Miss Taylor. Mary Taylor was beside herself with impatient anger﻿—and anger intensified by a conviction of utter helplessness to cope with any strained or unusual situations between herself and these two.
“Alwyn,” she said sharply, “I shall report Zora for stealing. And you may report yourself to Miss Smith tonight for disrespect toward a teacher.”

===Chinese===
她一个利落的动作从口袋里抽出别针，扬起手臂就要把它掷进沼泽。布莱斯一把抓住了她的手。他轻轻地抓住，哀伤地笑进了她的眼。她迟疑了一瞬，随即一道回应的光跳上她的脸。她把胸针丢进他手里，转身朝小屋飞奔而去。
布莱斯默默地把它递给泰勒小姐。玛丽·泰勒气得几近失态——而这股怒气，又因她深信自己完全无力应付她与这两人之间任何紧张或异常的局面，而愈发强烈。
「阿尔文，」她尖刻地说，「我要上报佐拉偷窃。至于你，今晚自己去向史密斯小姐报告对老师不敬。」

## VIII Mr. Harry Cresswell / VIII 哈里·克雷斯韦尔先生

===Original===
The Cresswells, father and son, were at breakfast. The daughter was taking her coffee and rolls upstairs in bed.
“P’sh! I don’t like it!” declared Harry Cresswell, tossing the letter back to his father. “I tell you, it is a damned Yankee trick.”
He was a man of thirty-five, smooth and white, slight, well-bred and masterful. His father, St. John Cresswell, was sixty, white-haired, mustached and goateed; a stately, kindly old man with a temper and much family pride.
“Well, well,” he said, his air half preoccupied, half unconcerned, “I suppose so﻿—and yet”﻿—he read the letter again, aloud: “ ‘Approaching you as one of the most influential landowners of Alabama, on a confidential matter’﻿—h’m﻿—h’m﻿—‘a combination of capital and power, such as this nation has never seen’﻿—‘cotton manufacturers and cotton growers.’﻿ ﻿… Well, well! Of course, I suppose there’s nothing in it. And yet, Harry, my boy, this cotton-growing business is getting in a pretty tight pinch. Unless relief comes somehow﻿—well, we’ll just have to quit. We simply can’t keep the cost of cotton down to a remunerative figure with niggers getting scarcer and dearer. Every year I have to pinch ’em closer and closer. I had to pay Maxwell two hundred and fifty to get that old darky and his boys turned over to me, and one of the young ones has run away already.”

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔父子正在用早餐。女儿则在楼上床上喝咖啡、吃小圆面包。
「呸！我不喜欢这个！」哈里·克雷斯韦尔（Harry Cresswell）把信扔回给父亲，宣称，「我告诉你，这是北佬的鬼把戏。」
他三十五岁，皮肤白净光滑，身形清瘦，教养良好，一副颐指气使的派头。他父亲圣约翰·克雷斯韦尔（St. John Cresswell）六十岁，头发花白，留着八字胡和山羊须；是个气度庄重、和蔼的老绅士，只是脾气不小，家族自尊心极强。
「好了好了，」他说，神气一半在想心事、一半漫不经心，「我想也是——可是——」他把信又朗声读了一遍：「『鉴于您是阿拉巴马最有影响的地主之一，兹就一桩机密之事趋前商洽』——嗯——嗯——『一场此国前所未见的资本与权力之大联合』——『棉花制造商与棉花种植者。』……好了好了！当然，我估摸这里头没什么名堂。可是，哈里，我的孩子，这桩种棉花的营生是越来越紧巴了。除非有救兵从天而降——唉，我们只好洗手不干了。黑鬼越来越稀罕、越来越贵，我们实在压不下棉花的成本，压不到有钱赚的数目。我一年比一年把他们卡得紧。我花了二百五十块才从麦克斯韦手里把那个老黑奴和他几个儿子弄过来，一个小的已经跑了。」

===Original===
Harry lighted a cigarette.
“We must drive them more. You’re too easy, father; they understand that. By the way, what did that letter say about a ‘sister’?”
“Says he’s got a sister over at the nigger school whom perhaps we know. I suppose he thinks we dine there occasionally.” The old man chuckled. “That reminds me, Elspeth is sending her girl there.”
“What’s that?” An angry gleam shot into the younger man’s eye.
“Yes. She announced this morning, pert as you please, that she couldn’t tote clothes any more﻿—she had to study.”
“Damn it! This thing is going too far. We can’t keep a maid or a ploughboy on the place because of this devilish school. It’s going to ruin the whole labor system. We’ve been too mild and decent. I’m going to put my foot down right here. I’ll make Elspeth take that girl out of school if I have to horsewhip her, and I’ll warn the school against further interference with our tenants. Here, in less than a week, go two plough-hands﻿—and now this girl.”

===Chinese===
哈里点上一支烟。
「我们得把他们往死里逼。你太手软了，爹；他们摸得清清楚楚。对了，那封信提起一个『妹妹』是怎么回事？」
「说他有个妹妹在那所黑鬼学校，说不定咱们认识。我猜他以为我们隔三岔五上那儿吃饭呢。」老头子嘿嘿一笑。「这倒提醒我了，埃尔斯贝思要把她闺女送去那儿。」
「什么？」一道怒光闪进年轻人的眼里。
「是啊。她今早大大咧咧地宣布，她再也不能送衣裳了——她得念书。」
「妈的！这事闹得太离谱了。就因为这所邪门的学校，我们这地方一个女佣、一个犁地的长工都留不住。整个用工的规矩都要给它毁了。我们向来太宽厚、太客气了。我这儿就得立个规矩。我就是用马鞭抽，也得叫埃尔斯贝思把那丫头从学校领出来，我还要警告那学校，少再插手我们的佃户。瞧瞧，不到一个礼拜，跑了两名犁地的长工——如今又是这丫头。」

===Original===
The old man smiled.
“You’ll hardly miss any work Zora does,” he said.
“I’ll make her work. She’s giving herself too many damned airs. I know who’s back of this﻿—it’s that nigger we saw talking to the white woman in the field the other day.”
“Well, don’t work yourself up. The wench don’t amount to much anyhow. By the way, though, if you do go to the school it won’t hurt to see this Taylor’s sister and size the family up.”
“Pshaw! I’m going to give the Smith woman such a scare that she’ll keep her hands off our niggers.” And Harry Cresswell rode away.

===Chinese===
老头子笑了。
「佐拉干不干活，你也少不了什么。」他说。
「我要叫她干活。她那副臭架子摆得太他妈的足了。我知道这背后是谁——就是那天咱们瞧见的、在田里跟白人女子说话的那个黑鬼。」
「好了，别把自己气着了。那丫头反正也成不了什么气候。不过话又说回来，你要是真去那学校，顺便瞧瞧这个泰勒的妹妹、估估这家人的斤两，倒也无妨。」
「呸！我要把那史密斯女人吓个半死，叫她往后少碰我们的黑鬼。」哈里·克雷斯韦尔说罢，策马而去。

===Original===
Mary Taylor had charge of the office that morning, while Miss Smith, shut up in her bedroom, went laboriously over her accounts. Miss Mary suddenly sat up, threw a hasty glance into the glass and felt the back of her belt. It was﻿—it couldn’t be﻿—surely, it was Mr. Harry Cresswell riding through the gateway on his beautiful white mare. He kicked the gate open rather viciously, did not stop to close it, and rode straight across the lawn. Miss Taylor noticed his riding breeches and leggings, his white linen and white, clean-cut, high-bred face. Such apparitions were few about the country lands. She felt inclined to flutter, but gripped herself.
“Good morning,” she said, a little stiffly.

===Chinese===
那天上午是玛丽·泰勒在办公处当值，史密斯小姐则关在卧室里费力地核对账目。玛丽小姐猛地坐直，匆匆照了照镜子，又摸了摸腰带后面。是——不会吧——没错，是哈里·克雷斯韦尔先生骑着那匹漂亮的白马穿过大门而来。他相当凶蛮地把门踢开，也没停下关门，径直骑过草坪。泰勒小姐留意到他的马裤和绑腿、他的白亚麻衫、他那张白皙、轮廓分明、教养良好的脸。这般人物在乡间是难得一见的。她心里有点想慌乱一番，可又强自镇定住了。
「早安。」她说，略带生硬。

===Original===
Mr. Cresswell halted and stared; then lifting the hat which he had neglected to remove in crossing the hall, he bowed in stately grace. Miss Taylor was no ordinary picture. Her brown hair was almost golden; her dark eyes shone blue; her skin was clear and healthy, and her white dress﻿—happy coincidence!﻿—had been laundered that very morning. Her half-suppressed excitement at the sudden duty of welcoming the great aristocrat of the county, gave a piquancy to her prettiness.
“The﻿—devil!” commented Mr. Harry Cresswell to himself. But to Miss Taylor:
“I beg pardon﻿—er﻿—Miss Smith?”
“No﻿—I’m sorry. Miss Smith is engaged this morning. I am Miss Taylor.”
“I cannot share Miss Taylor’s sorrow,” returned Mr. Cresswell gravely, “for I believe I have the honor of some correspondence with Miss Taylor’s brother.” Mr. Cresswell searched for the letter, but did not find it.

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔先生顿住，盯着她看；随后，他举起方才穿过门厅时忘了摘的帽子，风度雍容地一躬到地。泰勒小姐可不是寻常的景象。她那棕发近乎金黄；深色眼眸闪着蓝光；皮肤光洁健康；她那身白裙——巧得很！——恰恰是今早才洗的。她因忽然要接待全县头号贵族这份差使而半抑着兴奋，给她的娇俏平添了几分动人。
「见——鬼！」哈里·克雷斯韦尔先生心里嘀咕。可嘴上对泰勒小姐说的是：
「恕我冒昧——呃——史密斯小姐在吗？」
「不在——很抱歉。史密斯小姐今早有事。我是泰勒小姐。」
「那我可无法与泰勒小姐同感遗憾，」克雷斯韦尔先生一本正经地答道，「因为我想我有幸与泰勒小姐的令兄通过几封信。」克雷斯韦尔先生摸了摸那封信，却没摸着。

===Original===
“Oh! Has John written you?” She beamed suddenly. “I’m so glad. It’s more than he’s done for me this three-month. I beg your pardon﻿—do sit down﻿—I think you’ll find this one easier. Our stock of chairs is limited.”
It was delightful to have a casual meeting receive this social stamp; the girl was all at once transfigured﻿—animated, glowing, lovely; all of which did not escape the caller’s appraising inspection.
“There!” said Mr. Cresswell. “I’ve left your gate gaping.”
“Oh, don’t mind﻿ ﻿… I hope John’s well?”
“The truth is,” confessed Cresswell, “it was a business matter﻿—cotton, you know.”
“John is nothing but cotton; I tell him his soul is fibrous.”
“He mentioned your being here and I thought I’d drop over and welcome you to the South.”

===Chinese===
「哦！约翰给您写信了？」她忽然眉开眼笑。「太好了。这三个月他给我连封信都没写。恕我失礼——请坐——我看这把您坐得舒服些。我们的椅子存货有限。」
一次不期而遇竟得了这般社交上的印证，令人心花怒放；姑娘一下子变了个人——神采飞扬、容光焕发、楚楚动人；这一切都没逃过来客那估量打量的目光。
「哎呀！」克雷斯韦尔先生说。「贵府的大门我让它敞着了。」
「哦，没关系……约翰他好吧？」
「实不相瞒，」克雷斯韦尔坦白道，「是桩生意上的事——棉花，您知道的。」
「约翰除了棉花什么也不是；我跟他说，他的灵魂都是纤维做的。」
「他提起您在这儿，我便想过来看看您，欢迎您到南方来。」

===Original===
“Thank you,” returned Miss Taylor, reddening with pleasure despite herself. There was a real sincerity in the tone. All this confirmed so many convictions of hers.
“Of course, you know how it is in the South,” Cresswell pursued, the opening having been so easily accomplished.
“I understand perfectly.”
“My sister would be delighted to meet you, but﻿—”
“Oh I realize the﻿—difficulties.”
“Perhaps you wouldn’t mind riding by some day﻿—it’s embarrassing to suggest this, but, you know﻿—”
Miss Taylor was perfectly self-possessed.
“Mr. Cresswell,” she said seriously, “I know very well that it wouldn’t do for your sister to call here, and I shan’t mind a bit coming by to see her first. I don’t believe in standing on stupid ceremony.”

===Chinese===
「谢谢您。」泰勒小姐答道，不由自主地因喜悦而脸红。那语气里有真正的诚恳。这一切印证了她那么多的信念。
「当然，您知道南方是什么情形，」克雷斯韦尔趁开场如此轻易便告成功，乘胜追击。
「我完全明白。」
「舍妹很愿意见您，可是——」
「哦，那些——难处，我明白。」
「也许您改日肯顺道驾临——这话我说起来都难为情，可是，您知道的——」
泰勒小姐镇定自若。
「克雷斯韦尔先生，」她正色道，「我很清楚令妹不便登门拜访，我一点也不介意先去拜访她。我才不信那一套迂腐的客套。」

===Original===
Cresswell thanked her with quiet cordiality, and suggested that when he was driving by he might pick her up in his gig some morning. Miss Taylor expressed her pleasure at the prospect. Then the talk wandered to general matters﻿—the rain, the trees, the people round about, and, inevitably﻿—the Negro.
“Oh, by the by,” said Mr. Cresswell, frowning and hesitating over the recollection of his errand’s purpose, “there was one matter”﻿—he paused. Miss Taylor leant forward, all interest. “I hardly know that I ought to mention it, but your school﻿—”
This charming young lady disarmed his truculent spirit, and the usually collected and determined young man was at a loss how to proceed. The girl, however, was obviously impressed and pleased by his evidence of interest, whatever its nature; so in a manner vastly different from the one he had intended to assume, he continued:
“There is a way in which we may be of service to you, and that is by enlightening you upon points concerning which the nature of your position﻿—both as teacher and socially﻿—must keep you in the dark.”

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔不动声色地热忱道谢，又提议，改日他赶车路过时，哪天早上可以用马车顺道接她。泰勒小姐对此前景表示欣悦。随后话题便漫开了去——谈雨、谈树、谈左邻右舍，以及，不可避免地——谈黑人。
「哦，对了，」克雷斯韦尔先生皱着眉、犹犹豫豫地想起此行的目的，「有件事——」他顿了顿。泰勒小姐身子前倾，饶有兴致。「我都不知道该不该提，可是贵校——」
这位迷人的年轻小姐卸下了他那一身戾气，向来沉着果决的他，竟一时不知如何说起。然而不管这关切是什么性质，姑娘显然为他的这份关切所动、所喜；于是他以一种与他原打算截然不同的口气，接下去说：
「有一个法子，我们也许能帮上您的忙，那就是在某些问题上给您提个醒——而以您的身份，无论身为教师还是在社交上，本必然是蒙在鼓里的。」

===Original===
“For instance, all these Negroes are, as you know, of wretchedly low morals; but there are a few so depraved that it would be suicidal to take them into this school. We recognize the good you are doing, but we do not want it more than offset by utter lack of discrimination in choosing your material.”
“Certainly not﻿—have we﻿—” Miss Mary faltered. This beginning was a bit ominous, wholly unexpected.
“There is a girl, Zora, who has just entered, who﻿—I must speak candidly﻿—who ought not to be here; I thought it but right to let you know.”
“Thank you, so much. I’ll tell Miss Smith.” Mary Taylor suddenly felt herself a judge of character. “I suspected that she was﻿—not what she ought to be. Believe me, we appreciate your interest.”

===Chinese===
「譬如说，这里所有的黑人，您也知道，道德败坏到了极点；可其中有那么几个堕落得无以复加，收进贵校简直是自寻死路。我们承认你们做的善事，可不希望你们选材时毫无甄别，反倒把这点善事一笔抵销了。」
「那当然——难道我们——」玛丽小姐嗫嚅着。这个开头有点不祥，完全出乎她意料。
「有个姑娘，佐拉，刚入学不久，她——我必须直言——她不该待在这儿；我觉得理应让你们知道。」
「多谢，太感谢了。我会告诉史密斯小姐。」玛丽·泰勒忽然觉得自己颇擅识人。「我就疑心她——不大规矩。请相信，您的关照我们心领了。」

===Original===
A few more words, and Mr. Cresswell, after bending courteously over her hand with a deference no New Englander had ever shown, was riding away on his white mare.
For a while Mary Taylor sat very quietly. It was like a breath of air from the real world, this hour’s chat with a well-bred gentleman. She wondered how she had done her part﻿—had she been too eager and school-girlish? Had she met this stately ceremony with enough breeding to show that she too was somebody? She pounced upon Miss Smith the minute that lady entered the office.
“Miss Smith, who do you think has been here?” she burst out enthusiastically.
“I saw him on the lawn.” There was a suspicious lack of warmth in this brief affirmation.

===Chinese===
又寒暄了几句，克雷斯韦尔先生以新英格兰人从未有过的恭谨，对着她的手优雅地一躬到地，便骑着白马离去了。
玛丽·泰勒静静地坐了一会儿。与一位教养良好的绅士这一个钟头的闲谈，宛如从真实世界吹来的一缕清风。她琢磨自己这一面表现如何——是否过于热切、太像个女学生？她以足够的教养回应了这番隆重的礼数，足以显示自己也是个人物吗？史密斯小姐一进办公处，她便扑了上去。
「史密斯小姐，您猜谁来过了？」她热切地脱口而出。
「我在草坪上瞧见他了。」这句简短的确认里，可疑地缺了点热乎劲。

===Original===
“He was so gracious and kindly, and he knows my brother. And oh, Miss Smith! we’ve got to send that Zora right away.”
“Indeed”﻿—the observation was not even interrogatory. The preceptress of the struggling school for Negro children merely evinced patience for the younger woman’s fervency.
“Yes; he says she’s utterly depraved.”
“Said that, did he?” Miss Smith watched her with tranquil regard. Miss Taylor paused.
“Of course, we cannot think of keeping her.”
Miss Smith pursed her lips, offering her first expression of opinion.
“I guess we’ll worry along with her a little while anyhow,” she said.

===Chinese===
「他那么和蔼可亲，他还认识我哥哥。哦，史密斯小姐！我们得赶紧把那个佐拉送走。」
「是吗。」——这话连问都算不上。这位为黑人孩子苦苦支撑学校的女校长，只是对年轻女子的这份热切表现出一份耐心。
「是啊；他说她堕落得无可救药。」
「他是这么说的？」史密斯小姐平静地看着她。泰勒小姐顿了顿。
「当然，我们可想都不能想留下她。」
史密斯小姐抿了抿嘴，第一次表态。
「我想，我们好歹再带着她将就一阵子吧。」她说。

===Original===
The girl stared at Miss Smith in honest, if unpardonable, amazement.
“Do you mean to say that you are going to keep in this school a girl who not only lies and steals but is positively﻿—*immoral*?”
Miss Smith smiled, wholly unmoved.
“No; but I mean that *I* am here to learn from those whose ideas of right do not agree with mine, to discover *why* they differ, and to let them learn of me﻿—so far as I am worthy.”
Mary Taylor was not unappreciative of Miss Smith’s stern high-mindedness, but her heart hardened at this, to her, misdirected zeal. Echo of the spirit of an older day, Miss Smith seemed, to her, to be cramped and paralyzed in an armor of prejudice and sectionalisms. Plain-speaking was the only course, and Mary, if a little complacent perhaps in her frankness, was sincere in her purpose.

===Chinese===
姑娘直瞪着史密斯小姐，那份惊愕虽不可原谅，却是真心实意的。
「您是说，您打算在这所学校里留下一个不仅撒谎偷窃、而且根本——*不道德*的姑娘？」
史密斯小姐笑了，丝毫不为所动。
「不；但我的意思是，*我*在这儿，正是要从那些是非观与我不同的人身上去学习，去发现*为什么*他们不同，也让他们从我身上学点什么——就我配得上教他们的那一点而言。」
玛丽·泰勒并非不赏识史密斯小姐那份严正的高洁，可面对这份在她看来用错了地方的熱忱，她的心却硬了起来。史密斯小姐像是旧时代精神的回响，在她看来，被偏见和地域之见的一副铠甲束缚得动弹不得。打开天窗说亮话是唯一的出路，而玛丽，尽管坦率之中也许掺了点自得，用意却是诚恳的。

===Original===
“I think, Miss Smith, you are making a very grave mistake. I regard Zora as a very undesirable person from every point of view. I look upon Mr. Cresswell’s visit today as almost providential. He came offering an olive branch from the white aristocracy to this work; to bespeak his appreciation and safeguard the future. Moreover,” and Miss Taylor’s voice gathered firmness despite Miss Smith’s inscrutable eye, “moreover, I have reason to know that the disposition﻿—indeed, the plan﻿—in certain quarters to help this work materially depends very largely on your willingness to meet the advances of the Southern whites halfway.”
She paused for a reply or a question. Receiving neither, she walked with dignity up the stairs. From her window she could see Cresswell’s straight shoulders, as he rode toward town, and beyond him a black speck in the road. But she could not see the smile on Mr. Cresswell’s lips, nor did she hear him remark twice, with seeming irrelevance, “The devil!”

===Chinese===
「我以为，史密斯小姐，您正在犯一个极严重的错误。从哪一方面看，我都把佐拉视作一个极不可取的人。我把克雷斯韦尔先生今日的造访，几乎看作天意。他带来的是白人贵族对这份事业递出的橄榄枝；是来致意、并为未来未雨绸缪的。况且，」尽管史密斯小姐那双眼深不可测，泰勒小姐的嗓音却愈发坚定，「况且，我有理由知道，某些方面资助这项事业的意向——乃至计划——很大程度上，取决于您肯不肯迎南方白人一半的路。」
她停下来，等一个答复或一个问题。两者都没等到，她便端端庄庄地上了楼。从窗口她能望见克雷斯韦尔挺直的肩头，他正策马朝镇上去，再过去，路上有一个黑点。可她看不见克雷斯韦尔先生嘴角的笑，也没听见他似乎不着边际地念了两遍：「见鬼！」

===Original===
The rider, being closer to it, recognized in Mary Taylor’s “black speck” Bles Alwyn walking toward him rapidly with axe and hoe on shoulder, whistling merrily. They saw each other almost at the same moment and whistle and smile faded. Mr. Cresswell knew the Negro by sight and disliked him. He belonged in his mind to that younger class of half-educated blacks who were impudent and disrespectful toward their superiors, not even touching his hat when he met a white man. Moreover, he was sure that it was Miss Taylor with whom this boy had been talking so long and familiarly in the cotton-field last Spring﻿—an offence doubly heinous now that he had seen Miss Taylor.
His first impulse was to halt the Negro then and there and tell him a few plain truths. But he did not feel quarrelsome at the moment, and there was, after all, nothing very tangible to justify a berating. The fellow’s impudence was sure to increase, and then! So he merely reined his horse to the better part of the footpath and rode on.

===Chinese===
骑马的人离那黑点近些，认出玛丽·泰勒所谓的「黑点」正是布莱斯·阿尔文，肩上扛着斧头和锄头，快步朝他走来，嘴里欢快地吹着口哨。两人几乎同时看见对方，口哨和笑容都淡了下去。克雷斯韦尔先生认得这个黑人的模样，而且讨厌他。在他心里，这人属于那一类半受教育的年轻黑人，对上人放肆无礼，碰见白人连帽子都不摘。况且，他确信今春在棉田里跟这个小伙子谈得那么久、那么熟络的，正是泰勒小姐——如今他见过泰勒小姐了，这桩罪过便加倍地不可饶恕。
他的头一个冲动，便是当场拦下这个黑人，训他几句大实话。可他此刻并没有寻衅的心情，况且毕竟也没什么站得住的把柄好让他发作。这小子的无礼肯定会与日俱增，到那时！于是他只是把马勒到人行道较好的一边，继续前行。

===Original===
Bles, too, was thinking. He knew the well-dressed man with his milk-white face and overbearing way. He would expect to be greeted with raised hat but Bles bit his lips and pulled down his cap firmly. The axe, too, in some indistinct way felt good in his hand. He saw the horse coming in his pathway and stepping aside in the dust continued on his way, neither looking nor speaking.
So they passed each other by, Mr. Cresswell to town, Bles to the swamp, apparently ignorant of each other’s very existence. Yet, as the space widened between them, each felt a more vindictive anger for the other.

===Chinese===
布莱斯也在想心事。他认得这个衣冠楚楚、一张奶白色的脸、飞扬跋扈的男人。照规矩他是该摘帽行礼的，可布莱斯咬着嘴唇，把帽子往下拽了拽。那把斧头，也不知怎的，握在手里有种说不清的踏实。他看见马朝他这条道走来，便在尘土里让到一边，继续赶路，既不看对方，也不说话。
就这样他们擦肩而过，克雷斯韦尔先生朝镇上去，布莱斯朝沼泽去，仿佛全然不知对方的存在。然而，随着两人之间的距离拉大，彼此心头对对方的愤恨，也都愈发毒烈。

===Original===
How dares the black puppy to ignore a Cresswell on the highway? If this went on, the day would surely come when Negroes felt no respect or fear whatever for whites? And then﻿—my God! Mr. Cresswell struck his mare a vicious blow and dashed toward town.
The black boy, too, went his way in silent, burning rage. Why should he be elbowed into the roadside dust by an insolent bully? Why had he not stood his ground? Pshaw! All this fine frenzy was useless, and he knew it. The sweat oozed on his forehead. It wasn’t man against man, or he would have dragged the pale puppy from his horse and rubbed his face in the earth. It wasn’t even one against many, else how willingly, swinging his axe, would have stood his ground before a mob.
No, it was one against a world, a world of power, opinion, wealth, opportunity; and he, the one, must cringe and bear in silence lest the world crash about the ears of his people. He slowly plodded on in bitter silence toward the swamp. But the day was balmy, the way was beautiful; contempt slowly succeeded anger, and hope soon triumphed over all. For yonder was Zora, poised, waiting. And behind her lay the Field of Dreams.

===Chinese===
这个黑崽子竟敢在公路上无视一个克雷斯韦尔？再这么下去，黑人迟早会对白人既无敬畏也无恐惧，那天还会远吗？到那时——我的天！克雷斯韦尔先生狠狠抽了母马一鞭，朝镇上疾驰而去。
那个黑人小伙子，也怀着默默的、灼热的怒火走自己的路。凭什么他要被一个蛮横的恶霸挤到路边的尘土里去？他方才为何没站住脚跟？呸！这番激昂全是白费，他心里清楚。汗珠从他额头渗出。这不是人对人的较量，不然他早把那个白脸小子从马上拽下来，把他的脸按进土里搓了。这甚至不是一人对多人，否则他挥着斧头，多么乐意在一群暴徒面前站稳脚跟。
不，这是一个人对一整个世界，一个由权力、舆论、财富、机会构成的世界；而他，这一个人，却只能卑躬屈膝、默默忍受，唯恐这个世界在他族人的头顶轰然坍塌。他在痛苦的沉默里，一步一步朝沼泽走去。可天气和暖，一路风景宜人；鄙夷慢慢取代了愤怒，希望很快又压倒了一切。因为那边，佐拉正亭亭地立着，等他。而在她身后，便是那片梦之田。

## IX The Planting / IX 播种

===Original===
Zora looked down upon Bles, where he stood to his knees in mud. The toil was beyond exhilaration﻿—it was sickening weariness and panting despair. The great roots, twined in one unbroken snarl, clung frantically to the black soil. The vines and bushes fought back with thorn and bramble. Zora stood wiping the blood from her hands and staring at Bles. She saw the long gnarled fingers of the tough little trees and they looked like the fingers of Elspeth down there beneath the earth pulling against the boy. Slowly Zora forgot her blood and pain. Who would win﻿—the witch, or Jason?
Bles looked up and saw the bleeding hands. With a bound he was beside her.
“Zora!” The cry seemed wrung from his heart by contrition. Why had he not known﻿—not seen before! “Zora, come right out of this! Sit down here and rest.”

===Chinese===
佐拉俯视着布莱斯，他正站在齐膝深的泥里。这劳作早已越过了令人振奋的阶段——那是叫人作呕的疲倦和气喘吁吁的绝望。巨大的树根盘成一团解不开的乱结，死死地抓着黑土。藤蔓和灌木用荆棘和刺丛还击。佐拉站着擦去手上的血，盯着布莱斯。她看见那些坚韧小树长长的、扭曲如节瘤的手指，看着就像埃尔斯贝思的手指，从地底下伸上来，跟这男孩拔河。佐拉慢慢忘了自己的血和痛。谁会赢呢——是女巫，还是伊阿宋？
布莱斯抬起头，看见了那双流血的手。他一个箭步到了她身边。
「佐拉！」这一声喊，仿佛是懊悔从他心底绞出来的。他怎么早不知道——早没看见呢！「佐拉，快从这儿出来！坐这儿歇着。」

===Original===
She looked at him unwaveringly; there was no flinching of her spirit.
“I shan’t do it,” she said. “You’se working, and I’se going to work.”
“But﻿—Zora﻿—you’re not used to such work, and I am. You’re tired out.”
“So is you,” was her reply.
He looked himself over ruefully, and dropping his axe, sat down beside her on a great log. Silently they contemplated the land; it seemed indeed a hopeless task. Then they looked at each other in sudden, unspoken fear of failure.
“If we only had a mule!” he sighed. Immediately her face lighted and her lips parted, but she said nothing. He presently bounded to his feet.

===Chinese===
她毫不动摇地看着他；她的精神没有半点退缩。
「我不歇，」她说。「你在干活，我也要干。」
「可是——佐拉——你不惯干这种活，我惯。你累坏了。」
「你也一样。」她答。
他懊恼地把自己打量了一遍，丢下斧头，在她身边一段大圆木上坐下。两人默默地打量着这片地；这似乎真是桩毫无指望的活儿。接着他们忽然对视，眼里都闪过一种没说出口的、对失败的恐惧。
