「我们要是有头骡子就好了！」他叹道。她的脸当即一亮，嘴唇微张，却什么也没说。他很快又一跃而起。

===Original===
“Never mind, Zora. Tomorrow is Saturday, and I’ll work all day. We just *will* get it done﻿—sometime.” His mouth closed with determination.
“We won’t work any more today, then?” cried Zora, her eagerness betraying itself despite her efforts to hide it.
“*You* won’t,” affirmed Bles. “But I’ve got to do just a little﻿—”
But Zora was adamant: he was tired; she was tired; they would rest. Tomorrow with the rising sun they would begin again.
“There’ll be a bright moon tonight,” ventured Bles.
“Then I’ll come too,” Zora announced positively, and he had to promise for her sake to rest.

===Chinese===
「不要紧，佐拉。明天是礼拜六，我干一整天。我们*一定*能干完——总有那么一天。」他下定了决心，抿紧了嘴。
「那我们今儿个不再干活了？」佐拉嚷道，她竭力掩饰，那份急切还是露了馅。
「*你*不干了，」布莱斯一口咬定。「可我还得再干一点——」
可佐拉寸步不让：他累了；她累了；他们得歇着。明天太阳一出，他们再从头来过。
「今晚会有一轮明月。」布莱斯试探着说。
「那我也来。」佐拉一口断言，他为了她着想，只好答应也歇着。

===Original===
They went up the path together and parted diffidently, he watching her flit away with sorrowful eyes, a little disturbed and puzzled at the burden he had voluntarily assumed, but never dreaming of drawing back.
Zora did not go far. No sooner did she know herself well out of his sight than she dropped lightly down beside the path, listening intently until the last echo of his footsteps had died away. Then, leaving the cabin on her right, and the scene of their toil on her left, she cut straight through the swamp, skirted the big road, and in a half-hour was in the lower meadows of the Cresswell plantations, where the tired stock was being turned out to graze for the night. Here, in the shadow of the wood, she lingered. Slowly, but with infinite patience, she broke one strand after another of the barbed-wire fencing, watching, the while, the sun grow great and crimson, and die at last in mighty splendor behind the dimmer westward forests.

===Chinese===
他们一道沿小径上去，怯生生地分了手。他用忧愁的眼睛目送她掠影而去，对自己主动揽下的这副担子，多少有些不安与困惑，却从未想过退缩。
佐拉没走远。她一确认自己已出了他的视线，便轻盈地在小径旁一蹲，全神贯注地听着，直到他脚步的最后一丝回音也消散殆尽。然后，她把小屋留在右边，把两人劳作的地方留在左边，径直穿过沼泽，绕过大路，半个钟头后便到了克雷斯韦尔种植园下方的草场，疲倦的牲口正被放出来过夜吃草。她就在林子的阴影里流连。她慢慢地、却以无穷的耐心，一根接一根地弄断铁丝围栏的倒刺，一边看着太阳变得又大又红，终于在西方那更朦胧的森林背后，以恢宏的壮丽沉落。

===Original===
The voices of the hands and hostlers grew fainter and thinner in the distance of purple twilight until the last of them disappeared. Silence fell, deep and soft; the silence of a day sinking to sleep. Not until then did Zora steal forth from her hiding-place.
She had chosen her mule long before﻿—a big, black beast, snorting over his pile of corn﻿—and gliding up to him, she gathered his supper into her skirt, found a stout halter, and fed him sparingly as he followed her. Quickly she unfastened the pieces of the fence, led the animal through, and spliced them again; and then, with fox-like caution, she guided her prize through the labyrinthine windings of the swamp. It was dark and haunting, and ever and again rose lonely night cries. The girl trembled a little, but plodded resolutely on until the dim silver disk of the half-moon began to glimmer through the trees. Then she pressed on more swiftly, and fed more scantily, until finally, with the moonlight pouring over them at the black lagoon, Zora attempted to drive the animal into the still waters; but he gave a loud protesting snort and balked. By subtle temptings she gave him to understand that plenty lay beyond the dark waters, and quickly swinging herself to his back she started to ride him up and down along the edge of the lagoon, petting and whispering to him of good things beyond. Slowly her eyes grew wide; she seemed to be riding out of dreamland on some hobgoblin beast.

===Chinese===
帮工和马夫们的声音在紫色的暮霭里越来越细、越来越远，直到最后一人也消失了。寂静降临，深沉而柔软；那是一个白日沉入睡眠的寂静。直到那时，佐拉才从藏身处溜了出来。
她早就挑好了她的骡子——一头黑色的大牲口，正对着一堆玉米打响鼻——她滑到它跟前，把它的晚饭拢进裙裾，找了一根结实的笼头，少量少量地喂它，引它跟着自己走。她飞快地解开栅栏的铁丝，把牲口牵过去，又把铁丝接好；然后，以狐狸般的谨慎，引着她的战利品穿过沼泽迷宫般的曲径。四下里又黑又诡异，不时升起孤寂的夜啼。姑娘微微发抖，却毅然前行，直到半轮月亮那朦胧的银盘开始从树间闪现。这时她加快了脚步，喂得更省，直到最后，在黑色的潟湖边，月光倾泻在他们身上，佐拉试着把这牲口赶进静水里；可它大声抗议地打了个响鼻，死活不肯。她用巧妙的诱惑让它明白，黑水那头有的是好东西，随即灵巧地翻身上了它的背，沿着潟湖边骑上骑下，一边抚慰它，一边对它低语那头的种种好处。慢慢地，她的眼睛越睁越大；她仿佛骑着什么妖怪的坐骑，正从梦境里走出来。

===Original===
Deeper and deeper they penetrated into the dark waters. Now they entered the slime; now they stumbled on hidden roots; but deeper and deeper they waded until at last, turning the animal’s head with a jerk, and giving him a sharp stroke of the whip, she headed straight for the island. A moment the beast snorted and plunged; higher and higher the black still waters rose round the girl. They crept up her little limbs, swirled round her breasts and gleamed green and slimy along her shoulders. A wild terror gripped her. Maybe she was riding the devil’s horse, and these were the yawning gates of hell, black and sombre beneath the cold, dead radiance of the moon. She saw again the gnarled and black and claw-like fingers of Elspeth gripping and dragging her down.
A scream struggled in her breast, her fingers relaxed, and the big beast, stretching his cramped neck, rose in one mighty plunge and planted his feet on the sand of the island.

===Chinese===
他们越走越深地进了那黑水。时而踏进淤泥；时而绊着暗藏的树根；可他们越蹚越深，直到最后，她猛地一拽牲口的头，狠狠抽了一鞭，径直朝那座岛冲去。那牲口扬头打响鼻、往前一纵；黑沉沉的静水在姑娘身边越涨越高。水爬上她的小腿，在她胸口打旋，沿着她的肩头闪着绿莹莹、黏糊糊的光。一阵狂乱的恐惧攫住了她。说不定她骑的是魔鬼的马，而这下面便是地狱张着的大口，在月亮那冰冷、死寂的光辉之下，又黑又沉。她又看见了埃尔斯贝思那扭曲的、黑色的、鹰爪般的手指，正抓住她、把她往下拽。
一声尖叫在她胸膛里挣扎，她的手指松了，那大牲口伸直了发僵的脖子，猛地一跃而起，把蹄子踩在了岛上的沙地上。

===Original===
---
Bles, hurrying down in the morning with new tools and new determination, stopped and stared in blank amazement. Zora was perched in a tree singing softly and beneath a fat black mule was finishing his breakfast.
“Zora﻿—” he gasped, “how﻿—how did you do it?”
She only smiled and sang a happier measure, pausing only to whisper:
“Dreams﻿—dreams﻿—it’s all dreams here, I tells you.”
Bles frowned and stood irresolute. The song proceeded with less assurance, slower and lower, till it stopped, and the singer dropped to the ground, watching him with wide eyes. He looked down at her, slight, tired, scratched, but undaunted, striving blindly toward the light with stanch, unfaltering faith. A pity surged in his heart. He put his arm about her shoulders and murmured:
“You poor, brave child.”

===Chinese===
---
第二天清早，布莱斯拿着新家什、带着新的决心匆匆下来，却怔怔地愣住了。佐拉蹲在一棵树上轻声唱歌，下面一头肥肥的黑骡子正吃完了它的早饭。
「佐拉——」他倒抽一口气，「你——你是怎么做到的？」
她只是笑，唱了一支更欢快的调子，只停下来悄声说：
「梦——梦——这里全是梦，我跟你说过。」
布莱斯皱着眉，进退两难。歌声越来越没底气，越来越慢、越来越低，终于停了，唱歌的人跳到地上，睁着大眼看他。他低头看她——纤瘦、疲惫、遍体鳞伤，却毫不气馁，以一种坚韧的、毫不动摇的信念，盲目地朝光亮处挣扎。一阵怜悯涌上他心头。他伸手搂住她的肩，喃喃道：
「你这可怜的、勇敢的孩子。」

===Original===
And she shivered with joy.
All day Saturday and part of Sunday they worked feverishly. The trees crashed and the stumps groaned and crept up into the air, the brambles blazed and smoked; little frightened animals fled for shelter; and a wide black patch of rich loam broadened and broadened till it kissed, on every side but the sheltered east, the black waters of the lagoon. Late Sunday night the mule again swam the slimy lagoon, and disappeared toward the Cresswell fields. Then Bles sat down beside Zora, facing the fields, and gravely took her hand. She looked at him in quick, breathless fear.
“Zora,” he said, “sometimes you tell lies, don’t you?”

===Chinese===
而她欢喜得战栗起来。
整个礼拜六和礼拜天的一部分，他们发狂似地干活。大树轰然倒下，树桩呻吟着一寸寸被拽出地面，荆棘丛熊熊燃烧、冒着烟；受惊的小动物四散奔逃；一大片黑色的肥沃壤土越拓越宽，直到除了背风的东面之外，每一面都与潟湖的黑水相接。礼拜天深夜，那骡子又一次游过黏糊糊的潟湖，朝克雷斯韦尔的田地方向消失了。然后布莱斯在佐拉身边坐下，面朝那片田，郑重地握住她的手。她以骤然屏息的恐惧看着他。
「佐拉，」他说，「你有时候撒谎，对不对？」

===Original===
“Yes,” she said slowly; “sometimes.”
“And, Zora, sometimes you steal﻿—you stole the pin from Miss Taylor, and we stole Mr. Cresswell’s mule for two days.”
“Yes,” she said faintly, with a perplexed wrinkle in her brows, “I stole it.”
“Well, Zora, I don’t want you ever to tell another lie, or ever to take anything that doesn’t belong to you.”
She looked at him silently with the shadow of something like terror far back in the depths of her deep eyes.
“Always﻿—tell﻿—the truth?” she repeated slowly.
“Yes.”
Her fingers worked nervously.

===Chinese===
「是，」她慢慢地说，「有时候。」
「还有，佐拉，你有时候偷东西——你偷了泰勒小姐的别针，我们还偷了克雷斯韦尔先生的骡子，用了两天。」
「是，」她虚弱地说，眉间一道困惑的褶皱，「我偷了。」
「这个嘛，佐拉，我不要你以后再撒一个谎，也不要你拿任何不属于你的东西。」
她默默地看着他，那双深邃的眼底极深处，有一抹近乎恐惧的阴影。
「永远——说——真话？」她慢慢重复。
「对。」
她的手指紧张地动来动去。

===Original===
“All the truth?” she asked.
He thought a while.
“No,” said he finally, “it is not necessary always to tell all the truth; but never tell anything that isn’t the truth.”
“Never?”
“Never.”
“Even if it hurts me?”
“Even if it hurts. God is good, He will not let it hurt much.”
“He’s a fair God, ain’t He?” she mused, scanning the evening sky.
“Yes﻿—He’s fair, He wouldn’t take advantage of a little girl that did wrong, when she didn’t know it was wrong.”
Her face lightened and she held his hands in both hers, and said solemnly as though saying a prayer:
“I won’t lie any more, and I won’t steal﻿—and﻿—” she looked at him in startled wistfulness﻿—he remembered it in after years; but he felt he had preached enough.

===Chinese===
「全部的真话？」她问。
他想了一会儿。
「不，」他终于说，「不必永远说出全部的真话；但永远不要说任何不是真话的话。」
「永远不？」
「永远不。」
「就算它会伤着我？」
「就算伤着。上帝是好的，他不会让它伤得太重。」
「他是个公正的上帝，对吧？」她若有所思，端详着暮色的天空。
「对——他公正，他不会趁人之危，占一个做了错事的小姑娘的便宜——在她还不知道那是错事的时候。」
她的脸豁然开朗，她双手握住他的手，像念祷文一样郑重地说：
「我不再撒谎了，我也不再偷东西了——还有——」她以惊惶的渴望看着他——多年之后他还记得这一眼；可他觉得，道理讲得够了。

===Original===
“And now for the seed!” he interrupted joyously. “And then﻿—the Silver Fleece!”
That night, for the first time, Bles entered Zora’s home. It was a single low, black room, smoke-shadowed and dirty, with two dingy beds and a gaping fireplace. On one side of the fireplace sat the yellow woman, young, with traces of beauty, holding the white child in her arms; on the other, hugging the blaze, huddled a formless heap, wreathed in coils of tobacco smoke﻿—Elspeth, Zora’s mother.
Zora said nothing, but glided in and stood in the shadows.
“Good evening,” said Bles cheerily. The woman with the baby alone responded.

===Chinese===
「现在去取种子吧！」他欢快地打断。「然后——就是银羊毛了！」
那天夜里，布莱斯头一回走进佐拉的家。那是一间低矮的、黑黑的单间屋子，被烟熏得发暗、脏兮兮的，有两张灰扑扑的床和一个敞着口的壁炉。壁炉一边坐着那个黄皮肤女人，年轻，还残留着几分姿色，怀里抱着那个白人小孩；另一边，一个没形没样的东西蜷成一团抱着火，裹在一圈圈的烟草烟雾里——那便是埃尔斯贝思，佐拉的母亲。
佐拉什么也没说，滑进去，站在阴影里。
「晚上好。」布莱斯欢快地说。只有抱孩子的女人应了一声。

===Original===
“I came for the seed you promised us﻿—the cottonseed.”
The hag wheeled and approached him swiftly, grasping his shoulders and twisting her face into his. She was a horrible thing﻿—filthy of breath, dirty, with dribbling mouth and red eyes. Her few long black teeth hung loosely like tusks and the folds of fat on her chin curled down on her great neck. Bles shuddered and stepped back.
“Is you afeared, honey?” she whispered.
“No,” he said sturdily.
She chuckled drily. “Yes, you is﻿—everybody’s ’feared of old Elspeth; but she won’t hurt you﻿—you’s got the spell;” and wheeling again, she was back at the fire.
“But the seed?” he ventured.
She pointed impressively roofward. “The dark of the moon, boy, the dark of the moon﻿—the first dark﻿—at midnight.” Bles could not wring another word from her; nor did the ancient witch, by word or look, again give the slightest indication that she was aware of his presence.

===Chinese===
「我来取您答应我们的种子——棉籽。」
那老巫婆一转身，飞快地凑上来，抓住他的肩膀，把脸扭到他面前。她是个骇人的东西——口臭扑鼻、肮脏不堪，嘴角淌着口水，一双红眼。她那几颗又长又黑的牙松松地挂着，像獠牙，下巴上的层层肥肉卷到大脖子上。布莱斯一个寒战，退后一步。
「怕了吗，宝贝儿？」她悄声道。
「没有。」他硬邦邦地说。
她干笑了几声。「怕的，你是——人人都怕老埃尔斯贝思；可她不会伤你——你身上有咒；」说罢又一转身，回到了火边。
「可种子呢？」他大着胆子问。
她郑重其事地朝屋顶一指。「月黑的时候，孩子，月黑的时候——头一回调黑——子夜时分。」布莱斯再也从她嘴里榨不出半个字；这古老的女巫，无论言语或神色，都再没流露出半点察觉到他在场的迹象。

===Original===
With reluctant farewell, Bles turned home. For a space Zora watched him, and once she started after him, but came slowly back, and sat by the fireplace.
Out of the night came voices and laughter, and the sound of wheels and galloping horses. It was not the soft, rollicking laughter of black men, but the keener, more metallic sound of white men’s cries, and Bles Alwyn paused at the edge of the wood, looked back and hesitated, but decided after a moment to go home and to bed.
Zora, however, leapt to her feet and fled into the night, while the hag screamed after her and cursed. There was tramping of feet on the cabin floor, and loud voices and singing and cursing.
“Where’s Zora?” someone yelled, with an oath. “Damn it! where is she? I haven’t seen her for a year, you old devil.”

===Chinese===
布莱斯依依不舍地道了别，转身回家。佐拉目送了他一会儿，有一次她想追上去，却又慢慢折回来，坐在壁炉边。
夜色里传来人声和笑声，还有车轮声和疾驰的马蹄声。那不是黑人软绵绵、闹哄哄的笑，而是更尖利、更金属的白人的叫嚷，布莱斯·阿尔文在林子边停住脚，回头望了望，犹豫了一下，片刻后还是决定回家睡觉。
佐拉却一跃而起，逃进了夜色里，老巫婆在她身后尖叫、咒骂。小屋的地板上响起杂沓的脚步，还有高声的谈笑、歌唱与咒骂。
「佐拉呢？」有人破口大骂地嚷道。「妈的！她人呢？我整整一年没见着她了，你这老妖婆。」

===Original===
The hag whimpered and snarled. Far down in the field of the Fleece, Zora lay curled beneath a tall dark tree asleep. All night there was coming and going in the cabin; the talk and laughter grew loud and boisterous, and the red fire glared in the night.
---
The days flew by and the moon darkened. In the swamp, the hidden island lay spaded and bedbed, and Bles was throwing up a dyke around the edge; Zora helped him until he came to the black oak at the western edge. It was a large twisted thing with one low flying limb that curled out across another tree and made a mighty seat above the waters.
“Don’t throw the dirt too high there,” she begged; “it’ll bring my seat too near the earth.”
He looked up.
“Why, it’s a throne,” he laughed.
“It needs a roof,” he whimsically told her when his day’s work was done. Deftly twisting and intertwining the branches of tree and bush, he wove a canopy of living green that shadowed the curious nest and warded it snugly from wind and water.

===Chinese===
老巫婆呜咽着、狺狺地叫。而在遥远的羊毛田那头，佐拉蜷在一棵高大黝黑的树下睡着了。整夜小屋里人来人往；谈笑声越来越响、越来越放肆，红色的火光在夜色里熊熊燃烧。
---
日子飞逝，月亮一点点暗了下去。沼泽里，那座隐秘的岛已翻好、整好了畦，布莱斯正在四周垒一道堤；佐拉帮着他，直垒到西边那棵黑橡树前。那是一棵粗大的、扭曲的树，有一根低低横出的枝桠，弯到另一棵树上，在水面上方结成一把结实的座椅。
「那儿的土别垒太高，」她央求道，「会把我的座位弄得离地太近的。」
他抬头一看。
「哎呀，这是一把宝座呢。」他笑道。
「它还缺个顶。」他干完一天的活，异想天开地告诉她。他灵巧地把树和灌木的枝条扭结、交织，编出一顶活的绿叶华盖，给这个奇特的窝遮了荫，把风雨都舒舒服服地挡在外头。

===Original===
Early next morning Bles slipped down and improved the nest; adding footrests to make the climbing easy, peepholes east and west, a bit of carpet over the bark, and on the rough main trunk, a little picture in blue and gold of Bougereau’s Madonna. Zora sat hidden and alone in silent ecstasy. Bles peeped in﻿—there was not room to enter: the girl was staring silently at the Madonna. She seemed to feel rather than hear his presence, and she inquired softly:
“Who’s it, Bles?”
“The mother of God,” he answered reverently.
“And why does she hold a lily?”
“It stands for purity﻿—she was a good woman.”
“With a baby,” Zora added slowly.
“Yes﻿—” said Bles, and then more quickly﻿—“It is the Christ Child﻿—God’s baby.”

===Chinese===
第二天一大早，布莱斯溜下来，把这个窝又改进了一番：添了脚踏好爬上去，开了东边和西边的瞭望孔，树皮上铺了一小块毯子，还在粗糙的主干上贴了一幅蓝金两色的小画——布格罗（Bougereau）画的圣母。佐拉独自藏在里面，默默出神。布莱斯探头一瞧——里头没地方进去：姑娘正默默凝视着圣母。她似乎是感觉到、而非听见他在，便轻声问：
「是谁，布莱斯？」
「是上帝的母亲。」他恭恭敬敬地答。
「她为什么拿着一朵百合？」
「那代表纯洁——她是个好女人。」
「抱着个孩子。」佐拉慢慢补充。
「对——」布莱斯说，随即又更快地接下去——「那是圣婴——上帝的婴孩。」

===Original===
“God is the father of all the little babies, ain’t He, Bles?”
“Why, yes﻿—yes, of course; only this little baby didn’t have any other father.”
“Yes, I know one like that,” she said﻿—and then she added softly: “Poor little Christ-baby.”
Bles hesitated, and before he found words Zora was saying:
“How white she is; she’s as white as the lily, Bles; but﻿—I’m sorry she’s white﻿—Bles, what’s purity﻿—just whiteness?”
Bles glanced at her awkwardly but she was still staring wide-eyed at the picture, and her voice was earnest. She was now so old and again so much a child, an eager questioning child, that there seemed about her innocence something holy.

===Chinese===
「上帝是所有小娃娃的父亲，对吧，布莱斯？」
「哎呀，是——是，当然；只不过这个小娃娃没有别的父亲。」
「嗯，我认识一个这样的，」她说——随即又轻声补了一句：「可怜的小基督娃娃。」
布莱斯迟疑了一下，他还没找到话，佐拉已经说道：
「她多白啊；她白得跟那朵百合一样，布莱斯；可是——我很难过她是白的——布莱斯，纯洁是什么——就是白吗？」
布莱斯尴尬地瞟了她一眼，可她还睁大眼盯着那幅画，声音很认真。她此刻如此老成，转眼又如此像个孩子，一个急切发问的孩子，以至她的天真里似乎带着某种神圣。

===Original===
“It means,” he stammered, groping for meanings﻿—“it means being good﻿—just as good as a woman knows how.”
She wheeled quickly toward him and asked him eagerly:
“Not better﻿—not better than she knows, but just as good, in﻿—lying and stealing and﻿—and everything?”
Bles smiled.
“No﻿—not better than she knows, but just as good.”
She trembled happily.
“I’m﻿—pure,” she said, with a strange little breaking voice and gesture. A sob struggled in his throat.
“Of course you are,” he whispered tenderly, hiding her little hands in his.
“I﻿—I was so afraid﻿—sometimes﻿—that I wasn’t,” she whispered, lifting up to him her eyes streaming with tears. Silently he kissed her lips.

===Chinese===
「它的意思是，」他结结巴巴地摸索着含义——「意思是做个好人——尽一个女子所知所能地好。」
她飞快地转向他，急切地问：
「不是更好——不是比她所知的更好，而是尽她所知地好，在——撒谎和偷东西，还有——还有一切事情上？」
布莱斯笑了。
「对——不是比她所知的更好，而是尽她所知地好。」
她幸福地颤抖起来。
「我——是纯洁的。」她说，嗓音和手势都带着一种奇异的小小的破碎。一声哽咽在他喉咙里挣扎。
「你当然是。」他柔声低语，把她的小手攥进自己手里。
「我——我好害怕——有时候——怕自己不是。」她悄声道，抬起一双泪眼望着他。他默默地吻了她的唇。

===Original===
From that day on they walked together in a new world. No revealing word was spoken; no vows were given, none asked for; but a new bond held them. She grew older, quieter, taller, he humbler, more tender and reverent, as they toiled together.
So the days passed. The sun burned in the heavens; but the silvered glory of the moon grew fainter and fainter and each night it rose later than the night before. Then one day Zora whispered:
“Tonight!”

===Chinese===
从那天起，他们一同走在一个崭新的世界里。没有一句挑明的话；没有誓言，也没人讨要誓言；却有一份新的羁绊拴住了他们。她变得老成了、沉静了、长高了，他变得更谦卑、更温柔、更虔敬，他们这样并肩劳作着。
日子就这样过去。太阳在天庭燃烧；可月亮那镀银的辉光却越来越淡，每夜都比前一夜升起得更晚。终于有一天，佐拉悄声道：
「今夜！」

===Original===
Bles came to the cabin, and he and Zora and Elspeth sat silently around the fireplace with its meagre embers. The night was balmy and still; only occasionally a wandering breeze searching the hidden places of the swamp, or the call and song of night birds, jarred the stillness. Long they sat, until the silence crept into Bles’s flesh, and stretching out his hand, he touched Zora’s, clasping it.
After a time the old woman rose and hobbled to a big black chest. Out of it she brought an old bag of cotton seed﻿—not the white-green seed which Bles had always known, but small, smooth black seeds, which she handled carefully, dipping her hands deep down and letting them drop through her gnarled fingers. And so again they sat and waited and waited, saying no word.
Not until the stars of midnight had swung to the zenith did they start down through the swamp. Bles sought to guide the old woman, but he found she knew the way better than he did. Her shadowy figure darting in and out among the trunks till they crossed the tree bridge, moved ever noiselessly ahead.

===Chinese===
布莱斯来到小屋，他和佐拉、埃尔斯贝思围着只剩微弱余烬的壁炉默默坐着。夜和暖、宁静；只有偶尔一阵游荡的微风在沼泽的隐秘之处搜寻，或夜鸟的啼叫与歌声，打破一下寂静。他们坐了很久，直到那寂静爬进了布莱斯的肉里，他伸出手，触到佐拉的手，握住它。
过了一会儿，老妇人起身，一瘸一拐地走到一口黑色的大箱子前。她从里头掏出一只旧棉籽袋——不是布莱斯一向熟识的那种白绿色的籽，而是小小的、光滑的黑色种子，她小心翼翼地捧着，把手深深地插进去，让种子从她扭曲的指缝间漏下。于是他们又坐下等啊等啊，一言不发。
直到子夜的星辰转到了天顶，他们才动身穿过沼泽下去。布莱斯想给老妇人引路，却发现她比他还认得路。她那影影绰绰的身影在树干间忽隐忽现，直到他们过了那座树桥，始终无声地走在前头。

===Original===
She motioned the boy and girl away to the thicket at the edge, and stood still and black in the midst of the cleared island. Bles slipped his arm protectingly around Zora, glancing fearfully about in the darkness. Slowly a great cry rose and swept the island. It struck madly and sharply, and then died away to uneasy murmuring. From afar there seemed to come the echo or the answer to the call. The form of Elspeth blurred the night dimly far off, almost disappearing, and then growing blacker and larger. They heard the whispering *swish-swish* of falling seed; they felt the heavy tread of a great coming body. The form of the old woman suddenly loomed black above them, hovering a moment formless and vast then fading again away, and the *swish-swish* of the falling seed alone rose in the silence of the night.
At last all was still. A long silence. Then again the air seemed suddenly filled with that great and awful cry; its echoing answer screamed afar and they heard the raucous voice of Elspeth beating in their ears:
“*De seed done sowed! De seed done sowed!*”

===Chinese===
她示意男孩和女孩退到边缘的灌木丛里，自己一动不动、黑黢黢地站在清理出来的岛中央。布莱斯把胳膊保护性地搂住佐拉，在黑暗里恐惧地四下张望。慢慢地，一声巨大的呼号升起，掠过全岛。它凌厉地、尖锐地击下，随即消逝为不安的低语。远远的，似乎传来这呼号的回声、或是它的应答。埃尔斯贝思的身影在远处模模糊糊地模糊了夜色，几乎消失，随即又变得更黑、更大。他们听见落种的低语般的*沙——沙——*声；他们感到一个庞大的身躯沉重的脚步正在逼近。老妇人的身影忽然在他们上方黑压压地浮现，无形而庞大地悬了一瞬，随即又淡去，只有落种的*沙——沙——*声在夜的寂静里升起。
终于，一切都静了。一段长长的寂静。然后，空气似乎又骤然被那巨大而可怖的呼号充满；它的回声在远方尖叫，他们听见埃尔斯贝思那粗厉的嗓音在耳边擂响：
「*种下去了！种下去了！（De seed done sowed! De seed done sowed!）*」

## X Mr. Taylor Calls / X 泰勒先生造访

===Original===
“Thinking the matter over,” said Harry Cresswell to his father, “I’m inclined to advise drawing this Taylor out a little further.”
The Colonel puffed his cigar and one eye twinkled, the lid of the other being at the moment suggestively lowered.
“Was she pretty?” he asked; but his son ignored the remark, and the father continued:
“I had a telegram from Taylor this morning, after you left. He’ll be passing through Montgomery the first of next month, and proposes calling.”
“I’ll wire him to come,” said Harry, promptly.

===Chinese===
「把这事再琢磨琢磨，」哈里·克雷斯韦尔对父亲说，「我倾向于劝您，把这位泰勒再往外钓一钓。」
上校喷着雪茄，一只眼睛眨了眨，另一只的眼睛这会儿意味深长地垂着眼皮。
「她漂亮吗？」他问；可儿子不理这茬，父亲便接着说：
「你走了之后，我今天早上收到泰勒一封电报。他下月初路过蒙哥马利，提议前来拜访。」
「我给他发电报让他来。」哈里当即说。

===Original===
At this juncture the door opened and a young lady entered. Helen Cresswell was twenty, small and pretty, with a slightly languid air. Outside herself there was little in which she took very great interest, and her interest in herself was not absorbing. Yet she had a curiously sweet way. Her servants liked her and the tenants could count on her spasmodic attentions in time of sickness and trouble.
“Good morning,” she said, with a soft drawl. She sauntered over to her father, kissed him, and hung over the back of his chair.
“Did you get that novel for me, Harry?”﻿—expectantly regarding her brother.
“I forgot it, Sis. But I’ll be going to town again soon.”
The young lady showed that she was annoyed.
“By the by, Sis, there’s a young lady over at the Negro school whom I think you’d like.”
“Black or white?”
“A young lady, I said. Don’t be sarcastic.”

===Chinese===
正说着，门开了，一位年轻小姐走了进来。海伦·克雷斯韦尔（Helen Cresswell）二十岁，娇小漂亮，神态略带慵懒。除了她自己，没什么事能让她太上心，而她对自己的那份上心也算不上投入。可她有一种奇异的甜美做派。仆人们都喜欢她，佃户们生了病、遭了难，也指望得到她时断时续的关照。
「早安。」她拖着软软的长音说。她踱到父亲跟前，吻了吻他，便俯身靠在他椅背上。
「你替我把那本小说带来了吗，哈里？」——满怀期待地看着哥哥。
「我忘了，妹妹。可我不久还要上镇去一趟。」
小姐露出不悦的神色。
「对了，妹妹，黑人学校那边有位年轻小姐，我想你会喜欢她的。」
「黑的还是白的？」
「我说的是一位小姐。别阴阳怪气的。」

===Original===
“I heard you. I did not know whether you were using our language or others’.”
“She’s really unusual, and seems to understand things. She’s planning to call some day﻿—shall you be at home?”
“Certainly not, Harry; you’re crazy.” And she strolled out to the porch, exchanged some remarks with a passing servant, and then nestled comfortably into a hammock. She helped herself to a chocolate and called out musically:
“Pa, are you going to town today?”
“Yes, honey.”
“Can I go?”
“I’m going in an hour or so, and business at the bank will keep me until after lunch.”
“I don’t care, I just must go. I’m clean out of anything to read. And I want to shop and call on Dolly’s friend﻿—she’s going soon.”
“All right. Can you be ready by eleven?”
She considered.
“Yes﻿—I reckon,” she drawled, prettily swinging her foot and watching the treetops above the distant swamp.

===Chinese===
「我听见了。我只是不知道你说的是咱们的语言，还是别人的语言罢了。」
「她真的很特别，好像挺懂事的。她打算改天来拜访——你会在家吗？」
「当然不会在家，哈里；你疯了。」她溜达到门廊上，跟一个路过的仆人说了几句话，便舒舒服服地窝进一张吊床里。她自己拿了一颗巧克力，用唱歌般的嗓音喊道：
「爸，您今天上镇吗？」
「上的，宝贝儿。」
「我能去吗？」
「我一个钟头左右动身，银行里有事要办，得忙到吃过午饭。」
「我不管，我非去不可。我简直一点可读的东西都没了。我要去买东西，还要去看看多莉的朋友——她快走了。」
「行。你十一点前能准备好吗？」
她想了想。
「能——我瞧能。」她拖着长音说，娇娇地荡着脚，望着远处沼泽上方的树梢。

===Original===
Harry Cresswell, left alone, rang the bell for the butler.
“Still thinking of going, are you, Sam?” asked Cresswell, carelessly, when the servant appeared. He was a young, light-brown boy, his manner obsequious.
“Why, yes, sir﻿—if you can spare me.”
“Spare you, you black rascal! You’re going anyhow. Well, you’ll repent it; the North is no place for niggers. See here, I want lunch for two at one o’clock.” The directions that followed were explicit and given with a particularity that made Sam wonder. “Order my trap,” he finally directed.
Cresswell went out on the high-pillared porch until the trap appeared.
“Oh, Harry! I wanted to go in the trap﻿—take me?” coaxed his sister.
“Sorry, Sis, but I’m going the other way.”
“I don’t believe it,” said Miss Cresswell, easily, as she settled down to another chocolate. Cresswell did not take the trouble to reply.

===Chinese===
哈里·克雷斯韦尔独自留下，摇铃叫管家。
「还在想着走，是吗，山姆？」仆人来了，克雷斯韦尔漫不经心地问。他是个年轻的浅棕色皮肤的小伙子，态度卑躬屈膝。
「哎呀，是，先生——要是您能放我走的话。」
「放你走，你这个黑混球！你横竖是要走的。哼，你将来会后悔的；北方可不是黑人待的地方。听好，我一点钟要两个人的午饭。」接下来的吩咐十分明确，事无巨细，倒叫山姆纳闷起来。「把我的轻便马车备好。」他最后吩咐道。
克雷斯韦尔走到高柱门廊上，等着马车出来。
「哦，哈里！我想坐马车去——带上我？」妹妹撒娇。
「对不住，妹妹，我走的是另一条路。」
「我才不信。」克雷斯韦尔小姐随口说道，又靠回去吃巧克力了。克雷斯韦尔懒得搭理她。

===Original===
Miss Taylor was on her morning walk when she saw him spinning down the road, and both expressed surprise and pleasure at the meeting.
“What a delightful morning!” said the schoolteacher, and the glow on her face said even more.
“I’m driving round through the old plantation,” he explained; “won’t you join me?”
“The invitation is tempting,” she hesitated; “but I’ve got just oodles of work.”
“What! on Saturday?”
“Saturday is my really busy day, don’t you know. I guess I could get off; really, though, I suspect I ought to tell Miss Smith.”
He looked a little perplexed; but the direction in which her inclinations lay was quite clear to him.
“It﻿—it would be decidedly the proper thing,” he murmured, “and we could, of course, invite Miss﻿—”
She saw the difficulty and interrupted him:
“It’s quite unnecessary; she’ll think I have simply gone for a long walk.” And soon they were speeding down the silent road, breathing the perfume of the pines.

===Chinese===
泰勒小姐正在做晨间散步，看见他顺着大路飞驰而来，两人都为这次相遇表示了惊喜。
「多美好的早晨啊！」这位女教师说，脸上的光彩说的比这更多。
「我正要绕旧种植园一圈，」他解释道，「您不愿同去吗？」
「这邀请挺诱人，」她犹豫着，「可我有堆积如山的工作。」
「什么！礼拜六还工作？」
「礼拜六才是我真正忙的日子呢，您不知道吗。我估摸我能脱身；不过说实话，我猜我该跟史密斯小姐说一声。」
他看起来有点为难；可她心之所向，他是一清二楚的。
「这——这当然是合宜的做法，」他喃喃道，「我们当然也可以邀请史密斯小——」
她看出了难处，打断他：
「完全没必要；她会以为我不过是去散一个长步罢了。」不多时，他们便在寂静的大路上疾驰，呼吸着松林的芬芳。

===Original===
Now a ride of an early spring morning, in Alabama, over a leisurely old plantation road and behind a spirited horse, is an event to be enjoyed. Add to this a man bred to be agreeable and outdoing his training, and a pretty girl gay with newfound companionship﻿—all this is apt to make a morning worth remembering.
They turned off the highway and passed through long stretches of ploughed and tumbled fields, and other fields brown with the dead ghosts of past years’ cotton standing straggling and weatherworn. Long, straight, or curling rows of ploughers passed by with steaming, struggling mules, with whips snapping and the yodle of workers or the sharp guttural growl of overseers as a constant accompaniment.
“They’re beginning to plough up the land for the cotton-crop,” he explained.
“What a wonderful crop it is!” Mary had fallen pensive.
“Yes, indeed﻿—if only we could get decent returns for it.”

===Chinese===
在阿拉巴马，一个早春清晨，在一条悠闲的旧种植园大路上、驾着一匹精神抖擞的马出游，是一桩赏心乐事。再加上一个自幼受训练讨人欢心的男子、此刻正使出浑身解数超越他的教养，以及一个因新结良伴而欢欣的漂亮姑娘——这一切加在一起，便足以叫一个早晨令人难忘。
他们拐下公路，穿过一片片犁过的、翻得高低不平的田，又穿过另一些褐色的田，里面东倒西歪地立着往年棉花枯死的幽灵般的残株，饱经风雨。一长排一长排笔直的或弯弯曲曲的犁地人走了过去，身后是冒着热气、挣扎着的骡子，鞭子啪啪作响，伴随着犁地人的吆喝，或监工喉间尖锐的呵斥。
「他们开始翻地，种这一季棉花了。」他解释。
「棉花可真是了不起的庄稼！」玛丽陷入了沉思。
「是啊，可不是——要是我们能换来像样的回报就好了。」

===Original===
“Why, I thought it was a most valuable crop.” She turned to him inquiringly.
“It is﻿—to Negroes and manufacturers, but not to planters.”
“But why don’t the planters do something?”
“What can be done with Negroes?” His tone was bitter. “We tried to combine against manufacturers in the Farmers’ League of last winter. My father was president. The pastime cost him fifty thousand dollars.”
Miss Taylor was perplexed, but eager. “You must correspond with my brother, Mr. Cresswell,” she gravely observed. “I’m sure he﻿—” Before she could finish, an overseer rode up. He began talking abruptly, with a quick side-glance at Mary, in which she might have caught a gleam of surprised curiosity.
“That old nigger, Jim Sykes, over on the lower place, sir, ain’t showed up again this morning.”

===Chinese===
「怎么，我一直以为它是最值钱的庄稼呢。」她探询地转向他。
「它值钱——对黑人和工厂老板而言，对种植园主可不值。」
「那种植园主为何不采取点行动呢？」
「跟黑人能有什么办法？」他的语气苦涩。「去冬我们在『农民同盟』里试着联合起来对付工厂老板。我父亲是主席。这场消遣花了他五万块。」
泰勒小姐困惑，却很热切。「您一定要跟我哥哥通信，克雷斯韦尔先生，」她郑重地说，「我肯定他——」话还没说完，一个监工骑马过来。他突兀地开口说起话来，飞快地侧眼瞟了玛丽一下，那目光里她或许捕捉到了一丝惊讶的好奇。
「底下那块地的老黑奴吉姆·赛克斯（Jim Sykes），先生，今早又没露面。」

===Original===
Cresswell nodded. “I’ll drive by and see,” he said carelessly.
The old man was discovered sitting before his cabin with his head in his hands. He was tall, black, and gaunt, partly bald, with tufted hair. One leg was swathed in rags, and his eyes, as he raised them, wore a cowed and furtive look.
“Well, Uncle Jim, why aren’t you at work?” called Cresswell from the roadside. The old man rose painfully to his feet, swayed against the cabin, and clutched off his cap.
“It’s my leg again, Master Harry﻿—the leg what I hurt in the gin last fall,” he answered, uneasily.
Cresswell frowned. “It’s probably whiskey,” he assured his companion, in an undertone; then to the man:
“You must get to the field tomorrow,”﻿—his habitually calm, unfeeling positiveness left no ground for objection; “I cannot support you in idleness, you know.”

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔点点头。「我顺路过去瞧瞧。」他漫不经心地说。
那老人被发现坐在小屋前，双手抱着头。他又高又黑又瘦，半秃了，头发一簇一簇的。一条腿缠着破布，他抬起眼时，眼神怯懦而鬼祟。
「怎么回事，吉姆大叔，你为啥不下地干活？」克雷斯韦尔在路边喊道。老人艰难地站起身，晃了晃靠在小屋上，一把抓下帽子。
「又是我的腿，哈里少爷——去年秋天在轧花机里伤着的那条腿。」他不安地答。
克雷斯韦尔皱眉。「八成是威士忌喝多了。」他低声向同伴断言；随即对那人说：
「你明天务必下地，」——他素来那种冷静、无情的笃定，不留半点反驳的余地——「我可不能养闲人，你知道。」

===Original===
“Yes, Master Harry,” the other returned, with conciliatory eagerness; “I knows that﻿—I knows it and I ain’t shirking. But, Master Harry, they ain’t doing me right ’bout my cabin﻿—I just wants to show you.” He got out some dirty papers, and started to hobble forward, wincing with pain. Mary Taylor stirred in her seat under an involuntary impulse to help, but Cresswell touched the horse.
“All right, Uncle Jim,” he said; “we’ll look it over tomorrow.”
They turned presently to where they could see the Cresswell oaks waving lazily in the sunlight and the white gleam of the pillared “Big House.”
A pause at the Cresswell store, where Mr. Cresswell entered, afforded Mary Taylor an opportunity further to extend her fund of information.

===Chinese===
「是，哈里少爷，」对方陪着笑脸急切地应道，「我知道这个——我知道，我也没偷懒。可是，哈里少爷，他们没好好待我这小屋的事——我就想给您瞧瞧。」他掏出几张脏兮兮的纸，忍着痛一瘸一拐地要往前凑。玛丽·泰勒身不由己地动了动，想下去帮一把，可克雷斯韦尔一策马。
「好啦，吉姆大叔，」他说，「我们明天再来仔细看看。」
他们不久便转到了能望见克雷斯韦尔家橡树在阳光下慵懒摇曳的地方，望见了那柱廊式「大宅」白色的闪光。
在克雷斯韦尔家铺子前停了一下，克雷斯韦尔先生走了进去，这给了玛丽·泰勒一个进一步扩充见闻的机会。

===Original===
“Do you go to school?” she inquired of the black boy who held the horse, her mien sympathetic and interested.
“No, ma’am,” he mumbled.
“What’s your name?”
“Buddy﻿—I’se one of Aunt Rachel’s chilluns.”
“And where do you live, Buddy?”
“I lives with granny, on de upper place.”
“Well, I’ll see Aunt Rachel and ask her to send you to school.”
“Won’t do no good﻿—she done ast, and Mr. Cresswell, he say he ain’t going to have no more of his niggers﻿—”
But Mr. Cresswell came out just then, and with him a big, fat, and greasy black man, with little eyes and soft wheedling voice. He was following Cresswell at the side but just a little behind, hat in hand, head aslant, and talking deferentially. Cresswell strode carelessly on, answering him with good-natured tolerance.

===Chinese===
「你上学吗？」她问那个替他们牵马的黑人男孩，神情同情而关切。
「不上，太太。」他咕哝。
「你叫什么名字？」
「巴迪（Buddy）——我是雷切尔大婶的孩子之一。」
「你住哪儿，巴迪？」
「我跟奶奶住，在上头那块地。」
「好，我去找雷切尔大婶，请她送你上学。」
「没用的——她问过了，克雷斯韦尔先生说，他不能再让他家的黑奴——」
可克雷斯韦尔先生这会儿出来了，跟他一起的是一个又大又肥、油光水滑的黑人，小眼睛，软款款的哄人嗓音。他侧着身、稍稍落后地跟着克雷斯韦尔，手里拿着帽子，歪着头，恭恭敬敬地说着话。克雷斯韦尔大步随意地走着，以和善的宽容之态答着他。

===Original===
The black man stopped with humility before the trap and swept a profound obeisance. Cresswell glanced up quizzically at Miss Taylor.
“This,” he announced, “is Jones, the Baptist preacher﻿—begging.”
“Ah, lady,”﻿—in mellow, unctuous tones﻿—“I don’t know what we poor black folks would do without Mr. Cresswell﻿—the Lord bless him,” said the minister, shoving his hand far down into his pocket.
Shortly afterward they were approaching the Cresswell Mansion, when the young man reined in the horse.
“If you wouldn’t mind,” he suggested, “I could introduce my sister to you.”
“I should be delighted,” answered Miss Taylor, readily.

===Chinese===
那黑人恭恭敬敬地在马车前停下，深深一躬到地。克雷斯韦尔揶揄地瞟了泰勒小姐一眼。
「这一位，」他介绍道，「是琼斯，浸信会的传道人——来化缘的。」
「啊，夫人，」——嗓音圆润、油腻——「我不知道，没有克雷斯韦尔先生，我们这些可怜的黑人可怎么活——主保佑他。」这位牧师说着，把手深深插进口袋里。
不多时他们便驶近了克雷斯韦尔宅邸，年轻人勒住了马。
「您要是不介意的话，」他提议，「我可以把舍妹介绍给您。」
「荣幸之至。」泰勒小姐爽快地答。

===Original===
When they rolled up to the homestead under its famous oaks the hour was past one. The house was a white oblong building of two stories. In front was the high pillared porch, semicircular, extending to the roof with a balcony in the second story. On the right was a broad veranda looking toward a wide lawn, with the main road and the red swamp in the distance.
The butler met them, all obeisance.
“Ask Miss Helen to come down,” said Mr. Cresswell.
Sam glanced at him.
“Miss Helen will be dreadful sorry, but she and the Colonel have just gone to town﻿—I believe her Aunty ain’t well.”
Mr. Cresswell looked annoyed.

===Chinese===
当他们在这所宅子著名的橡树下驶到门前时，已过了一点钟。房子是一栋两层、白色的长方形建筑。前面是高柱门廊，半圆形，一直延伸到屋顶，二楼带阳台。右边是一道宽阔的游廊，朝着一片大草坪，远处是大路和红色的沼泽。
管家迎出来，一躬到地。
「去请海伦小姐下来。」克雷斯韦尔先生说。
山姆瞟了他一眼。
「海伦小姐会非常遗憾的，可她和上校刚刚进城去了——我估摸是她姑姑身子不爽。」
克雷斯韦尔先生面露不悦。

===Original===
“Well, well! that’s too bad,” he said. “But at any rate, have a seat a moment out here on the veranda, Miss Taylor. And, Sam, can’t you find us a sandwich and something cool? I could not be so inhospitable as to send you away hungry at this time of day.”
Miss Taylor sat down in a comfortable low chair facing the refreshing breeze, and feasted her eyes on the scene. Oh, this was life: a smooth green lawn, and beds of flowers, a vista of brown fields, and the dark line of wood beyond. The deft, quiet butler brought out a little table, spread with the whitest of cloths and laid with the brightest of silver, and “found” a dainty lunch. There was a bit of fried chicken breast, some crisp bacon, browned potatoes, little round beaten biscuit, and rose-colored sherbet with a whiff of wine in it. Miss Taylor wondered a little at the bounty of Southern hospitality; but she was hungry, and she ate heartily, then leaned back dreamily and listened to Mr. Cresswell’s smooth Southern *r*’s, adding a word here and there that kept the conversation going and brought a grave smile to his pale lips. At last with a sigh she arose to her feet.
“I must go! What shall I tell Miss Smith! No, no﻿—no carriage; I must walk.” Of course, however, she could not refuse to let him go at least halfway, ostensibly to tell her of the coming of her brother. He expressed again his disappointment at his sister’s absence.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，哎呀！那太遗憾了。」他说。「可无论如何，泰勒小姐，请在外头游廊上坐一会儿。山姆，你能不能给我们找块三明治、来点凉的？这个时候我怎么也不能让您饿着肚子走，那太不近人情了。」
泰勒小姐在一张舒服的矮椅上坐下，迎着沁人的凉风，饱览眼前的景色。啊，这才是生活：一片平整碧绿的草坪，一畦畦的花，褐色的田野向远处铺展，再过去是深色的林带。那手脚麻利、不声不响的管家端出一张小桌，铺着最白的桌布、摆着最亮的银器，又「找」来一顿精致的午餐。有一小块煎鸡胸肉，几片脆培根，烤成金黄的土豆，圆圆的小擀面饼干，还有粉红色的果汁冰糕，里头掺着一缕酒香。泰勒小姐对南方待客之殷略有诧异；可她饿了，便大快朵颐，然后如梦似幻地往后一靠，听着克雷斯韦尔先生圆润的南方口音里那些 r 音，时不时插上一言半语，好让话头不断，引得他那苍白的唇上浮起一丝庄重的微笑。终于她叹了口气，站起身来。
「我得走了！我怎么跟史密斯小姐说呢！不，不——不要马车；我得走回去。」当然，她也不能不让他至少送上一半路——名义上是去告诉她她哥哥要来的消息。他再次为舍妹不在家表示遗憾。

===Original===
Somewhat to Miss Taylor’s surprise Miss Smith said nothing until they were parting for the night, then she asked:
“Was Miss Cresswell at home?”
Mary reddened.
“She had been called suddenly to town.”
“Well, my dear, I wouldn’t do it again.”
The girl was angry.
“I’m not a schoolgirl, but a grown woman, and capable of caring for myself. Moreover, in matter of propriety I do not think you have usually found my ideas too lax﻿—rather the opposite.”
“There, there, dear; don’t be angry. Only I think if your brother knew﻿—”
“He will know in a very few weeks; he is coming to visit the Cresswells.” And Miss Taylor sailed triumphantly up the stairs.

===Chinese===
令泰勒小姐有些意外的是，史密斯小姐一言不发，直到两人夜里道别时，才问了一句：
「克雷斯韦尔小姐在家吗？」
玛丽脸红了。
「她临时被叫进城去了。」
「好吧，亲爱的，这种事下不为例。」
姑娘生了气。
「我又不是女学生，是个成年女子，能照顾好自己。况且，在得体这件事上，您一向不觉得我的想法太松——毋宁说恰恰相反。」
「好了好了，亲爱的；别生气。我只是想，要是你哥哥知道——」
「不出几个礼拜他就会知道的；他要来拜访克雷斯韦尔家。」泰勒小姐得意洋洋地上了楼。

===Original===
But John Taylor was not the man to wait weeks when a purpose could be accomplished in days or hours. No sooner was Harry Cresswell’s telegram at hand than he hastened back from Savannah, struck across country, and the week after his sister’s ride found him striding up the carriageway of the Cresswell home.
John Taylor had prospered since summer. The cotton manufacturers’ combine was all but a fact; Mr. Easterly had discovered that his chief clerk’s sense and executive ability were invaluable, and John Taylor was slated for a salary in five figures when things should be finally settled, not to mention a generous slice of stock﻿—watery at present, but warranted to ripen early.
While Mr. Easterly still regarded Taylor’s larger trust as chimerical, some occurrences of the fall made him take a respectful attitude toward it. Just as the final clauses of the combine agreement were to be signed, there appeared a shortage in the cotton-crop, and prices began to soar. The cause was obviously the unexpected success of the new Farmers’ League among the cotton-growers. Mr. Easterly found it comparatively easy to overthrow the corner, but the flurry made some of the manufacturers timid, and the trust agreement was postponed until a year later. This experience and the persistence of Mr. Taylor induced Mr. Easterly to take a step toward the larger project: he let in some eager outside capital to the safer manufacturing scheme, and withdrew a corresponding amount of Mrs. Grey’s money. This he put into John Taylor’s hands to invest in the South in bank stock and industries with the idea of playing a part in the financial situation there.

===Chinese===
可约翰·泰勒不是那种能等上几礼拜的人——几天、甚至几个钟头能办成的事，他绝不多等。哈里·克雷斯韦尔的电报一到手，他便从萨凡纳（Savannah）赶回来，斜穿乡野，在妹妹那次出游之后的下一个礼拜，便大步走上了克雷斯韦尔家的车道。
入夏以来，约翰·泰勒发了迹。棉纺厂的合并几成定局；伊斯特利先生发现他这位首席职员的眼光与才干无价可估，待事成之后，约翰·泰勒已内定拿五位数的薪水，外加一大笔股份——眼下虽还是虚股，但保准早早兑现。
虽说伊斯特利先生仍把泰勒那个更大的托拉斯计划看作痴人说梦，秋天发生的几件事却让他对此肃然起敬起来。合并协议的最后条款眼看就要签字了，棉花却出现了短缺，价格开始飙升。原因显然是新的「农民同盟」在棉农当中出乎意料的成功。伊斯特利先生发觉推翻这个囤积还算容易，可这一阵骚动让一些厂主胆怯了，托拉斯协议便推迟了一年。这一番经历，加上泰勒先生的坚持，促使伊斯特利先生朝那个更大的计划迈出了一步：他放了一些急切的外部资本进较稳妥的制造业方案，又相应地抽回了格雷太太的一笔钱。这笔钱他交到约翰·泰勒手里，让他在南方投资银行股和实业，意图在南方的金融局势里插上一脚。

===Original===
“It’s a risk, Taylor, of course, and we’ll let the old lady take the risk. At the worst it’s safer than the damned foolishness she has in mind.”
So it happened that John Taylor went South to look after large investments and, as Mr. Easterly expressed it, “to bring back facts, not dreams.” His investment matters went quickly and well, and now he turned to his wider and bigger scheme. He wrote the Cresswells tentatively, expecting no reply, or an evasive one; planning to circle around them, drawing his nets closer, and trying them again later. To his surprise they responded quickly.
“Humph! Hard pressed,” he decided, and hurried to them.
So it was the week after Mary Taylor’s ride that found him at Cresswell’s front door, thin, eagle-eyed, fairly well dressed and radiating confidence.

===Chinese===
「这当然有风险，泰勒，可这风险让老太太去担好了。最不济，也比她脑子里那套该死的蠢主意稳妥。」
于是约翰·泰勒便南下，照看大宗投资，用伊斯特利先生的话说，「带回来的得是事实，不是梦。」他的投资事宜进展又快又顺，如今他转向了他那个更宏大的计划。他试探性地给克雷斯韦尔家写了信，本不指望有回音，或指望一个含糊其辞的答复；盘算着绕着他们兜圈子，把网收紧，日后再试。不料他们很快便回了信。
「哼！被逼到墙角了。」他断定，便匆匆赶来。
于是，在玛丽·泰勒出游的下一个礼拜，他便出现在克雷斯韦尔家的门前——清瘦，鹰眼，衣着还算体面，浑身上下透着自信。

===Original===
“John Taylor,” he announced to Sam, jerkily, thrusting out a card. “Want to see Mr. Cresswell; soon as possible.”
Sam made him wait a half-hour, for the sake of discipline, and then brought father and son.
“Good morning, Mr. Cresswell, and Mr. Cresswell again,” said Mr. Taylor, helping himself to a straight-backed chair. “Hope you’ll pardon this unexpected visit. Found myself called through Montgomery, just after I got your wire; thought I’d better drop over.”
At Harry’s suggestion they moved to the veranda and sat down over whiskey and soda, which Taylor refused, and plunged into the subject without preliminaries.

===Chinese===
「约翰·泰勒。」他朝山姆生硬地自报家门，递出一张名片。「要见克雷斯韦尔先生；越快越好。」
山姆为了立规矩，让他等了半个钟头，才把父子俩请出来。
「早安，克雷斯韦尔先生，还有这一位克雷斯韦尔先生。」泰勒先生说着，给自己拉过一把直背椅坐下。「希望您原谅我这趟不速之访。接到您的电报之后，我正好有事路过蒙哥马利；想着还是过来一趟的好。」
在哈里的提议下，他们挪到游廊上坐下，摆上威士忌苏打——泰勒谢绝了——便直截了当地切入正题。

===Original===
“I’m assuming that you gentlemen are in the cotton business for making money. So am I. I see a way in which you and your friends can help me and mine, and clear up more millions than all of us can spend; for this reason I’ve hunted you up. This is my scheme.
“See here; there are a thousand cotton-mills in this country, half of them in the South, one-fourth in New England, and one-fourth in the Middle States. They are capitalized at six hundred million dollars. Now let me tell you: we control three hundred and fifty millions of that capitalization. The trust is going through capitalization at a billion. The only thing that threatens it is child-labor legislation in the South, the tariff, and the control of the supply of cotton. Pretty big hindrances, you say. That’s so, but look here: we’ve got the stock so placed that nothing short of a popular upheaval can send any Child Labor bill through Congress in six years. See? After that we don’t care. Same thing applies to the tariff. The last bill ran ten years. The present bill will last longer, or I lose my guess﻿—‘specially if Smith is in the Senate.

===Chinese===
「我且假定两位先生做棉花生意是为了挣钱。我也是。我看见了一条路子，您和您的朋友们能帮我和我这边人的忙，挣出我们所有人都花不完的几百万来；正因如此，我才找上了你们。我的计划是这样的。
「您瞧；这个国家有一千家棉纺厂，一半在南方，四分之一在新英格兰，四分之一在中部各州。它们的资本合计六亿元。现在让我告诉您：这六亿里头，有三亿五千万在我们掌控之下。托拉斯将以十亿的资本额成立。唯一威胁它的，是南方的童工立法、关税，以及棉花供给的控制。您要说这是挺大的阻碍。是不错，可您瞧：我们已经把股票布局得妥妥帖帖，除非来一场民变，六年之内别想有任何童工法案在国会通过。明白吗？六年之后我们就不在乎了。关税也是同理。上一个关税案管了十年。眼下这个会管得更久，不然我就不算准了——尤其是如果史密斯进了参议院的话。

===Original===
“Well, then, there remains raw cotton. The connection of cotton-raising and its raw material is too close to risk a manufacturing trust that does not include practical control of the raw material. For that reason we’re planning a trust to include the raising and manufacturing of cotton in America. Then, too, cornering the cotton market here means the whip-hand of the industrial world. Gentlemen, it’s the biggest idea of the century. It beats steel.”
Colonel Cresswell chuckled.
“How do you spell that?” he asked.
But John Taylor was not to be diverted; his thin face was pale, but his gray eyes burned with the fire of a zealot. Harry Cresswell only smiled dimly and looked interested.

===Chinese===
「那么，剩下的就是原棉了。棉花种植与原棉这一原料之间的关系太紧密了，若不把对原料的实际控制包括在内，贸然成立制造业托拉斯是冒险。正因如此，我们筹划的托拉斯，将把美国境内棉花的种植与制造一并纳入。况且，在这里垄断棉市，便等于握住了工业世界的鞭子。两位先生，这是本世纪最大的构想。它胜过钢铁。」
克雷斯韦尔上校嘿嘿一笑。
「这话怎么讲？」他问。
可约翰·泰勒不为所动；他清瘦的脸发白，灰色的眼睛却燃着信徒般的火。哈里·克雷斯韦尔只是隐约一笑，做出一副感兴趣的样子。

===Original===
“Now, again,” continued John Taylor. “There are a million cotton farms in the South, half run by colored people and half by whites. Leave the colored out of account as long as they are disfranchised. The half million white farms are owned or controlled by five thousand wholesale merchants and three thousand big landowners, of whom you, Colonel Cresswell, are among the biggest with your fifty thousand acres. Ten banks control these eight thousand people﻿—one of these is the Jefferson National of Montgomery, of which you are a silent director.”
Colonel Cresswell started; this man evidently had inside information. Did he know of the mortgage, too?
“Don’t be alarmed. I’m safe,” Taylor assured him. “Now, then, if we can get the banks, wholesale merchants, and biggest planters into line we can control the cotton crop.”

===Chinese===
「再者，」约翰·泰勒接着说，「南方有一百万个棉花农场，一半由黑人经营，一半由白人经营。只要黑人还被剥夺选举权，就把他们先撇开不论。那五十万个白人农场，由五千名批发商和三千名大地主拥有或控制——克雷斯韦尔上校，您那五万亩地，使他们当中最大的之一。十家银行控制着这八千人——其中一家便是蒙哥马利的杰斐逊国民银行（Jefferson National），您是它的隐名董事。」
克雷斯韦尔上校一惊；此人显然有内线消息。那笔抵押他知道吗？
「别慌。我靠得住。」泰勒向他保证。「那么，只要我们能让银行、批发商和最大的种植园主站到一条线上，我们就能控制棉花产量。」

===Original===
“But,” objected Harry Cresswell, “while the banks and the large merchants may be possibilities, do you know what it means to try to get planters into line?”
“Yes, I do. And what I don’t know you and your father do. Colonel Cresswell is president of the Farmers’ League. That’s the reason I’m here. Your success last year made you indispensable to our plans.”
“Our success?” laughed Colonel Cresswell, ruefully, thinking of the fifty thousand dollars lost and the mortgage to cover it.
“Yes, sir﻿—success! You didn’t know it; we were too careful to allow that; and I say frankly you wouldn’t know it now if we weren’t convinced you were too far involved and the League too discouraged to repeat the dose.”

===Chinese===
「可是，」哈里·克雷斯韦尔反驳，「银行和大批发商或许还有可能，可您知道要把种植园主拉到一条线上是什么意思吗？」
「我知道。我不知道的，您和令尊知道。克雷斯韦尔上校是农民同盟的主席。这正是我在这里的原因。贵方去年的成功，使你们成为我们计划里不可或缺的一环。」
「我们的成功？」克雷斯韦尔上校苦笑起来，想起那损失的五万块和为弥补亏空而欠下的抵押。
「是，先生——是成功！您不知道罢了；我们当时太小心，不会让您知道；我坦白说，要不是我们确信您陷得太深、同盟又太灰心，断不会再来这么一遭，您现在也不会知道。」

===Original===
“Now, look here, sir,” began Colonel Cresswell, flushing and drawing himself erect.
“There, there, Colonel Cresswell, don’t misunderstand me. I’m a plain man. I’m playing a big game﻿—a tremendous one. I need you, and I know you need me. I find out about you, and my sources of knowledge are wide and unerring. But the knowledge is safe, sir; it’s buried. Last year when you people curtailed cotton acreage and warehoused a big chunk of the crop you gave the mill men the scare of their lives. We had a hasty conference and the result was that the bottom fell out of your credit.”
Colonel Cresswell grew pale. There was a disquieting, relentless element in this unimpassioned man’s tone.
“You failed,” pursued John Taylor, “because you couldn’t get the banks and the big merchants behind you. We’ve got ’em behind us﻿—with big chunks of stock and a signed ironclad agreement. You can wheel the planters into line﻿—will you do it?” John Taylor bent forward tense but cool and steel-like. Harry Cresswell laid his hand on his father’s arm and said quietly:
“And where do we come in?”

===Chinese===
「您听我说，先生，」克雷斯韦尔上校涨红了脸，挺直了身子。
「好了好了，克雷斯韦尔上校，别误会我。我是个直人。我在下一盘大棋——一盘惊天大棋。我需要您，我也知道您需要我。我把您的情况摸得一清二楚，我的情报来源广，准。可这些情报很安全，先生；都埋着呢。去年你们削减棉田、把一大块收成囤进仓库，可把厂主们吓破了胆。我们匆匆开了个会，结果就是——你们的信贷一落千丈。」
克雷斯韦尔上校脸都白了。这个不动声色的人的语调里，有一种令人不安的、无情的成分。
「你们失败了，」约翰·泰勒接着说，「因为你们背后没有银行和大批发商。我们背后有他们——用大把的股票和一份签了字的铁板协议。你们能把种植园主拉到一条线上——干不干？」约翰·泰勒前倾着身子，紧绷、却冷静如钢。哈里·克雷斯韦尔把手放在父亲手臂上，平静地说：
「那我们呢？」

===Original===
“That’s business,” affirmed John Taylor. “You and two hundred and fifty of the biggest planters come in on the ground-floor of the two-billion-dollar All-Cotton combine. It can easily mean two million to you in five years.”
“And the other planters?”
“They come in for high-priced cotton until we get our grip.”
“And then?”
The quiet question seemed to invoke a vision for John Taylor; the gray eyes took on the faraway look of a seer; the thin, bloodless lips formed a smile in which there was nothing pleasant.
“They keep their mouths shut or we squeeze ’em and buy the land. We propose to own the cotton belt of the South.”
Colonel Cresswell started indignantly from his seat.
“Do you think﻿—by God, sir!﻿—that I’d betray Southern gentlemen to﻿—”

===Chinese===
「这才像谈生意。」约翰·泰勒一口咬定。「您和二百五十个最大的种植园主，将以原始股东的身份，加入这个二十亿元的全棉托拉斯。五年之内，轻轻松松意味着您能进账两百万。」
「那其余的种植园主呢？」
「在我们牢牢攥住之前，他们能卖出好价钱的棉花。」
「然后呢？」
这平静的一问，仿佛在约翰·泰勒眼前唤起了一幅图景；那双灰眼染上了先知般的悠远神情；那两片薄的、无血的嘴唇，弯出一个毫无悦人之意的微笑。
「他们要么闭嘴，要么就被我们挤垮，然后把地卖给我们。我们打算把南方的产棉带据为己有。」
克雷斯韦尔上校愤然从座位上跳起来。
「您以为——天哪，先生！——我会出卖南方的绅士们，去——」

===Original===
But Harry’s hand and impassive manner restrained him; he cooled as suddenly as he had flared up.
“Thank you very much, Mr. Taylor,” he concluded; “we’ll consider this matter carefully. You’ll spend the night, of course.”
“Can’t possibly﻿—must catch that next train back.”
“But we must talk further,” the Colonel insisted. “And then, there’s your sister.”
“By Jove! Forgot all about Mary.” John Taylor after a little desultory talk, followed his host upstairs.
The next afternoon John Taylor was sitting beside Helen Cresswell on the porch which overlooked the terrace, and was, on the whole, thinking less of cotton than he had for several years. To be sure, he was talking cotton; but he was doing it mechanically and from long habit, and was really thinking how charming a girl Helen Cresswell was. She fascinated him. For his sister Taylor had a feeling of superiority that was almost contempt. The idea of a woman trying to understand and argue about things men knew! He admired the dashing and handsome Miss Easterly, but she scared him and made him angrily awkward. This girl, on the other hand, just lounged and listened with an amused smile, or asked the most childlike questions. She required him to wait on her quite as a matter of course﻿—to adjust her pillows, hand her the bonbons, and hunt for her lost fan. Mr. Taylor, who had not waited on anybody since his mother died, and not much before, found a quite inexplicable pleasure in these little domesticities. Several times he took out his watch and frowned; yet he managed to stay with her quite happily.

===Chinese===
可哈里的手和不动声色的神态制止了他；他像方才发火一样突如其来地冷了下来。
「多谢您了，泰勒先生，」他总结道，「这事我们会仔细考虑。您今晚当然要住下。」
「不成啊——得赶下一班火车回去。」
「可这事我们还得再谈。」上校坚持。「况且，还有令妹呢。」
「哎哟！我把玛丽忘得一干二净。」约翰·泰勒有一搭没一搭地扯了几句，便随主人上楼去了。
第二天下午，约翰·泰勒坐在能俯瞰露台的那道门廊上，挨着海伦·克雷斯韦尔，总的说来，他这几年来想到棉花的时候都没有今天这么少。当然，他嘴上是在谈棉花；可那只是机械地、出于长久习惯地谈着，他心里真正想的是：海伦·克雷斯韦尔这姑娘，多么迷人啊。她迷住了他。对他妹妹，泰勒怀有一种近乎轻蔑的优越感。女人竟想弄懂、竟想就男人们了然于胸的事情来争论！他欣赏那位张扬漂亮的伊斯特利小姐，可她叫他害怕，叫他恼怒得笨手笨脚。眼前这个姑娘却不同，她只是懒洋洋地歪着，带着觉得有趣儿的笑听着，要不就问些最孩子气的问题。她理直气壮地要他伺候她——给她摆弄靠垫，递糖给她，替她找丢了扇子。泰勒先生自母亲去世后便没伺候过谁——母亲生前也伺候得不多——却在这些小小的家常里，尝到一种说不清的快活。他好几回掏出表来皱眉；却还是乐呵呵地陪着她。

===Original===
On her part Miss Cresswell was vastly amused. Her acquaintance with men was not wide, but it was thorough so far as her own class was concerned. They were all well-dressed and leisurely, fairly good looking, and they said the same words and did the same things in the same way. They paid her compliments which she did not believe, and they did not expect her to believe. They were charmingly deferential in the matter of dropped handkerchiefs, but tyrannical of opinion. They were thoughtful about candy and flowers, but thoughtless about feelings and income. Altogether they were delightful, but cloying. This man was startlingly different; ungainly and always in a desperate, unaccountable hurry. He knew no pretty speeches, he certainly did not measure up to her standard of breeding, and yet somehow he was a gentleman. All this was new to Helen Cresswell, and she liked it.
Meanwhile the men above-stairs lingered in the Colonel’s office﻿—the older one perturbed and sputtering, the younger insistent and imperturbable.

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔小姐这边呢，则大大地觉着有趣。她认识的男子不多，可就她本阶级而言，认识得够透。他们个个衣着得体、从容不迫，长相还算周正，说的话一模一样，做事也如出一辙。他们恭维她，她不信，他们也不指望她信。手帕掉地这种事上，他们殷勤得迷人，可一论起观点，又专横得厉害。他们贴心地送糖送花，却对别人的感受和收入漫不经心。总而言之，他们可爱，却腻人。这个男人则截然不同地叫人吃惊；笨手笨脚，永远处于一种莫名其妙的、拼命的赶时间状态。他不会说漂亮话，他的教养显然够不上她的标准，可不知怎的，他是个绅士。这一切对海伦·克雷斯韦尔来说都是新鲜的，而她喜欢。

===Original===
“The fact is, father,” he was saying, “as you yourself have said, one bad crop of cotton would almost ruin us.”
“But the prospects are good.”
“What are prospects in March? No, father, this is the situation﻿—three good crops in succession will wipe off our indebtedness and leave us facing only low prices and a scarcity of niggers; on the other hand﻿—” The father interrupted impatiently.
“Yes, on the other hand, if we plunge deeper in debt and betray our friends we may come out millionaires or﻿—paupers.”
“Precisely,” said Harry Cresswell, calmly. “Now, our plan is to take no chances; I propose going North and looking into this matter thoroughly. If he represents money and has money, and if the trust has really got the grip he says it has, why, it’s a case of crush or get crushed, and we’ll have to join them on their own terms. If he’s bluffing, or the thing looks weak, we’ll wait.”

===Chinese===
与此同时，楼上的两个男人还在上校的书房里磨蹭——年长的那个心神不宁、嘟嘟囔囔，年轻的那个寸步不让、镇定自若。
「事实是，父亲，」他正说着，「您自己也说过，棉花只要一季歉收，就差不多能毁了咱们。」
「可前景不错啊。」
「三月里谈什么前景？不，父亲，情况是这样的——连着三季丰收，就能还清咱们的债，到时候咱们面对的，不过是低价和黑奴短缺罢了；可另一方面——」父亲不耐烦地打断。
「是啊，另一方面，要是我们更深地陷入债务、出卖朋友，我们要么变成百万富翁，要么——变成穷光蛋。」
「一点不错。」哈里·克雷斯韦尔平静地说。「这样，咱们的计划是不冒任何风险；我提议北上，把这事彻查一番。要是他后面真有资本、真有钱，要是那个托拉斯真有他说的那么紧的把控，那么，这就是个要么挤人、要么被挤的局面，我们只能按他们的条件入伙。要是他在虚张声势，或者这事看着不牢靠，我们就等着。」

===Original===
It all ended as matters usually did end, in Harry’s having his way. He came downstairs, expecting, indeed, rather hoping, to find Taylor impatiently striding to and fro, watch in hand; but here he was, ungainly, it might be, but quite docile, drawing the picture of a power-loom for Miss Cresswell, who seemed really interested. Harry silently surveyed them from the door, and his face lighted with a new thought.
Taylor, espying him, leapt to his feet and hauled out his watch.
“Well﻿—I﻿—” he began lamely.
“No, you weren’t either,” interrupted Harry, with a laugh that was unmistakably cordial and friendly. “You had quite forgotten what you were waiting for﻿—isn’t that so, Sis?”
Helen regarded her brother through her veiling lashes: what meant this sudden assumption of warmth and amiability?
“No, indeed; he was raging with impatience,” she returned.

===Chinese===
事情最后照例是哈里说了算。他下了楼，本指望——毋宁说是希望——看见泰勒手里捏着表、不耐烦地踱来踱去；可他却在这儿，也许是笨手笨脚的，却相当温顺，正给克雷斯韦尔小姐画一幅动力织机的图，姑娘似乎真看得津津有味。哈里在门口默默打量着他们俩，脸上因一个新的念头亮了起来。
泰勒一瞧见他，便一跃而起，掏出表来。
「这个——我——」他笨拙地开口。
「得了吧，您才没有呢。」哈里打断，那笑是分明的热忱与友善。「您早把等什么忘得一干二净了——是不是，妹妹？」
海伦透过垂帘般的睫毛看着哥哥：他这突如其来的热络和蔼，是什么意思？
「哪儿的话；他正急得火冒三丈呢。」她答。

===Original===
“Why, Miss Cresswell, I﻿—I﻿—” John Taylor forsook social amenities and pulled himself together. “Well,” shortly, “now for that talk﻿—ready?” And quite forgetting Miss Cresswell, he bolted into the parlor.
“The decision we have come to is this,” said Harry Cresswell. “We are in debt, as you know.”
“Forty-nine thousand, seven hundred and forty-two dollars and twelve cents,” responded Taylor; “in three notes, due in twelve, twenty-four, and thirty-six months, interest at eight percent, held by﻿—”
The Colonel snorted his amazement, and Harry Cresswell cut in:
“Yes,” he calmly admitted; “and with good crops for three years we’d be all right; good crops even for two years would leave us fairly well off.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，克雷斯韦尔小姐，我——我——」约翰·泰勒顾不上社交客套，把自己拉回了正题。「好，」他简短地说，「现在谈正事——准备好了吗？」说完便把克雷斯韦尔小姐忘到了脑后，一头冲进客厅。
「我们的决定是这样的，」哈里·克雷斯韦尔说。「我们欠了债，这您知道。」
「四万九千七百四十二块一毛二，」泰勒应声而答；「分三张期票，十二、二十四、三十六个月到期，年息八厘，持票人是——」
上校惊讶得直哼了一声，哈里·克雷斯韦尔插进来：
「是，」他平静地承认，「可只要三年丰收，我们就没事；哪怕两年丰收，我们的日子也还算过得去。」

===Original===
“You mean it would relieve you of the present stringency and put you face to face with the falling price of cotton and rising wages,” was John Taylor’s dry addendum.
“Rising price of cotton, you mean,” Harry corrected.
“Oh, temporarily,” John Taylor admitted.
“Precisely, and thus postpone the decision.”
“No, Mr. Cresswell. I’m offering to let you in on the ground floor﻿—*now*﻿—not next year, or year after.”
“Mr. Taylor, have you any money in this?”
“Everything I’ve got.”

===Chinese===
「您的意思是，这能暂时缓过这口气，然后您就直接面对棉价下跌和工资上涨了。」约翰·泰勒干巴巴地补了一句。
「您是想说棉价上涨吧。」哈里纠正。
「哦，暂时的。」约翰·泰勒承认。
「一点不错，于是就把决定推迟了。」
「不，克雷斯韦尔先生。我请您以原始股东身份入伙——是*现在*——不是明年，也不是后年。」
「泰勒先生，这桩买卖里您自己投钱了吗？」
「我所有的钱。」

===Original===
“Well, the thing is this way: if you can prove to us that conditions are as you say, we’re in for it.”
“Good! Meet me in New York, say﻿—let’s see, this is March tenth﻿—well, May third.”
Young Cresswell was thinking rapidly. This man without doubt represented money. He was anxious for an alliance. Why? Was it all straight, or did the whole move conceal a trick?
His eyes strayed to the porch where his pretty sister sat languidly, and then toward the school where the other sister lived. John Taylor looked out on the porch, too. They glanced quickly at each other, and each wondered if the other had shared his thought. Harry Cresswell did not voice his mind for he was not wholly disposed to welcome what was there; but he could not refrain from saying in tones almost confidential:
“You could recommend this deal, then, could you﻿—to your own friends?”
“To my own family,” asserted John Taylor, looking at Harry Cresswell with sudden interest. But Mr. Cresswell was staring at the end of his cigar.

===Chinese===
「那么，事情就是这样：要是您能向我们证明情况如您所说，我们就入伙。」
「好！在纽约见我，时间嘛——我想想，今天是三月十号——好，五月三号。」
小克雷斯韦尔脑筋飞转。此人毫无疑问后面有钱。他急于结盟。为什么？这一切是光明正大，还是整桩行动里藏着花招？
他的目光溜向门廊——他那漂亮的妹妹正懒洋洋地坐在那儿——又转向那所学校，另一个妹妹住在那儿。约翰·泰勒也朝门廊望去。两人飞快地对视了一眼，各自揣测对方是否与自己所想相同。哈里·克雷斯韦尔没把心里话说出来，因为他并不完全乐见那个念头；可他还是忍不住以近乎交心的口吻说：
「那么这桩买卖，您能向您自己的朋友推荐喽？」
「向我自己的家人。」约翰·泰勒一口咬定，带着突如其来的兴味看着哈里·克雷斯韦尔。可克雷斯韦尔先生只是盯着自己雪茄的烟头。

## XI The Flowering of the Fleece / XI 羊毛开花

===Original===
“Zora,” observed Miss Smith, “it’s a great blessing not to need spectacles, isn’t it?”
Zora thought that it was; but she was wondering just what spectacles had to do with the complaint she had brought to the office from Miss Taylor.
“I’m always losing my glasses and they get dirty and﻿—Oh, dear! now where is that paper?”
Zora pointed silently to the complaint.
“No, not that﻿—another paper. It must be in my room. Don’t you want to come up and help me look?”
They went up to the clean, bare room, with its white iron bed, its cool, spotless shades and shining windows. Zora walked about softly and looked, while Miss Smith quietly searched on desk and bureau, paying no attention to the girl. For the time being she was silent.

===Chinese===
「佐拉，」史密斯小姐说，「不需要戴眼镜，可真是天大的福气，对不对？」
佐拉觉得是；可她正纳闷，眼镜跟她从泰勒小姐那儿带到办公处来的那桩告状，究竟有什么相干。
「我老是把眼镜弄丢，眼镜又老弄脏，还有——哎呀，我的天！那张纸这会儿哪儿去了？」
佐拉默默地朝那桩告状一指。
「不是那个——另一张纸。准是在我房里。你愿不愿意上来帮我找找？」
她们上了楼，进了一间洁净、空荡的房间，白铁床，清爽无瑕的窗帘，窗玻璃擦得锃亮。佐拉轻轻地走来走去看着，史密斯小姐则在书桌和梳妆台上静静地翻找，并不理会那姑娘。眼下她一言不发。

===Original===
“I sometimes wish,” she began at length, “I had a bright-eyed girl like you to help me find and place things.”
Zora made no comment.
“Sometimes Bles helps me,” added Miss Smith, guilefully.
Zora looked sharply at her. “Could I help?” she asked, almost timidly.
“Why, I don’t know,”﻿—the answer was deliberate. “There are one or two little things perhaps﻿—”
Placing a hand gently upon Zora’s shoulder, she pointed out a few odd tasks, and left the girl busily doing them; then she returned to the office, and threw Miss Taylor’s complaint into the wastebasket.

===Chinese===
「有时候我真希望，」她终于开口，「能有个像你这样眼睛亮亮的姑娘，帮我找找东西、归归位。」
佐拉没作声。
「有时候布莱斯帮我。」史密斯小姐狡黠地补了一句。
佐拉锐利地看了她一眼。「我能帮忙吗？」她几乎是怯生生地问。
「这个嘛，我不知道，」——回答是不紧不慢的。「也许有一两件小事——」
她把一只手轻轻搭在佐拉肩上，指点了几桩零碎活儿，便留下姑娘忙着去做；然后她回到办公处，把泰勒小姐的告状扔进了废纸篓。

===Original===
For a week or more Zora slipped in every day and performed the little tasks that Miss Smith laid out: she sorted papers, dusted the bureau, hung a curtain; she did not do the things very well, and she broke some china, but she worked earnestly and quickly, and there was no thought of pay. Then, too, did not Bles praise her with a happy smile, as together, day after day, they stood and watched the black dirt where the Silver Fleece lay planted? She dreamed and sang over that dark field, and again and again appealed to him: “S’pose it shouldn’t come up after all?” And he would laugh and say that of course it would come up.
One day, when Zora was helping Miss Smith in the bedroom, she paused with her arms full of clothes fresh from the laundry.
“Where shall I put these?”
Miss Smith looked around. “They might go in there,” she said, pointing to a door. Zora opened it. A tiny bedroom was disclosed, with one broad window looking toward the swamp; white curtains adorned it, and white hangings draped the plain bureau and washstand and the little bed. There was a study table, and a small bookshelf holding a few books, all simple and clean. Zora paused uncertain, and surveyed the room.

===Chinese===
足足一礼拜有余，佐拉天天溜进来，做史密斯小姐给她安排的那些小活儿：她整理文件、掸梳妆台、挂窗帘；这些事她做得并不好，还打碎了几件瓷器，可她干得认真、麻利，根本没想过工钱。况且，布莱斯不是天天跟她一道站着、望着那片埋着银羊毛的黑土、带着快活的笑夸她吗？她在那片黑黝黝的田上做梦、唱歌，一遍又一遍地问他：「要是它到头来不出芽呢？」他便笑，说当然会出芽。
有一天，佐拉在卧室里帮史密斯小姐的忙，怀里抱满刚从洗衣房取来的衣裳，停了下来。
「这些放哪儿？」
史密斯小姐四下看了看。「可以放那里面。」她朝一扇门一指说。佐拉推开门。里面是一间小小的卧室，有一扇宽阔的窗子朝向沼泽；白窗帘装点着，白色的帷帐罩着朴素的梳妆台、脸盆架和那张小床。有一张书桌，还有一个小书架，上面有几本书，一切都简朴、洁净。佐拉迟疑地站住，打量着这个房间。

===Original===
“Sometimes when you’re tired and want to be alone you can come up here, Zora,” said Miss Smith carelessly. “No one uses this room.”
Zora caught her breath sharply, but said nothing. The next day Miss Smith said to her when she came in:
“I’m busy now, dear, but you go up to your little room and read and I’ll call.”
Zora quietly obeyed. An hour later Miss Smith looked in, then she closed the door lightly and left. Another hour flew by before Zora hurried down.
“I was reading, and I forgot,” she said.
“It’s all right,” returned Miss Smith. “I didn’t need you. And any day, after you get all your lessons, I think Miss Taylor will excuse you and let you go to your room and read.” Miss Taylor, it transpired, was more than glad.

===Chinese===
「有时候你累了、想一个人待着，就可以上这儿来，佐拉。」史密斯小姐漫不经心地说。「没人用这间房。」
佐拉猛地吸了一口气，却什么也没说。第二天佐拉进来时，史密斯小姐对她说：
「我这会儿忙，亲爱的，可你上你那间小屋去读书吧，我会叫你的。」
佐拉乖乖地照办了。一个钟头后，史密斯小姐探头看了看，又轻轻把门带上走了。又飞过了一个钟头，佐拉才匆匆下楼。
「我在读书，我忘了。」她说。
「没关系。」史密斯小姐答道。「我也用不着你。往后哪天，你把功课都做完了，我想泰勒小姐会准你假，让你去你那间屋读书的。」后来才知道，泰勒小姐是求之不得。

===Original===
Day after day Bles and Zora visited the field; but ever the ground lay an unrelieved black beneath the bright sun, and they would go reluctantly home again, today there was much work to be done, and Zora labored steadily and eagerly, never pausing, and gaining in deftness and care.
In the afternoon Bles went to town with the school wagon. A light shower flew up from the south, lingered a while and fled, leaving a fragrance in the air. For a moment Zora paused, and her nostrils quivered; then without a word she slipped downstairs, glided into the swamp, and sped away to the island. She swung across the tree and a low, delighted cry bubbled on her lips. All the rich, black ground was sprinkled with tender green. She bent above the verdant tenderness and kissed it; then she rushed back, bursting into the room.
“*It’s come! It’s come!﻿—the Silver Fleece!*”
Miss Smith was startled.
“The Silver Fleece!” she echoed in bewilderment.

===Chinese===
日复一日，布莱斯和佐拉去探望那片田；可那地在明晃晃的日头底下，永远是一成不变的黑，他们便又不情愿地回家。今天活儿多，佐拉一刻不停地、热切地干着，越发地麻利、越发地仔细。
下午布莱斯跟学校的车进镇去了。一阵小雨从南边飞来，盘桓片刻又逃走，在空气里留下了一缕清香。佐拉停了一下，鼻翼翕动；然后一言不发地溜下楼，钻进沼泽，朝那座岛飞奔而去。她荡过那棵树，一声低低的、欢喜的叫从唇边冒出来。那整片肥沃的黑土地上，撒满了嫩绿。她俯身在那一片娇嫩的绿上亲了亲；然后奔回去，冲进房间。
「*出来了！出来了！——银羊毛！*」
史密斯小姐吓了一跳。
「银羊毛！」她茫然地应了一声。

===Original===
Zora hesitated. It came over her all at once that this one great all-absorbing thing meant nothing to the gaunt tired-look woman before her.
“Would Bles care if I told?” she asked doubtfully.
“No,” Miss Smith ventured.
And then the girl crouched at her feet and told the dream and the story. Many factors were involved that were quite foreign to the older woman’s nature and training. The recital brought to her New England mind many questions of policy and propriety. And yet, as she looked down upon the dark face, hot with enthusiasm, it all seemed somehow more than right. Slowly and lightly Miss Smith slipped her arm about Zora, and nodded and smiled a perfect understanding. They looked out together into the darkening twilight.
“It is so late and wet and you’re tired tonight﻿—don’t you think you’d better sleep in your little room?”

===Chinese===
佐拉犹豫了。她这才一下子意识到，这桩令她全副心神都沉醉其中的大事，对眼前这个瘦削、面带倦色的女人而言，竟毫无意义。
「要是我说出来，布莱斯会在意吗？」她迟疑地问。
「不会。」史密斯小姐大着胆子说。
于是姑娘蜷在她脚边，把那个梦、把整个故事都讲了出来。其中牵涉的许多东西，与这位年长女子的天性和教养全然相悖。这一番陈述，在她那颗新英格兰的脑海里，唤起了许多关乎策略与得体的疑问。然而，当她低头看着那张因热忱而滚烫的黝黑脸庞，这一切似乎不知怎的，比「对」还要对。史密斯小姐慢慢地、轻轻地把胳膊搭上佐拉的肩，点点头，笑出一个心领神会。她们一同望着外面渐渐暗下去的暮色。
「天这么晚了，又湿，你今晚也累了——你不觉得还是在你那间小屋睡为好吗？」

===Original===
Zora sat still. She thought of the noisy flaming cabin and the dark swamp; but a contrasting thought of the white bed made her timid, and slowly she shook her head. Nevertheless Miss Smith led her to the room.
“Here are things for you to wear,” she pointed out, opening the bureau, “and here is the bathroom.” She left the girl standing in the middle of the floor.
In time Zora came to stay often at Miss Smith’s cottage, and to learn new and unknown ways of living and dressing. She still refused to board, for that would cost more than she could pay yet, and she would accept no charity. Gradually an undemonstrative friendship sprang up between the pale old gray-haired teacher and the dark young black-haired girl. Delicately, too, but gradually, the companionship of Bles and Zora was guided and regulated. Of mornings Zora would hurry through her lessons and get excused to fly to the swamp, to work and dream alone. At noon Bles would run down, and they would linger until he must hurry back to dinner. After school he would go again, working while she was busy in Miss Smith’s office, and returning later, would linger awhile to tell Zora of his day while she busied herself with her little tasks. Saturday mornings they would go to the swamp and work together, and sometimes Miss Smith, stealing away from curious eyes, would come and sit and talk with them as they toiled.

===Chinese===
佐拉静静地坐着。她想起那吵闹、火光熊熊的小屋，想起幽暗的沼泽；可一个对照的念头——那张白色的床——叫她怯懦了，她慢慢地摇了摇头。然而史密斯小姐还是领她去了那个房间。
「这儿有给你穿的。」她打开梳妆台指点着，「这儿是浴室。」她留下姑娘一个人站在屋子当中。
渐渐地，佐拉常常到史密斯小姐的小屋来住，学着全新的、素所未闻的起居与穿戴之道。她仍不肯搭伙食——那要花的钱她还付不起，而她绝不接受施舍。一种不形于色的情谊，在这位苍白、灰发、上了年纪的老师与这个黝黑、黑发、年轻的姑娘之间，慢慢滋生起来。同样，也是微妙地、却渐渐地，布莱斯与佐拉的相伴，得到了引导与节制。早晨，佐拉会匆匆赶完功课，请假飞去沼泽，独自劳作、做梦。正午，布莱斯会跑下来，两人盘桓到他非赶回去吃午饭不可。放学后他再去，趁她在史密斯小姐办公处忙活时干活，晚些回来，再逗留一会儿，趁她忙那些小活儿的当口，把一天的见闻讲给她听。礼拜六早上，他们会一道去沼泽干活，有时候史密斯小姐也会悄悄躲开好奇的眼睛，来跟他们坐一坐，在他们劳作时和他们说说话。

===Original===
In those days, for these two souls, earth came very near to heaven. Both were in the midst of that mighty change from youth to womanhood and manhood. Their manner toward each other by degrees grew shyer and more thoughtful. There was less of comradeship, but the little meant more. The rough good fellowship was silently put aside; they no longer lightly clasped hands; and each at times wondered, in painful self-consciousness, if the other cared.
Then began, too, that long and subtle change wherein a soul, until now unmindful of its wrappings, comes suddenly to consciousness of body and clothes; when it gropes and tries to adjust one with the other, and through them to give to the inner deeper self, finer and fuller expression. One saw it easily, almost suddenly, in Alwyn’s Sunday suit, vivid neckties, and awkward fads.
Slower, subtler, but more striking was the change in Zora, as she began to earn bits of pin money in the office and to learn to sew. Dresses hung straighter; belts served a better purpose; stockings were smoother; underwear was daintier. Then her hair﻿—that great dark mass of immovable infinitely curled hair﻿—began to be subdued and twisted and combed until, with steady pains and study, it lay in thick twisted braids about her velvet forehead, like some shadowed halo. All this came much more slowly and spasmodically than one tells it. Few noticed the change much; none noticed all; and yet there came a night﻿—a student’s social﻿—when with a certain suddenness the whole school, teachers and pupils, realized the newness of the girl, and even Bles was startled.

===Chinese===
那些日子里，对这两个灵魂而言，人间几乎就是天堂。两人都正处在从少年迈向成年男女的那场巨变之中。他们对彼此的态度，渐渐变得羞涩、变得体贴。同伴之谊少了，可那一点点意味却重了。那种粗放的交情被悄悄搁到一边；他们不再轻易地握手；两人都时常在痛苦的自省里揣测，对方到底在不在乎自己。
与此同时，也开始了一场漫长而微妙的变化——一个至今不曾留意自己躯壳的灵魂，忽然对自己的身体和衣裳有了意识；它摸索着，试着把两者彼此调适，并借它们给那更深处的内在自我，以更精致、更饱满的表达。这一点，在阿尔文礼拜天穿的外套、鲜艳的领带和笨拙的讲究上，几乎是一下子就能看出来的。
更慢、更微妙、却也更惊人的，是佐拉的变化——她开始在办公处挣些零花，也学起了缝纫。裙子挂得更直了；腰带起了更好的作用；袜子更平整了；贴身衣物更精致了。还有她的头发——那一大团纹丝不动、卷得无边无际的黑发——开始被征服、被扭结、被梳理，直到，凭着不懈的苦心和钻研，它化作一条条粗粗的辫子盘在她那天鹅绒般的额头上，像一道幽暗的光轮。这一切，远比口述的来得慢、来得断断续续。少有人留意到这变化；没有人留意到全部；然而终于来了那么一个晚上——一场学生联谊会——全校师生，以一种突如其来的方式，意识到了这姑娘焕然一新，连布莱斯都吃了一惊。

===Original===
He had bought her in town, at Christmas time, a pair of white satin slippers, partly to test the smallness of her feet on which in younger days he had rallied her, and partly because she had mentioned a possible white dress. They were a cheap, plain pair but dainty, and they fitted well.
When the evening came and the students were marching and the teachers, save Miss Smith, were sitting rather primly apart and commenting, she entered the room. She was a little late, and a hush greeted her. One boy, with the inimitable drawl of the race, pushed back his ice-cream and addressed it with a mournful head-shake:
“Go ’way, honey, yo’ los’ yo’ tas’e!”
The dress was plain and fitted every curving of a healthy girlish form. She paused a moment white-bodied and white-limbed but dark and velvet-armed, her full neck and oval head rising rich and almost black above, with its deep-lighted eyes and crown of silent darkling hair.

===Chinese===
圣诞时节，他在镇上给她买了一双白缎子便鞋，一来是想试试她的脚到底有多小——他从前常拿这个取笑她——二来是因为她提过可能会有一身白裙子。鞋是便宜的、素净的，却很精致，穿着正合脚。
晚会那晚，学生们正排着队行进，老师们——除了史密斯小姐——都板板正正地坐在一起品评着，这时她进了屋。她来得稍晚，迎接她的是一片静默。一个男孩，用本族那种学不来的拖长腔，把他的冰淇淋推开，冲着它哀伤地直摇头：
「去吧，宝贝儿，你没了味儿啦！」
那裙子是素净的，贴合着一个健康少女身躯的每一道曲线。她驻足片刻，身躯和四肢是雪白的，手臂却是黝黑、天鹅绒般的；她那丰润的、近乎漆黑的颈项和椭圆形的头颅在上方升起，配着那双深亮的眼、和一顶沉默的、暗色的发冠。

===Original===
To some, such a revelation of grace and womanliness in this hoyden, the gentle swelling of lankness to beauty, of lowliness to shy self-poise, was a sudden joy, to others a mere blindness. Mary Taylor was perplexed and in some indefinite way amazed; and many of the other teachers saw no beauty, only a strangeness that brought a smile. They were such as know beauty by convention only, and find it lip-ringed, hoop-skirted, tattooed, or corsetted, as time and place decree.
The change in Zora, however, had been neither cataclysmic nor revolutionary and it was yet far﻿—very far﻿—from complete. She still ran and romped in the woods, and dreamed her dreams; she was still passionately independent and “queer.” Tendencies merely had become manifest, some dominant. She would, unhindered, develop to a brilliant, sumptuous womanhood; proud, conquering, full-blooded, and deep bosomed﻿—a passionate mother of men. Herein lay all her early wildness and strangeness. Herein lay, as yet half hidden, dimly sensed and all unspoken, the power of a mighty all-compelling love for one human soul, and, through it, for all the souls of men. All this lay growing and developing; but as yet she was still a girl, with a new shyness and comeliness and a bold, searching heart.

===Chinese===
对有些人而言，在这个野丫头身上竟显露出这般优雅与女子气，那份从瘦长到丰盈的温柔蜕变、从卑微到怯生生地自持的蜕变，是一种突如其来的喜悦；对另一些人而言，则只是视而不见。玛丽·泰勒困惑，又说不清地惊异；其余许多老师看不见美，只看见一种引人发笑的古怪。他们这种人，只懂得按规矩去认美，非得美是涂唇环、穿箍裙、刺青、束腰的——全凭时与地所定。
然而，佐拉的变化既非天翻地覆，也非革命性的，而且离完成还远着——远着呢。她仍会在林子里奔跑嬉闹，做她的梦；她仍是那般激烈地独立、「古怪」。不过是些倾向显了出来罢了，其中有几样占了上风。她若不受阻碍，定会出落成一个光彩照人、丰艳成熟的妇人；骄傲、征服者般、血气旺盛、胸脯深厚——一个热烈的、生养男儿的母亲。她早年所有的野性与古怪，尽在于此。也尽在于此——那股对某一个人类灵魂的、强大而无可抗拒的爱的力量，以及透过这一个、对一切人类灵魂的爱——目前还半隐着，只是隐隐感到、全未说出口。这一切都在生长、在发展；可她眼下仍是个姑娘，带着新的羞怯与俏丽，和一颗大胆的、探询的心。

===Original===
In the field of the Silver Fleece all her possibilities were beginning to find expression. These newborn green things hidden far down in the swamp, begotten in want and mystery, were to her a living wonderful fairy tale come true. All the latent mother in her brooded over them; all her brilliant fancy wove itself about them. They were her dream-children, and she tended them jealously; they were her Hope, and she worshipped them. When the rabbits tried the tender plants she watched hours to drive them off, and catching now and then a pulsing pink-eyed invader, she talked to it earnestly:
“Brer Rabbit﻿—poor little Brer Rabbit, don’t you know you mustn’t eat Zora’s cotton? Naughty, naughty Brer Rabbit.” And then she would show it where she had gathered piles of fragrant weeds for it and its fellows.

===Chinese===
在银羊毛的田里，她所有的可能性都开始找到了表达。这些新生的绿东西，藏在沼泽深处，孕育于贫困与神秘之中，于她是一部活的、奇妙的、成真的童话。她身上所有潜藏的母性都盘桓在它们之上；她所有绚烂的幻想都围绕它们编织。它们是她梦中的孩子，她呵护它们到了斤斤计较的地步；它们是她的希望，她膜拜它们。兔子来啃那嫩苗时，她一连守望几个钟头赶它们走，时不时捉住一只心跳扑扑、红眼睛的入侵者，便一本正经地对它说：
「兔哥哥——可怜的小兔哥哥，你不知道不该吃佐拉的棉花吗？淘气，淘气的兔哥哥。」然后她会把它领到一个地方，那儿她为它和它的同伴堆了好些芳香的杂草。

===Original===
The golden green of the first leaves darkened, and the plants sprang forward steadily. Never before was such a magnificent beginning, a full month ahead of other cotton. The rain swept down in laughing, bubbling showers, and laved their thirsty souls, and Zora held her beating breast day by day lest it rain too long or too heavily. The sun burned fiercely upon the young cotton plants as the spring hastened, and they lifted their heads in darker, wilder luxuriance; for the time of hoeing was at hand.
These days were days of alternate hope and doubt with Bles Alwyn. Strength and ambition and inarticulate love were fighting within him. He felt, in the dark thousands of his kind about him, a mighty calling to deeds. He was becoming conscious of the narrowness and straightness of his black world, and red anger flashed in him ever and again as he felt his bonds. His mental horizon was broadening as he prepared for the college of next year; he was faintly grasping the wider, fuller world, and its thoughts and aspirations.

===Chinese===
头一批叶子的金黄绿色变深了，植株稳健地蹿了起来。从未有过这般壮丽的开端，比别的棉花足足早了一个月。雨水带着笑、冒着泡倾泻而下，润泽它们干渴的灵魂，佐拉日复一日地捂着怦怦跳的胸口，生怕雨下得太久、太猛。春光渐迫，日头狠命地晒在幼小的棉株上，它们便抬起头，长得更深、更野、更茂盛；因为锄地的时节到了。
这些日子，对布莱斯·阿尔文而言，是希望与疑虑交替的日子。力量、雄心，和说不出口的爱，在他心里交战。他感到，在他周围那成千成万黑暗中的同类之中，有一股强有力的、要他建功立业的召唤。他渐渐意识到他那黑人世界的逼仄与狭隘，每当他感到自己的枷锁，便有一阵赤红的怒火闪过。他一面为来年的大学做准备，心智的地平线也随之开阔；他隐约把握到了一个更宽广、更丰盈的世界，和它的思想与抱负。

===Original===
But beside and around and above all this, like subtle, permeating ether, was﻿—Zora. His feelings for her were not as yet definite, expressed, or grasped; they were rather the atmosphere in which all things occurred and were felt and judged. From an amusing pastime she had come to be a companion and thought-mate; and now, beyond this, insensibly they were drifting to a silenter, mightier mingling of souls. But drifting, merely﻿—not arrived; going gently, irresistibly, but not yet at the realized goal.
He felt all this as the stirring of a mighty force, but knew not what he felt. The teasing of his fellows, the common love-gossip of the school yard, seemed far different from his plight. He laughed at it and indignantly denied it. Yet he was uncomfortable, restless, unhappy. He fancied Zora cared less for his company, and he gave her less, and then was puzzled to find time hanging so empty, so wretchedly empty, on his hands. When they were together in these days they found less to talk about, and had it not been for the Silver Fleece which in magic wilfulness opened both their mouths, they would have found their companionship little more than a series of awkward silences. Yet in their silences, their walks, and their sittings there was a companionship, a glow, a satisfaction, as came to them nowhere else on earth, and they wondered at it.

===Chinese===
可在这一切之侧、之围、之上，像精微、弥漫的以太一样存在的，是——佐拉。他对她的感情，此刻还不明确、未表达、未把握；它毋宁说是一切事物发生于其中、被感受、被评判的那种气氛。她从一个有趣的消遣，变成了一位同伴、一位思想上的伴侣；如今，在这之外，他们不知不觉地漂向了一种更静默、更磅礴的灵魂交融。但只是漂去——尚未抵达；在轻柔地、不可抗拒地前行，却还未到那意识到的终点。
他把这一切感受为一种磅礴之力的涌动，却不知自己感受到的是什么。同伴们的打趣、校园里司空见惯的恋爱闲话，似乎都与他这处境相去甚远。他对此一笑置之，又愤愤地否认。然而他不安、烦躁、不快乐。他臆想佐拉不那么在乎他的陪伴了，便也少给她陪伴；随后又纳闷地发现，时间竟在自己手里空得这般厉害，这般凄凉地空。这些日子他们在一起时，能说的话越来越少，若不是那片银羊毛以一种魔性的执拗打开了两人的嘴，他们的相伴怕也不过是一连串尴尬的沉默。然而在他们的沉默里、散步里、闲坐里，却有一种相伴、一种暖意、一种满足，是世上别处都得不到的，他们自己也啧啧称奇。

===Original===
They were both wondering at it this morning as they watched their cotton. It had seemingly bounded forward in a night and it must be hoed forthwith. Yet, hoeing was murder﻿—the ruthless cutting away of tenderer plants that the sturdier might thrive the more and grow.
“I hate it, Bles, don’t you?”
“Hate what?”
“Killing any of it; it’s all so pretty.”
“But it must be, so that what’s left will be prettier, or at least more useful.”
“But it shouldn’t be so; everything ought to have a chance to be beautiful and useful.”
“Perhaps it ought to be so,” admitted Bles, “but it isn’t.”
“Isn’t it so﻿—anywhere?”
“I reckon not. Death and pain pay for all good things.”

===Chinese===
这天早上，他们俩正一边望着他们的棉花、一边为这事称奇呢。那棉花仿佛一夜之间便跃进了一大截，得赶紧锄了。然而锄地便是杀戮——无情地砍掉较柔嫩的植株，好让较壮实的长得更旺、更高。
「我恨这个，布莱斯，你不恨吗？」
「恨什么？」
「杀死它们里头的任何一株；它们全都这么漂亮。」
「可非这样不可，好让留下来的更漂亮，至少更有用。」
「可不该是这样；每一样东西都该有机会既美丽又有用。」
「也许该是这样，」布莱斯承认，「可事实并非如此。」
「哪儿都不这样吗？」
「我估摸不是。死亡和痛苦，为一切美好的东西付账。」

===Original===
She hoed away silently, hesitating over the choice of the plants, pondering this world-old truth, saddened by its ruthless cruelty.
“Death and pain,” she murmured; “what a price!”
Bles leaned on his hoe and considered. It had not occurred to him till now that Zora was speaking better and better English: the idioms and errors were dropping away; they had not utterly departed, however, but came crowding back in moments of excitement. At other times she clothed Miss Smith’s clear-cut, correct speech in softer Southern accents. She was drifting away from him in some intangible way to an upper world of dress and language and deportment, and the new thought was pain to him.
So it was that the Fleece rose and spread and grew to its wonderful flowering; and so these two children grew with it into theirs. Zora never forgot how they found the first white flower in that green and billowing sea, nor her low cry of pleasure and his gay shout of joy. Slowly, wonderfully the flowers spread﻿—white, blue, and purple bells, hiding timidly, blazing luxuriantly amid the velvet leaves; until one day﻿—it was after a southern rain and the sunlight was twinkling through the morning﻿—all the Fleece was in flower﻿—a mighty swaying sea, darkling rich and waving, and upon it flecks and stars of white and purple foam. The joy of the two so madly craved expression that they burst into singing; not the wild light song of dancing feet, but a low, sweet melody of her fathers’ fathers, whereunto Alwyn’s own deep voice fell fitly in minor cadence.

===Chinese===
她默默地锄着，在挑选植株时犹豫不决，琢磨着这条亘古的真理，为其无情的残酷而伤怀。
「死亡和痛苦，」她喃喃道，「这是怎样的代价！」
布莱斯倚着锄头寻思。他直到此刻才发觉，佐拉的英语说得越来越好了：那些俚语和错误正在脱落；不过它们并未彻底离去，而是一到激动的时刻便又蜂拥而回。在别的时候，她把史密斯小姐那清晰、规范的言语，裹上一层更柔和的南方口音。她正以某种难以言说的方式，从他身边漂向一个更高的、关于衣着、言语和仪态的世界，这个新的念头令他痛苦。
就这样，羊毛升起来、铺展开、长到了它那奇妙的开花；这两个孩子也随之一同长成了他们自己。佐拉永远忘不了他们如何在那片碧绿、起伏的海洋里发现了头一朵白花，忘不了她那声低低的、欢喜的叫，和他那快活的欢呼。慢慢地、奇妙地，花朵铺展开来——白的、蓝的、紫的铃铛，怯怯地躲着，又在天鹅绒般的叶子间恣意盛放；直到有一天——那是在一场南方的雨之后，阳光在晨光里闪烁——整片羊毛都开了花——一片巍峨的、摇曳的海，幽暗而丰饶，波涛起伏，上面缀着白沫与紫沫般的斑点和星辰。两人的欢乐如此疯狂地渴求表达，竟迸发成歌；不是那种轻快的、踏着舞步的野歌，而是一支她祖辈的祖辈传下的低回、甜美的旋律，阿尔文自己的深沉嗓音，便以小调的节拍妥帖地落入其中。

===Original===
Miss Smith and Miss Taylor, who were sorting the mail, heard them singing as they came up out of the swamp. Miss Taylor looked at them, then at Miss Smith.
But Miss Smith sat white and rigid with the first opened letter in her hand.

===Chinese===
正在分拣信件的史密斯小姐和泰勒小姐，听见他们从沼泽里出来时唱着歌。泰勒小姐看了看他们，又看了看史密斯小姐。
可史密斯小姐却脸色煞白、僵坐着，手里捏着刚拆开的第一封信。

## XII The Promise / XII 诺言

===Original===
Miss Smith sat with her face buried in her hands while the tears trickled silently through her thin fingers. Before her lay the letter, read a dozen times:
“Old Mrs. Grey has been to see me, and she has announced her intention of endowing five colored schools, yours being one. She asked if $500,000 would do it. She has plenty of money, so I told her $750,000 would be better﻿—$150,000 apiece. She’s arranging for a Board of Trust, etc. You’ll probably hear from her soon. You’ve been so worried about expenses that I thought I’d send this word on; I knew you’d be glad.”
Glad? Dear God, how flat the word fell! For thirty years she had sown the seed, planting her lifeblood in this work, that had become the marrow of her soul.

===Chinese===
史密斯小姐坐着，脸埋在手里，泪水悄悄地从她瘦削的指缝间淌下来。她面前摊着那封信，已经读了十来遍：
「老格雷太太来看过我，她宣布了资助五所黑人学校的打算，贵校是其中之一。她问五十万够不够。她有的是钱，所以我告诉她七十五万更好——每所十五万。她正在安排一个托管董事会之类。你大概不久就会收到她的来信。你一直为经费发愁，我想先把这话送来；我知道你会高兴的。」
高兴？老天爷，这个字眼落下来是何等轻飘！三十年来她播撒种子，把自己的心血种进这桩事业里，这事业已成了她灵魂的骨髓。

===Original===
Successful? No, it had not been successful; but it had been human. Through yonder doorway had trooped an army of hundreds upon hundreds of bright and dull, light and dark, eager and sullen faces.
