There had been good and bad, honest and deceptive, frank and furtive. Some had caught, kindled and flashed to ambition and achievement; some, glowing dimly, had plodded on in a slow, dumb faithful work worth while; and yet others had suddenly exploded, hurtling human fragments to heaven and to hell. Around this school home, as around the centre of some little universe, had whirled the sorrowful, sordid, laughing, pulsing drama of a world: birth pains, and the stupor of death; hunger and pale murder; the riot of thirst and the orgies of such red and black cabins as Elspeth’s, crouching in the swamp.

===Chinese===
成功？不，它算不上成功；可它是人间的。穿过那边那道门，涌进来过一支大军——成百上千张面孔，有聪颖的也有愚钝的，有白皙的也有黝黑的，有热切的也有阴沉的。有好人，也有坏人；有诚实的，也有奸诈的；有坦率的，也有鬼祟的。有的被点燃了，迸发出雄心与成就的光芒；有的微微发着光，在一种缓慢、沉默而忠诚的、值得去做的活计里埋头前行；还有一些则猝然爆炸，把人的碎片炸上天堂、也炸进地狱。围绕这座学校这个家，一如围绕某个小小宇宙的中心，旋转着一出悲伤的、鄙琐的、欢笑的、搏动的世界大戏：分娩的阵痛，和死亡的昏沉；饥饿，和苍白的谋杀；干渴的狂乱，以及像埃尔斯贝思那座瑟缩在沼泽里的、又红又黑的小屋中的纵欲。

===Original===
She groaned as she read of the extravagances of the world and saw her own vanishing revenues; but the funds continued to dwindle until Sarah Smith asked herself: “What will become of this school when I die?” With trembling fingers she had sat down to figure how many teachers must be dropped next year, when her brother’s letter came, and she slipped to her knees and prayed.
Mrs. Grey’s decision was due in no little way to Mary Taylor’s reports. Slowly but surely the girl had begun to think that she had found herself in this new world. She would never be attuned to it thoroughly, for she was set for different music. The veil of color and race still hung thickly between her and her pupils; and yet she seemed to see some points of penetration. No one could meet daily a hundred or more of these lighthearted, good-natured children without feeling drawn to them. No one could cross the thresholds of the cabins and not see the old and well-known problems of life and striving. More and more, therefore, the work met Miss Taylor’s approval and she told Mrs. Grey so.

===Chinese===
她读到世人的奢靡便要叹息，眼见自己的进项日渐干涸；可资金仍一路缩减，终于有一天，萨拉·史密斯（Sarah Smith）自问：「我死之后，这所学校会怎样？」她用发抖的手指坐下来算，明年得辞掉几位老师——就在这时，哥哥的信来了，她跪倒在地，祷告起来。
格雷太太的决定，很大程度上要归功于玛丽·泰勒的汇报。这姑娘慢慢地、却笃定地，开始觉得自己在这个新世界里找到了位置。她永远不会与之彻底合拍，因为她生来是配另一种曲调的。肤色与种族的帷幕，仍沉沉地挂在她和她的学生之间；然而她似乎看到了几处可以透进的缝隙。日复一日地与一百来个这样乐天、和气的孩子相处，任谁都会被他们吸引。迈过那些小屋的门槛，任谁都会看到那些旧的、人所共知的生活与挣扎的难题。于是，这桩事业越来越得到泰勒小姐的认可，她也将此如实告知了格雷太太。

===Original===
At the same time Mary Taylor had come to some other definite conclusions: she believed it wrong to encourage the ambitions of these children to any great extent; she believed they should be servants and farmers, content to work under present conditions until those conditions could be changed; and she believed that the local white aristocracy, helped by Northern philanthropy, should take charge of such gradual changes.
These conclusions she did not pretend to have originated; but she adopted them from reading and conversation, after hesitating for a year before such puzzling contradictions as Bles Alwyn and Harry Cresswell. For her to conclude to treat Bles Alwyn as a man despite his color was as impossible as to think Mr. Cresswell a criminal. Some compromise was imperative which would save her the pleasure of Mr. Cresswell’s company and at the same time leave open a way of fulfilling the world’s duty to this black boy. She thought she had found this compromise and she wrote Mrs. Grey suggesting a chain of endowed Negro schools under the management of trustees composed of Northern business men and local Southern whites. Mrs. Grey acquiesced gladly and announced her plan, eventually writing Miss Smith of her decision “to second her noble efforts in helping the poor colored people,” and she hoped to have the plan under way before next fall.

===Chinese===
与此同时，玛丽·泰勒也得出了另一些明确的结论：她认为，过分鼓励这些孩子的雄心是错的；她认为他们应当做仆人和农夫，安于在现状下劳作，直到那些状况得以改变；她认为，由北方的慈善事业资助、当地白人望族主导，来掌管这种渐进的改变。
这些结论，她并不自称是自己首创的；她是在一年的犹豫之后——面对布莱斯·阿尔文和哈里·克雷斯韦尔这样令人困惑的矛盾——从书本和谈话里采纳了它们。要她得出结论、把布莱斯·阿尔文当作一个男子来对待（不顾他的肤色），正如要她把克雷斯韦尔先生当作罪犯一样，是不可能的。某种妥协势在必行——既保住她享受克雷斯韦尔先生作伴的乐趣，又同时为这世界对这个黑人小伙子应尽的责任留一条出路。她自以为找到了这个妥协，便写信给格雷太太，建议设立一条由北方商人和当地南方白人组成的董事会管理的、受资助的黑人学校链。格雷太太欣然同意，宣布了她的计划，最后写信给史密斯小姐，告知她的决定——「以襄助她帮助可怜有色人种的高尚义举」——并希望能在来年秋天之前把计划铺开。

===Original===
The sharpness of Miss Smith’s joy did not let her dwell on the proposed “Board of Trust”; of course, it would be a board of friends of the school.
She sat in her office looking out across the land. School had closed for the year and Bles with the carryall was just taking Miss Taylor to the train with her trunk and bags. Far up the road she could see dotted here and there the little dirty cabins of Cresswell’s tenants﻿—the Cresswell domain that lay like a mighty hand around the school, ready at a word to squeeze its life out. Only yonder, to the eastward, lay the way out; the five hundred acres of the Tolliver plantation, which the school needed so sadly for its farm and community. But the owner was a hard and ignorant white man, hating “niggers” only a shade more than he hated white aristocrats of the Cresswell type. He had sold the school its first land to pique the Cresswells; but he would not sell any more, she was sure, even now when the promise of wealth faced the school.

===Chinese===
史密斯小姐欢喜得尖锐，顾不上去细想提议中的那个「托管董事会」；当然，那会是一个由学校的朋友们组成的董事会。
她坐在办公处，望着窗外那片土地。本学年已经结课，布莱斯正驾着大马车，送泰勒小姐和她的箱包去火车站。沿着大路远远望去，她能看见克雷斯韦尔家佃户们那些脏兮兮的小屋，这儿一个那儿一个地散落着——克雷斯韦尔的领地，像一只巨手环抱着学校，一句话便能把它攥死。只有那边，朝东的方向，才有出路；那便是托利弗种植园的五百亩地，学校多么需要它来做农场、做社区。可那地主是个又硬又无知的白人，他恨「黑鬼」（注：原文 niggers，蔑称）只比他恨克雷斯韦尔那类白人望族轻那么一点点。当初他把头一块地卖给学校，是为了怄克雷斯韦尔家；可他不会再卖了，她有把握，哪怕如今学校眼看要有钱了。

===Original===
She lay back and closed her eyes and fell lightly asleep. As she slept an old woman came toiling up the hill northward from the school, and out of the eastward spur of the Cresswell barony. She was fat and black, hooded and aproned, with great round head and massive bosom. Her face was dull and heavy and homely, her old eyes sorrowful. She moved swiftly, carrying a basket on her arm. Opposite her, to the southward, but too far for sight, an old man came out of the lower Cresswell place, skirting the swamp. He was tall, black, and gaunt, part bald with tufted hair, and a cowed and furtive look was in his eyes. One leg was crippled, and he hobbled painfully.

===Chinese===
她往后一靠，闭上眼，轻轻地睡着了。她睡着时，一个老妇人从学校北边的山上、从克雷斯韦尔领地东边的那个尖角那儿，吃力地爬了上来。她又胖又黑，戴头巾、系围裙，滚圆的大脑袋，厚实的胸脯。她的脸沉沉的、笨笨的、不好看，一双老眼悲苦。她走得飞快，臂上挎着一只篮子。与她相对的、在南边——太远了看不见——一个老头从克雷斯韦尔下方那块地出来，沿着沼泽边走。他又高又黑又瘦，半秃了，头发一簇一簇，眼里有一种怯懦而鬼祟的神情。一条腿瘸了，他一瘸一拐地、痛苦地走着。

===Original===
Up the road to the eastward that ran past the school, with the morning sun at his back, strode a young man, yellow, crisp-haired, strong-faced, with darkly knit brows. He greeted Bles and the teacher coldly, and moved on in nervous haste. A woman, hurrying out of the westward swamp up the path that led from Elspeth’s, saw him and shrank back hastily. She turned quickly into the swamp and waited, looking toward the school. The old woman hurried into the back gate just as the old man appeared to the southward on the road. The young man greeted him cordially and they stopped a moment to talk, while the hiding woman watched.
“Howdy, Uncle Jim.”
“Howdy, son. Hit’s hot, ain’t it? How is you?”
“Tolerable, how are you?”
“Poorly, son, poorly﻿—and worser in mind. I’se goin’ up to talk to old Miss.”

===Chinese===
朝东、从学校门前经过的那条大路上，一个年轻人背着晨光大步走来，黄皮肤，卷曲的头发，脸庞刚毅，眉头阴沉地锁着。他冷冷地跟布莱斯和老师打了个招呼，便神经质地急匆匆往前走了。一个女人，从西边的沼泽、顺着从埃尔斯贝思那儿出来的一条小径匆匆上来，看见了他，赶忙缩了回去。她飞快地拐进沼泽等着，朝学校那边张望。那老妇人匆匆进了后门，正巧那老头在南边的大路上露了面。年轻人热络地跟他打招呼，两人停下来说了一会儿话，那藏着的女人在一旁看着。
「您好啊，吉姆大叔。」
「您好啊，孩子。天真热，是不是？您身子怎么样？」
「还凑合，您呢？」
「不大好，孩子，不大好——心里头更不好。俺要上去跟老小姐说说话。」

===Original===
“So am I, but I just see Aunt Rachel going in. We’d better wait.”
Miss Smith started up at the timid knocking, and rubbed her eyes. It was long since she had slept in the daytime and she was annoyed at such laziness. She opened the back door and led the old woman to the office.
“Now, what have you got there?” she demanded, eyeing the basket.
“Just a little chicken fo’ you and a few aigs.”
“Oh, you are so thoughtful!” Sarah Smith’s was a grateful heart.
“Go ’long now﻿—hit ain’t a thing.”

===Chinese===
「俺也是，可俺刚瞧见雷切尔大婶进去了。咱们还是等等吧。」
史密斯小姐被怯生生的敲门声惊醒，揉了揉眼睛。她白日里睡觉已是多年前的事了，她为自己的这般惫懒着恼。她开了后门，把老妇人领进办公处。
「嗳，您这篮子里是什么呀？」她盯着篮子问。
「就给您的几只小鸡，还有几个鸡蛋。」
「哦，您真是太费心啦！」萨拉·史密斯是个知恩图报的人。
「哪里的话——这算个啥。」

===Original===
Then came a pause, the old woman sliding into the proffered seat, while over her genial, dimpled smile there dropped a dull veil of care. Her eyes shifted uneasily. Miss Smith tried not to notice the change.
“Well, are you all moved, Aunt Rachel?” she inquired cheerfully.
“No’m, and we ain’t gwine to move.”
“But I thought it was all arranged.”
“It was,” gloomily, “but de ole Cunnel, he won’t let us go.”
The listener was instantly sympathetic. “Why not?” she asked.
“He says we owes him.”

===Chinese===
接着是一阵沉默，老妇人溜进让给她的椅子里，那一脸和蔼的、带酒窝的笑上头，落下了一层沉沉的忧愁之幕。她的眼不安地转来转去。史密斯小姐尽量不去留意这变化。
「那么，你们都搬好了吗，雷切尔大婶？」她快活地问。
「没呢，太太，俺们也不打算搬了。」
「可我当这事都安排妥了呢。」
「是妥了，」她闷闷地说，「可老上校不让大家走。」
听者立时同情起来。「为什么不？」她问。
「他说俺们欠他的。」

===Original===
“But didn’t you settle at Christmas?”
“Yas’m; but when he found we was goin’ away, he looked up some more debts.”
“How much?”
“I don’t know ’zactly﻿—more’n a hundred dollars. Den de boys done got in dat trouble, and he paid their fines.”
“What was the trouble?”
“Well, one was a-gambling, and the other struck the overseer what was a-whippin’ him.”
“Whipping him!”﻿—in horrified exclamation, quite as much at Aunt Rachel’s matter-of-fact way of regarding the matter as at the deed itself.
“Yas’m. He didn’t do his work right and he whipped him. I speck he needed it.”
“But he’s a grown man,” Miss Smith urged earnestly.
“Yas’m; he’s twenty now, and big.”

===Chinese===
「可是你们圣诞节不是结清了吗？」
「是清了，太太；可他一听说俺们要走，又翻出些旁的债来。」
「多少？」
「俺说不准——一百多块。后来几个孩子又闯了祸，是他替他们缴的罚款。」
「闯了什么祸？」
「这个嘛，一个赌钱，另一个打了监工——那监工正抽他呢。」
「抽他！」——这一声惊呼充满了骇然，既是为那行径本身，也同样是为雷切尔大婶谈起这事时那副习以为常的口气。
「是，太太。他活儿没干对，监工就抽了他。俺估摸他也欠收拾。」
「可他是成年的人了啊。」史密斯小姐恳切地劝说。
「是，太太；他二十了，块头也大。」

===Original===
“Whipped him!” Miss Smith repeated. “And so you can’t leave?”
“No’m, he say he’ll sell us out and put us in de chain-gang if we go. The boys is plumb mad, but I’se a-pleadin’ with ’em not to do nothin’ rash.”
“But﻿—but I thought they had already started to work a crop on the Tolliver place?”
“Yes’m, dey had; but, you see, dey were arrested, and then Cunnel Cresswell took ’em and ’lowed they couldn’t leave his place. Ol’ man Tolliver was powerful mad.”
“Why, Aunt Rachel, it’s slavery!” cried the lady in dismay. Aunt Rachel did not offer to dispute her declaration.
“Yas’m, hit’s slavery,” she agreed. “I hates it mighty bad, too, ’cause I wanted de little chillens in school; but﻿—” The old woman broke down and sobbed.

===Chinese===
「抽他！」史密斯小姐重复道。「所以你们走不成了？」
「走不成，太太，他说俺们要是走，就把俺们卖掉、送上苦役队。孩子们气得不行，可俺一个劲儿劝他们别莽撞。」
「可——可我以为他们已经在托利弗那块地上种上一季庄稼了呢？」
「是种了，太太；可是您瞧，他们给抓了，然后克雷斯韦尔上校把他们领走，说他们不能离开他那块地。托利弗老头气坏了。」
「哎呀，雷切尔大婶，这是奴役啊！」夫人惊恐地叫道。雷切尔大婶并未表示异议。
「是，太太，是奴役，」她附和道，「俺也恨透了，因为俺想让几个小的上学；可是——」老妇人撑不住，抽噎起来。

===Original===
A knocking came at the door; hastily wiping her eyes Aunt Rachel rose.
“I’ll﻿—I’ll see what I can do, Aunt Rachel﻿—I must do something,” murmured Miss Smith hastily, as the woman departed, and an old black man came limping in. Miss Smith looked up in surprise.
“I begs pardon, Mistress﻿—I begs pardon. Good morning.”
“Good morning﻿—” she hesitated.
“Sykes﻿—Jim Sykes﻿—that’s me.”
“Yes, I’ve heard of you, Mr. Sykes; you live over south of the swamp.”
“Yes, ma’am, that’s me; and I’se got a little shack dar and a bit of land what I’se trying to buy.”

===Chinese===
门上响起了敲门声；雷切尔大婶赶忙擦了擦眼，站起身来。
「我——我想想法子，雷切尔大婶——我总得做点什么。」史密斯小姐匆匆地喃喃道，那妇人便出去了，一个黑人老头一瘸一拐地走了进来。史密斯小姐惊讶地抬起头。
「求您恕罪，夫人——求您恕罪。早安。」
「早安——」她犹豫了一下。
「赛克斯——吉姆·赛克斯（Jim Sykes）——就是俺。」
「哦，我听说过您，赛克斯先生；您住在沼泽南边。」
「是，太太，就是俺；俺在那儿有个小棚屋，还有一小块地，俺正想把它买下来。」

===Original===
“Of Colonel Cresswell?”
“Yas’m, of de Cunnel.”
“And how long have you been buying it?”
“Going on ten year now; and dat’s what I comes to ask you about.”
“Goodness me! And how much have you paid a year?”
“I gen’rally pays ’bout three bales of cotton a year.”
“Does he furnish you rations?”
“Only sugar and coffee and a little meat now and then.”
“What does it amount to a year?”
“I doesn’t rightly know﻿—but I’se got some papers here.”

===Chinese===
「向克雷斯韦尔上校买的？」
「是，太太，向上校买的。」
「您买了多少年了？」
「快十年了；俺就是为这个来问您的。」
「我的天！您一年付多少？」
「俺一般一年付三包棉花。」
「他供给您口粮吗？」
「只给点糖和咖啡，还有时不时一点肉。」
「一年合计多少呢？」
「俺说不准——可俺这儿带了些字据来。」

===Original===
Miss Smith looked them over and sighed. It was the same old tale of blind receipts for money “on account”﻿—no items, no balancing. By his help she made out that last year his total bill at Cresswell’s store was perhaps forty dollars.
“An’ last year’s bill was bigger’n common ’cause I hurt my leg working at the gin and had to have some medicine.”
“Why, as far as I can see, Mr. Sykes, you’ve paid Cresswell about a thousand dollars in the last ten years. How large is your place?”
“About twenty acres.”
“And what were you to pay for it?”
“Four hundred.”

===Chinese===
史密斯小姐一份份看过去，叹了口气。又是那套老掉牙的故事——「预付」的糊涂收据，没有细目，没有结清。靠他的帮忙，她算出去年他在克雷斯韦尔铺子上的账，总共不过四十块上下。
「去年账比平常大，是因为俺在轧花机那儿干活伤了腿，得吃药。」
「怎么，依我看，赛克斯先生，这十年里您付给克雷斯韦尔的，差不多有一千块了。您那块地多大？」
「差不多二十亩。」
「说好多少钱买的？」
「四百。」

===Original===
“Have you got the deed?”
“Yes’m, but I ain’t finished paying yet; de Cunnel say as how I owes him two hundred dollars still, and I can’t see it. Dat’s why I come over here to talk wid you.”
“Where is the deed?”
He handed it to her and her heart sank. It was no deed, but a complicated contract binding the tenant hand and foot to the landlord. She sighed, he watching her eagerly.
“I’se getting old,” he explained, “and I ain’t got nobody to take care of me. I can’t work as I once could, and de overseers dey drives me too hard. I wants a little home to die in.”

===Chinese===
「您拿到地契了吗？」
「拿了，太太，可俺还没付完呢；上校说俺还欠他两百块，俺怎么也想不通。俺就为这个过来跟您商量。」
「地契呢？」
他递给她，她的心沉了下去。那不是地契，而是一份把佃户上上下下都绑死在地主身上的复杂契约。她叹了口气，他急切地看着她。
「俺老了，」他解释道，「又没人照应俺。俺不能像从前那样干活了，监工们又把俺逼得太紧。俺想有个小小的家，好死在里头。」

===Original===
Miss Smith’s throat swelled. She couldn’t tell him that he would never get one at the present rate; she only said:
“I’ll﻿—look this up. You come again next Saturday.”
Then sadly she watched the ragged old slave hobble away with his cherished “papers.” He greeted the young man at the gate and passed out, while the latter walked briskly up to the door and knocked.
“Why, how do you do, Robert?”
“How do you do, Miss Smith?”
“Well, are you getting things in shape so as to enter school early next year?”
Robert looked embarrassed.
“That’s what I came to tell you, Miss Smith. Mr. Cresswell has offered me forty acres of good land.”

===Chinese===
史密斯小姐喉咙发紧。她不能告诉他，照这个样子，他这辈子也休想有个家；她只说：
「我——把这事查一查。您下礼拜六再来。」
然后她悲伤地目送那个衣衫褴褛的老奴隶，揣着他视若珍宝的「字据」一瘸一拐地走了。他在门口跟那年轻人打了个招呼，便出去了，后者则快步走到门前敲门。
「哎呀，你好啊，罗伯特（Robert）？」
「您好，史密斯小姐？」
「怎么样，东西都收拾妥当，好明年一开学就入学了吗？」
罗伯特面露尴尬。
「我就是来跟您说这个的，史密斯小姐。克雷斯韦尔先生许了我四十亩好地。」

===Original===
Miss Smith looked disheartened.
“Robert, here you are almost finished, and my heart is set on your going to Atlanta University and finishing college. With your fine voice and talent for drawing﻿—”
A dogged look settled on Robert’s young bright face, and the speaker paused.
“What’s the use, Miss Smith﻿—what opening is there for a﻿—a nigger with an education?”
Miss Smith was shocked.
“Why﻿—why, every chance,” she protested, “and where there’s none *make* a chance!”
“Miss Taylor says”﻿—Miss Smith’s heart sank; how often had she heard that deadening phrase in the last year!﻿—“that there’s no use. That farming is the only thing we ought to try to do, and I reckon she thinks there ain’t much chance even there.”

===Chinese===
史密斯小姐面露沮丧。
「罗伯特，你都快读完了，我一心盼着你去亚特兰大大学（Atlanta University）把大学读完呢。凭你那副好嗓子，还有画画的天分——」
一股倔强的神色落到罗伯特那张年轻、明朗的脸上，说话的人便停住了。
「有什么用呢，史密斯小姐——一个——一个受了教育的黑鬼，又能有什么出路？」
史密斯小姐大吃一惊。
「怎么——怎么，机会有的是，」她反驳，「哪儿没有，就*自己闯*出一条来！」
「泰勒小姐说——」史密斯小姐的心一沉；这一年来她听过这句扫兴的话多少回了！——「说没用。说种地才是我们唯一该试着干的，我估摸她连这个也觉得没多大指望呢。」

===Original===
“Robert, farming is a noble calling. Whether you’re suited to it or not, I don’t yet know, but I’d like nothing better than to see you settled here in a decent home with a family, running a farm. But, Robert, farming doesn’t call for less intelligence than other things; it calls for more. It is because the world thinks any training good enough for a farmer that the Southern farmer is today practically at the mercy of his keener and more intelligent fellows. And of all people, Robert, your people need trained intelligence to cope with this problem of farming here. Without intelligence and training and some capital it is the wildest nonsense to think you can lead your people out of slavery. Look round you.” She told him of the visitors. “Are they not hard working honest people?”
“Yes, ma’am.”
“Yet they are slaves﻿—dumb driven cattle.”
“But they have no education.”

===Chinese===
「罗伯特，务农是高尚的营生。你适不适合，我还说不准，可我最乐意看到的，莫过于你在这儿安下一个体面的家、娶妻生子、经营一个农场了。可是，罗伯特，务农所要求的智力，不比别的事少；它要求得更多。正因为世人觉得什么样的训练都配得上一个农夫，南方农夫今天才会任由那些更精明、更有头脑的人宰割。在所有人里头，罗伯特，你们的人最需要受过训练的头脑，来对付这儿这桩种地的难事。没有头脑、没有训练、没有一点本钱，却妄想能领着你的人走出奴役，那是痴人说梦。你瞧瞧周围。」她把今天来的几位访客讲给他听。「他们难道不是勤劳诚实的人吗？」
「是，太太。」
「然而他们是奴隶——一群哑巴、被赶着走的牲口。」
「可他们没受过教育。」

===Original===
“And you have a smattering; therefore are ready to pit yourself against the organized plantation system without capital or experience. Robert, you may succeed; you may find your landlord honest and the way clear; but my advice to you is﻿—finish your education, develop your talents, and then come to your life work a full-fledged man and not a half-ignorant boy.”
“I’ll think of it,” returned the boy soberly. “I reckon you’re right. I know Miss Taylor don’t think much of us. But I’m tired of waiting; I want to get to work.”
Miss Smith laid a kindly hand upon his shoulder.
“I’ve been waiting thirty years, Robert,” she said, with feeling, and he hung his head.
“I wanted to talk about it,” he awkwardly responded, turning slowly away. But Miss Smith stopped him.
“Robert, where is the land Cresswell offers you?”
“It’s on the Tolliver place.”
“The Tolliver place?”
“Yes, he is going to buy it.”

===Chinese===
「而你呢，懂了点皮毛；于是便准备好，既无本钱、又无经验，就要去跟那有组织的种植园体制作对了。罗伯特，你也许会成功；你也许会发现你的地主诚实、道路通畅；可我劝你的是——把书读完，把你的天分发挥出来，然后以一个成熟男子的身份去赴你一生的事业，而不是一个半懂不懂的毛头小子。」
「我想想。」小伙子严肃地答。「我瞧您说得对。我知道泰勒小姐瞧不上我们。可我等厌了；我想干活去。」
史密斯小姐慈爱地把手搭在他肩上。
「我已经等了三十年了，罗伯特。」她动情地说，他低下了头。
「我是想跟您说说这事。」他笨拙地应着，慢慢转身要走。可史密斯小姐叫住了他。
「罗伯特，克雷斯韦尔许你的地在哪儿？」
「在托利弗那块地上。」
「托利弗那块地？」
「对，他要把它买下来。」

===Original===
Miss Smith dismissed the boy absently and sat down. The crisis seemed drawing near. She had not dreamed the Tolliver place was for sale. The old man must be hard pressed to sell to the Cresswells.
She started up. Why not go see him? Perhaps a mortgage on the strength of the endowment? It was dangerous﻿—but﻿—
She threw a veil over her hair, and opened the door. A woman stood there, who shrank and cowered, as if used to blows. Miss Smith eyed her grimly, then slowly stepped back.
“Come in,” she commanded briefly, motioning the woman to a chair.

===Chinese===
史密斯小姐心不在焉地把小伙子打发走，坐了下来。危机似乎正在逼近。她做梦也没想到托利弗那块地会出卖。那老头一定是被逼得紧了，才会卖给克雷斯韦尔家。
她霍地站起。何不去找他谈谈？也许凭着那笔捐助做抵押？这很冒险——可是——
她在头发上罩了一层面纱，推开门。门口站着一个女人，缩着、蜷着，像是挨惯了打的样子。史密斯小姐冷冷地打量她，然后慢慢退后一步。
「进来。」她简短地命令，示意那女人坐到椅子上。

===Original===
But she stood, a pathetic figure, faded, worn, yet with unmistakable traces of beauty in her golden face and soft brown hair. Miss Smith contemplated her sadly. Here was her most haunting failure, this girl whom she first had seen twelve years ago in her wonderful girlish comeliness. She had struggled and fought for her, but the forces of the devil had triumphed. She caught glimpses of her now and then, but today was the first time she had spoken to her for ten years. She saw the tears that gathered but did not fall; then her hands quivered.
“Bertie,” she began brokenly. The girl shivered, but stood aloof.
“Miss Smith,” she said. “No﻿—don’t talk﻿—I’m bad﻿—but I’ve got a little girl, Miss Smith, ten years old, and﻿—and﻿—I’m afraid for her; I want you to take her.”

===Chinese===
可她站着，一个可怜的身影，憔悴了、磨损了，却仍在她那张金黄色的脸和柔软的棕色头发里，留着不容置疑的美貌痕迹。史密斯小姐悲伤地端详她。这是她最叫她魂牵梦萦的一桩失败——这个姑娘，她头一回见她是十二年前，那时她少女的明艳令人惊叹。她曾为她挣扎过、战斗过，可魔鬼的力量占了上风。她时不时能瞥见她一眼，可今天，是她十年来头一回跟她说话。她看见那盈盈却不落下的泪；然后，那女人的手颤了。
「伯蒂（Bertie）。」她断断续续地开口。姑娘一哆嗦，却冷冷地站着。
「史密斯小姐，」她说，「不——别说了——我不好——可我有个小女儿，史密斯小姐，十岁了，还有——还有——我为她害怕；我要您收下她。」

===Original===
“I have no place for one so young. And why are you afraid for her?”
“The men there are beginning to notice her.”
“Where?”
“At Elspeth’s.”
“Do you stay there now?”
“Yes.”
“Why?”
“*He* wants me to.”
“Must you do as he wants?”
“Yes. But I want the child﻿—different.”
“Don’t *you* want to be different?”
The woman quivered again but she answered steadily: “No.”

===Chinese===
「这么小的孩子，我这里没地方收。你为什么为她害怕？」
「那里的男人开始注意到她了。」
「哪里？」
「埃尔斯贝思那儿。」
「你现在住在那儿吗？」
「是。」
「为什么？」
「*他*要我住。」
「他想要的，你就非得照做？」
「是。可我要这孩子——不一样。」
「*你*自己不想不一样吗？」
那女人又颤了一下，却稳稳地答：「不。」

===Original===
Miss Smith sank into a chair and moistened her dry lips.
“Elspeth’s is an awful place,” she affirmed solemnly.
“Yes.”
“And Zora?”
“She is not there much now, she stays away.”
“But if she escapes, why not you?”
“She wants to escape.”
“And you?”
“I don’t want to.”
This stubborn depravity was so distressing that Sarah Smith was at an utter loss what to say or do.
“I can do nothing﻿—” she began.
“For me,” the woman quickly replied; “I don’t ask anything; but for the child﻿—she isn’t to blame.”

===Chinese===
史密斯小姐跌进椅子里，润了润干裂的嘴唇。
「埃尔斯贝思那儿是个可怕的地方。」她郑重地断言。
「是。」
「那佐拉呢？」
「她如今不大在那儿了，她躲着。」
「可她能逃，你为什么不能？」
「她想逃。」
「你呢？」
「我不想。」
这份顽梗的堕落如此令人痛心，萨拉·史密斯简直不知说什么、做什么才好。
「我什么也做不了——」她开口。
「为我的事，」那女人赶忙接道，「我什么都不求；可为了这孩子——她没有错。」

===Original===
The older woman wavered.
“Won’t you try?” pleaded the younger.
“Yes﻿—I’ll try, I’ll try; I am trying all the time, but there are more things than my weak strength can do. Goodbye.”
Miss Smith stood a long time in the doorway, watching the fading figure and vaguely trying to remember what it was that she had started to do, when the sharp staccato step of a mule drew her attention to a rider who stopped at the gate. It was her neighbor, Tolliver﻿—a gaunt, yellow-faced white man, ragged, rough, and unkempt; one of the poor whites who had struggled up and failed. He spent no courtesy on the “nigger” teacher, but sat in his saddle and called her to the gate, and she went.
“Say,” he roughly opened up, “I’ve got to sell some land and them damn Cresswells are after it. You can have it for five thousand dollars if you git the cash in a week.” With a muttered oath he rode abruptly off; but not before she had seen the tears in his eyes.

===Chinese===
年长的女人动摇了。
「你不肯试试吗？」年轻的恳求。
「好——我试，我试；我一直都在试，可有许多事，超出了我这微弱之力所能为。再见。」
史密斯小姐在门口站了很久，望着那渐淡的身影，模模糊糊地想记起自己原本要去做什么，这时一头骡子那短促、顿挫的蹄声，把她的注意力引向一个在门口停下的骑者。那是她的邻居，托利弗（Tolliver）——一个清瘦、黄脸的白人，衣衫褴褛、粗鲁、不修边幅；是那些挣扎着爬上来、却又失败了的穷白人之一。他对这位「黑鬼」老师一个客气字也不肯破费，只是坐在鞍上，把她叫到门口来，她便过去了。
「我说，」他粗鲁地开口，「我得卖块地，那该死的克雷斯韦尔家正盯着呢。你要是能在一礼拜之内拿出现钱，五千块就归你。」他嘟囔着骂了一句，便陡然策马而去；可她已看见了他眼里的泪。

===Original===
All night Sarah Smith lay thinking, and all day she thought and dreamed. Toward dark she walked slowly out the gate and up the highway toward the Cresswell oaks. She had never been within the gates before, and she looked about thoughtfully. The great trees in their regular curving rows must have been planted more than half a century ago. The lawn was well tended and the flowers. Yes, there were signs of taste and wealth. “But it was built on a moan,” cried Miss Smith to herself, passionately, and she would not look round any more, but stared straight ahead where she saw old Colonel Cresswell smoking and reading on the veranda.
The Colonel saw her, too, and was uneasy, for he knew that Miss Smith had a sharp tongue and a most disconcerting method of argument, which he, as a Southern gentleman, courteous to all white females, even if they did eat with “niggers,” could not properly answer. He received her with courtesy, offered a chair, laid aside his cigar, and essayed some general remarks on cotton weather. But Miss Smith plunged into her subject:
“Colonel Cresswell, I’m thinking of raising some money from a mortgage on our school property.”

===Chinese===
萨拉·史密斯整夜躺着盘算，整日里也在想、在做梦。将近天黑时，她慢慢地走出大门，沿大路朝克雷斯韦尔家的橡树走去。她从前从未进过这道门，便若有所思地四下打量。那一排排整齐、弯曲成行的大树，想必是半个多世纪前栽下的。草坪打理得很好，花也是。是的，处处有品位与财富的迹象。「可它是建在呻吟之上的。」史密斯小姐激昂地在心里喊道，便不再四下看，只直直地朝前望，望见老克雷斯韦尔上校正在游廊上抽烟、读书。
上校也看见了她，心里不自在，因为他知道史密斯小姐嘴利，论证起来又最令人招架不住，而他身为南方绅士，对一切白人女性——哪怕她们真跟「黑鬼」一同吃饭——都彬彬有礼，不好正经作答。他客客气气地接待她，让座，搁下雪茄，试着扯了几句关于棉花天气的闲话。可史密斯小姐径直切入了正题：
「克雷斯韦尔上校，我正琢磨着，把我们学校的地产抵押一笔钱出来。」

===Original===
The Colonel’s face involuntarily lighted up. He thought he saw the beginning of the end of an institution which had been a thorn in his flesh ever since Tolliver, in a fit of rage, had sold land for a Negro school.
“H’m,” he reflected deprecatingly, wiping his brow.
“I need some ready money,” she continued, “to keep from curtailing our work.”
“Indeed?”
“I have good prospects in a year or so”﻿—the Colonel looked up sharply, but said nothing﻿—“and so I thought of a mortgage.”
“Money is pretty tight,” was the Colonel’s first objection.
“The land is worth, you know, at least fifty dollars an acre.”
“Not more than twenty-five dollars, I fear.”

===Chinese===
上校的脸不由自主地亮了一下。他以为自己看见了那个肉中刺走向终结的开端——自打托利弗当年一怒之下、把地卖给一所黑人学校以来，这所学校就成了他的肉中刺。
「嗯，」他装作不以为然地沉吟，擦了擦额头。
「我需要一笔现钱，」她接着说，「免得缩减我们的事业。」
「是吗？」
「一两年内我前景不错——」上校锐利地抬起头，却没作声——「所以我想到了抵押。」
「钱挺紧的。」上校头一个反对。
「您知道的，这地每亩至少值五十块。」
「恐怕不超过二十五块。」

===Original===
“Why, you wanted seventy-five dollars for poorer land last year! We have two hundred acres.” It was not for nothing that this lady had been born in New England.
“I wouldn’t reckon it as worth more than five thousand dollars,” insisted the Colonel.
“And ten thousand dollars for improvements.”
But the Colonel arose. “You had better talk to the directors of the Jefferson Bank,” he said politely. “They may accommodate you﻿—how much would you want?”
“Five thousand dollars,” Miss Smith replied. Then she hesitated. That would buy the land, to be sure; but money was needed to develop and run it; to install tenants; and then, too, for new teachers. But she said nothing more, and, nodding to his polite bow, departed. Colonel Cresswell had noticed her hesitation, and thought of it as he settled to his cigar again.

===Chinese===
「怎么，去年您那块更差的地，还开七十五块一亩呢！我们有二百亩。」这位女士生在新英格兰，可不是白生的。
「我估它值不过五千块。」上校一口咬定。
「还有一万块的改良呢。」
可上校站起身来。「您最好去跟杰斐逊银行的董事们谈谈，」他客气地说，「他们也许能通融——您要多少？」
「五千块。」史密斯小姐答。然后她犹豫了。这倒是能买下那块地，可开发经营、安置佃户还得要钱，还有新聘老师的开销。可她没再多说，朝他礼貌的一躬点了点头，便告辞了。克雷斯韦尔上校留意到了她的犹豫，重新叼起雪茄时，还把它琢磨了一番。

===Original===
Bles Alwyn arose next morning and examined the sky critically. He feared rain. The season had been quite wet enough, particularly down on the swamp land, and but yesterday Bles had viewed his dykes with apprehension for the black pool scowled about them. He dared not think what a long heavy rain might do to the wonderful island of cotton which now stood fully five feet high, with flowers and squares and budding bolls. It might not rain, but the safest thing would be to work at those dykes, so he started for spade and hoe. He heard Miss Smith calling, however.
“Bles﻿—hitch up!”
He was vexed. “Are you﻿—in a hurry, Miss Smith?” he asked.
“Yes, I am,” she replied, with unmistakable positiveness.

===Chinese===
第二天清早，布莱斯·阿尔文起来，仔细地审度天色。他怕下雨。这一季雨水已经够多了，尤其是沼泽那块洼地，就在昨天，布莱斯还忧心忡忡地查看过他的堤坝，因为那黑色的水潭正阴沉地在四周打转。他不敢想，一场漫长的大雨会对那座奇妙的棉花岛造成什么——那棉花如今足足有五英尺高，开着花、结着蕾、棉铃也冒了头。也许不会下，可最稳当的法子还是去加固那些堤，于是他动身去拿锹和锄。然而他听见史密斯小姐在喊他。
「布莱斯——套车！」
他有些恼。「您——您急吗，史密斯小姐？」他问。
「急，急。」她答，语气笃定得不容置疑。

===Original===
He started off, and hesitated. “Miss Smith, would Jim do to drive?”
“No,” sharply. “I want you particularly.” At another time she might have observed his anxiety, but today she was agitated. She knew she was taking a critical step.
Slowly Bles hitched up. After all it might not rain, he argued as they jogged toward town. In silence they rode on. Bles kept looking at the skies. The south was getting darker and darker. It might rain. It might rain only an hour or so, but, suppose it should rain a day﻿—two days﻿—a week?
Miss Smith was looking at her own skies and despite the promised sunrise they loomed darkly. Five thousand was needed for the land and at least another thousand for repairs. Two thousand would “buy” a half dozen desirable tenants by paying their debts to their present landlords. Then two thousand would be wanted for new teachers and a carpenter shop﻿—ten thousand dollars!

===Chinese===
他迈开步子，又犹豫了一下。「史密斯小姐，让吉姆来驾行吗？」
「不行，」她没好气地说，「我特地要你。」换在别的时候，她或许会留意到他的焦虑，可今天她自己心绪不宁。她知道自己正迈出关键的一步。
布莱斯慢吞吞地套上车。毕竟也许不会下雨嘛，他一路上朝镇上颠着时这样宽慰自己。两人默默前行。布莱斯不停地看天。南边越来越黑了。也许会下。也许只下一两个钟头，可万一要是一天——两天——一个礼拜呢？
史密斯小姐望着她自己的天空，尽管允诺过的日出已在望，那片天却阴沉沉地压下来。买地要五千，修缮至少还要一千。花两千能「买」下半打称心的佃户——替他们还清欠现在地主的债。然后还得两千请新老师、盖个木工房——一万元！

===Original===
It was a great temptation. And yet, once in the hands of these past-masters of debt-manipulation, would her school be safe? Suppose, after all, this Grey gift﻿—but she caught her breath sharply just as a wet splash of rain struck upon her forehead. No. God could not be so cruel. She pushed her bonnet back: how good and cool the water felt! But on Bles as he raised the buggy top it felt hot and fiery.
He felt the coming of some great calamity, the end of a dream. This rain might stay for days; it looked like such a downpour; and that would mean the end of the Silver Fleece; the end of Zora’s hopes; the end of everything. He gulped in despairing anger and hit the staid old horse the smartest tap she had known all summer.
“Why, Bles, what’s the matter?” called Miss Smith, as the horse started forward. He murmured something about getting wet and drew up at the Toomsville bank.

===Chinese===
这是个极大的诱惑。然而，一旦落进这些操纵债务的老手手里，她的学校还能安然无恙吗？万一，万一格雷这笔馈赠——可就在这时，一滴湿漉漉的雨溅在她额头上，她猛地倒抽一口气。不。上帝不会这么残忍。她把软帽往后一推：这水多舒坦、多清凉啊！可在正撑起车篷的布莱斯身上，这雨却滚烫灼热。
他感到一场大祸临头，一个梦的终结。这雨也许会下好几天；看这阵势，简直是倾盆大雨；那就意味着银羊毛的末日；佐拉希望的末日；一切的末日。他在绝望的愤怒里咽了口唾沫，给了那匹稳当的老马一鞭子——这是它整夏挨过的最重的一下。
「哎呀，布莱斯，怎么了？」史密斯小姐见马儿一冲，喊道。他咕哝了句什么淋雨之类的话，便在图姆斯维尔银行门前停住了。

===Original===
Miss Smith was invited politely into the private parlor. She explained her business. The President was there and Colonel Cresswell and one other local director.
“I have come for a mortgage. Our land is, as you know, gentlemen, worth at least ten thousand dollars; the buildings cost fifteen thousand dollars; our property is, therefore, conservatively valued at twenty-five thousand dollars. Now I want to mortgage it for”﻿—she hesitated﻿—“five thousand dollars.”
Colonel Cresswell was silent, but the president said:
“Money is rather scarce just now, Miss Smith; but it happens that I have ten thousand dollars on hand, which we prefer, however, to loan in one lump sum. Now, if the security were ample, I think perhaps you might get this ten thousand dollars.”

===Chinese===
史密斯小姐被客气地请进了私室。她说明了来意。行长在，克雷斯韦尔上校也在，还有一位本地的董事。
「我是来办抵押的。我们的地，各位知道，至少值一万元；房屋造价一万五千元；因此，我们的财产保守估值二万五千元。现在我想把它抵押——」她犹豫了一下——「五千元。」
克雷斯韦尔上校沉默不语，行长却说：
「眼下钱挺紧的，史密斯小姐；不过巧了，我手头有一万元，我们宁可整笔整笔地贷。这个嘛，要是抵押充足的话，我想您也许能贷到这一万元。」

===Original===
Miss Smith grew white; it was the sum she wanted. She tried to escape the temptation, yet the larger amount was more than twice as desirable to her as the smaller, and she knew that they knew it. They were trying to tempt her; they wanted as firm a hold on the school property as possible. And yet, why should she hesitate? It was a risk, but the returns would be enormous﻿—she must do it. Besides, there was the endowment; it was certain; yes﻿—she felt forced to close the bargain.
“Very well,” she declared her decision, and they handed her the preliminary papers. She took the pen and glanced at Mr. Cresswell; he was smiling slightly, but nevertheless she signed her name grimly, in a large round hand, “Sarah Smith.”

===Chinese===
史密斯小姐脸都白了；这正是她想要的数目。她竭力想躲开这诱惑，可那更大的数目对她而言，比小的那个称心不止一倍，她也知道他们知道这一点。他们在诱她上钩；他们想尽可能牢牢地攥住学校的地产。然而，她有何可犹豫的？这是冒险，可回报将极为丰厚——她非做不可。况且还有那笔捐助；那是笃定的；是的——她觉得自己非拍板不可。
「好极了。」她宣布了决定，他们把初步文件递给她。她拿起笔，瞟了一眼克雷斯韦尔先生；他微微地笑着，可她还是毅然地、用大大的圆体字，签下了自己的名字——「萨拉·史密斯」。

## XIII Mrs. Grey Gives a Dinner / XIII 格雷太太设宴

===Original===
The Hon. Charles Smith, Miss Sarah’s brother, was walking swiftly uptown from Mr. Easterly’s Wall Street office and his face was pale. At last the Cotton Combine was to all appearances an assured fact and he was slated for the Senate. The price he had paid was high: he was to represent the interests of the new trust and sundry favorable measures were already drafted and reposing in the safe of the combine’s legal department. Among others was one relating to child labor, another that would effect certain changes in the tariff, and a proposed law providing for a cotton bale of a shape and dimensions different from the customary﻿—the last constituting a particularly clever artifice which, under the guise of convenience in handling, would necessitate the installation of entirely new gin and compress machinery, to be supplied, of course, by the trust.

===Chinese===
可敬的查尔斯·史密斯（Charles Smith），萨拉小姐的哥哥，正从伊斯特利先生的华尔街办公室快步往城里走，脸色发白。棉花合并案总算万事俱备、只欠东风了，而他也内定了参议院的位置。他付的代价可不低：他要代表新托拉斯的利益，几桩有利的法案已经拟好，躺在托拉斯法律部的保险柜里了。其中包括一项关于童工的，另一项会对关税作若干改动，还有一项拟议中的法律，规定一种与惯例不同的形状和尺寸的棉包——最后这一项是个特别精巧的伎俩，名为便于搬运，实则必须安装全新的轧花和压包设备，而这些设备，当然，概由托拉斯供应。

===Original===
As Mr. Smith drew near Mrs. Grey’s Murray Hill residence his face had melted to a cynical smile. After all why should he care? He had tried independence and philanthropy and failed. Why should he not be as other men? He had seen many others that very day swallow the golden bait and promise everything. They were gentlemen. Why should he pose as better than his fellows? There was young Cresswell. Did his aristocratic air prevent his succumbing to the lure of millions and promising the influence of his father and the whole Farmer’s League to the new project? Mr. Smith snapped his fingers and rang the bell. The door opened softly. The dark woodwork of the old English wainscoting glowed with the crimson flaming of logs in the wide fireplace. There was just the touch of early autumn chill in the air without, that made both the fire and the table with its soft linen, gold and silver plate, and twinkling glasses a warming, satisfying sight.

===Chinese===
史密斯先生走近格雷太太在默里山（Murray Hill）的宅邸时，脸上的神情已化作一抹讥诮的笑。说到底，他何必在乎呢？他试过独立、试过慈善，都失败了。他为何不能跟旁人一样？就在今天，他还亲眼看见不少人吞下那金色的诱饵，什么都一口答应下来。他们都是体面人。他又何必端出一副比同侪清高的架势？小克雷斯韦尔不就是吗？他那副贵族气派，可曾妨碍他屈服于百万之利的诱惑、把他父亲和整个农民同盟的影响力，都许给了这项新计划？史密斯先生弹了个响指，按响了门铃。门轻轻地开了。古老的英国护墙板那深色的木活，在宽大壁炉里熊熊燃烧的圆木的猩红火光映照下，闪着暖光。外头的空气里恰好带着一丝初秋的凉意，这一来，壁炉和那张铺着柔软桌布、摆着金银餐具、闪烁着玻璃杯的餐桌，便成了一幅暖人、惬意的景象。

===Original===
Mrs. Grey was a portly woman, inclined to think much of her dinner and her clothes, both of which were always rich and costly. She was not herself a notably intelligent woman; she greatly admired intelligence or whatever looked to her like intelligence in others. Her money, too, was to her an ever worrying mystery and surprise, which she found herself always scheming to husband shrewdly and spend philanthropically﻿—a difficult combination.
As she awaited her guests she surveyed the table with both satisfaction and disquietude, for her social functions were few, tonight there were﻿—she checked them off on her fingers﻿—Sir James Creighton, the rich English manufacturer, and Lady Creighton, Mr. and Mrs. Vanderpool, Mr. Harry Cresswell and his sister, John Taylor and his sister, and Mr. Charles Smith, whom the evening papers mentioned as likely to be United States Senator from New Jersey﻿—a selection of guests that had been determined, unknown to the hostess, by the meeting of cotton interests earlier in the day.

===Chinese===
格雷太太是位富态的夫人，颇为看重自己的晚宴和衣裳，这两样她都向来办得奢华、昂贵。她自己算不上多么聪慧的女子；可她极慕聪明，或任何在她看来像是聪明的东西。她的钱对她而言，也是一种恒久的烦忧与惊奇，她发现自己总在筹谋着如何精明地节流、又如何慷慨地做慈善——这是一桩难以兼得的事。
她等候宾客时，打量着餐桌，既满意，又不安，因为她举办社交活动的次数不多。今晚到场的有——她扳着指头数——富有的英国厂主詹姆斯·克雷顿爵士（Sir James Creighton）和克雷顿夫人（Lady Creighton），范德普尔夫妇，哈里·克雷斯韦尔先生和他的妹妹，约翰·泰勒和他的妹妹，还有查尔斯·史密斯先生——晚报上说此人有可能成为新泽西州的联邦参议员——这一桌客人的选定，女主人并不知情，实是当天上午那场棉花利益各方集会的结果。

===Original===
Mrs. Grey’s chef was high-priced and efficient, and her butler was the envy of many; consequently, she knew the dinner would be good. To her intense satisfaction, it was far more than this. It was a most agreeable couple of hours; all save perhaps Mr. Smith unbent, the Englishman especially, and the Vanderpools were most gracious; but if the general pleasure was owing to any one person particularly it was to Mr. Harry Cresswell. Mrs. Grey had met Southerners before, but not intimately, and she always had in mind vividly their cruelty to “poor Negroes,” a subject she made a point of introducing forthwith. She was therefore most agreeably surprised to hear Mr. Cresswell express himself so cordially as approving of Negro education.
“Why, I thought,” said Mrs. Grey, “that you Southerners rather disapproved﻿—or at least﻿—”
Mr. Cresswell inclined his head courteously.
“We Southerners, my dear Mrs. Grey, are responsible for a variety of reputations.” And he told an anecdote that set the table laughing. “Seriously, though,” he continued, “we are not as black as the blacks paint us, although on the whole I *prefer* that Helen should marry﻿—a white man.”

===Chinese===
格雷太太的厨师高价而能干，她的管家更令许多人艳羡；因此她知道，这顿晚饭错不了。令她大为满意的是，这远不止于不错。这是极为惬意的两个钟头；除了史密斯先生可能例外，人人都松弛下来，那位英国人尤甚，范德普尔夫妇也最为和蔼；可若说这份普遍的愉快要特别归功于谁，那便是哈里·克雷斯韦尔先生。格雷太太从前见过南方人，却不熟，她脑海里总是鲜明地记得他们对「可怜的黑人」的残酷——这个题目她向来是一上来就一定要提起的。因此，听见克雷斯韦尔先生这般热忱地表示赞成黑人教育，她是大喜过望的。
「哎呀，我还以为，」格雷太太说，「你们南方人相当反对呢——至少——」
克雷斯韦尔先生客气地点了点头。
「亲爱的格雷太太，我们南方人为种种名声负着责任呢。」他讲了个趣事，引得全桌大笑。「不过说正经的，」他接着说，「我们并不像黑人把咱们描得那么黑，虽然总的来说，我*还是*宁愿海伦嫁——一个白人。」

===Original===
They all glanced at Miss Cresswell, who lay softly back in her chair like a white lily, gleaming and bejewelled, her pale face flushing under the scrutiny; Mrs. Grey was horrified.
“Why﻿—why the idea!” she sputtered. “Why, Mr. Cresswell, how can you conceive of anything else﻿—no Northerner dreams﻿—”
Mr. Cresswell sipped his wine slowly.
“No﻿—no﻿—I do not think you do *mean* that﻿—” He paused and the Englishman bent forward.
“Really, now, you do not mean to say that there is a danger of﻿—of amalgamation, do you?” he sang.
Mr. Cresswell explained. No, of course there was no immediate danger; but when people were suddenly thrust beyond their natural station, filled with wild ideas and impossible ambitions, it meant terrible danger to Southern white women.

===Chinese===
众人都瞟了克雷斯韦尔小姐一眼——她软软地靠在椅背上，像一朵白百合，珠光宝气地闪着光，在那打量之下，苍白的脸泛起了红晕；格雷太太吓坏了。
「怎么——怎么有这种想法！」她结结巴巴地说。「哎呀，克雷斯韦尔先生，您怎么会想象出别的什么来——没有一个北方人会梦想——」
克雷斯韦尔先生慢慢地品着酒。
「不——不——我不觉得您是*当真*那个意思——」他顿了顿，那位英国人探过身来。
「此话当真，您总不是要说，有那种——那种血统混杂的危险吧，是不是？」他拖着调子说。
克雷斯韦尔先生解释了一番。不，当然没有什么迫在眉睫的危险；可当人被猛地推到他们天生的地位之上、满脑子狂野的念头和不可能的野心时，这对南方的白人妇女就意味着可怕的危险。

===Original===
“But you believe in some education?” asked Mary Taylor.
“I believe in the training of people to their highest capacity.” The Englishman here heartily seconded him.
“But,” Cresswell added significantly, “capacity differs enormously between races.”
The Vanderpools were sure of this and the Englishman, instancing India, became quite eloquent. Mrs. Grey was mystified, but hardly dared admit it. The general trend of the conversation seemed to be that most individuals needed to be submitted to the sharpest scrutiny before being allowed much education, and as for the “lower races” it was simply criminal to open such useless opportunities to them.
“Why, I had a colored servant-girl once,” laughed Mrs. Vanderpool by way of climax, “who spent half her wages in piano lessons.”

===Chinese===
「可您是赞成某种教育的吧？」玛丽·泰勒问。
「我赞成把人培养到他力所能及的最高程度。」英国人在这里热烈地附和他。
「不过，」克雷斯韦尔意味深长地补了一句，「各族之间的能力，差别可大了去了。」
范德普尔夫妇对此深信不疑，英国人举印度为例，竟讲得头头是道。格雷太太给弄糊涂了，却又不敢承认。整场谈话的总趋向似乎是：多数人在被允许受多少教育之前，都得经过最严格的审查；至于那些「低等种族」，给他们开启这种无用的机会简直是犯罪。
「哎呀，我从前有个有色人种的女佣，」范德普尔太太作为压轴笑了起来，「把自己一半的工钱花在了学钢琴上。」

===Original===
Then Mary Taylor, whose conscience was uncomfortable, said:
“But, Mr. Cresswell, you surely believe in schools like Miss Smith’s?”
“Decidedly,” returned Mr. Cresswell, with enthusiasm, “it has done great good.”
Mrs. Grey was gratified and murmured something of Miss Smith’s “sacrifice.”
“Positively heroic,” added Cresswell, avoiding his sister’s eyes.
“Of course,” Mary Taylor hastened to encourage this turn of the conversation, “there are many points on which Miss Smith and I disagree, but I think everybody admires her work.”
Mrs. Grey wanted particulars. “What did you disagree about?” she asked bluntly.
“I may be responsible for some of the disagreement,” interrupted Mr. Cresswell, hesitatingly; “I’m afraid Miss Smith does not approve of us white Southerners.”

===Chinese===
这时，良心不安的玛丽·泰勒说道：
「可是，克雷斯韦尔先生，您总归是相信史密斯小姐那样的学校的吧？」
「毫无疑问。」克雷斯韦尔先生热忱地答道，「它做了大好事。」
格雷太太心满意足，咕哝了几句史密斯小姐的「牺牲」。
「简直是英雄壮举。」克雷斯韦尔附和道，避开了妹妹的眼睛。
「当然啦，」玛丽·泰勒赶忙顺着这话题鼓励下去，「我跟史密斯小姐在许多问题上有分歧，可我想人人都钦佩她的事业。」
格雷太太要听具体。「你们分歧什么？」她直截了当地问。
「有些分歧，恐怕要算在我头上，」克雷斯韦尔先生迟疑地插话，「我怕史密斯小姐并不赞许我们这些南方白人。」

===Original===
“But you mean to say you can’t even advise her?”
“Oh, no; we can. But﻿—we’re not﻿—er﻿—exactly welcomed. In fact,” said Cresswell gravely, “the chief criticism I have against your Northerners’ schools for Negroes is, that they not only fail to enlist the sympathy and aid of the *best* Southerners, but even repel it.”
“That is very wrong﻿—very wrong,” commented the Englishman warmly, a sentiment in which Mrs. Grey hastened to agree.
“Of course,” continued Cresswell, “I am free to confess that I have no personal desire to dabble in philanthropy, or conduct schools of any kind; my hands are full of other matters.”
“But it’s precisely the advice of such disinterested men that philanthropic work needs,” Mr. Vanderpool urged.
“Well, I volunteered advice once in this case and I shan’t repeat the experiment soon,” said Cresswell laughing. Mrs. Grey wanted to hear the incident, but the young man was politely reluctant. Mary Taylor, however, related the tale of Zora to Mrs. Grey’s private ear later.

===Chinese===
「可您是 说，您连给她出出主意都不行？」
「哦，不；我们能。可是——我们并不——呃——怎么受欢迎。事实上，」克雷斯韦尔郑重地说，「我对你们北方人办的那些黑人学校，最大的批评就是：它们不但不去争取*最好的*南方人的同情和帮助，反倒把人家拒之门外。」
「这太不对了——太不对了。」英国人热烈地评论，格雷太太也赶忙附和。
「当然，」克雷斯韦尔接着说，「我坦白说，我个人并不想染指什么慈善，也不想办什么学校；我手头别的事已经够多了。」
「可慈善事业所需要的，恰恰正是这种无私之人的忠告啊。」范德普尔先生劝道。
「这个嘛，这一回我倒是自告奋勇出过一次主意，我不打算很快就再试这一招了。」克雷斯韦尔笑道。格雷太太想听这件事的始末，可年轻人客气地不愿多讲。不过，玛丽·泰勒后来私下把佐拉的事讲给了格雷太太听。

===Original===
“Fortunately,” said Mr. Vanderpool, “Northerners and Southerners are arriving at a better mutual understanding on most of these matters.”
“Yes, indeed,” Cresswell agreed. “After all, they never were far apart, even in slavery days; both sides were honest and sincere.”
All through the dinner Mr. Smith had been preoccupied and taciturn. Now he abruptly shot a glance at Cresswell.
“I suppose that one was right and one was wrong.”
“No,” said Cresswell, “both were right.”
“I thought the only excuse for fighting was a great Right; if Right is on neither side or simultaneously on both, then War is not only Hell but Damnation.”
Mrs. Grey looked shocked and Mrs. Vanderpool smiled.
“How about fighting for exercise?” she suggested.
“At any rate,” said Cresswell, “we can all agree on helping these poor victims of our quarrel as far as their limited capacity will allow﻿—and no farther, for that is impossible.”

===Chinese===
「幸好，」范德普尔先生说，「在大多数这类问题上，北方人和南方人正达成更好的相互理解。」
「是啊，一点不错。」克雷斯韦尔附和。「说到底，他们从来就相去不远，即便在蓄奴时代也是；双方都是诚实的、诚恳的。」
整个晚宴期间，史密斯先生一直心不在焉、沉默寡言。这时他突然朝克雷斯韦尔投去一瞥。
「我猜，是一方对、一方错吧。」
「不，」克雷斯韦尔说，「两方都对。」
「我原以为，打仗的唯一借口是一个宏大的『正义』；要是双方都不占正义，或者同时都占正义，那么战争就不仅是地狱，简直是万劫不复了。」
格雷太太面露惊骇，范德普尔太太笑了笑。
「那为了锻炼身体而打呢？」她提议。
「无论如何，」克雷斯韦尔说，「我们都能达成一致：在这场我们彼此争斗的可怜受害者力所能及的范围内——去帮助他们，且仅止于此，因为再多就不可能了。」

===Original===
Very soon after dinner Charles Smith excused himself. He was not yet inured to the ways of high finance, and the programme of the cotton barons, as unfolded that day, lay heavy on his mind, despite all his philosophy.
“I have had a﻿—full day,” he explained to Mrs. Grey.

===Chinese===
晚饭后不久，查尔斯·史密斯便告辞了。他尚未习惯高级金融的那一套，尽管他满腹的达观哲学，那些棉花大王们（cotton barons）当天摊开的计划，仍沉甸甸地压在他心头。
「我今天——过得挺充实。」他向格雷太太解释。

## XIV Love / XIV 爱

===Original===
The rain was sweeping down in great thick winding sheets. The wind screamed in the ancient Cresswell oaks and swirled across the swamp in loud, wild gusts. The waters roared and gurgled in the streams, and along the roadside. Then, when the wind fell murmuring away, the clouds grew blacker and blacker and rain in long slim columns fell straight from Heaven to earth digging itself into the land and throwing back the red mud in angry flashes.
So it rained for one long week, and so for seven endless days Bles watched it with leaden heart. He knew the Silver Fleece﻿—his and Zora’s﻿—must be ruined. It was the first great sorrow of his life; it was not so much the loss of the cotton itself﻿—but the fantasy, the hopes, the dreams built around it. If it failed, would not they fail? Was not this angry beating rain, this dull spiritless drizzle, this wild war of air and earth, but foretaste and prophecy of ruin and discouragement, of the utter futility of striving? But if his own despair was great his pain at the plight of Zora made it almost unbearable. He did not see her in these seven days. He pictured her huddled there in the swamp in the cheerless leaky cabin with worse than no companions. Ah! the swamp, the cruel swamp! It was a fearful place in the rain. Its oozing mud and fetid vapors, its clinging slimy draperies﻿—how they twined about the bones of its victims and chilled their hearts. Yet here his Zora﻿—his poor disappointed child﻿—was imprisoned.

===Chinese===
大雨正一片一片、又厚又卷地扫下来。风在古老的克雷斯韦尔橡树间尖啸，一阵阵又响又野地卷过沼泽。溪水里、路边，水在咆哮、在汩汩作响。而后，风呜咽着沉了下去，云却越积越黑，雨化作一道道细长的水柱，笔直地从天上落到人间，钻进泥土，在愤怒的闪光中把红色的泥翻溅起来。
雨就这样下了一整周，布莱斯就这样以铅一般沉的心，望着这七个无尽的白昼。他知道，那银羊毛——他和佐拉的——一定毁了。这是他平生头一桩大悲恸；倒不全是棉花本身的损失——而是围绕着它筑起的幻想、希望和梦境。要是它完了，他们不也就完了吗？这场愤怒地拍打着的雨，这场阴沉、了无生气的毛毛细雨，这场天空与大地的狂野之战，岂不就是毁灭与气馁的预尝与预言，岂不就是对一切挣扎皆属徒劳的预言？可如果说他自己的绝望已重，他为佐拉的处境所感到的痛苦，更几乎叫他无法承受。这七天里他没见过她。他想象她瑟缩在沼泽那漏着雨、了无暖意的小屋里，身边连个作伴的人都没有，比没有还糟。啊！那沼泽，那残酷的沼泽！下雨天它是个可怖的地方。它渗出的烂泥和腥臭的水汽，它那些黏糊糊、湿漉漉、攀附人的帷幔——它们如何缠绕住受害者的骨头，如何凉透他们的心。然而，他的佐拉——他那可怜的、失望的孩子——就被囚在那里。

===Original===
Child? He had always called her child﻿—but now in the inward illumination of these dark days he knew her as neither child nor sister nor friend, but as the One Woman. The revelation of his love lighted and brightened slowly till it flamed like a sunrise over him and left him in burning wonder. He panted to know if she, too, knew, or knew and cared not, or cared and knew not. She was so strange and human a creature. To her all things meant something﻿—nothing was aimless, nothing merely happened. Was this rain beating down and back her love for him, or had she never loved? He walked his room, gripping his hands, peering through the misty windows toward the swamp﻿—rain, rain, rain, nothing but rain. The world was water veiled in mists.
Then of a sudden, at midday, the sun shot out, hot and still; no breath of air stirred; the sky was like blue steel; the earth steamed. Bles rushed to the edge of the swamp and stood there irresolute. Perhaps﻿—if the water had but drained from the cotton!﻿—it was so strong and tall! But, pshaw! Where was the use of imagining? The lagoon had been level with the dykes a week ago; and now? He could almost see the beautiful Silver Fleece, bedraggled, drowned, and rolling beneath the black lake of slime. He went back to his work, but early in the morning the thought of it lured him again. He must at least see the grave of his hope and Zora’s, and out of it resurrect new love and strength.

===Chinese===
孩子？他一向叫她孩子——可如今，在这些黑暗日子里内心的照亮之下，他认出她既非孩子，也非姐妹，也非朋友，而是那唯一的女人。他这份爱的启示，慢慢地亮起来、亮起来，终于像一轮日出在他头顶燃烧，把他留在灼热的惊奇里。他渴切地想知道，她是否也知道，或知道却不在乎，或在乎却不知道。她是这样一个奇异而有人情气的生灵。于她，凡事都有意义——没有一件事是漫无目的的，没有一件事只是偶然发生。这场雨是在把她对他的爱打下去、压回去，还是她从未爱过？他在房里走来走去，攥紧双手，透过雾蒙蒙的窗子朝沼泽张望——雨、雨、雨，除了雨什么也没有。这世界是水，裹在雾里。
忽然，正午时分，太阳射了出来，灼热而沉静；没有一丝风；天空像蓝色的钢；大地蒸腾。布莱斯冲到沼泽边上，迟疑地站着。也许——要是水已经从棉花上排走了呢！——它那么壮、那么高！可是，呸！瞎想有什么用？一礼拜前，那潟湖的水就跟堤坝齐平了；现在呢？他几乎能看见那美丽的银羊毛，湿淋淋的、淹死了，在黑色的泥湖底下翻滚。他回去干活，可一大早这念头又把他引了来。他至少得去看看他和佐拉那份希望的坟，并从坟里唤起新的爱与力量。

===Original===
Perhaps she, too, might be there, waiting, weeping. He started at the thought. He hurried forth sadly. The raindrops were still dripping and gleaming from the trees, flashing back the heavy yellow sunlight. He splashed and stamped along, farther and farther onward until he neared the rampart of the clearing, and put foot upon the tree-bridge. Then he looked down. The lagoon was dry. He stood a moment bewildered, then turned and rushed upon the island. A great sheet of dazzling sunlight swept the place, and beneath lay a mighty mass of olive green, thick, tall, wet, and willowy. The squares of cotton, sharp-edged, heavy, were just about to burst to bolls! And underneath, the land lay carefully drained and black! For one long moment he paused, stupid, agape with utter amazement, then leaned dizzily against a tree.
The swamp, the eternal swamp, had been drained in its deepest fastness; but, how?﻿—how? He gazed about, perplexed, astonished. What a field of cotton! what a marvellous field! But how had it been saved?

===Chinese===
也许她也在那里，等着，哭着呢。这个念头叫他一惊。他哀伤地匆匆出来。雨滴仍从树上滴落、闪光，把浓重的金黄阳光反射回来。他一路蹚着、踩着水，越走越远，直走到那片开垦地的屏障跟前，把脚踏上了树桥。然后他朝下一望。潟湖干了。他怔怔地站了片刻，随即转身冲上岛去。一大片炫目的阳光扫过这地方，底下是一大片橄榄绿的庞然大物，又密、又高、又湿、如柳般柔韧。棉花的青铃，棱角分明、沉甸甸的，眼看就要胀裂成棉铃了！而底下，那地被仔细地排干了水，黑油油的！他愣愣地站了好一会儿，惊得合不拢嘴，然后晕眩地靠在一棵树上。
那沼泽，那亘古的沼泽，竟在最深的腹地被排干了水；可是，怎么做到的？——怎么做到的？他困惑地、惊奇地四下张望。多好的一片棉花！多奇妙的一片棉花！可它是怎么得救的？

===Original===
He skirted the island slowly, stopping near Zora’s oak. Here lay the reading of the riddle: with infinite work and pain, someone had dug a canal from the lagoon to the creek, into which the former had drained by a long and crooked way, thus allowing it to empty directly. The canal went straight, a hundred yards through stubborn soil, and it was oozing now with slimy waters.
He sat down weak, bewildered, and one thought was uppermost﻿—Zora! And with the thought came a low moan of pain. He wheeled and leapt toward the dripping shelter in the tree. There she lay﻿—wet, bedraggled, motionless, gray-pallid beneath her dark-drawn skin, her burning eyes searching restlessly for some lost thing, her lips a-moaning.
In dumb despair he dropped beside her and gathered her in his arms. The earth staggered beneath him as he stumbled on; the mud splashed and sunlight glistened; he saw long snakes slithering across his path and fear-struck beasts fleeing before his coming. He paused for neither path nor way but went straight for the school, running in mighty strides, yet gently, listening to the moans that struck death upon his heart. Once he fell headlong, but with a great wrench held her from harm, and minded not the pain that shot through his ribs. The yellow sunshine beat fiercely around and upon him, as he stumbled into the highway, lurched across the mud-strewn road, and panted up the porch.
“Miss Smith﻿—!” he gasped, and then﻿—darkness.

===Chinese===
他慢慢地绕岛而行，在佐拉的橡树附近停下。谜底就在这里：有人费了无穷的劳作与痛苦，从潟湖挖了一条渠，通到那条小溪，湖水便沿着一条又长又弯的路排了进去，从而直接泄干。那条渠笔直地穿过一百码倔强的泥土，此刻还渗着黏糊糊的水。
他虚弱地、茫然地坐下，一个念头占满了他的心——佐拉！伴随这念头而来的，是一声低低的、痛苦的呻吟。他猛地转身，朝那棵树上滴着水的窝扑过去。她躺在那里——湿透了、狼狈不堪、一动不动，黝黑紧绷的皮肤下透出灰白，她燃烧的眼睛不安地搜寻着什么丢失的东西，嘴唇呻吟着。
他在绝望的沉默中俯身到她身边，把她抱进怀里。大地在他脚下摇晃，他踉跄前行；泥水四溅，阳光闪烁；他看见长长的蛇从路上滑过，受惊的野兽在他到来前四散奔逃。他不顾路径，径直朝学校奔去，迈着巨大的步子飞奔，却又小心翼翼，听着那一声声让他心死的呻吟。有一次他扑倒在地，可他用大力把她护住，没让她伤着，自己肋间那一阵钻痛也浑不在意。金黄的阳光在他四周、在他身上猛烈地炙烤，他跌跌撞撞地冲上大路，踉踉跄跄地越过泥泞的道路，气喘吁吁地爬上门廊。
「史密斯小姐——！」他喘着气喊了一声，然后——眼前一黑。

===Original===
The years of the days of her dying were ten. The boy that entered the darkness and the shadow of death emerged a man, a silent man and grave, working furiously and haunting, day and night, the little window above the door. At last, of one gray morning when the earth was stillest, they came and told him, “She will live!” And he went out under the stars, lifted his long arms and sobbed: “Curse me, O God, if I let me lose her again!” And God remembered this in after years.
The hope and dream of harvest was upon the land. The cotton crop was short and poor because of the great rain; but the sun had saved the best, and the price had soared. So the world was happy, and the face of the black-belt green and luxuriant with thickening flecks of the coming foam of the cotton.
Up in the sick room Zora lay on the little white bed. The net and web of endless things had been crawling and creeping around her; she had struggled in dumb, speechless terror against some mighty grasping that strove for her life, with gnarled and creeping fingers; but now at last, weakly, she opened her eyes and questioned.

===Chinese===
她濒死的那几天日子，漫长得像十年。走进那黑暗和死亡阴影的男孩，出来时已是个男人——一个沉默而凝重的男人，发疯似地干活，日日夜夜地守在那扇门上方的小窗前。终于，在一个万物最静的、灰蒙蒙的早晨，他们来告诉他：「她会活的！」他便走到星空之下，举起两条长臂，哽咽道：「上帝啊，你诅咒我吧，要是我再让自己失去她！」日后上帝记起了这一句。
收获的希望和梦想，降临在大地上。因那场大雨，棉花歉收、欠佳；可太阳救下了最好的那些，价格飞涨。于是世界是欢快的，黑土地带的面容是一片绿意葱茏，随着即将到来的棉花白沫，点点斑斑愈发浓密。
楼上病室里，佐拉躺在那张白色的小床上。无尽事物的罗网和蛛丝曾围着爬行蠕动；她曾在哑默无言的恐惧中，与某种攫取她生命的、长着扭曲而爬行的手指的庞然之力搏斗；可如今她终于虚弱地睁开了眼，发问。

===Original===
Bles, where was he? The Silver Fleece, how was it? The Sun, the Swamp? Then finding all well, she closed her eyes and slept. After some days they let her sit by the window, and she saw Bles pass, but drew back timidly when he looked; and he saw only the flutter of her gown, and waved.
At last there came a day when they let her walk down to the porch, and she felt the flickering of her strength again. Yet she looked different; her buxom comeliness was spiritualized; her face looked smaller, and her masses of hair, brought low about her ears, heightened her ghostly beauty; her skin was darkly transparent, and her eyes looked out from velvet veils of gloom. For a while she lay in her chair, in happy, dreamy pleasure at sun and bird and tree. Bles did not know yet that she was down; but soon he would come searching, for he came each hour, and she pressed her little hands against her breast to still the beating of her heart and the bursting wonder of her love.

===Chinese===
布莱斯，他在哪儿？银羊毛，它怎么样了？太阳呢，沼泽呢？发现一切无恙之后，她闭上眼，睡着了。过了几天，他们让她坐到窗边，她看见布莱斯走过，他一望过来她便怯怯地缩回去；他只看见她衣裳的一闪，挥了挥手。
终于来了那么一天，他们让她下到门廊去走走，她又感到自己的力量在轻轻跳动。可她看上去不一样了；她那丰盈的俏丽灵性化了；她的脸显得小了，她那厚重的头发低低地拢在耳边，更衬出她幽灵般的美；她的皮肤是深色的、透明的，她的眼睛从天鹅绒般的幽暗帷幕后面望出来。她在椅子里躺了一会儿，对着太阳、鸟儿和树，怀着快乐的、如梦似幻的愉悦。布莱斯还不知道她下来了；可他很快就会来找，因为他每个钟头都来，她把两只小手按在胸口，好按住心跳，按住那份几乎要迸裂的爱之惊奇。

===Original===
Then suddenly a panic seized her. He must not find her here﻿—not here; there was but one place in all the earth for them to meet, and that was yonder in the Silver Fleece. She rose with a fleeting glance, gathered the shawl round her, then gliding forward, wavering, tremulous, slipped across the road and into the swamp. The dark mystery of the Swamp swept over her; the place was hers. She had been born within its borders; within its borders she had lived and grown, and within its borders she had met her love. On she hurried until, sweeping down to the lagoon and the island, lo! the cotton lay before her! A great white foam was spread upon its brown and green; the whole field was waving and shivering in the sunlight. A low cry of pleasure burst from her lips; she forgot her weakness, and picking her way across the bridge, stood still amid the cotton that nestled about her shoulders, clasping it lovingly in her hands.
He heard that she was downstairs and ran to meet her with beating heart. The chair was empty; but he knew. There was but one place then for these two souls to meet. Yet it was far, and he feared, and ran with startled eyes.

===Chinese===
忽然一阵慌乱攫住了她。他不能在这里找到她——不能在这儿；全天底下他们相会的地方只有一个，那就是那边的银羊毛里。她飞快地一瞥，站起身，把披肩裹紧，然后向前滑去，摇摇晃晃、颤颤巍巍地，溜过大路，钻进了沼泽。沼泽那幽暗的神秘笼罩了她；这地方是她的。她生在它的疆界之内；在它的疆界之内她生活、长大；在它的疆界之内她遇见了她的爱。她一路急赶，直到下到潟湖和岛边，瞧！棉花就在她眼前！一片巨大的白沫铺在它的褐与绿之上；整片田在阳光里波动、战栗。一声低低的欢喜的叫从她唇间迸出；她忘了自己的虚弱，挑着路过了桥，静静地立在齐肩的棉花丛中，用双手爱怜地把它揽住。
他听说她下楼了，便心跳如鼓地跑去见她。椅子是空的；可他知道。这两个灵魂相会的地方，此刻只有一个。然而那儿远，他害怕，瞪着受惊的眼奔去。

===Original===
She stood on the island, ethereal, splendid, like some tall, dark, and gorgeous flower of the storied East. The green and white of the cotton billowed and foamed about her breasts; the red scarf burned upon her neck; the dark brown velvet of her skin pulsed warm and tremulous with the uprushing blood, and in the midnight depths of her great eyes flamed the mighty fires of long-concealed and newborn love.
He darted through the trees and paused, a tall man strongly but slimly made. He threw up his hands in the old way and hallooed; happily she crooned back a low mother-melody, and waited. He came down to her slowly, with fixed, hungry eyes, threading his way amid the Fleece. She did not move, but lifted both her dark hands, white with cotton; and then, as he came, casting it suddenly to the winds, in tears and laughter she swayed and dropped quivering in his arms. And all the world was sunshine and peace.

===Chinese===
她立在岛上，空灵、辉煌，像传说中东方一朵高挑、黝黑、绚烂的花。棉花的绿与白在她胸前翻涌、起沫；红围巾在她颈上燃烧；她那深棕天鹅绒般的肌肤，随着上涌的血液温暖地、颤动地搏动，在她那双大眼睛的子夜深处，长久隐藏的、新生的爱之烈焰正熊熊燃烧。
他穿过树丛一冲而至，顿住——一个高大的男子，结实而清瘦。他以从前的姿态一扬手，高声一呼；她快活地低声回了一支母亲的旋律，等着。他朝她慢慢走来，眼睛定定的、饥渴的，在羊毛间穿行。她没有动，只是举起两只黝黑的手，手上有棉花的白；然后，待他到来，她猛地一扬手，把棉花撒向风中，在泪水和笑声里，她摇晃着，颤动着，落进了他的怀抱。于是整个世界，都是阳光，都是安宁。

## XV Revelation / XV 揭露

===Original===
Harry Cresswell was scowling over his breakfast. It was not because his apartment in the New York hotel was not satisfactory, or his breakfast unpalatable; possibly a rather bewildering night in Broadway was expressing its influence; but he was satisfied that his ill-temper was due to a paragraph in the morning paper:
“It is stated on good authority that the widow of the late multimillionaire, Job Grey, will announce a large and carefully planned scheme of Negro education in the South, and will richly endow schools in South Carolina, Georgia, Alabama, Louisiana, and Texas.”
Cresswell finally thrust his food away. He knew that Mrs. Grey helped Miss Smith’s school, and supposed she would continue to do so; with that in mind he had striven to impress her, hoping that she might trust his judgment in later years. He had no idea, however, that she meant to endow the school, or entertained wholesale plans for Negro education. The knowledge made him suspicious. Why had neither Mary nor John Taylor mentioned this? Was there, after all, some “nigger-loving” conspiracy back of the cotton combine? He took his hat and started downtown.

===Chinese===
哈里·克雷斯韦尔正对着早餐皱眉。这并不是因为他在纽约那家旅馆的套房不满意，也不是早餐不可口；也许是在百老汇那场颇为令人晕头转向的一夜所产生的影响；可他认定，自己的坏脾气是早晨报纸上那段话引起的：
「据可靠消息，已故亿万富翁约伯·格雷的遗孀，将宣布一项宏大而缜密的南方黑人教育计划，并将在南卡罗来纳、佐治亚、阿拉巴马、路易斯安那和得克萨斯各州慷慨资助学校。」
克雷斯韦尔终于把食物推开。他知道格雷太太资助史密斯小姐的学校，料想她会继续资助；正是为此，他一直努力给她留下好印象，指望她日后能信赖自己的判断。可他万万没想到，她竟打算资助这所学校，还怀着整套黑人教育的宏大计划。这一消息让他起了疑。为什么玛丽和约翰·泰勒谁也没提起过这事？莫非棉花合并案的背后，到底有什么「疼黑鬼」的阴谋？他拿起帽子，朝城里走去。

===Original===
Once in John Taylor’s Broadway office, he opened the subject abruptly﻿—the more so perhaps because he felt a resentment against Taylor for certain unnamed or partially voiced assumptions. Here was a place, however, for speech, and he spoke almost roughly.
“Taylor, what does this mean?” He thrust the clipping at him.
“Mean? That Mrs. Grey is going to get rid of some of her surplus cash﻿—is going to endow some nigger schools,” Taylor drily retorted.
“It must be stopped,” declared Cresswell.
The other’s brows drew up.
“Why?” in a surprised tone.

===Chinese===
一进约翰·泰勒在百老汇的办公室，他便单刀直入地挑起话头——之所以如此，也许还因为他因泰勒某些未言明、或半吐半露的揣测而怀着一股怨气。然而，这是个说话的场合，他便近乎粗暴地说了。
「泰勒，这是怎么回事？」他把剪报朝他一推。
「怎么回事？格雷太太要花掉她一点闲钱——要资助几所黑鬼学校呗。」泰勒干巴巴地顶了一句。
「这事非叫停不可。」克雷斯韦尔宣称。
对方挑起了眉。
「为什么？」语气颇为惊讶。

===Original===
“Why? Why? Do you think the plantation system can be maintained without laborers? Do you think there’s the slightest chance of cornering cotton and buying the Black Belt if the niggers are unwilling to work under present conditions? Do you know the man that stands ready to gobble up every inch of cotton land in this country at a price which no trust can hope to rival?”
John Taylor’s interest quickened.
“Why, no,” he returned sharply. “Who?”
“The Black Man, whose woolly head is filled with ideas of rising. We’re striving by main force to prevent this, and here come your damned Northern philanthropists to plant schools. Why, Taylor, it’ll knock the cotton trust to hell.”
“Don’t get excited,” said Taylor, judicially. “We’ve got things in our hands; it’s the Grey money, you know, that is back of us.”

===Chinese===
「为什么？为什么？你以为没有了劳工，种植园体制还能维持吗？你以为，要是黑鬼不肯在眼下这种条件下干活，我们还有丝毫机会垄断棉花、买下黑土地带吗？你知不知道，有这么个人，正虎视眈眈地要把这个国家每一寸棉地都吞下去，而且开出的价钱，任何托拉斯都休想企及？」
约翰·泰勒的兴趣提了起来。
「这个嘛，不知道。」他锐利地回道，「是谁？」
「黑人。那个满脑袋瓜都是往上爬念头的、毛茸茸脑袋的黑人。我们正拼了命地阻止这件事，可你们那些该死的北方慈善家倒好，跑来办学堂。哎呀，泰勒，这会把棉花托拉斯砸个稀巴烂的。」
「别激动，」泰勒以法官的口吻说，「事情在我们手心里；要知道，我们背后撑腰的可是格雷的钱。」

===Original===
“That’s just what confounds me,” declared the perplexed young man. “Are you men fools, or rascals? Don’t you see the two schemes can’t mix? They’re dead opposite, mutually contradictory, absolutely﻿—” Taylor checked him; it was odd to behold Harry Cresswell so disturbed.
“Well, wait a moment. Let’s see. Sit down. Wish I had a cigar for you, but I don’t smoke.”
“Do you happen to have any whiskey handy?”
“No, I don’t drink.”
“Well, what the devil﻿—Oh, well, fire away.”
“Now, see here. We control the Grey millions. Of course, we’ve got to let her play with her income, and that’s considerable. Her favorite game just now is Negro education, and she’s planning to go in heavy. Her adviser in this line, however, is Smith, and he belongs to us.”

===Chinese===
「这正是叫我糊涂的地方，」这位困惑的年轻人宣称。「你们这些人到底是傻瓜，还是无赖？你们难道看不出，这两套计划根本合不到一块儿去吗？它们是死对头，彼此矛盾，绝对——」泰勒制止了他；看着哈里·克雷斯韦尔这般失态，倒叫人稀罕。
「好了，等一下。咱们想想。坐吧。恨不得有支雪茄给你，可我不抽烟。」
「手边碰巧有威士忌没有？」
「没有，我不喝酒。」
「那这他妈的——唉，算了，说吧。」
「你瞧。格雷那几百万在我们手里。当然，我们得让她拿收益来玩玩，这笔数目可不小。她眼下最爱玩的是黑人教育，正打算大干一场。可她在这上头的顾问是史密斯，而史密斯是我们的人。」

===Original===
“What Smith?”
“Why, the man who’s going to be Senator from New Jersey. He has a sister teaching in the South﻿—you know, of course; it’s at your home where my sister Mary taught.”
“Great Scott! Is that woman’s brother going to spend this money? Why, are you daft? See here! American cotton-spinning supremacy is built on cheap cotton; cheap cotton is built on cheap niggers. Educating, or rather *trying* to educate niggers, will make them restless and discontented﻿—that is, scarce and dear as workers. Don’t you see you’re planning to cut off your noses? This Smith School, particularly, has nearly ruined our plantation. It’s stuck almost in our front yard; *you* are planning to put our plough-hands all to studying Greek, and at the same time to corner the cotton crop﻿—rot!”
John Taylor caressed his lean jaw.
“New point of view to me; I sort of thought education would improve things in the South,” he commented, unmoved.

===Chinese===
「哪个史密斯？」
「嗨，就是那个要当新泽西参议员的人。他有个妹妹在南方教书——你当然知道；就在你老家，我妹妹玛丽教过书的地方。」
「我的天！那个女人的哥哥要来花这笔钱？怎么，你们疯了？你听好！美国棉纺业的霸权，是建立在廉价棉花之上的；廉价棉花，是建立在廉价黑鬼之上的。教育黑鬼，或者说*试图*教育黑鬼，会让他们坐立不安、心怀不满——也就是说，作为工人，他们会变得稀缺、变得昂贵。你没看出来，你们是在打算割自己的鼻子吗？尤其是那所史密斯学校，几乎把我们的种植园毁了。它就杵在我们家前院门口；*你*却打算让我们的犁地长工统统去读希腊文，同时还要垄断棉花——荒唐！」
约翰·泰勒摩挲着他那清瘦的下巴。
「这倒是新观点；我原以为教育能改善南方的状况呢。」他不为所动地评论。

===Original===
“It would if we ran it.”
“We?”
“Yes﻿—we Southerners.”
“Um!﻿—I see﻿—there’s light. See here, let’s talk to Easterly about this.” They went into the next office, and after a while got audience with the trust magnate. Mr. Easterly heard the matter carefully and waved it aside.
“Oh, that doesn’t concern us, Taylor; let Cresswell take care of the whole thing. We’ll see that Smith does what Cresswell wants.”
But Taylor shook his head.
“Smith would kick. Mrs. Grey would get suspicious, and the devil be to pay. This is better. Form a big committee of Northern business men like yourself﻿—philanthropists like Vanderpool, and Southerners like Cresswell; let them be a sort of Negro Education steering-committee. We’ll see that on such committee you Southerners get what you want﻿—control of Negro education.”

===Chinese===
「要是我们来办，就能改善。」
「我们？」
「对——我们南方人。」
「嗯！——我明白了——有门儿。这样，咱们去跟伊斯特利谈谈这事。」他们进了隔壁办公室，过了一阵见到了那位托拉斯巨头。伊斯特利先生仔细听了这事，一挥手把它搁到一边。
「哦，这不归我们管，泰勒；让克雷斯韦尔全权处理吧。我们保证让史密斯按克雷斯韦尔的意思办就是。」
可泰勒摇头。
「史密斯会跳脚的。格雷太太会起疑，那就麻烦大了。这样更好：成立一个由像您这样的北方商人——像范德普尔这样的慈善家、和像克雷斯韦尔这样的南方人组成的大委员会；让它当一个黑人教育的指导委员会。我们保证，在这个委员会里，你们南方人能拿到你们想要的——对黑人教育的掌控。」

===Original===
“That sounds fair. But how about the Smith School? My father writes me that they are showing signs of expecting money right off﻿—is that true? If it is, I want it stopped; it will ruin our campaign for the Farmers’ League.”
John Taylor looked at Cresswell. He thought he saw something more than general policy, or even racial prejudice﻿—something personal﻿—in his vehemence. The Smith School was evidently a severe thorn in the flesh of this man. All the more reason for mollifying him. Then, too, there was something in his argument. It was not wise to start educating these Negroes and getting them discontented just now. Ignorant labor was not ideal, but it was worth too much to employers to lose it now. Educated Negro labor might be worth more to Negroes, but not to the cotton combine. “H’m﻿—well, then﻿—” and John Taylor went into a brown study, while Cresswell puffed impatiently at a cigarette.
“I have it,” said Taylor. Cresswell sat up. “First, let Mr. Easterly get Smith.” Easterly turned to the telephone.

===Chinese===
「这听着还公道。可史密斯学校怎么办？我父亲来信说，他们有迹象指望钱马上就到——是真的吗？要是真的，我要叫它停下来；那会把我们的农民同盟运动毁了的。」
约翰·泰利打量着克雷斯韦尔。他觉得，在他这份激烈里，自己看到的不只是总体方针，也不只是种族偏见——还有某种个人的东西。史密斯学校显然是这个男人肉里的一根大刺。那就更该安抚他了。况且，他的论点也有几分道理。眼下就开始教育这些黑人、让他们心生不满，并不明智。无知的劳工不算理想，可对雇主而言，此时此刻失去它代价太大。受过教育的黑人劳工，或许对黑人自己更有价值，却对棉花托拉斯无益。「嗯——那么——」约翰·泰利陷入了沉思，克雷斯韦尔不耐烦地喷着烟。
「有了。」泰利说。克雷斯韦尔坐直了。「首先，让伊斯特利先生把史密斯叫来。」伊斯特利转向电话。

===Original===
“Is that you, Smith?”
“Well, this is Easterly.﻿ ﻿… Yes﻿—how about Mrs. Grey’s education schemes?﻿ ﻿… Yes﻿ ﻿… h’m﻿—well﻿—see here Smith, we must go a little easy there.﻿ ﻿… Oh, no, no﻿—but to advertise just now a big scheme of Negro Education would drive the Cresswells, the Farmers’ League, and the whole business South dead against us.﻿ ﻿… Yes, yes indeed; they believe in education all right, but they ain’t in for training lawyers and professors just yet.﻿ ﻿… No, I don’t suppose her school is.﻿ ﻿… Well, then; see here. She’ll be reasonable, won’t she, and placate the Cresswells?﻿ ﻿… No, I mean run the school to suit their ideas.﻿ ﻿… No, no, but in general along the lines which they could approve.﻿ ﻿… Yes, I thought so﻿ ﻿… of course﻿ ﻿… goodbye.”
“Inclined to be a little nasty?” asked Taylor.
“A little sharp﻿—but tractable. Now, Mr. Cresswell, the thing is in your hands. We’ll get this committee which Taylor suggests appointed, and send it on a junket to Alabama; you do the rest﻿—see?”
“Who’ll be the committee?” asked Cresswell.
“Name it.”
Mr. Cresswell smiled and left.

===Chinese===
「是史密斯吗？」
「我是伊斯特利。……是——格雷太太的教育计划怎么样了？……是……嗯——这个——你听我说，史密斯，这事儿我们得悠着点。……哦，不不——可这会儿要是大张旗鼓地宣扬一个黑人教育的大计划，会把克雷斯韦尔家、农民同盟、整个南方的实业界都推到我们死对头那一边去的。……是，一点不错；他们是赞成教育的，这没错，可他们眼下还不想培养律师和教授。……不，我不觉得她的学校是那样的。……那么，你听我说。她会通情达理的，对吧，会安抚一下克雷斯韦尔家吧？……不，我是说按他们的想法来办学校。……不不，是总体上沿着他们能赞同的方向。……好，我就知道……当然……再见。」
「有点想找别扭？」泰利问。
「有点冲——但还驯顺。那么，克雷斯韦尔先生，事情就交到您手里了。我们把泰利建议的这个委员会任命起来，派它到阿拉巴马去转一趟；其余的您来办——明白？」
「委员会都有谁？」克雷斯韦尔问。
「您点名。」
克雷斯韦尔先生笑了笑，走了。

===Original===
The winter started in severely, and it was easy to fill two private cars with members of the new Negro Education Board right after Thanksgiving. Cresswell had worked carefully and with caution. There was Mrs. Grey, comfortable and beaming, Mr. Easterly, who thought this a good business opportunity, and his family. Mrs. Vanderpool liked the South and was amused at the trip, and had induced Mr. Vanderpool to come by stories of shooting.
“Ah!” said Mr. Vanderpool.
Mr. Charles Smith and John Taylor were both too busy to go, but bronchial trouble induced the Rev. Dr. Boldish of St. Faith’s rich parish to be one of the party, and at the last moment Temple Bocombe, the sociologist, consented to join.
“Awfully busy,” he said, “but I’ve been reading up on the Negro problem since you mentioned the matter to me last week, Mr. Cresswell, and I think I understand it thoroughly. I may be able to help out.”

===Chinese===
那年冬天来势汹汹，感恩节一过，要坐满两节私家车厢的新任黑人教育委员会成员，是再容易不过了。克雷斯韦尔小心谨慎地操持。有舒舒服服、满面春风的格雷太太，有认为这是个好商机的伊斯特利先生和他一家。范德普尔太太喜欢南方，又觉得这趟旅行有趣，便拿打猎的故事把范德普尔先生也劝来了。
「啊哈！」范德普尔先生说。
查尔斯·史密斯先生和约翰·泰利都太忙，去不了，可一场支气管的小毛病，倒成全了圣信会那富裕堂区的博尔迪什牧师博士（Rev. Dr. Boldish）随行；最后一刻，社会学家坦普尔·博科姆（Temple Bocombe）也答应加入。
「忙得要命，」他说，「可自从您上礼拜跟我提起这事，克雷斯韦尔先生，我就一直在恶补黑人问题的资料，我想我已经彻底弄懂了。说不定能帮上点忙呢。」

===Original===
The necessary spice of young womanhood was added to the party by Miss Taylor and Miss Cresswell, together with the silent Miss Boldish. They were a comfortable and sometimes merry party. Dr. Boldish pointed out the loafers at the stations, especially the black ones; Mr. Bocombe counted them and estimated the number of hours of work lost at ten cents an hour.
“Do they get that﻿—ten cents an hour?” asked Miss Taylor.
“Oh, I don’t know,” replied Mr. Bocombe; “but suppose they do, for instance. That is an average wage today.”
“They look lazy,” said Mrs. Grey.
“They are lazy,” said Mr. Cresswell.
“So am I,” added Mrs. Vanderpool, suppressing a yawn.
“It is uninteresting,” murmured her husband, preparing for a nap.

===Chinese===
这一行人里，必不可少的年轻女性的一点调味，便由泰利小姐和克雷斯韦尔小姐、加上沉默寡言的博尔迪什小姐凑成。这是一支舒坦、有时还挺快活的队伍。博尔迪什博士一一指点车站上那些游手好闲的人，尤其是黑人；博科姆先生则数着他们，估算着按一毛钱一个钟头算，损失了多少工时。
「他们能挣到那么多——一毛钱一个钟头？」泰利小姐问。
「哦，这我不知道，」博科姆先生答，「可假定他们挣得到吧。这算今天的平均工钱。」
「他们看着挺懒的。」格雷太太说。
「他们是懒。」克雷斯韦尔先生说。
「我也是。」范德普尔太太强忍着一个哈欠，补了一句。
「挺没意思的。」她丈夫喃喃道，准备打个盹。

===Original===
On the whole the members of the party enjoyed themselves from the moment they drew out of Jersey City to the afternoon when, in four carriages, they rolled beneath the curious eyes of all Toomsville and swept under the shadowed rampart of the swamp.
“The Christmas” was coming and all the Southern world was busy. Few people were busier than Bles and Zora. Slowly, wonderfully for them, heaven bent in these dying days of the year and kissed the earth, and the tremor thrilled all lands and seas. Everything was good, all things were happy, and these two were happiest of all. Out of the shadows and hesitations of childhood they had stepped suddenly into manhood and womanhood, with firm feet and uplifted heads. All the day that was theirs they worked, picking the Silver Fleece﻿—picking it tenderly and lovingly from off the brown and spent bodies which had so utterly yielded life and beauty to the full fruition of this long and silken tendril, this white beauty of the cotton. November came and flew, and still the unexhausted field yielded its frothing fruit.

===Chinese===
总的说来，从他们驶出泽西城那一刻起，直到那天下午——他们分乘四辆马车，从全图姆斯维尔好奇的目光下滚过，掠过沼泽那阴沉的屏障——这一行人都玩得挺尽兴。
「圣诞节」将近，整个南方世界一片忙碌。很少有人比布莱斯和佐拉更忙了。对他们而言，在这岁末的将死之日里，天慢慢地、奇妙地弯下身来，亲吻大地，那一阵颤栗激荡了所有的陆地与海洋。一切都是好的，万物都是欢愉的，而这两个人是最欢愉的。他们从童年的阴影与踟蹰中，一脚踏入了成年男女的境地，脚跟稳稳的，头颅高昂。属于他们的那一整天，他们都在劳作，摘着银羊毛——温柔地、爱怜地，从那些褐色、耗尽了的躯壳上把它摘下来，那些躯壳已把生命与美，完完全全地献给了这一缕绵长的丝般嫩须、这一朵棉花的白色之美。十一月来了又去，那片未竭的田，仍在吐着它泡沫般的果实。

===Original===
Today seemed doubly glorious, for Bles had spoken of their marriage; with twined hands and arms, and lips ever and again seeking their mates, they walked the leafy way.
Unconscious, rapt, they stepped out into the Big Road skirting the edge of the swamp. Why not? Was it not the King’s Highway? And Love was King. So they talked on, unknowing that far up the road the Cresswell coaches were wheeling along with precious burdens. In the first carriage were Mrs. Grey and Mrs. Vanderpool, Mr. Cresswell and Miss Taylor. Mrs. Vanderpool was lolling luxuriously, but Mrs. Grey was a little stiff from long travel and sat upright. Mr. Cresswell looked clean-cut and handsome, and Miss Taylor seemed complacent and responsible. The dying of the day soothed them all insensibly. Groups of dark little children passed them as they neared the school, staring with wide eyes and greeting timidly.

===Chinese===
今天似乎格外辉煌，因为布莱斯谈到了他们的婚事；他们十指相扣、臂膀交缠，嘴唇一次又一次地寻着彼此，走在那条绿叶成荫的路上。
浑然不觉、如痴如醉地，他们迈上了沼泽边上那条大路。有何不可呢？那岂不是王者之路吗？而爱，正是王者。他们就这么谈着，浑然不知远远的路那头，克雷斯韦尔家的马车正载着贵客辚辚而来。头一辆马车里坐着格雷太太和范德普尔太太，克雷斯韦尔先生和泰勒小姐。范德普尔太太慵懒地歪着，可格雷太太因长途旅行有些僵硬，坐得笔直。克雷斯韦尔先生轮廓分明、仪表堂堂，泰勒小姐则一副自得又尽责的样子。日之将暮，不知不觉地抚慰了他们所有人。将近学校时，一群群黝黑的小孩打他们身边走过，睁大眼盯着，怯怯地打招呼。

===Original===
“There seems to be marrying and giving in marriage,” laughed Mrs. Vanderpool.
“Not very much,” said Mr. Cresswell drily.
“Well, at least plenty of children.”
“Plenty.”
“But where are the houses?” asked Mrs. Grey.
“Perhaps in the swamp,” said Mrs. Vanderpool lightly, looking up at the sombre trees that lined the left.
“They live where they please and do as they please,” Cresswell explained; to which Mrs. Vanderpool added: “Like other animals.”
Mary Taylor opened her lips to rebuke this levity when suddenly the coachman called out and the horses swerved, and the carriage’s four occupants faced a young man and a young woman embracing heartily.
Out through the wood Bles and Zora had come to the broad red road; playfully he celebrated all her beauty unconscious of time and place.

===Chinese===
「瞧着像是嫁的嫁、娶的娶呢。」范德普尔太太笑道。
「也不算多。」克雷斯韦尔先生干巴巴地说。
「嗯，至少孩子多的是。」
「多的是。」
「可房子在哪儿呢？」格雷太太问。
「兴许在沼泽里吧。」范德普尔太太轻飘飘地说，抬头望着左边那一排阴沉沉的树。
「他们爱住哪儿住哪儿，爱干嘛干嘛。」克雷斯韦尔解释道；范德普尔太太补了一句：「跟别的畜生一样。」
玛丽·泰勒正要张口驳斥这份轻佻，车夫忽然一喊，马一闪，车里四个人便正正对着一对青年男女——他们正热烈地拥抱在一起。
布莱斯和佐拉从林子里出来，上了那条宽阔的红土大路；他戏谑地颂扬着她所有的美，浑然忘了时间与地点。

===Original===
“You are tall and bend like grasses on the swamp,” he said.
“And yet look up to you,” she murmured.
“Your eyes are darkness dressed in night.”
“To see you brighter, dear,” she said.
“Your little hands are much too frail for work.”
“They must grow larger, then, and soon.”
“Your feet are far too small to travel on.”
“They’ll travel on to you﻿—that’s far enough.”
“Your lips﻿—your full and purple lips﻿—were made alone for kissing, not for words.”
“They’ll do for both.”

===Chinese===
「你又高又弯，像沼泽上的草。」他说。
「可还是仰望着你。」她低语。
「你的眼睛，是黑夜穿上了黑暗。」
「好把你看得更亮些，亲爱的。」她说。
「你这双小手太纤弱了，干不了活。」
「那它们就快快长大吧。」
「你这双脚太小了，走不了远路。」
「它们会走到你跟前——那就够远了。」
「你的嘴唇——你那丰盈紫红的嘴唇——只为亲吻而生，不为言语。」
「两样都成。」

===Original===
He laughed in utter joy and touched her hair with light caressing hands.
“It does not fly with sunlight,” she said quickly, with an upward glance.
“No,” he answered. “It sits and listens to the night.”
But even as she nestled to him happily there came the harsh thunder of horses’ hoofs, beating on their ears. He drew her quickly to him in fear, and the coach lurched and turned, and left them facing four pairs of eyes. Miss Taylor reddened; Mrs. Grey looked surprised; Mrs. Vanderpool smiled; but Mr. Cresswell darkened with anger. The couple unclasped shamefacedly, and the young man, lifting his hat, started to stammer an apology; but Cresswell interrupted him:
“Keep your﻿—your philandering to the woods, or I shall have you arrested,” he said slowly, his face colorless, his lips twitching with anger. “Drive on, John.”
Miss Taylor felt that her worst suspicions had been confirmed; but Mrs. Vanderpool was curious as to the cause of Cresswell’s anger. It was so genuine that it needed explanation.
“Are kisses illegal here?” she asked before the horses started, turning the battery of her eyes full upon him. But Cresswell had himself well in hand.
“No,” he said. “But the girl is﻿—notorious.”

===Chinese===
他纵情地笑出声来，用爱抚的手轻轻触着她的头发。
「它不会随阳光飞扬。」她飞快地说，向上瞟了他一眼。
「不，」他答，「它静静地坐着，倾听夜晚。」
可就在她幸福地依偎着他时，马蹄那粗暴的雷声传来了，在他们耳畔擂响。他惊惶地把她一把拉到怀里，马车一歪、一转，把他们留在四双眼睛的注视下。泰勒小姐红了脸；格雷太太一脸惊讶；范德普尔太太笑了；可克雷斯韦尔先生却气得脸色发青。这一对羞愧地松开，年轻人摘下帽，结结巴巴地想要道歉；克雷斯韦尔却打断了他：
「把你那——你那调情留到林子里去，不然我让人抓你。」他慢慢地说，脸色煞白，嘴唇气得抽搐。「赶车，约翰。」
泰勒小姐觉得自己最坏的怀疑得到了证实；可范德普尔太太却好奇克雷斯韦尔怒从何来。这怒气如此真切，倒需要解释一番了。
「这儿接吻犯法吗？」马还没起步，她便问，一双眸子如炮火般全数射向他。可克雷斯韦尔已把自己稳住了。
「不犯法，」他说，「可那姑娘——名声在外。」

===Original===
On the lovers the words fell like a blow. Zora shivered, and a grayish horror mottled the dark burning of her face. Bles started in anger, then paused in shivering doubt. What had happened? They knew not; yet involuntarily their hands fell apart; they avoided each other’s eyes.
“I﻿—I must go now,” gasped Zora, as the carriage swept away.
He did not hold her, he did not offer the farewell kiss, but stood staring at the road as she walked into the swamp. A moment she paused and looked back; then slowly, almost painfully, she took the path back to the field of the Fleece, and reaching it after long, long minutes, began mechanically to pick the cotton. But the cotton glowed crimson in the failing sun.

===Chinese===
这几个字像一记重锤落在恋人身上。佐拉一颤，一层灰惨惨的恐惧，斑驳了她那张黝黑滚烫的脸。布莱斯先是一怒，随即又颤巍巍地犹豫起来。发生了什么？他们不知道；可两人的手却不由自主地松开了；他们避开了彼此的眼睛。
「我——我得走了。」马车驶远时，佐拉喘着气说。
他没有挽留她，没有送上告别的吻，只是呆呆地盯着大路，看着她走进沼泽。她停了一会儿，回头望了一眼；然后慢慢地、几乎是痛苦地，走上了回银羊毛田的小径，走了好久好久才到，便机械地摘起棉花来。可那棉花，在将逝的夕阳里，泛着猩红的光。

===Original===
Bles walked toward the school. What had happened? he kept asking. And yet he dared not question the awful shape that sat somewhere, cold and still, behind his soul. He heard the hoofs of horses again. It was Miss Taylor being brought back to the school to greet Miss Smith and break the news of the coming of the party. He raised his hat. She did not return the greeting, but he found her pausing at the gate. It seemed to her too awful for this foolish fellow thus to throw himself away. She faced him and he flinched as from some descending blow.
“Bles,” she said primly, “have you absolutely no shame?”
He braced himself and raised his head proudly.
“I am going to marry her; it is no crime.” Then he noted the expression on her face, and paused.

===Chinese===
布莱斯朝学校走去。发生了什么？他不住地问自己。可他不敢去质问那个蹲在他灵魂某处、又冷又静的、可怕的形体。他又听见了马蹄声。是泰勒小姐被送回学校，好去见史密斯小姐，通报考察团到来的消息。他举了举帽。她没有回礼，可他发现她在门口停了下来。在她看来，这个愚蠢的家伙竟这样自暴自弃，未免太可怕了。她迎面朝他走来，他像挨了一记劈头落下的重击似的，一缩。
「布莱斯，」她端着一副架子说，「你难道一丝羞耻都没有吗？」
他撑住自己，骄傲地昂起头。
「我要娶她；这不是罪。」随后他留意到她脸上的神情，便顿住了。

===Original===
She stepped back, scandalized.
“Can it be, Bles Alwyn,” she said, “that you don’t know the sort of girl she is?”
He raised his hands and warded off her words, dumbly, as she turned to go, almost frightened at the havoc she saw. The heavens flamed scarlet in his eyes and he screamed.
“It’s a lie! It’s a damned lie!” He wheeled about and tore into the swamp.
“It’s a damned lie!” he shouted to the trees. “Is it?﻿—is it?” chirped the birds. “It’s a cruel falsehood!” he moaned. “Is it?﻿—is it?” whispered the devils within.

===Chinese===
她倒退一步，简直骇然。
「布莱斯·阿尔文，难道说，」她说，「你竟不知道她是哪种姑娘？」
他举起双手，哑然地挡开她的话，她转身要走时，几乎被自己所见的满目狼藉吓住了。他眼里天火般一片猩红，他尖叫起来。
「这是谎话！这是该死的谎话！」他猛地一转身，冲进了沼泽。
「这是该死的谎话！」他冲着树喊。「是吗？——是吗？」鸟儿啁啾。「这是恶毒的中伤！」他呻吟。「是吗？——是吗？」心里的恶魔低语。

===Original===
It seemed to him as though suddenly the world was staggering and faltering about him. The trees bent curiously and strange breathings were upon the breezes. He unbuttoned his collar that he might get more air. A thousand things he had forgotten surged suddenly to life. Slower and slower he ran, more and more the thoughts crowded his head. He thought of that first red night and the yelling and singing and wild dancing; he thought of Cresswell’s bitter words; he thought of Zora telling how she stayed out nights; he thought of the little bower that he had built her in the cotton field. A wild fear struggled with his anger, but he kept repeating, “No, no,” and then, “At any rate, she will tell me the truth.” She had never lied to him; she would not dare; he clenched his hands, murder in his heart.

===Chinese===
他只觉得天地忽然在他周围摇摇晃晃、踉踉跄跄。树木好奇地弯下腰，一阵阵古怪的呼吸拂过微风。他解开衣领，好多吸点空气。一千件他早已忘掉的事，骤然苏醒。他越跑越慢，脑子里越来越挤。他想起头一个红彤彤的夜，那叫嚷、歌唱和狂舞；他想起克雷斯韦尔那句刻毒的话；他想起佐拉说她如何夜里在外头过夜；他想起他在棉田里给她搭的那个小小的窝。一种狂乱的恐惧与他的怒火搏斗，可他不停地说：「不，不，」然后又说：「无论如何，她会跟我说实话的。」她从没对他撒过谎；她不敢；他攥紧了拳头，心里满是杀机。

===Original===
Slowly and more slowly he ran. He knew where she was﻿—where she must be, waiting. And yet as he drew near huge hands held him back, and heavy weights clogged his feet. His heart said: “On! quick! She will tell the truth, and all will be well.” His mind said: “Slow, slow; this is the end.” He hurled the thought aside, and crashed through the barrier.
She was standing still and listening, with a huge basket of the piled froth of the field upon her head.
