「你到过埃尔斯贝思的小屋；那是个邪门的地方，给这一方、给过我恶果。埃尔斯贝思是我母亲生前最得用的仆人，也是我自己的奶妈。我母亲在我十岁那年死了，把我丢给了她那点『慈悲』。她事事由着我，纵着我身上坏的那些。我居然没彻底毁了，真是奇事。她那小屋成了喝酒胡闹的聚点。我告诉过我父亲，可他，懒懒地、漫不经心地，一口咬定那地方不见得比所有黑人的小屋更糟，什么也没做。我不去了。可她仍然使尽一切手段勾引，还在黑人里散布些假话，甚至在我设法搭救佐拉的时候也不肯罢手。我跟你说这些，是因为我知道你听见过些坏的传言。我一直不是个好人——玛丽；可我爱你，而你能让我变好。」

===Original===
Perhaps no other appeal would have stirred Mary Taylor. She was in many respects an inexperienced girl. But she thought she knew the world; she knew that Harry Cresswell was not all he should be, and she knew too that many other men were not. Moreover, she argued he had not had a fair chance. All the school-ma’am in her leaped to his teaching. What he needed was a superior person like herself. She loved him, and she deliberately put her arms about his neck and lifted her face to be kissed.

===Chinese===
换作别的话，也许都打动不了玛丽·泰勒。她在许多方面还是个没阅历的姑娘。可她自以为懂得世道；她知道哈里·克雷斯韦尔并不是他本该是的那样，她也知道许多别的男人也都不是。况且，她辩解说，他从来没得到过一个公道的机会。她身上那股子女教师的劲头，一下子全跳出来要教化他了。他所需要的，正是她这样一个出类拔萃的人。她爱他，于是她有意地伸出双臂搂住他的脖子，仰起脸，等他来吻。

===Original===
Back by the place of the Silver Fleece they wandered, across the Big Road, up to the mansion. On the steps stood John Taylor and Helen Cresswell hand in hand and they all smiled at each other. The Colonel came out, smiling too, with the paper in his hands.
“Easterly’s right,” he beamed, “the stock of the Cotton Combine﻿—” he paused at the silence and looked up. The smile faded slowly and the red blood mounted to his forehead. Anger struggled back of surprise, but before it burst forth silently the Colonel turned, and muttering some unintelligible word, went slowly into the house and slammed the door.

===Chinese===
他们又溜达到银色羊毛那块地边，穿过大路，回到大宅。台阶上，约翰·泰勒和海伦·克雷斯韦尔手拉手站着，几个人彼此相视而笑。上校也走了出来，手里拿着报纸，也笑着。
「伊斯特利说得对，」他喜形于色，「棉花联合体那只股票——」他在那一片沉默里顿住了，抬起头。笑容慢慢地褪去，红血直冲上他的额头。愤怒在惊讶背后挣扎着，可还没等它发作出来，上校便默默地一转身，嘴里嘟囔着一句听不清的话，慢慢走进屋去，砰地关上了门。

===Original===
So for Harry Cresswell the day burst, flamed, and waned, and then suddenly went out, leaving him dull and gray; for Mary and her brother had gone North, Helen had gone to bed, and the Colonel was in town. Outside the weather was gusty and lowering with a chill in the air. He paced the room fitfully.
Well, he was happy. Or, was he happy?

===Chinese===
哈里·克雷斯韦尔这一天，就这样绽放了，燃烧了，又暗淡下去，随即骤然熄灭，留下他闷闷的、灰扑扑的；因为玛丽和她哥哥已经北上，海伦睡下了，上校进了城。屋外天气阵阵作响、阴沉沉地压着，空气里有股寒意。他在房里一阵阵踱步。
嗯，他是快活的。或者说，他快活吗？

===Original===
He gnawed his mustache, for already his quick, changeable nature was feeling the rebound from glory to misery. He was a little ashamed of his exaltation; a bit doubtful and uncertain. He had stooped low to this Yankee school-ma’am, lower than he had ever stooped to a woman. Usually, while he played at loving, women grovelled; for was he not a Cresswell? Would this woman recognize that fact and respect him accordingly?

===Chinese===
他咬着胡子，因为他那多变、转瞬即变的性子，已经在感受从荣耀跌回愁苦的反噬了。他有点为自己方才的得意忘形惭愧；有点犹疑、没底。他对这个扬基女教师屈尊得太低了，低过他向任何女人屈尊过的程度。往常，他装模作样谈着爱的时候，都是女人们匍匐在地；因为他不是个克雷斯韦尔吗？这个女人，会认清这一层，从而对他敬重有加吗？

===Original===
Then there was Zora; what had she said and hinted to Mary? The wench was always eluding and mocking him, the black devil! But, pshaw!﻿—he poured himself a glass of brandy﻿—was he not rich and young? The world was his.

===Chinese===
还有佐拉呢；她跟玛丽说了些什么、暗示了些什么？这丫头老是从他手边溜掉、嘲弄他，这黑皮的魔鬼！可，呸！——他给自己倒了一杯白兰地——他不又富又年轻吗？天下是他的。

===Original===
His valet knocked.
“Gentleman is asking if you forgits it’s Saturday night, sir?” said Sam.
Cresswell walked thoughtfully to the window, swept back the curtain, and looked toward the darkness and the swamp. It lowered threateningly; behind it the night sky was tinged with blood.
“No,” he said; “I’m not going.” And he shut out the glow.

===Chinese===
他的贴身仆役敲了敲门。
「先生问您是不是忘了今晚是礼拜六晚上，老爷？」萨姆说。
克雷斯韦尔若有所思地走到窗前，刷地拉开窗帘，朝那片黑暗和沼泽望去。沼泽阴沉地压着，一副威胁相；它背后，夜空染着一层血色。
「不，」他说，「我不去。」于是他把那片光亮关在了窗外。

===Original===
Yet he grew more and more restless. The devil danced in his veins and burned in his forehead. His hands shook. He heard a rustle of departing feet beneath his window, then a pause and a faint halloo.
“All right,” he called, and in a moment went downstairs and out into the night. As he closed the front door there seemed to come faintly up from the swamp a low ululation, like the prolonged cry of some wild bird, or the wail of one’s mourning for his dead.

===Chinese===
可他越来越坐立不安。魔鬼在他血管里跳舞，在他额头上烧。他的手在抖。他听见窗下有离去的脚步的窸窣声，接着一顿，一声隐隐的吆喝。
「好咧，」他应了一声，过了一会儿便下了楼，走进夜色里。他关上正门的那一刻，仿佛从沼泽那边隐隐传来一声低低的哀号，像某只野鸟拖长的啼叫，又像一个人为死者举哀的恸哭。

===Original===
Within the cabin, Elspeth heard. Tremblingly, she swayed to her feet, a haggard, awful sight. She motioned Zora away, and stretching her hands palms upward to the sky, cried with dry and fear-struck gasp:
“I’se called! I’se called!”
On the bed the child smiled in its dreaming; the red flame of the firelight set the gold to dancing in her hair. Zora shrank back into the shadows and listened. Then it came. She heard the heavy footsteps crashing through the underbrush﻿—coming, coming, as from the end of the world. She shrank still farther back, and a shadow swept the door.

===Chinese===
小屋里，埃尔斯贝思听见了。她颤巍巍地摇晃着站起身来，形容枯槁，煞是骇人。她示意佐拉走开，然后双手掌心朝天伸出去，用一种又干又惊恐的喘息喊道：
「我被召唤了！我被召唤了！」
床上，那孩子在梦里笑着；炉火的红光把她头发里的金色照得直跳舞。佐拉缩回阴影里听着。然后它来了。她听见沉重的脚步踏过下层灌木丛——来了，来了，仿佛从世界的尽头而来。她更深地往阴影里缩，一道黑影扫过门口。

===Original===
He was a mighty man, black and white-haired, and his eyes were the eyes of death. He bent to enter the door, and then uplifting himself and stretching his great arms, his palms touched the blackened rafters.
Zora started forward. Thick memories of some forgotten past came piling in upon her. Where had she known him? What was he to her?

===Chinese===
他是个魁梧的汉子，黑皮肤，头发花白，一双眼睛是死神的眼睛。他弯腰进得门来，随即一挺身，张开两条巨臂，两只手掌触到了熏黑的椽木。
佐拉猛地一惊，向前一步。某段被遗忘的往昔的浓重记忆，一齐朝她涌来。她在哪儿见过他？他对她算什么？

===Original===
Slowly Elspeth, with quivering hands, unwound the black and snakelike object that always guarded her breast. Without a word, he took it, and again his hands flew heavenward. With a low and fearful moan the old woman lurched sideways, then crashed, like a fallen pine, upon the hearthstone. She lay still﻿—dead.

===Chinese===
埃尔斯贝思颤抖着双手，慢慢解开那个常年护在胸前的、黑蛇般的物件。他一言不发地接过去，双手又向天上一扬。老妇人发出一声低低的、可怖的呻吟，身子一歪，便像一棵倒下的松树，砰地栽倒在炉石上。她躺着不动了——死了。

===Original===
Three times the man passed his hands, wavelike, above the dead. Three times he murmured, and his eyes burned into the shadows, where the girl trembled. Then he turned and went as he had come, his heavy feet crashing through the underbrush, on and on, fainter and fainter, as to the end of the world.

===Chinese===
那汉子在死者上方，波浪般地挥了三次手。他低语了三次，目光灼灼地烧进阴影里，烧向那发抖的姑娘。然后他转过身，像来时一样地走了，沉重的双脚踏过下层灌木丛，一路向前、向前，越来越轻、越来越远，仿佛走向世界的尽头。

===Original===
Zora shook herself from the trance-like horror and passed her hands across her eyes to drive out the nightmare. But, no! there lay the dead upon the hearth with the firelight flashing over her, a bloated, hideous, twisted thing, distorted in the rigor of death. A moment Zora looked down upon her mother. She felt the cold body whence the wandering, wrecked soul had passed. She sat down and stared death in the face for the first time. A mighty questioning arose within, a questioning and a yearning.

===Chinese===
佐拉从那恍惚的惊恐里挣脱出来，用手抹过眼睛，想赶走这场噩梦。可不行！死者就那样躺在炉石上，炉火的光在她身上闪动——一具浮肿、狰狞、扭曲的物件，在死亡的僵直里变了形。佐拉俯视了她母亲片刻。她摸了那冰冷的躯体——那游荡的、毁掉的灵魂，已从这躯体里走了。她坐下，头一回与死亡正面相视。一个巨大的诘问在她心底升起，一个诘问，一种渴望。

===Original===
Was Elspeth now at peace? Was Death the Way﻿—the wide, dark Way? She had never thought of it before, and as she thought she crept forward and looked into the fearful face pityingly.
“Mammy!” she whispered﻿—with bated breath﻿—“Mammy Elspeth!” Out of the night came a whispered answer: “*Elspeth! Elspeth!*”

===Chinese===
埃尔斯贝思如今安宁了吗？死亡莫非就是那条路——那条宽阔、黑暗的路？她从前从没想过这个，想着想着，她向前爬了几步，怜悯地望进那张可怕的脸。
「奶娘！」她悄声道——屏着气——「奶娘埃尔斯贝思！」夜的那一头，传来一声悄声的应答：「*埃尔斯贝思！埃尔斯贝思！*」

===Original===
Zora sprang to her feet, alert, fearful. With a swing of her arm, she pulled the great oaken door to and dropped the bar into its place. Over the dead she spread a clean white sheet. Into the fire she thrust pine-knots. They glared in vague red, and shadowy brilliance, waving and quivering and throwing up thin swirling columns of black smoke. Then standing beside the fireplace with the white, still corpse between her and the door, she took up her awful vigil.

===Chinese===
佐拉跳起身来，警觉、惊惧。她一甩胳膊，把那扇厚重的橡木门拉上，落下门闩。她在死者身上覆了一条干净的白布。她往火里塞进几根松木节。火光红得幽幽地亮，光影绰约，摇曳着、颤动着，升起一缕缕细细盘旋的黑烟。然后她站在壁炉边，白色的、一动不动的尸体横在她和门之间，她开始了她那骇人的守夜。

===Original===
There came a low knocking at the door; then silence and footsteps wandering furtively about. The night seemed all footsteps and whispers. There came a louder knocking, and a voice:
“*Elspeth! Elspeth! Open the door; it’s me.*”
Then muttering and wandering noises, and silence again.

===Chinese===
门上响起一下轻轻的敲门声；接着是沉默，和蹑手蹑脚绕来绕去的脚步声。这一夜仿佛全是脚步和低语。又响起更重的敲门声，还有一个声音：
「*埃尔斯贝思！埃尔斯贝思！开门；是我。*」
接着是嘟嘟囔囔、东游西荡的声响，又是沉默。

===Original===
The child on the bed turned itself, murmuring uneasily in its dreams. And then *they* came. Zora froze, watching the door, wide-eyed, while the fire flamed redder. A loud quick knock at the door﻿—a pause﻿—an oath and a cry.
“*Elspeth! Open this door, damn you!*”
A moment of waiting and then the knocking came again, furious and long continued. Outside there was much trampling and swearing. Zora did not move; the child slept on. A tugging and dragging, a dull blow that set the cabin quivering; then﻿—

===Chinese===
床上的孩子翻了个身，在梦里不安地嘟囔。然后*他们*来了。佐拉僵住了，瞪大眼盯着门，炉火却烧得更红了。门上一下又急又响的敲门——一顿——一句咒骂，一声喊。
「*埃尔斯贝思！开门，妈的！*」
等了一阵，敲门声又起，凶狠地、好半天不停地响。外头是一大片踩踏和咒骂。佐拉一动不动；孩子睡着没醒。一阵拉扯拖拽，一声闷响震得小屋直颤；接着——

===Original===
*Bang! Crack! Crash!*﻿—the door wavered, splintered, and dropped upon the floor.
With a snarl, a crowd of some half-dozen white faces rushed forward, wavered and stopped. The awakened child sat up and stared with wide blue eyes. Slowly, with no word, the intruders turned and went silently away, leaving but one late comer who pressed forward.

===Chinese===
*砰！咔！哗啦！*——门晃了晃，裂开，掉落在地上。
一声咆哮，半打白人脸孔的人群向前涌来，一晃，停住了。被吵醒的孩子坐起来，瞪大一双蓝眼睛望着。慢慢地，一言不发，这些闯入者转过身，悄悄地走了，只留下一个迟到的人，向前挤来。

===Original===
“What damned mummery is this?” he cried, and snatching at the sheet, dragged it from the black distorted countenance of the corpse. He shuddered but for a moment he could not stir. He felt the midnight eyes of the girl﻿—he saw the twisted, oozing mouth of the hag, blue-black and hideous.

===Chinese===
「这他妈搞的什么鬼把戏？」他喊，一把扯住白布，从那张黑色、扭曲的尸体脸上扯了下来。他打了个寒颤，可一时竟动弹不得。他感到那姑娘子夜般的眼睛——他看见那老巫婆扭曲的、渗着液体的嘴，青黑、狰狞。

===Original===
Suddenly back behind there in the darkness a shriek split the night like a sudden flash of flame﻿—a great ringing scream that cracked and swelled and stopped. With one wild effort the man hurled himself out the door and plunged through the darkness. Panting and cursing, he flashed his huge revolver﻿—*bang! bang! bang!* it cracked into the night. The sweat poured from his forehead; the terror of the swamp was upon him. With a struggling and tearing in his throat, he tripped and fell fainting under the silent oaks.

===Chinese===
忽然，在他身后的黑暗里，一声尖叫划破了夜，像一道骤起的火光——一声响亮的、回荡的尖叫，破了音、涨起来、又止住。那人拼了命地一个挣扎，从门里扑了出去，扎进黑暗里。他一边喘一边骂，挥动他那把大左轮——*砰！砰！砰！*——朝夜色里连开几枪。汗从他额头上直往下淌；沼泽的恐怖攫住了他。他喉咙里又堵又撕，一脚绊倒，在沉默的橡树下昏了过去。

## XX The Weaving of the Silver Fleece / XX 银色羊毛的编织

===Original===
The Silver Fleece, darkly cloaked and girded, lay in the cotton warehouse of the Cresswells, near the store. Its silken fibres, cramped and close, shone yellow-white in the sunlight; sadly soiled, yet beautiful. Many came to see Zora’s twin bales, as they lay, handling them and questioning, while Colonel Cresswell grew proud of his possession.

===Chinese===
银色羊毛，裹着、束着黑沉沉的外衣，躺在克雷斯韦尔家棉花仓库里，就在铺子旁边。它那丝一般的纤维，挤得紧紧的、密密的，在阳光里闪着黄白色的光；脏得叫人伤心，却仍是美的。许多人来看佐拉那两包并排放着的棉包，摸着、问着，克雷斯韦尔上校也渐渐为自己这笔所有物得意起来。

===Original===
The world was going well with the Colonel. Freed from money cares, praised for his generalship in the cotton corner, able to entertain sumptuously, he was again a Southern gentleman of the older school, and so in his envied element. Yet today he frowned as he stood poking absently with his cane at the baled Fleece.

===Chinese===
这阵子，世事对上校是顺遂的。卸下了银钱上的烦忧，又因棉花囤积一役的『将才』受人称赞，还能大手大脚地款待客人，他又成了一个老派的南方绅士，正得其所在、令人艳羡。然而今天他皱着眉，手杖漫不经心地戳着那打成包的羊毛。

===Original===
This marriage﻿—or, rather, these marriages﻿—were not to his liking. It was a mésalliance of a sort that pricked him tenderly; it savored grossly of bargain and sale. His neighbors regarded it with disconcerting equanimity. They seemed to think an alliance with Northern millions an honor for Cresswell blood, and the Colonel thumped the nearer bale vigorously. His cane slipped along the iron bands suddenly, and the old man lurching forward, clutched in space to save himself and touched a human hand.

===Chinese===
这桩婚事——或者说，这两桩婚事——都不合他的意。这是一种门户不当的姻缘，刺得他隐隐作痛；它透着一股赤裸裸的买卖成交的味道。他的邻居们却以一种令人不安的泰然看待此事。他们似乎觉得，能与北方的百万家财结亲，是克雷斯韦尔家血的荣耀，上校便用力地捶了捶靠得近些的那包棉花。他的手杖猛地顺着铁箍一滑，老人身子一栽向前，在空中乱抓着要稳住自己，却触到了一只人的手。

===Original===
Zora, sitting shadowed on the farther bale, drew back her hand quickly at the contact, and started to move away.
“What’s that?” thundered the Colonel, more angry at his involuntary fright than at the intrusion. “Here, boys!”
But Zora had come forward into the space where the sunlight of the wide front doors poured in upon the cotton bales.
“It’s me, Colonel,” she said.

===Chinese===
佐拉坐在远处那包棉花的阴影里，一经碰触，便飞快地缩回手，起身要走。
「是谁？」上校一声怒喝，与其说是恼人闯入，不如说是恼自己这不由自主的一惊。「来人哪，小子们！」
可佐拉已经走上前来，走到宽阔的正门倾泻进来的阳光照亮棉包的那片空地上。
「是我，上校，」她说。

===Original===
He glared at her. She was taller and thinner than formerly, darkly transparent of skin, and her dark eyes shone in strange and dusky brilliance. Still indignant and surprised, the Colonel lifted his voice sharply.
“What the devil are you doing here?﻿—sleeping when you ought to be at work! Get out! And see here, next week cotton chopping begins﻿—you’ll go to the fields or to the chain-gang. I’ll have no more of your loafing about my place.”

===Chinese===
他瞪着她。她比从前更高、更瘦了，皮肤黑得近乎透明，一双黑眼睛闪着奇异的、幽暗的光华。上校仍是又惊又恼，尖起嗓门。
「你在这儿搞什么鬼？——该干活的时候睡觉！滚出去！还有，你给我听好，下礼拜棉花间苗就开始了——你要么下地，要么就去服苦役。我这地方，再也容不得你这么瞎晃悠。」

===Original===
Awaiting no reply, the Colonel, already half ashamed of his vehemence, stormed out into the sunlight and climbed upon his bay mare.
But Zora still stood silent in the shadow of the Silver Fleece, hearing and yet not hearing. She was searching for the Way, groping for the threads of life, seeking almost wildly to understand the foundations of understanding, piteously asking for answer to the puzzle of life. All the while the walls rose straight about her and narrow. To continue in school meant charity, yet she had nowhere to go and nothing to go with. To refuse to work for the Cresswells meant trouble for the school and perhaps arrest for herself. To work in the fields meant endless toil and a vista that opened upon death.

===Chinese===
不等回答，上校——已对自己方才的声厉内荏有几分惭愧——气冲冲地走进了阳光里，跨上他那匹栗色母马。
可佐拉仍旧默默站在银色羊毛的阴影里，听见了，又像没听见。她在寻找那条路，摸索着生命的线头，近乎狂乱地想要弄明白『明白』本身的根基，可怜巴巴地求一个人生之谜的答案。而四面墙壁始终笔直地、窄窄地围着她。继续上学意味着受施舍，可她无处可去，也没有任何可傍身之物。拒绝给克雷斯韦尔家干活，意味着给学校招祸，说不定自己也得坐牢。下地干活，则意味着无尽的苦役，和一条通向死亡的远景。

===Original===
Like a hunted thing the girl turned and twisted in thought and faced everywhere the blank Impossible. Cold and dreamlike without, her shut teeth held back seething fires within, and a spirit of revolt that gathered wildness as it grew. Above all flew the dream, the fantasy, the memory of the past, the vision of the future. Over and over she whispered to herself: “This is not the End; this can not be the End.”

===Chinese===
姑娘像一头被猎逐的困兽，在思绪里转过来折过去，到头来面对的都是一片空白的『不可能』。外表冷冷的、如梦如幻，紧咬的牙关却压着心头翻腾的烈火，和一股越聚越烈、越聚越狂的反叛之念。在这一切之上，飞翔着梦、幻想、对往昔的追忆、对未来的憧憬。她一遍又一遍地对自己低语：「这不是终点；这不可能是终点。」

===Original===
Somehow, somewhere, would come salvation. Yet what it would be and what she expected she did not know. She sought the Way, but what way and whither she did not know, she dared not dream.
One thing alone lay in her wild fancy like a great and wonderful fact dragging the dream to earth and anchoring it there. That was the Silver Fleece. Like a brooding mother, Zora had watched it. She knew how the gin had been cleaned for its pressing and how it had been baled apart and carefully covered. She knew how proud Colonel Cresswell was of it and how daily he had visitors to see it and finger the wide white wound in its side.

===Chinese===
不知怎的、不知在哪里，总会来的，那救赎。可它将是什么、她又盼着什么，她自己也不知道。她在寻找那条路，可那是条什么路、通向何方，她不知道，也不敢去梦。
只有一样东西，落在她那狂乱的幻想里，像一个巨大而奇妙的实事，把那梦拽到地上，牢牢地锚在那里。那就是银色羊毛。佐拉像一只孵雏的母鸟，守望着它。她知道轧花机在压包之前是怎样被清理干净的，知道它是怎样单独打成包、又怎样被仔细地罩起来的。她知道克雷斯韦尔上校为它有多得意，知道他每天都迎来客人看它，摸它侧面那道宽宽的、白色的伤口。

===Original===
“Yes, sir, grown on my place, by my niggers, sir!” he assured them; and they marvelled.
To Zora’s mind, this beautiful baled fibre was hers; it typified happiness; it was an holy thing which profane hands had stolen. When it came back to her (as come it must, she cried with clenched hands) it would bring happiness; not the great Happiness﻿—that was gone forever﻿—but illumination, atonement, and something of the power and the glory. So, involuntarily almost, she haunted the cotton storehouse, flitting like a dark and silent ghost in among the workmen, greeting them with her low musical voice, warding them with the cold majesty of her eyes; each day afraid of some last parting, each night triumphant﻿—it was still there!

===Chinese===
「没错，先生，长在我的地头上，是我手底下那些黑鬼种的，先生！」（译注：原文 niggers，系白人种族主义者的蔑称，作者杜波依斯如实记录以揭露南方压迫现实）他向客人们保证；客人们啧啧称奇。
在佐拉心里，这打成包的、美丽的纤维是她的；它是幸福的象征；是一件被亵渎之手偷走的圣物。等它回到她手里（它一定会回来的，她攥紧双拳喊道），它会带来幸福；不是那至大的幸福——那个已永远地去了——而是光照、是赎罪，和几分权能与荣耀。于是，她几乎是不由自主地，老在那棉花仓房一带徘徊，像一个幽暗沉默的鬼影，飘进工人们中间，用她那低低的、乐音般的嗓音招呼他们，又用眼睛里冷冷的威严把他们挡开；每天害怕这便是最后一次分别，每夜又扬眉吐气——它还在那里！

===Original===
The Colonel﻿—Zora already forgotten﻿—rode up to the Cresswell Oaks, pondering darkly. It was bad enough to contemplate Helen’s marriage in distant prospect, but the sudden, almost peremptory desire for marrying at Eastertide, a little less than two months away, was absurd. There were “business reasons arising from the presidential campaign in the fall,” John Taylor had telegraphed; but there was already too much business in the arrangement to suit the Colonel. With Harry it was different. Indeed it was his own quiet suggestion that made John Taylor hurry matters.

===Chinese===
上校——佐拉早已被忘到脑后——策马回到克雷斯韦尔橡树庄，一路闷闷地思量。远远地盘算海伦的婚事，已经够糟的了，可忽然之间，几乎是斩钉截铁地要求在复活节期间成婚——离现在不到两个月——这简直荒唐。「秋季总统大选带来些生意上的因由，」约翰·泰勒电报里说；可这套安排里生意气已经太重，不合上校的意。哈里那边却不同。其实，正是上校自己不动声色的提议，才让约翰·泰勒把这事催得这么紧。

===Original===
Harry trusted to the novelty of his father’s new wealth to make the latter complacent; he himself felt an impatient longing for the haven of a home. He had been too long untethered. He distrusted himself. The devil within was too fond of taking the bit in his teeth. He would remember to his dying day one awful shriek in the night, as of a soul tormenting and tormented. He wanted the protection of a good woman, and sometimes against the clear whiteness of her letters so joyous and generous, even if a bit prim and didactic, he saw a vision of himself reflected as he was, and he feared.

===Chinese===
哈里指望父亲新富的那股新鲜劲能让老人家顺从；他自己则感到一种急不可耐的、对一个家庭港湾的渴望。他太久了没有系绊。他不信任自己。他心里那魔鬼太喜欢脱缰撒野。他到死都不会忘记那一夜一声可怖的尖叫，像是一个折磨人、又被人折磨的灵魂发出的。他需要一个好女人的庇护，有时，对照着她那些信——欢快、大方，纵然有那么点一本正经、好为人师——那清清白白的底色，他会照见自己本来的面目，于是他怕了。

===Original===
It was distinctively disconcerting to Colonel Cresswell to find Harry quite in favor of early nuptials, and to learn that the sole objection even in Helen’s mind was the improbability of getting a wedding-gown in time. Helen had all a child’s naive love for beautiful and dainty things, and a wedding-gown from Paris had been her life dream. On this point, therefore, there ensued spirited arguments and much correspondence, and both her brother and her lover evinced characteristic interest in the planning.

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔上校颇为狼狈地发现，哈里竟十分赞成早早成婚，而海伦心里唯一的顾虑，竟只是赶不赶得及置办嫁衣。海伦有着一个孩子对精美雅致之物天真的喜爱，一件巴黎来的嫁衣，是她一生的梦。于是在这件事上，掀起了一番热烈的争论和大量的通信，她哥哥和她的情人，都对这场筹划表现出各具特色的兴致。

===Original===
Said Harry: “Sis, I’ll cable to Paris today. They can easily hurry the thing along.”
Helen was delighted; she handed over a telegram just received from John Taylor. “Send me, express, two bales best cotton you can get.”
The Colonel read the message. “I don’t see the connection between this and hurrying up a wedding-gown,” he growled. None of them discerned the handwriting of Destiny.

===Chinese===
哈里说：「妹子，我今天就给巴黎打电报。他们催一催，这事不难办。」
海伦欢喜极了；她递过来一封刚收到的、约翰·泰勒发来的电报。「速寄，快运，两包你处所能弄到最好的棉花。」
上校看了电报。「我看不出这跟催嫁衣有什么关系，」他咕噜道。他们谁也没看出这背后命运的笔迹。

===Original===
“Neither do I,” said Harry, who detected yielding in his father’s tone. “But we’d better send him the two prize bales; it will be a fine advertisement of our plantation, and evidently he has a surprise in store for us.”
The Colonel affected to hesitate, but next morning the Silver Fleece went to town.
Zora watched it go, and her heart swelled and died within her. She walked to town, to the station. She did not see Mrs. Vanderpool arriving from New Orleans; but Mrs. Vanderpool saw her, and looked curiously at the tall, tragic figure that leaned so dolorously beside the freight car. The bales were loaded into the express car; the train pulled away, its hoarse snorting waking vague echoes in the forest beyond. But to the girl who stood at the End, looking outward to darkness, those echoes roared like the crack of doom. A passing band of contract hands called to her mockingly, and one black giant, laughing loudly, gripped her hand.

===Chinese===
「我也看不出，」哈里说，他听出父亲语气里的松动。「不过咱们还是把那两包得奖的棉包寄给他吧；这正好给咱们种植园做个体面广告，再说他显然要给咱们一个惊喜。」
上校装出犹豫的样子，可第二天一早，银色羊毛就进城去了。
佐拉眼睁睁看着它被运走，心在胸膛里胀起来，又死过去。她走到镇上，走到车站。她没看见从新奥尔良来的范德普尔太太下车；可范德普尔太太看见了她，好奇地打量这个高高的、悲苦的身影——她那样凄然地倚在货车车厢边。棉包装进了快运车厢；火车开走了，它粗哑的喘息唤醒了那边林中隐隐的回声。可对这个站在终点、向外望着黑暗的姑娘来说，那些回声轰响如末日的崩裂。一队路过的合同工嘲弄地冲她叫嚷，其中一个黑皮肤的壮汉大声笑着，一把攥住了她的手。

===Original===
“Come, honey,” he shouted, “you’se a’dreaming! Come on, honey!”
She turned abruptly and gripped his hand, as one drowning grips anything offered﻿—gripped till he winced. She laughed a loud mirthless laugh, that came pouring like a sob from her deep lungs.
“Come on!” she mocked, and joined them.

===Chinese===
「来呀，宝贝，」他嚷，「你在做梦哪！来吧，宝贝！」
她猛地一转身，反攥住他的手，像溺水的人抓住递过来的任何东西——攥得他直皱眉。她放声大笑，没有一点笑意，那笑像一声呜咽，从她深深的肺腑里涌出来。
「来呀！」她嘲弄地应着，加入了他们。

===Original===
They were a motley crowd, ragged, swaggering, jolly. There were husky, big-limbed youths, and boldfaced, loud-tongued girls. Tomorrow they would start upcountry to some backwoods barony in the kingdom of cotton, and work till Christmas time. Today was the last in town; there was craftily advanced money in their pockets and riot in their hearts. In the gathering twilight they marched noisily through the streets; in their midst, wide-eyed and laughing almost hysterically, marched Zora.

===Chinese===
这是一群杂色的人，破破烂烂、大摇大摆、快快活活。有嗓音粗哑、手脚粗大的小伙子，有脸皮放肆、嗓门敞亮的姑娘。明天他们就要启程上乡，去棉花王国某处偏僻林子的庄园里，一直干到圣诞节。今天是他们在镇上的最后一天；口袋里有老板精明地预支的钱，心里头是放纵的冲动。在渐浓的暮色里，他们吵吵嚷嚷地穿过街道；在他们中间，瞪着眼、近乎歇斯底里地笑着的，行走的正是佐拉。

===Original===
Mrs. Vanderpool meantime rode thoughtfully out of town toward Cresswell Oaks. She was returning from witnessing the Mardi Gras festivities at New Orleans and at the urgent invitation of the Cresswells had stopped off. She might even stay to the wedding if the new plans matured.
Mrs. Vanderpool was quite upset. Her French maid, on whom she had depended absolutely for five years or more, had left her.

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太则一路若有所思地乘车出镇，往克雷斯韦尔橡树庄去。她是从新奥尔良看罢狂欢节回来，应克雷斯韦尔家一再相邀，中途下了车。若新计划成熟，她甚至可能留下来参加婚礼。
范德普尔太太颇为心烦。她那位五年来事事仰仗的法国贴身女仆，离开她了。

===Original===
“I think I want to try a colored maid,” she told the Cresswells, laughingly, as they drove home. “They have sweet voices and they can’t doff their uniform. Helene without her cap and apron was often mistaken for a lady, and while I was in New Orleans a French confectioner married her under some such delusion. Now, haven’t you a girl about here who would do?”
“No,” declared Harry decisively, but his sister suggested that she might ask Miss Smith at the colored school.

===Chinese===
「我想，我想试试用一个有色人种女仆，」在乘车回家的路上，她笑着告诉克雷斯韦尔家的人。「她们嗓音甜，又脱不下那身制服。海伦摘了帽子围裙，常被人误认作一位淑女，我在新奥尔良的时候，一个法国糕点匠就在这种错觉下娶了她。那么，你们这一带有没有合适的姑娘？」
「没有，」哈里断然说，可他妹妹提议，她可以去问问那所有色人种学校的史密斯小姐。

===Original===
Again Mrs. Vanderpool laughed, but after tea she wandered idly down the road. The sun behind the swamp was crimsoning the world. Mrs. Vanderpool strolled alone to the school, and saw Sarah Smith. There was no cordiality in the latter’s greeting, but when she heard the caller’s errand her attention was at once arrested and held. The interests of her charges were always uppermost in her mind.
“Can’t I have the girl Zora?” Mrs. Vanderpool at last inquired.

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太又笑了笑，可喝过茶后，她便无所事事地顺着大路溜达。沼泽那边的落日把整个世界染成一片绯红。范德普尔太太独自溜达到学校，见到了莎拉·史密斯。后者的招呼里毫无热络，可一听来访者的来意，她的注意立刻被攫住、被留住了。她那些受她照管之人的利益，在她心里始终是头等大事。
「我不能要那个叫佐拉的姑娘吗？」范德普尔太太终于问。

===Original===
Miss Smith started, for she was thinking of Zora at that very instant. The girl was later than usual, and she was momentarily expecting to see her tall form moving languidly up the walk.
She gave Mrs. Vanderpool a searching look. Mrs. Vanderpool glanced involuntarily at her gown and smiled as she did it.
“Could I trust you with a human soul?” asked Miss Smith abruptly.

===Chinese===
史密斯小姐一怔，因为她此刻正想着佐拉。姑娘今天比往常迟，她正一刻一刻地盼着看见她那高高的身影懒懒地走上小径来。
她审视地看了范德普尔太太一眼。范德普尔太太不由自主地瞥了一眼自己的衣裙，笑了笑。
「我能把一个人的灵魂托付给你吗？」史密斯小姐突兀地问。

===Original===
Mrs. Vanderpool looked up quickly. The half mocking answer that rose involuntarily to her lips was checked. Within, Mrs. Vanderpool was a little puzzled at herself. Why had she asked for this girl? She had felt a strange interest in her﻿—a peculiar human interest since she first saw her and as she saw her again this afternoon. But would she make a satisfactory maid? Was it not a rather dangerous experiment? Why had she asked for her? She certainly had not intended to when she entered the house.

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太猛地抬起头。一句半带嘲弄、不由自主涌到唇边的答话，被咽了回去。范德普尔太太心里对自己也有几分纳罕。她为什么要这个姑娘？她对她生出了一种奇特的兴趣——一种特别的人之兴趣，从她第一次见她时起就有，今天下午再见到她时又涌起。可她做一个称职的女仆吗？这岂不是个相当危险的尝试？她为什么要她？她进门时可绝没打算要她的。

===Original===
In the silence Miss Smith continued: “Here is a child in whom the fountains of the great deep are suddenly broken up. With peace and care she would find herself, for she is strong. But here there is no peace. Slavery of soul and body awaits her and I am powerless to protect her. She must go away. That going away may make or ruin her. She knows nothing of working for wages and she has not the servant’s humility; but she has loyalty and pluck. For one she loves there is nothing she would not do; but she cannot be driven. Or rather, if she is driven, it may rouse in her the devil incarnate. She needs not exactly affection﻿—she would almost resent that﻿—but intelligent interest and care. In return for this she will gradually learn to serve and serve loyally. Frankly, Mrs. Vanderpool, I would not have chosen you for this task of human education. Indeed, you would have been my last thought﻿—you seem to me﻿—I speak plainly﻿—a worldly woman. Yet, perhaps﻿—who can tell?﻿—God has especially set you to this task. At any rate, I have little choice. I am at my wits’ end. Elspeth, the mother of this child, is not long dead; and here is the girl, beautiful, unprotected; and here am I, almost helpless. She is in debt to the Cresswells, and they are pressing the claim to her service. Take her if you can get her﻿—it is, I fear, her only chance. Mind you﻿—if you can persuade her; and that may be impossible.”

===Chinese===
在沉默里，史密斯小姐继续说：「这孩子，她心底那大深渊的泉源，忽然间决了口。若有安宁和照应，她会找回她自己，因为她生得坚韧。可这里没有安宁。身与心的奴役正等着她，而我无力护她。她必须离开。这一走，也许成全她，也许毁了她。她全然不懂得为工钱而劳作，也没有做仆人的那份谦卑；可她有忠诚，有胆气。为一个她爱的人，她什么都肯做；可她不能被驱使。或者更确切地说，倘若她被驱使，那或许会激起她心里那个化身的魔鬼。她需要的并不完全是情爱——那个她几乎会抗拒——而是明智的关切和照应。作为回报，她会慢慢地学会服侍人，且忠心耿耿地服侍。坦白说，范德普尔太太，若论这桩『教人』的差事，我本不会挑中你。说实话，你是我最后一个会想到的人——在我看来——我直说了——你是个世俗的女人。然而，也许——谁知道呢？——是上帝特意把你安到这桩事上的。无论如何，我别无选择。我已智穷力竭。这孩子的母亲埃尔斯贝思，新近才死；而这姑娘，美丽、无人护佑；而我呢，几乎无能为力。她欠着克雷斯韦尔家的债，他们正逼着要她做工抵债。你若能得到她，就把她带去吧——我怕，这是她唯一的机会了。记住——前提是你能说服她；而那也许根本不可能。」

===Original===
“Where is she now?”
Miss Smith glanced out at the darkening landscape, and then at her watch.
“I do not know; she’s very late. She’s given to wandering, but usually she is here before this time.”
“I saw her in town this afternoon,” said Mrs. Vanderpool.
“Zora? In town?” Miss Smith rose. “I’ll send her to you tomorrow,” she said quietly. Mrs. Vanderpool had hardly reached the Oaks before Miss Smith was driving toward town.

===Chinese===
「她现在在哪儿？」
史密斯小姐望了望窗外渐暗的天色，又看了看表。
「我不知道；她今天太迟了。她爱四处游荡，可往常这时候她早该到了。」
「我今天下午在镇上见过她，」范德普尔太太说。
「佐拉？在镇上？」史密斯小姐站起身。「我明天送她到你那儿，」她平静地说。范德普尔太太还没走到橡树庄，史密斯小姐已经驱车赶往镇上了。

===Original===
A small cabin on the town’s ragged fringe was crowded to suffocation. Within arose noisy shouts, loud songs, and raucous laughter; the scraping of a fiddle and whine of an accordion. Liquor began to appear and happy faces grew red-eyed and sodden as the dances whirled. At the edge of the orgy stood Zora, wild-eyed and bewildered, mad with the pain that gripped her heart and hammered in her head, crying in tune with the frenzied music﻿—“the End﻿—the End!”

===Chinese===
镇子破破烂烂的边缘上，一间小屋挤得密不透风。屋里响起嘈杂的吆喝、高亢的歌声、刺耳的笑声；有提琴的刮擦声，有手风琴的呜咽。酒开始上了，一张张快活的脸渐渐变得眼红、浮肿，旋舞一圈圈转着。在这场纵酒狂欢的边缘，站着佐拉，眼神狂乱、神志迷惘，揪心的痛、锤打的头痛逼得她发狂，和着那癫狂的乐声喊着——「终点——终点！」

===Original===
Abruptly she recognized a face despite the wreck and ruin of its beauty.
“Bertie!” she cried as she seized the mother of little Emma by the arm.
The woman staggered and offered her glass.
“Drink,” she cried, “drink and forget.”

===Chinese===
猛地，她认出了一张脸，尽管那脸的美已被摧残殆尽、面目全非。
「伯蒂！」她喊，一把抓住小埃玛她娘的胳膊。
那女人踉跄了一下，把自己的杯子递过来。
「喝，」她喊，「喝了，就忘了。」

===Original===
In a moment Zora sprang forward and seized the burning liquid in both hands. A dozen hands clapped a devil’s tattoo. A score of voices yelled and laughed. The shriek of the music was drowned beneath the thunder of stamping feet. Men reeled to singing women’s arms, but above the roar rose the song of the voice of Zora﻿—she glided to the middle of the room, standing tiptoed with skirts that curled and turned; she threw back her head, raised the liquor to her lips, paused and looked into the face of Miss Smith.

===Chinese===
一眨眼，佐拉抢上前，双手抓起那灼烫的液体。十几只手拍出魔鬼般的鼓点。二十几条嗓子又叫又笑。乐器的尖啸被跺脚的雷鸣淹没。男人们踉跄地倒进唱歌的女人们臂里，可这一切喧嚣之上，升起了佐拉的歌声——她滑到屋子正中，踮着脚站着，裙裾旋卷翻飞；她仰起头，把酒举到唇边，停住，却望进了史密斯小姐的脸。

===Original===
A silence fell like a lightning flash on the room as that white face peered in at the door. Slowly Zora’s hands fell and her eyes blinked as though waking from some awful dream. She staggered toward the woman’s outstretched arms.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
那张白脸在门口探望的一瞬，一片沉默像闪电般落在屋里。慢慢地，佐拉的双手垂下，她眨了眨眼，仿佛从一场可怖的梦里醒来。她踉跄着扑向那女人张开的双臂。……

===Original===
Late that night the girl lay close in Miss Smith’s motherly embrace.
“I was going to hell!” she whispered, trembling.
“Why, Zora?” asked Miss Smith calmly.
“I couldn’t find the Way﻿—and I wanted to forget.”
“People in hell don’t forget,” was the matter-of-fact comment. “And, Zora, what way do you seek? The way where?”

===Chinese===
深夜，姑娘紧紧依偎在史密斯小姐母亲般的怀抱里。
「我要下地狱了！」她颤着声低语。
「为什么呢，佐拉？」史密斯小姐平静地问。
「我找不到那条路——而我想忘掉。」
「地狱里的人，忘不掉的，」这是句就事论事的回答。「那么，佐拉，你找的是哪条路？通向哪儿的路？」

===Original===
Zora sat up in bed, and lifted a gray and stricken face.
“It’s a lie,” she cried, with hoarse earnedstness, “the way nowhere. There is no Way! You know﻿—I want *him*﻿—I want nothing on earth but him﻿—and him I can’t ever have.”
The older woman drew her down tenderly.
“No, Zora,” she said, “there’s something you want more than him and something you can have!”
“What?” asked the wondering girl.
“His respect,” said Sarah Smith, “and I know the Way.”

===Chinese===
佐拉在床上坐起来，抬起一张灰白的、惨痛的脸。
「是谎话，」她沙哑而恳切地喊，「通向哪儿都不的路。根本就没有那条路！你知道——我要的是*他*——这世上除了他我什么都不要——可他，我永远也得不到。」
年长的女人温柔地把她按躺下。
「不，佐拉，」她说，「有一样东西你比要他更想要，而且是你能够得到的！」
「什么？」姑娘惊奇地问。
「他的敬重，」莎拉·史密斯说，「而我，知道那条路。」

## XXI The Marriage Morning / XXI 新婚之晨

===Original===
Mrs. Vanderpool watched Zora as she came up the path beneath the oaks. “She walks well,” she observed. And laying aside her book, she waited with a marked curiosity.

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太望着佐拉从橡树下的那条小径走来。「她走路走得真好，」她评议道。于是放下书，带着一种明显的好奇，等着。

===Original===
The girl’s greeting was brief, almost curt, but unintentionally so, as one could easily see, for back in her eyes lurked an impatient hunger; she was not thinking of greetings. She murmured a quick word, and stood straight and tall with her eyes squarely on the lady.

===Chinese===
姑娘的招呼很简短，近乎生硬，但显然并非有意为之——谁都看得出来，因为她眼底藏着一股急切的渴盼；她心思不在打招呼上。她匆匆咕哝了一声，便直直地、高高地站着，两眼正正地落在这位太太脸上。

===Original===
In the depths of Mrs. Vanderpool’s heart something strange﻿—not new, but very old﻿—stirred. Before her stood this tall black girl, quietly returning her look. Mrs. Vanderpool had a most uncomfortable sense of being judged, of being weighed﻿—and there arose within her an impulse to self-justification.

===Chinese===
在范德普尔太太心底，有什么陌生的东西——不是新的，而是非常古老的——动了一下。在她面前站着这个高高的黑人姑娘，静静地回望着她。范德普尔太太有一种极不自在的感觉——自己正在被审视、被称量——于是她心里涌起一股自我辩白的冲动。

===Original===
She smiled and said sweetly, “Won’t you sit?” But despite all this, her mind seemed leaping backward a thousand years; back to a simpler, primal day when she herself, white, frail, and fettered, stood before the dusky magnificence of some bejewelled barbarian queen and sought to justify herself. She shook off the fantasy﻿—and yet how well the girl stood. It was not everyone that could stand still and well.

===Chinese===
她笑了笑，甜甜地说：「你不坐吗？」可纵然这样，她的心思仿佛一下子跃回了一千年前；回到一个更简单、更初民的时代，那时她自己——白皙、柔弱、戴着枷——站在某个幽暗华美、遍身珠玉的蛮族女王面前，竭力为自己辩白。她甩掉这个幻想——然而这姑娘站得真好。站得一动不动、站得好看，并不是人人都能做到的。

===Original===
“Please sit down,” she repeated with her softest charm, not dreaming that outside the school white persons did not ask this girl to sit in their presence. But even this did not move Zora. She sat down. There was in her, walking, standing, sitting, a simple directness which Mrs. Vanderpool sensed and met.

===Chinese===
「请坐吧，」她又用最柔软的风韵重复了一遍，做梦也没想到，在学校以外，白人从不会请这姑娘在他们跟前落座。可即便这样也打动不了佐拉。她坐下了。她身上——无论是走、是站、是坐——有一种简朴的直截了当，范德普尔太太感觉到了，也以同样的直截了当回应。

===Original===
“Zora, I need someone to help me﻿—to do my hair and serve my coffee, and dress and take care of me. The work will not be hard, and you can travel and see the world and live well. Would you like it?”
“But I do not know how to do all these things,” returned Zora, slowly. She was thinking rapidly﻿—Was this the Way? It sounded wonderful. The World, the great mysterious World, that stretched beyond the swamp and into which Bles and the Silver Fleece had gone﻿—did it lead to the Way? But if she went there what would she see and do, and would it be possible to become such a woman as Miss Smith pictured?

===Chinese===
「佐拉，我需要一个人帮我——帮我梳头、端咖啡，帮我穿衣、照料我。活儿不重，你还能出门见世面、过好日子。你愿意吗？」
「可这些事我都不会做呀，」佐拉慢慢回道。她脑子转得飞快——这就是那条路吗？听起来妙极了。那世界，那浩大而神秘的世界，铺展在沼泽的那一边，布莱斯和银色羊毛都进了那里面——它通向那条路吗？可她若去了那里，会看见什么、做什么，而她有可能变成史密斯小姐所描绘的那种女人吗？

===Original===
“What is the world like?” asked Zora.
Mrs. Vanderpool smiled. “Oh, I meant great active cities and buildings, myriads of people and wonderful sights.”
“Yes﻿—but back of it all, what is it really? What does it look like?”
“Heavens, child! Don’t ask. Really, it isn’t worth while peering back of things. One is sure to be disappointed.”

===Chinese===
「那世界是什么样的？」佐拉问。
范德普尔太太笑了。「哦，我是说那些热闹的大城和大楼，无数的人，好看的景致。」
「是——可这一切背后呢，它到底是什么样的？它看着像什么？」
「天哪，孩子！别问了。真的，凡事都要往背后去瞅，不值当的。准叫人失望。」

===Original===
“Then what’s the use of seeing the world?”
“Why, one must live; and why not be happy?” answered Mrs. Vanderpool, amused, baffled, spurred for the time being from her chronic ennui.
“Are you happy?” retorted Zora, looking her over carefully, from silken stockings to garden hat. Mrs. Vanderpool laid aside her little mockery and met the situation bravely.
“No,” she replied simply. Her eyes grew old and tired.

===Chinese===
「那见世面还有什么用？」
「咦，人总得活嘛；那为什么不快快活活的呢？」范德普尔太太答道，觉得有趣、又觉无措，一时间反倒从她那长年的倦怠里被撅了起来。
「你快活吗？」佐拉反诘，把她从头到脚细细打量了一番，从丝袜到园艺帽。范德普尔太太收起了那点小小的嘲弄，硬着头皮直面这个场面。
「不，」她简单地答。她的眼神一下子老而倦了。

===Original===
Involuntarily Zora’s hand crept out protectingly and lay a moment over the white jewelled fingers. Then quickly recovering herself, she started hastily to withdraw it, but the woman’s fingers closed around the darker ones, and Mrs. Vanderpool’s eyes became dim.
“I need you, Zora,” she said; and then, seeing the half-formed question, “Yes, and you need me; we need each other. In the world lies opportunity, and I will help you.”

===Chinese===
佐拉的手不由自主地伸了出去，带着庇护的意味，在那戴着珠宝的白皙手指上搭了片刻。随即她猛地回过神，慌忙要缩回手，可那女人的手指却合拢来，握住了这双颜色更深的手，范德普尔太太的眼睛也蒙眬了。
「我需要你，佐拉，」她说；随即看出那欲吐未吐的疑问，又道，「是的，你也需要我；我们彼此需要。这世上有机遇，而我会帮你。」

===Original===
Zora rose abruptly, and Mrs. Vanderpool feared, with a tightening of heart, that she had lost this strangely alluring girl.
“I will come tomorrow,” said Zora.

===Chinese===
佐拉霍地站起身，范德普尔太太心一紧，生怕自己失掉了这个出奇地动人的姑娘。
「我明天来，」佐拉说。

===Original===
As Mrs. Vanderpool went in to lunch, reaction and lingering doubts came trouping back. To replace the daintiest of trained experts with the most baffling semi-barbarian, well!
“Have you hired a maid?” asked Helen.
“I’ve engaged Zora,” laughed Mrs. Vanderpool, lightly; “and now I’m wondering whether I have a jewel or﻿—a white elephant.”
“Probably neither,” remarked Harry Cresswell, drily; but he avoided the lady’s inquiring eyes.

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太进去吃午饭时，反悔和余疑又一窝蜂地涌回来。用一个最叫人摸不透的半野蛮人，去顶替一个最精巧的训练有素的行家，唉！
「你雇到女仆了吗？」海伦问。
「我定了佐拉，」范德普尔太太轻轻地笑；「现在我正琢磨呢，我这是得了一颗宝石，还是——一个摆设。」
「多半哪个都不是，」哈里·克雷斯韦尔干巴巴地说；可他避开了这位太太探询的目光。

===Original===
Next morning Zora came easily into Mrs. Vanderpool’s life. There was little she knew of her duties, but little, too, that she could not learn with a deftness and divination almost startling. Her quietness, her quickness, her young strength, were like a soothing balm to the tired woman of fashion, and within a week she had sunk back contentedly into Zora’s strong arms.
“It’s a jewel,” she decided.

===Chinese===
第二天一早，佐拉便顺顺当当地走进了范德普尔太太的生活。她分内的事她几乎一无所知，可也几乎没有一样是她学不会的——那份灵巧，那份无师自通的领悟，简直叫人吃惊。她的安静、她的麻利、她年轻的力气，对这位疲惫的时髦太太来说，就像一帖抚慰的香膏，不出一个礼拜，她便心满意足地偎进了佐拉那双有力的臂膀。
「是一颗宝石，」她下了断语。

===Original===
With this verdict, the house agreed. The servants waited on “Miss Zora” gladly; the men scarcely saw her, and the ladies ran to her for help in all sorts. Harry Cresswell looked upon this transformation with an amused smile, but the Colonel saw in it simply evidence of dangerous obstinacy in a black girl who hitherto had refused to work.

===Chinese===
全家都附和这个判语。仆人们乐意为『佐拉小姐』效劳；男人们几乎看不见她，太太小姐们却大事小事都跑去找她帮忙。哈里·克雷斯韦尔带着好笑的微笑看着这场转变，上校却只把它看作一个黑人姑娘危险而执拗的明证——这姑娘从前可是一直不肯干活的。

===Original===
Zora had been in the house but a week when a large express package was received from John Taylor. Its unwrapping brought a cry of pleasure from the ladies. There lay a bolt of silken-like cambric of wondrous fineness and lustre, marked: “For the wedding-dress.” The explanation accompanied the package, that Mary Taylor had a similar piece in the North.

===Chinese===
佐拉进这屋子才一星期，约翰·泰勒便寄来了一个快运大件。一拆开，太太小姐们齐声欢呼。里面是一匹丝绸般的细布，精细光亮得惊人，上面标着：「用作嫁衣」。随件附有说明：玛丽·泰勒在北方也有一匹同样的。

===Original===
Helen and Harry said nothing of the cablegram to the Paris tailor, and Helen took no steps toward having the cambric dress made, not even when the wedding invitations appeared.
“A Cresswell married in cotton!” Helen was almost in tears lest the Paris gown be delayed, and sure enough a cablegram came at last saying that there was little likelihood of the gown being ready by Easter. It would be shipped at the earliest convenience, but it could hardly catch the necessary boat. Helen had a good cry, and then came a wild rush to get John Taylor’s cloth ready. Still, Helen was querulous. She decided that silk embroidery must embellish the skirt. The dressmaker was in despair.

===Chinese===
海伦和哈里对给巴黎裁缝打电报一事只字不提，海伦也迟迟不动手让人拿这细布做嫁衣，连婚礼请帖都发出去了也不做。『一个克雷斯韦尔家的人，竟穿着棉布成婚！』海伦差点急出眼泪，生怕巴黎那件嫁衣耽误了。果不其然，一封电报终于来了，说复活节前赶出嫁衣的希望渺茫。会尽早寄出，但怕是赶不上必要的船了。海伦好好哭了一场，于是又一阵风似的张罗起把约翰·泰勒那匹布赶制出来。可海伦还是满腹牢骚。她认定裙子上一定得加丝绣点缀。裁缝愁得要命。

===Original===
“I haven’t a single spare worker,” she declared.
Helen was appealing to Mrs. Vanderpool.
“I can do it,” said Zora, who was in the room.
“Do you know how?” asked the dressmaker.
“No, but I want to know.”
Mrs. Vanderpool gave a satisfied nod. “Show her,” she said. The dressmaker was on the edge of rebellion. “Zora sews beautifully,” added Mrs. Vanderpool.

===Chinese===
「我连一个闲下来的人手都没有，」她声言。
海伦正向范德普尔太太求助。
「我能做，」在屋里的佐拉说。
「你会做吗？」裁缝问。
「不会，可我想学。」
范德普尔太太满意地点点头。「教教她，」她说。裁缝几乎要造反了。「佐拉的针线活漂亮得很，」范德普尔太太又补了一句。

===Original===
Thus the beautiful cloth came to Zora’s room, and was spread in a glossy cloud over her bed. She trembled at its beauty and felt a vague inner yearning, as if some subtle magic of the woven web were trying to tell her its story.
She worked over it faithfully and lovingly in every spare hour and in long nights of dreaming. Wilfully she departed from the set pattern and sewed into the cloth something of the beauty in her heart. In new and intricate ways, with soft shadowings and coverings, she wove in that white veil her own strange soul, and Mrs. Vanderpool watched her curiously, but in silence.

===Chinese===
就这样，那匹美丽的布到了佐拉房里，像一片亮泽的云，铺在她床上。她为它的美而颤栗，感到一种隐隐的内在渴望，仿佛这织物里某种幽微的魔力，正想把它的故事告诉她。
她一片忠心、一片爱意地在每个空闲的时辰、在长长的如梦的夜里，伏在这匹布上劳作。她任性偏离了既定的花样，把自己心里的美，一针一线缝进了布。她以新奇而繁复的针法，以柔和的阴影和掩覆，把自己那奇异的灵魂织进了那方白纱，而范德普尔太太好奇地、却默默地望着她。

===Original===
Meantime all things were arranged for a double wedding at Cresswell Oaks. As John and Mary Taylor had no suitable home, they were to come down and the two brides to go forth from the Cresswell mansion. Accordingly the Taylors arrived a week before the wedding and the home took on a festive air. Even Colonel Cresswell expanded under the genial influences, and while his head still protested his heart was glad. He had to respect John Taylor’s undoubted ability; and Mary Taylor was certainly lovely, in spite of that assumption of cleverness of which the Colonel could not approve.

===Chinese===
与此同时，克雷斯韦尔橡树庄一场双人婚礼的一切都已备妥。因为约翰和玛丽·泰勒没有合适的住处，他们便要南下，两位新娘从克雷斯韦尔大宅里走出去。于是泰勒家的人在婚礼前一星期到了，宅子里有了喜庆的气象。连克雷斯韦尔上校在这番和乐的气氛里也舒展开来，尽管他理智上仍在反对，心却是高兴的。他不得不敬重约翰·泰勒那无可置疑的才干；而玛丽·泰勒也确实可爱，纵然她身上有那种自作聪明的劲儿，是上校所不以为然的。

===Original===
Mary returned to the old scenes with mingled feelings. Especially was she startled at seeing Zora a member of the household and apparently high in favor. It brought back something of the old uneasiness and suspicion.
All this she soon forgot under the cadence of Harry Cresswell’s pleasant voice and the caressing touch of his arm. He seemed handsomer than ever; and he was, for sleep and temperance and the wooing of a woman had put a tinge in his marble face, smoothed the puffs beneath his eyes, and given him a more distinguished bearing and a firmer hand. And Mary Taylor was very happy. So was her brother, only differently; he was making money; he was planning to make more, and he had something to pet which seemed to him extraordinarily precious and valuable.

===Chinese===
玛丽回到这些旧地，心情复杂。尤其让她吃惊的是，看见佐拉竟成了这宅子的一员，而且显然颇受宠。这勾起了她几分旧日的不安与疑心。
这一切，她很快就在哈里·克雷斯韦尔那悦耳的声调里、在他那爱抚般触着她的手臂间，忘了个干净。他似乎比从前更英俊了；他确实如此，因为睡眠、节制和一个女人的求欢，给他的大理石脸庞添了一丝血色，抚平了眼下的浮肿，也给了他更出众的气度和一只更稳的手。玛丽·泰勒很快活。她哥哥也快活，只是快活得不一样；他在赚钱；他在盘算着赚更多，而且他怀里有一样东西，他觉得格外金贵、格外值钱，正在那里抚弄。

===Original===
Taylor eagerly inquired after the cloth, and followed the ladies to Zora’s room, adjoining Mrs. Vanderpool’s, to see it. It lay uncut and shimmering, covered with dim silken tracery of a delicacy and beauty which brought an exclamation to all lips.
“That’s what we can do with Alabama cotton,” cried John Taylor in triumph.
They turned to him incredulously.
“But﻿—”
“No ‘buts’ about it; these are the two bales you sent me, woven with a silk woof.” No one particularly noticed that Zora had hastily left the room. “I had it done in Easterly’s New Jersey mills according to an old plan of mine. I’m going to make cloth like that right in this county some day,” and he chuckled gayly.

===Chinese===
泰勒热切地打听那匹布，跟着太太小姐们到佐拉那间紧挨范德普尔太太的房里去看。布还未经裁剪，闪着光亮，上面覆着一层隐隐的、丝绸般的勾花，精巧美丽，引得人人都惊叹出声。
「这就是我们能用阿拉巴马棉花做出的东西，」约翰·泰勒得意地叫起来。
众人将信将疑地转向他。
「可——」
「没什么『可是』的；这就是你寄给我的那两包棉花，织进了丝绸的纬线。」谁也没特别留意到，佐拉已匆匆离开了房间。「我是按自己一个旧方案，在伊斯特利的新泽西厂里织成的。改日我要在本县就地造出这样的料子来，」他轻快地笑着。

===Original===
But Zora was striding up and down the halls, the blood surging in her ears. After they were gone she came back and closed the doors. She dropped on her knees and buried her face in the filmy folds of the Silver Fleece.
“I knew it! I knew it!” she whispered in mingled tears and joy. “It called and I did not understand.”
It was her talisman newfound; her love come back, her stolen dream come true. Now she could face the world; God had turned it straight again. She would go into the world and find﻿—not Love, but the thing greater than Love. Outside the door came voices﻿—the dressmaker’s tones, Helen’s soft drawl, and Mrs. Vanderpool’s finished accents. Her face went suddenly gray. The Silver Fleece was not hers! It belonged﻿—She rose hastily. The door opened and they came in. The cutting must begin at once, they all agreed.

===Chinese===
可佐拉正在走廊里大步地走来走去，血在耳朵里轰轰直响。等人散了，她才回来，关上门。她双膝跪地，把脸埋进银色羊毛那轻纱般的褶层里。
「我知道的！我知道的！」她泪水与欢欣交织地低语。「它在呼唤，我却不明白。」
这是她新得的护符；是她的爱回来了，是她被偷走的梦成真了。如今她能面对这世界了；上帝又把它拨正了。她要走进世界，去寻——不是爱，而是比爱更大的那个东西。门外传来人声——裁缝的嗓音，海伦软软的拖腔，范德普尔太太圆熟的语调。她的脸骤然灰了。银色羊毛不是她的！它属于——她慌忙站起。门开了，她们走了进来。大家一致说，得马上动工裁剪。

===Original===
“Is it ready, Zora?” inquired Helen.
“No,” Zora quietly answered, “not quite, but tomorrow morning, early.” As soon as she was alone again, she sat down and considered. By and by, while the family was at lunch, she folded the Silver Fleece carefully and locked it in her new trunk. She would hide it in the swamp. During the afternoon she sent to town for oilcloth, and bade the black carpenter at Miss Smith’s make a cedar box, tight and tarred. In the morning she prepared Mrs. Vanderpool’s breakfast with unusual care. She was sorry for Mrs. Vanderpool, and sorry for Miss Smith. They would not, they could not, understand. What would happen to her? She did not know; she did not care. The Silver Fleece had returned to her. Soon it would be buried in the swamp whence it came. She had no alternative; she must keep it and wait.

===Chinese===
「布好了吗，佐拉？」海伦问。
「还没，」佐拉平静地答，「没全好，不过明天一大早就好。」等再无旁人，她坐下来盘算。过了一阵，趁一家人吃午饭，她把银色羊毛仔仔细细叠好，锁进了她那只新箱子。她要把它藏到沼泽里去。下午她打发人进城买油布，又央史密斯小姐那儿那个黑人木匠打一只严丝合缝、上了焦油的雪松木箱。第二天一早，她格外用心地给范德普尔太太备了早饭。她对不起范德普尔太太，也对不起史密斯小姐。她们不会、也不可能理解。她自己会怎样呢？她不知道；她也不在乎。银色羊毛已经回到她手里。很快，它就要被埋进它所由来的那片沼泽。她别无选择；她必须留下它，等着。

===Original===
She heard the dressmaker’s voice, and then her step upon the stair. She heard the sound of Harry Cresswell’s buggy, and a scurrying at the front door. On came the dressmaker’s footsteps﻿—then her door was unceremoniously burst open.
Helen Cresswell stood there radiant; the dressmaker, too, was wreathed in smiles. She carried a big red-sealed bundle.
“Zora!” cried Helen in ecstasy. “It’s come!” Zora regarded her coldly, and stood at bay. The dressmaker was ripping and snipping, and soon there lay revealed before them﻿—the Paris gown!

===Chinese===
她听见裁缝的嗓音，接着是裁缝上楼的脚步。她听见哈里·克雷斯韦尔那辆轻便马车的声响，还有正门前的一阵忙乱。裁缝的脚步越来越近——接着她的房门便被冒冒失失地推开了。
海伦·克雷斯韦尔光彩照人地站在那里；裁缝也满脸是笑。她捧着一个大红封印的大包袱。
「佐拉！」海伦狂喜地叫，「它来了！」佐拉冷冷地看着她，严阵以待。裁缝又撕又剪，很快，展现在她们面前的——是那件巴黎嫁衣！

===Original===
Helen was in raptures, but her conscience pricked her. She appealed to them. “Ought I to tell? You see, Mary’s gown will look miserably common beside it.”
The dressmaker was voluble. There was really nothing to tell; and besides, Helen was a Cresswell and it was to be expected, and so forth. Helen pursed her lips and petulantly tapped the floor with her foot.
“But the other gown?”
“Where is it?” asked the dressmaker, looking about. “It would make a pretty morning-dress﻿—”

===Chinese===
海伦欢喜若狂，可良心上却又过不去。她向众人求个公道。「我该不该说呢？你们瞧，玛丽的嫁衣搁在它旁边，可就显得寒碜透了。」
裁缝滔滔不绝。其实根本没什么好说的；况且海伦是克雷斯韦尔家的人，这是理所应当的，云云。海伦抿着嘴，赌气地用脚点着地。
「那另一件呢？」
「在哪儿呢？」裁缝四下张望着问。「正好做一件好看的晨衣——」

===Original===
But Helen had taken a sudden dislike to the thought of it.
“I don’t want it,” she declared. “And besides, I haven’t room for it in my trunks.”
Of a sudden she leaned down and whispered to Zora: “Zora, hide it and keep it if you want it. Come,” to the dressmaker, “I’m dying to try this on﻿—now.﻿ ﻿… Remember, Zora﻿—not a word.” And all this to Zora seemed no surprise; it was the Way, and it was opening before her because the talisman lay in her trunk.

===Chinese===
可海伦忽然对这念头生出反感。
「我不要它了，」她声言。「再说，我箱子里也没地方放。」
忽然她俯身对佐拉低语：「佐拉，你把它藏起来，想要就留着。来，」她对裁缝说，「我巴不得马上试这件——就现在。……记住，佐拉——一个字也别说。」而这一切对佐拉来说似乎毫不意外；这便是那条路，它正在她面前展开，因为那护符就躺在她的箱子里。

===Original===
So at last it came to Easter morning. The world was golden with jasmine, and crimson with azalea; down in the darker places gleamed the misty glory of the dogwood; new cotton shook, glimmered, and blossomed in the black fields, and over all the soft Southern sun poured its awakening light of life. There was happiness and hope again in the cabins, and hope and﻿—if not happiness, ambition, in the mansions.

===Chinese===
复活节的早晨终于到了。世界因茉莉而金黄，因杜鹃花而绯红；在那些更幽暗的角落里，闪着山茱萸那朦胧的光华；新棉在黑色的田地里摇曳、闪烁、开花，而南国柔和的阳光，把那唤醒生命的光倾泻在万物之上。小屋里又有了幸福和希望，而宅邸里也有了希望和——若说不上幸福，那便是野心。

===Original===
Zora, almost forgetting the wedding, stood before the mirror. Laying aside her dress, she draped her shimmering cloth about her, dragging her hair down in a heavy mass over ears and neck until she seemed herself a bride. And as she stood there, awed with the mystical union of a dead love and a living new born self, there came drifting in at the window, faintly, the soft sound of far-off marriage music.
“’Tis thy marriage morning, shining in the sun!”

===Chinese===
佐拉，几乎忘了婚礼这回事，站在镜前。她脱下衣裙，把那匹闪光的布披在身上，把头发沉甸甸地堆拉下来，盖住耳朵和脖颈，直到她自己看上去也像个新娘。她那样站着，为一桩死去的爱与一个鲜活的、新生的自我之间那神秘的合一而敬畏，这时，窗外隐隐飘进来远处婚礼乐声的柔音。
「这是你的新婚之晨，正沐在阳光中！」

===Original===
Two white and white-swathed brides were coming slowly down the great staircase of Cresswell Oaks, and two white and black-clothed bridegrooms awaited them. Either bridegroom looked gladly at the flow of his sister’s garments and almost darkly at his bride’s. For Helen was decked in Parisian splendor, while Mary was gowned in the Fleece.
“’Tis thy marriage morning, shining in the sun!”
Up floated the song of the little dark-faced children, and Zora listened.

===Chinese===
两位一身雪白、白纱裹身的新娘，正缓缓走下克雷斯韦尔橡树庄的大楼梯，两位白衣黑裤的新郎在等着她们。两位新郎都喜滋滋地望着自己妹妹衣裙的流转，而几乎阴沉地望着自己新娘的衣裙。因为海伦一身巴黎的华服，而玛丽穿的是那匹羊毛。
「这是你的新婚之晨，正沐在阳光中！」
一缕歌声升起，是那些小黑脸孩子唱的，佐拉静静地听着。

## XXII Miss Caroline Wynn / XXII 卡罗琳·怀恩小姐

===Original===
Bles Alwyn was seated in the anteroom of Senator Smith’s office in Washington. The Senator had not come in yet, and there were others waiting, too.
The young man sat in a corner, dreaming. Washington was his first great city, and it seemed a never-ending delight﻿—the streets, the buildings, the crowds; the shops, and lights, and noise; the kaleidoscopic panorama of a world’s doing, the myriad forms and faces, the talk and laughter of men. It was all wonderful magic to the country boy, and he stretched his arms and filled his lungs and cried: “Here I shall live!”

===Chinese===
布莱斯·阿尔文坐在华盛顿史密斯参议员办公室的前厅里。参议员还没到，还有别的人也在等着。
年轻人坐在角落里，做着梦。华盛顿是他见过的头一座大城市，它似乎是一种没完没了的乐事——街道、楼宇、人群；店铺、灯火和喧闹；万花筒般变幻的世事全景，千姿百态的人形和人面，人们的言谈和欢笑。这一切对这个乡下来的小伙子，都是奇妙的魔法，他伸展双臂，吸满一口气，喊道：「我要在这里生活！」

===Original===
Especially was he attracted by his own people. They seemed transformed, revivified, changed. Some might be mistaken for field hands on a holiday﻿—but not many. Others he did not recognize﻿—they seemed strange and alien﻿—sharper, quicker, and at once more overbearing and more unscrupulous.

===Chinese===
尤其吸引他的，是他本族的人。他们仿佛变了样、活了过来、改了形。有些倒可能被误当作过节打扮的田间长工——但不多。另一些他压根儿认不出来——他们显得陌生、疏远——更尖刻、更伶俐，同时又更盛气凌人、更肆无忌惮。

===Original===
There were yet others﻿—and at the sight of these Bles stood straighter and breathed like a man. They were well dressed, and well appearing men and women, who walked upright and looked one in the eye, and seemed like persons of affairs and money. They had arrived﻿—they were men﻿—they filled his mind’s ideal﻿—he felt like going up to them and grasping their hands and saying, “At last, brother!” Ah, it was good to find one’s dreams, walking in the light, in flesh and blood. Continually such thoughts were surging through his brain, and they were rioting through it again as he sat waiting in Senator Smith’s office.

===Chinese===
还有另一些人——一看见这些人，布莱斯便站得更直，呼吸也像个人样了。他们是些衣冠楚楚、仪表堂堂的男女，挺直腰板走路，坦然与人平视，看着像是办大事、有钱财的人物。他们已经成了事——他们是真正的男人——他们填满了他心里的理想——他恨不得走上前去握住他们的手，说：「到底见着了，兄弟！」啊，能找见自己的梦，它正行走在光天化日之下、是血肉之躯，真好。这样的念头不断地在他脑子里翻涌，此刻他坐在史密斯参议员办公室里等着，这些念头又在脑子里闹腾起来。

===Original===
The Senator was late this morning; when he came in he glanced at the morning paper before looking over his mail and the list of his callers. “Do fools like the American people deserve salvation?” he sneered, holding off the headlines and glancing at them.
“‘League Beats Trust.’… ‘Farmers of South Smash Effort to Bear Market… Send Cotton to Twelve Cents… Common People Triumph.’
“A man is induced to bite off his own nose and then to sing a paean of victory. It’s nauseating﻿—senseless. There is no earthly use striving for such blockheads; they’d crucify any Saviour.” Thus half consciously Senator Smith salved his conscience, while he extracted a certificate of deposit for fifty thousand dollars from his New York mail. He thrust it aside from his secretary’s view and looked at his list as he rang the bell: there was Representative Todd, and somebody named Alwyn﻿—nobody of importance. Easterly was due in a half-hour. He would get rid of Todd meantime.

===Chinese===
参议员今早来迟了；他进来后先扫了一眼晨报，才去翻他的邮件和访客名单。「像美国人民这样的蠢货，配得上救赎吗？」他嗤笑着，把头版头条举远些瞥了一眼。
「『同盟击溃托拉斯。』……『南方农民粉碎做空图谋……棉花冲上十二分……百姓得胜。』
一个人被哄得咬下了自己的鼻子，还要高唱一首胜利的凯歌。叫人作呕——毫无道理。为这样的榆木脑袋卖命，一点用处也没有；他们能把任何救主钉上十字架。」史密斯参议员这样半自觉地替自己的良心开脱，一边从纽约来的邮件里抽出一张五万元的存单。他把它推到秘书看不见的地方，按铃的同时看了一眼名单：有托德众议员，还有一个叫阿尔文的——无关紧要的小人物。伊斯特利半小时后到。他打算这会儿先把托德打发走。

===Original===
“Poor Todd,” he mused; “a lamb for the slaughter.”
But he patiently listened to him plead for party support and influence for his bill to prohibit gambling in futures.
“I was warned that it was useless to see you, Senator Smith, but I would come. I believe in you. Frankly, there is a strong group of your old friends and followers forming against you; they met only last night, but I did not go. Won’t you take a stand on some of these progressive matters﻿—this bill, or the Child Labor movement, or Low Tariff legislation?”

===Chinese===
「可怜的托德，」他暗想；「一头待宰的羔羊。」
可他还是耐着性子，听他为自己的《禁止期货投机法》恳求党的支持和影响。
「人家提醒过我，来找您是白费功夫，史密斯参议员，可我还是来了。我相信您。坦白说，您的一批老朋友、老追随者正在结成一伙反对您；他们就是昨晚开的会，可我没去。这些进步的事情上——这个法案，或者童工运动，或者低关税立法——您就不能表个态吗？」

===Original===
Mr. Smith listened but shook his head.
“When the time comes,” he announced deliberately, “I shall have something to say on several of these matters. At present I can only say that I cannot support this bill,” and Mr. Todd was ushered out. He met Mr. Easterly coming in and greeted him effusively. He knew him only as a rich philanthropist, who had helped the Neighborhood Guild in Washington﻿—one of Todd’s hobbies.
Easterly greeted Smith quietly.
“Got my letter?”
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
史密斯先生听着，摇了摇头。
「时机一到，」他慢条斯理地声言，「这几件事我是要发话的。眼下我只能说，我不能支持这个法案，」托德先生便被送了出去。他迎面撞见进来的伊斯特利先生，热络地招呼他。他只晓得伊斯特利是个阔气的慈善家，资助过华盛顿的邻里公会——那是托德的爱好之一。
伊斯特利静静地同史密斯打了招呼。
「收到我的信了？」
「收到了。」

===Original===
“Here are the three bills. You will go on the Finance Committee tomorrow; Sumdrich is chairman by courtesy, but you’ll have the real power. Put the Child Labor Bill first, and we’ll work the press. The Tariff will take most of the session, of course. We’ll put the cotton inspection bill through in the last days of the session﻿—see? I’m manoeuvring to get the Southern Congressmen into line.… Oh, one thing. Thompson says he’s a little worried about the Negroes; says there’s something more than froth in the talk of a bolt in the Northern Negro vote. We may have to give them a little extra money and a few more minor offices than usual. Talk with Thompson; the Negroes are sweet on you and he’s going to be the new chairman of the campaign, you know. Ever met him?”
“Yes.”
“Well﻿—so long.”

===Chinese===
「这是那三个法案。你明天就进财政委员会；桑德里奇挂个名当主席，实权在你手里。把《童工法》排在头里，我们来运作报纸。关税当然要占去这会期的大半时间。我们把棉花检验法案留到会期最后几天通过——明白吗？我正设法把南方的国会议员拉拢过来。……哦，还有一件事。汤普森说他有点担心那些黑人；说北方黑人票倒戈的说法，未必只是嘴上说说。我们也许得多给他们一点钱，比往常多给几个小职位。去找汤普森谈谈；黑人们挺喜欢你，而他嘛，要知道，就要当新一届竞选的主席了。你见过他吗？」
「见过。」
「好吧——回头见。」

===Original===
“Just a moment,” the statesman stayed the financier.
“Todd just let fall something of a combination against us in Congress﻿—know anything of it?”
“Not definitely; I heard some rumors. Better see if you can run it down. Well, I must hurry﻿—good day.”

===Chinese===
「等一下，」这位政治家叫住了这位金融家。
「托德刚才漏了句口风，说是国会里有人结伙对付我们——你知道点什么吗？」
「说不准；我听到些风声。你最好查查能不能顺藤摸瓜。行了，我得赶紧——日安。」

===Original===
While Bles Alwyn in the outer office was waiting and musing, a lady came in. Out of the corner of his eye he caught the curve of her gown, and as she seated herself beside him, the suggestion of a faint perfume. A vague resentment rose in him. Colored women would look as well as that, he argued, with the clothes and wealth and training. He paused, however, in his thought: he did not want them like the whites﻿—so cold and formal and precise, without heart or marrow. He started up, for the secretary was speaking to him.
“Are you the﻿—er﻿—the man who had a letter to the Senator?”
“Yes, sir.”
“Let me see it. Oh, yes﻿—he will see you in a moment.”

===Chinese===
当布莱斯·阿尔文在外间办公室等着、出神的时候，一位女士走了进来。他用眼角瞥见她衣裙的曲线，待她在身旁坐下，又若有若无地飘来一缕幽香。他心底泛起一阵莫名的愤懑。有色人种的女人，若有那样的衣着、财富和教养，看起来也会一样好看——他这样辩驳。可他的念头又顿住了：他不要她们也像白人那样——那么冷冰冰的、刻板、精确，没有心，也没有髓。他猛地一惊而起，因为秘书正在跟他说话。
「你就是那个——呃——带信来给参议员的那个人？」
「是的，先生。」
「我看看。哦，对了——他一会儿就见你。」

===Original===
Bles was returning the letter to his pocket when he heard a voice almost at his ear.
“I beg your pardon﻿—”
He turned and started. It was the lady next to him, and she was colored! Not extremely colored, but undoubtedly colored, with waving black hair, light brown skin, and the fuller facial curving of the darker world. And yet Bles was surprised, for everything else about her﻿—her voice, her bearing, the set of her gown, her gloves and shoes, the whole impression was﻿—Bles hesitated for a word﻿—well, “white.”

===Chinese===
布莱斯正把信收回口袋，却听见一个声音就在耳边响起。
「对不起——」
他一转身，怔住了。是坐在他旁边的那位女士，而她是有色人种！不是肤色很深的那种，但确凿无疑是有色人种，一头波浪似的黑发，浅棕色的皮肤，带着那个较暗的世界特有的、更为饱满的脸部曲线。然而布莱斯还是吃了一惊，因为她身上其余的一切——她的嗓音、她的仪态、衣裙的剪裁、手套和鞋子，整个印象——布莱斯一时找不出一个词——嗯，「白人」似的。

===Original===
“Yes﻿—yes, ma’am,” he stammered, becoming suddenly conscious that the lady had now a second time asked him if he was acquainted with Senator Smith. “That is, ma’am,”﻿—why was he saying “ma’am,” like a child or a servant?﻿—“I know his sister and have a letter for him.”
“Do you live in Washington?” she inquired.
“No﻿—but I want to. I’ve been trying to get in as a clerk, and I haven’t succeeded yet. That’s what I’m going to see Senator Smith about.”
“Have you had the civil-service examinations?”
“Yes. I made ninety-three in the examination for a treasury clerkship.”
“And no appointment? I see﻿—they are not partial to us there.”
Bles was glad to hear her say “us.”

===Chinese===
「是——是的，女士，」他结结巴巴地说，猛然意识到这位女士这是第二次问他认不认识史密斯参议员了。「是这样的，女士，」——他干嘛要说『女士』，像个孩子或仆人似的？——「我认识他妹妹，还带着一封给他的信。」
「你住在华盛顿吗？」她问。
「不——可我想住。我一直想谋个文书的差事，到现在还没成。这正是我要见史密斯参议员的事。」
「你考过文官考试了吗？」
「考了。财政部文书的考试我考了九十三分。」
「还没被录用？我明白了——那边对我们不太待见。」
布莱斯听她说『我们』，心里一喜。

===Original===
She continued after a pause:
“May I venture to ask a favor of you?”
“Certainly,” he responded.
“My name is Wynn,” lowering her voice slightly and leaning toward him. “There are so many ahead of me and I am in a hurry to get to my school; but I must see the Senator﻿—couldn’t I go in with you? I think I might be of service in this matter of the examination, and then perhaps I’d get a chance to say a word for myself.”
“I’d be very glad to have you come,” said Bles, cordially.
The secretary hesitated a little when the two started in, but Miss Wynn’s air was so quietly assured that he yielded.

===Chinese===
停了停，她又接着说：
「我能冒昧请你帮个忙吗？」
「当然，」他应道。
「我姓怀恩，」她稍稍压低了声音，向他倾了倾身。「我前面排着好多人，可我赶着回学校；然而我非见参议员不可——我能不能跟你一道进去？我想，在考试这件事上我也许能帮上忙，再者，说不定我也能为自己说上一句话的机会。」
「你能来我很高兴，」布莱斯热诚地说。
两人要一起进去时，秘书有些迟疑，可怀恩小姐的神态是那样从容笃定，他便让步了。

===Original===
Senator Smith looked at the tall, straight black man with his smooth skin and frank eyes. And for a second time that morning a vision of his own youth dimmed his eyes. But he spoke coldly:
“Mr. Alwyn, I believe.”
“Yes, sir.”
“And﻿—”
“My friend, Miss Wynn.”
The Senator glanced at Miss Wynn and she bowed demurely. Then he turned to Alwyn.
“Well, Mr. Alwyn, Washington is a bad place to start in the world.”

===Chinese===
史密斯参议员打量着这个高大挺拔的黑人，皮肤光洁，眼神坦率。那天早上，他第二回被自己青春岁月的一抹幻影模糊了双眼。可他冷冷地开口：
「我想，是阿尔文先生吧。」
「是的，先生。」
「还有——」
「我的朋友，怀恩小姐。」
参议员瞥了怀恩小姐一眼，她端庄地一欠身。然后他转向阿尔文。
「那么，阿尔文先生，华盛顿可不是个立身处世的好地方。」

===Original===
Bles looked surprised and incredulous. He could conceive of no finer starting-place, but he said nothing.
“It is a grave,” continued the Senator, “of ambitions and ideals. You would far better go back to Alabama”﻿—pausing and looking at the young man keenly﻿—“but you won’t﻿—you won’t﻿—not yet, at any rate.” And Bles shook his head slowly.
“No﻿—well, what can I do for you?”
“I want work﻿—I’ll do anything.”
“No, you’ll do one thing﻿—be a clerk, and then if you have the right stuff in you you will throw up that job in a year and start again.”
“I’d like at least to try it, sir.”
“Well, I can’t help you much there; that’s in civil-service, and you must take the examination.”
“I have, sir.”

===Chinese===
布莱斯又惊又疑。他想不出还有比这更好的起点，可他没作声。
「它是一座坟，」参议员接着说，「埋葬抱负和理想的坟。你不如回阿拉巴马去」——他顿了顿，锐利地打量着这个年轻人——「可你不会回去的——你不会——至少眼下还不会。」布莱斯缓缓摇了摇头。
「不会——好吧，我能为你做什么？」
「我要工作——我什么都肯干。」
「不，你只肯干一件事——当个文书，然后，要是你骨子里真有那点料，不出一年你就会撂下这差事，从头再来。」
「我至少想试一试，先生。」
「这个嘛，我帮不了你多少忙；那是文官系统的事，你得参加考试。」
「我考过了，先生。」

===Original===
“So? Where, and what mark?”
“In the Treasury Department; I got a mark of ninety-three.”
“What!﻿—and no appointment?” The Senator was incredulous.
“No, sir; not yet.”
Here Miss Wynn interposed.
“You see, Senator,” she said, “civil-service rules are not always impervious to race prejudice.”
The Senator frowned.
“Do you mean to intimate that Mr. Alwyn’s appointment is held up because he is colored?”
“I do.”

===Chinese===
「哦？在哪儿考的，多少分？」
「在财政部；我得了九十三分。」
「什么！——还没录用？」参议员难以置信。
「没有，先生；还没。」
怀恩小姐在这里插了话。
「您瞧，参议员，」她说，「文官制度的规矩，未必总能挡得住种族偏见。」
参议员皱起眉。
「你是想暗示，阿尔文先生的录用被压着，是因为他是有色人种？」
「我是这个意思。」

===Original===
“Well﻿—well!” The Senator rang for a clerk.
“Get me the Treasury on the telephone.”
In a moment the bell rang.
“I want Mr. Cole. Is that you, Mr. Cole? Good morning. Have you a young man named Alwyn on your eligible list? What? Yes?” A pause. “Indeed? Well, why has he no appointment? Of course, I know, he’s a Negro. Yes, I desire it very much﻿—thank you.”
“You’ll get an appointment tomorrow morning,” and the Senator rose. “How is my sister?” he asked absently.

===Chinese===
「这个——这个！」参议员按铃叫一个文书。
「给我接财政部。」
不一会儿铃响了。
「我找科尔先生。是您吗，科尔先生？早安。你们的合格名单上是不是有个叫阿尔文的年轻人？什么？是？」一顿。「当真？那么，他怎么还没被录用？我当然知道，他是个黑人。是的，我很希望此事促成——谢谢。」
「你明天早上就能收到录用通知，」参议员站起身。「我妹妹好吗？」他心不在焉地问。

===Original===
“She was looking worried, but hopeful of the new endowment when I left.” The Senator held out his hand; Bles took it and then remembered.
“Oh, I beg pardon, but Miss Wynn wanted a word on another matter.”
The Senator turned to Miss Wynn.
“I am a schoolteacher, Senator Smith, and like all the rest of us I am deeply interested in the appointment of the new school-board.”
“But you know the district committee attends to those things,” said the Senator hastily. “And then, too, I believe there is talk of abolishing the school-board and concentrating power in the hands of the superintendent.”

===Chinese===
「我走的时候，她看着有些发愁，可对那笔新的捐助还是抱着希望。」参议员伸出手；布莱斯握了，这才想起来。
「哦，失礼了，怀恩小姐还有件事想说。」
参议员转向怀恩小姐。
「我是个教书的，史密斯参议员，和我们所有人一样，我对新教育委员会的任命十分关切。」
「可你知道，这些事归区委会管，」参议员忙说。「再者，我相信还有人议论要撤销教育委员会，把权力集中到督学手里。」

===Original===
“Precisely,” said Miss Wynn. “And I came to tell you, Senator Smith, that the interests which are back of this attack upon the schools are no friends of yours.” Miss Wynn extracted from her reticule a typewritten paper.
He took the paper and read it intently. Then he keenly scrutinized the young woman, and she steadily returned his regard.
“How am I to know this is true?”
“Follow it up and see.”

===Chinese===
「正是，」怀恩小姐说。「而我正是来告诉您，史密斯参议员，这次对学校发难的背后势力，可不是您的朋友。」怀恩小姐从手提袋里抽出一张打字稿。
他接过纸，凝神读着。然后锐利地审视这个年轻女子，她也稳稳地回望着他。
「我怎么知道这是真的？」
「您去查一查便知。」

===Original===
He mused.
“Where did you get these facts?” he asked suddenly.
She smiled.
“It is hardly necessary to say.”
“And yet,” he persisted, “if I were sure of its source I would know my ground better and﻿—my obligation to you would be greater.”
She laughed and glanced toward Alwyn. He had moved out of earshot and was waiting by the window.
“I am a teacher in the M Street High School,” she said, “and we have some intelligent boys there who work their way through.”
“Yes,” said the Senator.
“Some,” continued Miss Wynn, tapping her boot on the carpet, “some﻿—wait on table.”
The Senator slowly put the paper in his pocket.

===Chinese===
他沉吟起来。
「你这材料从哪儿得来的？」他忽然问。
她微微一笑。
「这就不必说了吧。」
「可是，」他坚持，「我要是确知它的来源，便能更清楚地知道自己的立场，而且——我欠你的人情也就更大。」
她笑了笑，朝阿尔文那边瞥了一眼。他已走到听不见的地方，在窗边等着。
「我在 M 街中学教书，」她说，「那里有些聪明的男孩，半工半读念完书。」
「是吗，」参议员说。
「有些，」怀恩小姐接着说，一只脚的靴子在毯子上轻轻点着，「有些——在餐桌上伺候人。」
参议员慢慢地把那张纸揣进了口袋。

===Original===
“And now,” he said, “Miss Wynn, what can I do for you?”
She looked at him.
“If Judge Haynes is reappointed to the school-board I shall probably continue to teach in the M Street High School,” she said slowly.
The Senator made a memorandum and said:
“I shall not forget Miss Wynn﻿—nor her friends.” And he bowed, glancing at Alwyn.

===Chinese===
「那么现在，」他说，「怀恩小姐，我能为你做什么？」
她看着他。
「倘若海恩斯法官能再度受命进教育委员会，那我多半还能继续在 M 街中学教书，」她慢慢地说。
参议员记下一笔，说：
「我不会忘记怀恩小姐——也不会忘记她的朋友们。」他欠了欠身，瞥了阿尔文一眼。

===Original===
The woman contemplated Bles in momentary perplexity, then bowing in turn, left. Bles followed, debating just what he ought to say, how far he might venture to accompany her, what﻿—but she easily settled it all.
“I thank you﻿—goodbye,” she said briefly at the door, and was gone. Bles did not know whether to feel relieved or provoked, or disappointed, and by way of compromise felt something of all three.

===Chinese===
这女子一时有些为难地打量着布莱斯，随即也欠身回礼，走了。布莱斯跟在后面，心里盘算着自己到底该说什么、能冒昧地送她多远、要——可她三言两语便把这些都了结了。
「我谢谢你——再会，」她在门口简短地说，便不见了。布莱斯不知该感到如释重负，还是被冒犯，还是失望，折中的结果是，这三种滋味他都尝到了一点。

===Original===
The next morning he received notice of his appointment to a clerkship in the Treasury Department, at a salary of nine hundred dollars. The sum seemed fabulous and he was in the seventh heaven. For many days the consciousness of wealth, the new duties, the street scenes, and the city life kept him more than busy. He planned to study, and arranged with a professor at Howard University to guide him. He bought an armfully of books and a desk, and plunged desperately to work.

===Chinese===
第二天一早，他收到通知，被录用为财政部文书，年薪九百元。这笔数目简直像天方夜谭，他乐上了第七重天。一连许多天，那份有钱的意识、新的差事、街头的景象和都市生活，让他忙得不可开交。他计划读书，和霍华德大学的一位教授说好请他指导。他买了一抱书和一张书桌，一头扎进书里苦干起来。

===Original===
Gradually as he became used to the office routine, and in the hours when he was weary of study, he began to find time hanging a little heavily on his hands; indeed﻿—although he would not acknowledge it﻿—he was getting lonesome, homesick, amid the myriad men of a busy city. He argued to himself that this was absurd, and yet he knew that he was longing for human companionship. When he looked about him for fellowship he found himself in a strange dilemma: those black folk in whom he recognized the old sweet-tempered Negro traits, had also looser, uglier manners than he was accustomed to, from which he shrank. The upper classes of Negroes, on the other hand, he still observed from afar; they were strangers not only in acquaintance but because of a curious coldness and aloofness that made them cease to seem his own kind; they seemed almost at times like black white people﻿—strangers in way and thought.

===Chinese===
渐渐地，等他习惯了公事的例行流程，又在读得疲倦的时辰里，他开始觉得时间有点沉甸甸地压在手上；说真的——尽管他不愿承认——在这熙熙攘攘的城市、万千人海之中，他正变得寂寞、思乡。他对自己辩白说这太荒唐，可他心里明白，自己是在渴望人情的往来。当他四下里寻觅情谊时，却发现自己陷在一个奇怪的困境里：那些他在其中认出往日黑人那种温良性情的黑人，也带着比他习见的更松垮、更难看的举止，叫他望而却步。而另一边，那些黑人上层，他还只能远远地打量；他们不仅在认识上是陌生人，更因为一种古怪的冷淡和疏离，使他们不再像是他的同类；有时候他们简直像是黑皮肤的白人——在行事和心思上都是陌生人。

===Original===
He tried to shake off this feeling but it clung, and at last in sheer desperation, he promised to go out of a night with a fellow clerk who rather boasted of the “people” he knew. He was soon tired of the strange company, and had turned to go home, when he met a newcomer in the doorway.
“Why, hello, Sam! Sam Stillings!” he exclaimed delightedly, and was soon grasping the hand of a slim, well-dressed man of perhaps thirty, with yellow face, curling hair, and shifting eyes.
“Well, of all things, Bles﻿—er﻿—ah﻿—Mr. Alwyn! Thought you were hoeing cotton.”

===Chinese===
他设法甩掉这种感觉，可它粘着不走，末了他纯然出于绝望，答应某个晚上跟一个同僚文书出去走走——那人颇以自己认识的『人物』自夸。他很快就厌倦了这伙陌生人，正转身要回家，却在门口撞见一个新到的人。
「哎呀，你好，萨姆！萨姆·斯蒂林斯！」他欢喜地叫起来，随即握住了一个大约三十岁、身材瘦削、衣着讲究的男人的手，那人一张黄皮肤的脸，鬈发，一双游移不定的眼睛。
「嘿，真没想到，布莱斯——呃——啊——阿尔文先生！我还以为你在棉花地里锄草呢。」

===Original===
Bles laughed and continued shaking his head. He was foolishly glad to see the former Cresswell butler, whom he had known but slightly. His face brought back unuttered things that made his heart beat faster and a yearning surge within him.
“I thought you went to Chicago,” cried Bles.
“I did, but goin’ into politics﻿—having entered the political field, I came here. And you graduated, I suppose, and all that?”
“No,” Bless admitted a little sadly, as he told of his coming north, and of Senator Smith’s influence. “But﻿—but how are﻿—all?”

===Chinese===
布莱斯笑着，不住地摇着头。见到这个从前的克雷斯韦尔家的男仆，他傻乎乎地高兴——从前他也只是与他略有往来。这张脸带回了那些没说出口的事，叫他的心怦怦跳得更快，一阵渴望在胸中翻涌。
「我以为你去了芝加哥，」布莱斯叫道。
「我是去了，可我要从政——既已投身政界，我就来了这儿。你嘛，毕了业，我想，如此这般？」
「没有，」布莱斯略带伤感地承认，一边讲了自己北上的事，还有史密斯参议员的关照。「可是——可是大家都——好吧？」

===Original===
Abruptly Sam hooked his arm into Alwyn’s and pulled him with him down the street. Stillings was a type. Up from servility and menial service he was struggling to climb to money and power. He was shrewd, willing to stoop to anything in order to win. The very slights and humiliations of prejudice he turned to his advantage. When he learned all the particulars of Alwyn’s visit to Senator Smith and his cordial reception he judged it best to keep in touch with this young man, and he forthwith invited Bles to accompany him the next night to the Fifteenth Street Presbyterian Church.
“You’ll find the best people there,” he said; “the aristocracy. The Treble Clef gives a concert, and everybody that’s anybody will be there.”

===Chinese===
萨姆猛地勾住阿尔文的胳膊，拉着他顺街走去。斯蒂林斯是某一种典型。从奴性与仆役的差使里往上爬，他正挣扎着攀向金钱和权力。他精明，为了赢肯屈尊做任何事。就连偏见带来的那些轻慢和屈辱，他也拿来为己所用。等他打听到阿尔文拜访史密斯参议事的种种细节，以及受到的殷勤接待，他便断定最好跟这个年轻人保持来往，于是当即邀请布莱斯明晚陪他去第十五街长老会教堂。
「你在那儿能见到最好的人家，」他说；「上流社会。高音谱号俱乐部办音乐会，有头有脸的人都会到。」

===Original===
They met again the following evening and proceeded to the church. It was a simple but pleasant auditorium, nearly filled with well-dressed people. During the programme Bles applauded vociferously every number that pleased him, which is to say, every one﻿—and stamped his feet, until he realized that he was attracting considerable attention to himself. Then the entertainment straightway lost all its charm; he grew painfully embarrassed, and for the remainder of the evening was awkwardly self-conscious. When all was over, the audience rose leisurely and stood in little knots and eddies, laughing and talking; many moved forward to say a word to the singers and players, Stillings stepped aside to a group of men, and Bles was left miserably alone. A man came to him, a white-faced man, with slightly curling close gray hair, and high-bred ascetic countenance.
“You are a stranger?” he asked pleasantly, and Bles liked him.
“Yes, sir,” he answered, and they fell to talking. He discovered that this was the pastor of the church.

===Chinese===
第二天傍晚他们又见了面，一同去了教堂。那是个朴素却宜人的礼堂，几乎坐满了衣着讲究的人。节目进行间，布莱斯每听到一个他喜欢的节目便大声鼓掌——也就是说，每一个都喜欢——还跺脚，直到他意识到自己引起了相当多的注目。这一来，整场演出登时失了全部魅力；他难堪得要命，往后的整晚都笨拙地忸怩不安。一切结束后，听众们慢悠悠地起身，三五成群地站着，有说有笑；许多人走上前去跟演唱者、演奏者攀谈几句，斯蒂林斯走到一群男人那边去了，布莱斯被凄凄地独自撇下。一个人朝他走来，一个白面孔的男人，一头紧而微鬈的灰发，一副教养极高的、苦行者般的面容。
「你是生面孔？」他和气地问，布莱斯挺喜欢他。
「是的，先生，」他答，两人攀谈起来。他这才得知，此人便是这教堂的牧师。

===Original===
“Do you know no one in town?”
“One or two of my fellow clerks and Mr. Stillings. Oh, yes, I’ve met Miss Wynn.”
“Why, here is Miss Wynn now.”
Bles turned. She was right behind him, the centre of a group. She turned, slowly, and smiled.
“Oh!” she uttered twice, but with difference cadence. Then something like amusement lurked a moment in her eye, and she quietly presented Bles to her friends, while Stillings hovered unnoticed in the offing:
“Miss Jones﻿—Mr. Alwyn of﻿—” she paused a second﻿—“Alabama. Miss Taylor﻿—Mr. Alwyn﻿—and,” with a backward curving of her neck, “Mr. Teerswell,” and so on. Mr. Teerswell was handsome and indolent, with indecision in his face and a cynical voice. In a moment Bles felt the subtle antagonism of the group. He was an intruder. Mr. Teerswell nodded easily and turned away, continuing his conversation with the ladies.

===Chinese===
「你在城里一个熟人也没有？」
「一两个同僚文书，还有斯蒂林斯先生。哦，对了，我见过怀恩小姐。」
「哟，怀恩小姐这不就在这儿。」
布莱斯转过身。她就在他正后方，是一群人的中心。她慢慢地转过身，笑了笑。
「哦！」她说了两声，可两次的语调不同。随即一抹近乎玩味的神情在她眼底潜藏了一瞬，她便不动声色地把布莱斯介绍给她的朋友们，而斯蒂林斯在不远处无人留意地徘徊着：
「琼斯小姐——这位是阿尔文先生，来自——」她顿了一顿——「阿拉巴马。泰勒小姐——阿尔文先生——还有，」她向后微微一扬脖，「蒂尔斯韦尔先生，」如此这般。蒂尔斯韦尔先生生得俊朗而慵懒，脸上有种优柔寡断，嗓音里带着玩世不恭。不一会儿，布莱斯便感到这一圈人里微妙的敌意。他是个闯入者。蒂尔斯韦尔先生随意地点了点头，转过身去，继续和小姐太太们攀谈。

===Original===
But Miss Wynn was perverse and interrupted. “I saw you enjoyed the concert, Mr. Alwyn,” she said, and one of the young ladies rippled audibly. Bles darkened painfully, realizing that these people must have been just behind him. But he answered frankly:
“Yes, I did immensely﻿—I hope I didn’t disturb you; you see, I’m not used to hearing such singing.”
Mr. Teerswell, compelled to listen, laughed drily.
“Plantation melodies, I suppose, are more your specialty,” he said with a slight cadence.
“Yes,” said Bles simply. A slight pause ensued.

===Chinese===
可怀恩小姐偏偏别扭，插了进来。「我看你听音乐会听得很尽兴，阿尔文先生，」她说，其中一位年轻小姐清楚地扑哧了一声。布莱斯痛苦地涨红了脸，意识到这些人刚才一定就在他身后。可他坦率地答：
「是的，我非常尽兴——希望我没打扰到你们；要知道，这样的演唱我不大常听。」
蒂尔斯韦尔先生被迫听着，干笑了一声。
「我想，种植园小调才更是你的专长吧，」他略带一种抑扬地说。
「是的，」布莱斯简简单单地说。略微一顿。

===Original===
Then came the surprise of the evening for Bles Alwyn. Even his inexperienced eye could discern that Miss Wynn was very popular, and that most of the men were rivals for her attentions.
“Mr. Alwyn,” she said graciously, rising. “I’m going to trouble you to see me to my door; it’s only a block. Good night, all!” she called, but she bowed to Mr. Teerswell.

===Chinese===
接着，对布莱斯·阿尔文来说，这一晚的意外来了。就连他那双没阅历的眼睛也看得出，怀恩小姐极受欢迎，大多数男人都在争相献殷勤。
「阿尔文先生，」她和颜悦色地起身说。「要麻烦你送我到家门口了；就一个街区的事。各位，晚安！」她喊道，可她是冲蒂尔斯韦尔先生欠的身。

===Original===
Miss Wynn placed her hand lightly on Bles’s arm, and for a moment he paused. A thrill ran through him as he felt again the weight of a little hand and saw beside him the dark beautiful eyes of a girl. He felt again the warm quiver of her body. Then he awoke to the lighted church and the moving, well-dressed throng. The hand on his arm was not so small; but it was well-gloved, and somehow the fancy struck him that it was a cold hand and not always sympathetic in its touch.

===Chinese===
怀恩小姐轻轻把手搭在布莱斯臂上，他顿了一顿。一阵战栗流过他全身——他又一次感到一只小手的分量，看见身旁一双姑娘的、幽黑美丽的眼睛。他又感到了她身体温暖的颤动。随即他清醒过来，看见灯火通明的教堂、涌动的衣冠楚楚的人潮。搭在他臂上的那只手并不那么小；可它戴着手套，而且不知怎的，他忽生一种感觉：这是一只冷的手，触着的时候也不总是带着同情的。

## XXIII The Training of Zora / XXIII 佐拉的熏陶

===Original===
“I did not know the world was so large,” remarked Zora as she and Mrs. Vanderpool flew east and northward on the New York-New Orleans limited. For a long time the girl had given herself up to the sheer delight of motion. Gazing from the window, she compared the lands she passed with the lands she knew: noting the formation of the cotton; the kind and growth of the trees; the state of the roads. Then the comparisons became infinite, endless; the world stretched on and on until it seemed mere distance, and she suddenly realized how vast a thing it was and spoke.

===Chinese===
「我不知道这世界竟这么大，」佐拉说——她和范德普尔太太正坐在纽约—新奥尔兰快车上，向东、向北飞驰。有很长一段时间，姑娘都沉浸在运动本身纯粹的快乐里。她从车窗向外望，把她经过的那些土地与她熟悉的土地相比：留意棉花的种法、树木的种类和长势、道路的样子。后来这些比较变得无穷无尽、没完没了；世界一路伸展、一路伸展，直到它似乎只剩下距离本身，她这才猛然意识到这是何等浩大的一样东西，于是开了口。

===Original===
Mrs. Vanderpool was amused. “It’s much smaller than one would think,” she responded.
When they came to Atlanta Zora stared and wrinkled her brows. It was her first large city. The other towns were replicas of Toomsville; strange in number, not in kind; but this was different, and she could not understand it. It seemed senseless and unreasonable, and yet so strangely so that she was at a loss to ask questions. She was very solemn as they rode on and night came down with dreams.

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太觉得有趣。「它比人想象的要小得多，」她答道。
到了亚特兰大，佐拉瞪大了眼，皱起了眉。这是她见过的头一座大城市。别的镇子不过是图姆斯维尔的翻版；多在数量上稀奇，不在种类上稀奇；可这一座不同，她闹不明白。它显得既无道理又不合理，可偏偏又是一种奇异的『无道理』，弄得她连该怎样发问都无从下手。车子一路前行，夜带着梦降下来，她一路都很肃穆。

===Original===
She awoke in Washington to new fairylands and wonders; the endless going and coming of men; great piles that challenged heaven, and homes crowded on homes till one could not believe that they were full of living things. They rolled by Baltimore and Philadelphia, and she talked of everyday matters: of the sky which alone stood steadfast amid whirling change; of bits of empty earth that shook themselves here and there loose from their burden of men, and lay naked in the cold shining sunlight.

===Chinese===
她在华盛顿醒来，眼前是新的仙境和奇景；人来人往，川流不息；一座座高楼敢与天比高，一户挨一户的住家挤成一团，挤得人不敢相信里面竟住满了活物。火车驶过巴尔的摩和费城，她说起些日常的事：说起那片天——唯有它在旋转的变幻中岿然不动；说起这儿那儿有零星的空地，抖落了身上人的重负，赤裸裸地躺在冷冽闪亮的阳光里。

===Original===
All the while the greater questions were beating and curling and building themselves back in her brain, and above all she was wondering why no one had told her before of all this mighty world. Mrs. Vanderpool, to whom it seemed too familiar for comment, had said no word; or, if she had spoken, Zora’s ears had not been tuned to understand; and as they flew toward the towering ramparts of New York, she sat up big with the terror of a new thought: suppose this world were full yet of things she did not know nor dream of? How could she find out? She must know.

===Chinese===
这一路上，那些更大的问题始终在她脑子里敲打着、翻卷着、垒砌起来，而压倒一切的是，她纳罕：从前怎么就没有一个人告诉她，竟有这样广大的一个世界。范德普尔太太——这世界在她眼里熟悉得不值一提——一字未提；或者，她说过，只是佐拉的耳朵那时还没调到能听懂的弦上；而正当她们朝纽约那一座座高耸的壁垒飞驰而去，她坐直了身子，被一个新念头的恐惧撑得满满的：倘若这个世界，竟还装着她所不知道、所不敢梦的东西呢？她怎么能弄明白？她非知道不可。

===Original===
When finally they were settled in New York and sat high up on the Fifth Avenue front of the hotel, gradually the inarticulate questioning found words, albeit strange ones.
“It reminds me of the swamp,” she said.
Mrs. Vanderpool, just returned from a shopping tour, burst into laughter.
“It is﻿—but I marvel at your penetration.”

===Chinese===
等她们终于在纽约安顿下来，高高坐在旅馆临第五大道的那一面，那原本说不出口的诘问，渐渐地找到了话——尽管是些怪话。
「它叫我想起沼泽，」她说。
范德普尔太太刚购物回来，闻言放声大笑。
「倒也是——可我真佩服你的眼力。」

===Original===
“I mean, it is moving﻿—always moving.”
“The swamp seemed to me unearthly still.”
“Yes﻿—yes,” cried Zora, eagerly, brushing back the rumpled hair; “and so did the city, at first, to me.”
“Still! New York?”
“Yes. You see, I saw the buildings and forgot the men; and the buildings were so tall and silent against Heaven. And then I came to see the people, and suddenly I knew the city was like the swamp, always restless and changing.”

===Chinese===
「我是说，它总在动——总在动。」
「沼泽在我看可是超乎尘世的静。」
「对——对，」佐拉急切地喊，把蓬乱的头发往后一撩；「这城市起初在我眼里也是这样。」
「静！纽约？」
