「对。你瞧，我先看见的是楼，忘了人；那些楼那样高，衬着天，那样静。后来我才开始看见人，一下子就明白了，这城市也像沼泽，总不安静，总在变。」

===Original===
“And more beautiful?” suggested Mrs. Vanderpool, slipping her arms into her lounging-robe.
“Oh, no; not nearly so beautiful. And yet﻿—more interesting.” Then with a puzzled look: “I wonder why?”
“Perhaps because it’s people and not things.”
“It’s people in the swamp,” asserted Zora, dreamily, smoothing out the pillows of the couch, “‘little people,’ I call them. The difference is, I think, that there I know how the story will come out; everything is changing, but I know how and why and from what and to what. Now here, *every*thing seems to be happening; but what is it that is happening?”

===Chinese===
「也更好看吧？」范德普尔太太提议道，一边把胳膊伸进晨袍。
「哦，不；远不及那好看。然而——更有意思。」随即一脸困惑：「这是为什么呢？」
「也许因为这是人，不是东西。」
「沼泽里也是人，」佐拉梦一般地断言，一边把长沙发上的靠枕拍松拍平，「我把他们叫作『小人儿』。区别在于，我想，在那儿我知道故事会怎样收场；一切都在变，可我知道怎么变、为什么变、从什么变来、变到什么去。可在这儿，*每*一件事似乎都在发生；可到底发生的是什么？」

===Original===
“You must know what has happened, to know what may happen,” said Mrs. Vanderpool.
“But how can I know?”
“I’ll get you some books tomorrow.”
“I’d like to know what it means,” wistfully.
“It is meaningless.” The woman’s cynicism was lost upon Zora, of course, but it possessed the salutary effect of stimulating the girl’s thoughts, encouraging her to discover for herself.
“I think not; so much must mean something,” she protested.

===Chinese===
「你得先知道已经发生过什么，才能知道可能发生什么，」范德普尔太太说。
「可我怎么能知道呢？」
「我明天给你弄些书来。」
「我想知道这是什么意思，」她热切地说。
「它没什么意思。」这女人的愤世之论，佐拉当然没听进去，可它倒有一种好效果——刺激了姑娘的思绪，鼓励她自己去探求。
「我不信；这么多，总该有点意思的，」她争辩。

===Original===
Zora gathered up the clothes and things and shaded the windows, glancing the while down on the street.
“Everybody is going, going,” she murmured. “I wonder where. Don’t they ever get there?”
“Few arrive,” said Mrs. Vanderpool. Zora softly bent and passed her cool soft hand over her forehead.
“Then why do they go?”
“The zest of the search, perhaps.”
“No,” said Zora as she noiselessly left the room and closed the door; “no, they are searching for something they have lost. Perhaps they, too, are searching for the Way,” and the tears blinded her eyes.

===Chinese===
佐拉收拾起衣物什物，放下窗帘遮光，一面低头瞥着下面的街。
「人人都在走啊、走啊，」她低语。「也不知道去哪儿。他们就从来到不了吗？」
「到的人很少，」范德普尔太太说。佐拉轻轻俯下身，用她凉爽柔软的手拂过自己的额头。
「那他们为什么还要走？」
「也许是寻找的乐趣吧。」
「不，」佐拉一边悄无声息地走出房间、关上门，一边说；「不，他们是在找一样他们丢了的东西。说不定，他们也在找那条路呢，」想到这里，泪水模糊了她的眼睛。

===Original===
Mrs. Vanderpool lay in the quiet darkened room with a puzzled smile on her lips. A month ago she had not dreamed that human interest in anybody would take so strong a hold upon her as her liking for Zora had done. She was a woman of unusual personal charm, but her own interest and affections were seldom stirred. Had she been compelled to earn a living she would have made a successful teacher or manipulator of men. As it was, she viewed the human scene with detached and cynical interest. She had no children, few near relations, a husband who went his way and still was a gentleman.

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太躺在安静的、拉上窗帘的昏暗房间里，唇边带着一丝困惑的笑。一个月前，她做梦也没想到，对什么人的『人之兴趣』竟能像她对佐拉的喜欢这样，牢牢地攫住她。她是个魅力出众的女人，可她自己的兴致和情意很少被触动。倘若她不得不自食其力，她本可以做一个成功的教师，或者一个善弄人心的高手。如今这般，她只用一种超然的、玩世不恭的兴趣打量着人世。她没有孩子，没什么近亲，一个丈夫走自己的路，倒仍是个绅士。

===Original===
Essentially Mrs. Vanderpool was unmoral. She held the code of her social set with sportsmanlike honor; but even beyond this she stooped to no intrigue, because none interested her. She had all the elements of power save the motive for doing anything in particular. For the first time, perhaps, Zora gave her life a peculiar human interest. She did not love the girl, but she was intensely interested in her; some of the interest was selfish, for Zora was going to be a perfect maid. The girl’s language came to be more and more like Mrs. Vanderpool’s; her dress and taste in adornment had been Mrs. Vanderpool’s first care, and it led to a curious training in art and sense of beauty until the lady now and then found herself learner before the quick suggestiveness of Zora’s mind.

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太骨子里是无所谓道德的。她以运动员般的荣誉感信守她那个社交圈的规矩；可即便超出这一点，她也不屑于搞什么阴谋，因为没有一样能叫她上心。她具备权势的一切要素，唯独没有为某一桩事去运用的动机。也许是头一回，佐拉给了她的生命一种特别的人之兴趣。她并不爱这姑娘，可对她兴趣极浓；这兴趣有几分是自私的，因为佐拉将要成为一个无可挑剔的女仆。姑娘的言谈越来越像范德普尔太太；她的衣着和修饰的品味是范德普尔太太头一桩操心的，这引出了一场奇特的艺术与美感的熏陶，直到这位太太时不时发现，在佐拉那敏于触类旁通的心智跟前，自己反倒成了学生。

===Original===
When Mrs. Harry Cresswell called a month or so later the talk naturally included mention of Zora. Mary was happy and vivacious, and noted the girl’s rapid development.
“I wonder what I shall make out of her?” queried Mrs. Vanderpool. “Do you know, I believe I could mould her into a lady if she were not black.”
Mary Cresswell laughed. “With that hair?”

===Chinese===
大约一个月后，哈里·克雷斯韦尔太太来访，话题自然也提到了佐拉。玛丽快活而健谈，留意到姑娘飞速的长进。
「我不知道能把她调教成什么样呢？」范德普尔太太问道。「你信不信，要不是她皮肤黑，我能把她塑造成一位淑女。」
玛丽·克雷斯韦尔笑了。「顶着那一头头发？」

===Original===
“It has artistic possibilities. You should have seen my hairdresser’s face when I told her to do it up. Her face and Zora’s were a pantomime for the gods. Yet it was done. It lay in some great twisted cloud and in that black net gown of mine Zora was simply magnificent. Her form is perfect, her height is regal, her skin is satin, and my jewels found a resting place at last. Jewels, you know, dear, were never meant for white folk. I was tempted to take her to the box at the opera and let New York break its impudent neck.”

===Chinese===
「它有艺术上的潜力。你真该看看我那理发师听我说要给她盘起来时的脸色。她的脸和佐拉的脸，简直是一出给神仙看的哑剧。可到底还是盘成了。那头发盘成一大朵扭卷的云，佐拉穿着我那件黑纱长裙，简直光辉夺目。她的身段完美，个子有王者之气，皮肤像缎子，而我的珠宝总算找到了安身之所。亲爱的，要知道，珠宝这东西，本就不是为白人备的。我都动了心，想带她去剧院包厢，让纽约把那不知天高地厚的脖子扭断去。」

===Original===
Mary was shocked.
“But, Mrs. Vanderpool,” she protested, “is it right? Is it fair? Why should you spoil this black girl and put impossible ideas into her head? You can make her a perfect maid, but she can never be much more in America.”
“She is a perfect maid now; that’s the miracle of it﻿—she’s that deft and quick and quiet and thoughtful! The hotel employees think her perfect; my friends rave﻿—really, I’m the most blessed of women. But do you know I like the girl? I﻿—well, I think of her future.”

===Chinese===
玛丽大吃一惊。
「可是，范德普尔太太，」她抗议，「这样对吗？这公道吗？你何苦娇纵这个黑人姑娘，把些不可能的念头塞进她脑袋？你能把她调教成一个完美的女仆，可在美国，她至多也就这样了。」
「她现在就是个完美的女仆；奇就奇在这儿——她是那样的灵巧、麻利、安静、周到！旅馆的伙计都觉得她十全十美；我的朋友们赞不绝口——真的，我是天底下最有福的女人。可你知道吗，我喜欢这姑娘？我——嗯，我为她的将来着想。」

===Original===
“It’s wrong to treat her as you do. You make her an equal. Her room is one of the best and filled with books and bric-a-brac. She sometimes eats with you﻿—is your companion, in fact.”
“What of it? She loves to read, and I guide her while she keeps me up on the latest stuff. She can talk much better than many of my friends and then she piques my curiosity: she’s a sort of intellectual sauce that stirs my rapidly failing mental appetite. I think that as soon as I can make up my mind to spare her, I’ll take her to France and marry her off in the colonies.”

===Chinese===
「你这样待她是不对的。你把她当平等的人。她的房间是这里最好的几间之一，堆满了书和小摆设。她有时还跟你一起吃饭——实际上，是你的伴侣。」
「那又怎样？她爱读书，我给她指点，她反倒让我跟得上最新出版的那些。她说话比我的许多朋友得体得多，再者她勾起我的好奇心：她是一种心智上的调味汁，搅动着我那迅速衰退的脑力胃口。我想，等我能狠下心放她走，就带她去法国，在殖民地把她说个婆家。」

===Original===
“Well, that’s possible; but one doesn’t easily give up good servants. By the way, I learn from Miss Smith that the boy, Bles Alwyn, in whom Zora was so interested, is a clerk in the Treasury Department at Washington.”
“Indeed! I’m going to Washington this winter; I’ll look him over and see if he’s worth Zora﻿—which I greatly doubt.”
Mrs. Cresswell pursed her lips and changed the subject.
“Have you seen the Easterlys?”

===Chinese===
「嗯，这倒有可能；不过好仆人可不容易撒手。对了，我从史密斯小姐那儿听说，那个佐拉一度那么上心的男孩，布莱斯·阿尔文，如今是华盛顿财政部的文书。」
「当真！我今年冬天要去华盛顿；我要打量打量他，看他配不配得上佐拉——我是大大地怀疑的。」
克雷斯韦尔太太抿了抿嘴，转了话题。
「你见过伊斯特利家的人吗？」

===Original===
“The ladies left their cards﻿—they are quite impossible. Mr. Easterly calls this afternoon. I can’t imagine why, but he asked for an appointment. Will you go South with Mr. Cresswell? I’m glad to hear he’s entering politics.”
“No, I shall do some early house hunting in Washington,” said Mrs. Cresswell, rising as Mr. Easterly was announced.

===Chinese===
「几位太太留下了名片——她们实在叫人没法忍受。伊斯特利先生今天下午来访。我想不出所为何事，是他主动求见的。你要跟克雷斯韦尔先生南下吗？听说他要从政，我很高兴。」
「不，我要去华盛顿早些找房子，」克雷斯韦尔太太说着站起身，这时伊斯特利先生被通报进来。

===Original===
Mr. Easterly was not at home in Mrs. Vanderpool’s presence. She spoke a language different from his, and she had shown a disconcerting way, in the few times when he had spoken with her, of letting the weight of the conversation rest on him. He felt very distinctly that Mrs. Vanderpool was not particularly desirous of his company, nor that of his family. Nevertheless, he needed Mrs. Vanderpool’s influence just now, and he was willing to pay considerable for it. Once under obligation to him her services would be very valuable. He was glad to find Mrs. Cresswell there. It showed that the Cresswells were still intimate, and the Cresswells were bound to him and his interests by strong ties. He bowed as Mrs. Cresswell left, and then did not beat around the bush because, in this case, he did not know how.

===Chinese===
伊斯特利先生在范德普尔太太跟前并不自在。她说的是与他不同的另一套话，而且在他与她交谈的有限几次里，她表现出一种令人窘迫的做派——把话头的分量整个儿搁在他身上。他分明感到，范德普尔太太并不怎么盼望有他、或有他一家的陪伴。尽管如此，他此刻正需要范德普尔太太的影响力，并为此肯付不小的代价。一旦她欠下了他人情，她的用处可就大了。他很高兴在这里见到克雷斯韦尔太太。这表明克雷斯韦尔家仍是亲密的，而克雷斯韦尔家与他、与他的利益，有着牢固的纽带。克雷斯韦尔太太离去时他欠了欠身，然后也不再旁敲侧击——因为这一回，他根本不知该如何旁敲侧击。

===Original===
“Mrs. Vanderpool, I need your aid.”
Mrs. Vanderpool smiled politely, and murmured something.
“We are, you know, in the midst of a rather warm presidential campaign,” continued Mr. Easterly.
“Yes?” with polite interest.

===Chinese===
「范德普尔太太，我需要您的帮忙。」
范德普尔太太客气地笑笑，含混地应了一声。
「您知道，我们眼下正处在一场相当激烈的总统大选之中，」伊斯特利先生接着说。
「哦？」带着客气的兴趣。

===Original===
“We are going to win easily, but our majority in Congress for certain matters will depend on the attitude of Southerners and you usually spend the winters in Washington. If, now, you could drop a word here and there﻿—”
“But why should I?” asked Mrs. Vanderpool.
“Mrs. Vanderpool, to be frank, I know some excellent investments that your influence in this line would help. I take it you’re not so rich but that﻿—”
Mrs. Vanderpool smiled faintly.
“Really, Mr. Easterly, I know little about such matters and care less. I have food and clothes. Why worry with more?”

===Chinese===
「我们赢起来会很容易，可某些事上我们在国会的多数，要取决于南方人的态度，而您通常都在华盛顿过冬。倘若，您能在这儿那儿递一句话——」
「可我为什么要呢？」范德普尔太太问。
「范德普尔太太，坦白说，我知道几桩极好的投资，您在这方面的影响力会派上用场。我想，您还不至于阔到——」
范德普尔太太淡淡一笑。
「说真的，伊斯特利先生，这些事我懂得少，也懒得关心。我有吃有穿。何苦再操心更多的？」

===Original===
Mr. Easterly half expected this and he determined to deliver his last shot on the run. He arose with a disappointed air.
“Of course, Mrs. Vanderpool, I see how it is: you have plenty and one can’t expect your services or influence for nothing. It had occurred to me that your husband might like something political; but I presume not.”
“Something political?”
“Yes. You see, it’s barely possible, for instance, that there will be a change in the French ambassadorship. The present ambassador is old and﻿—well, I don’t know, but as I say, it’s possible. Of course though, that may not appeal to you, and I can only beg your good offices in charity if﻿—if you see your way to help us. Well, I must be going.”

===Chinese===
伊斯特利先生早料到一半，便决定临走时把这最后一枪放出去。他露出一副失望的神气站起身。
「当然，范德普尔太太，我明白了：您衣食丰足，总不能指望您白白出力、施加影响。我倒是想过，令夫也许会喜欢点政治上的东西；不过我想不会。」
「政治上的东西？」
「是的。您瞧，比方说，法国大使这个位子，是有可能换人的。现任大使年事已高，而且——嗯，我也不好说，但正如我方才所言，是有可能的。当然喽，这也许打动不了您，我只能恳请您出于慈悲施以援手——倘若您肯帮我们这个忙。好吧，我得走了。」

===Original===
“What is﻿—I thought the President appointed ambassadors.”
“To be sure, but we appoint Presidents,” laughed Mr. Easterly. “Good day. I shall hope to see you in Washington.”
“Good day,” Mrs. Vanderpool returned absently.

===Chinese===
「这是怎么——我以为大使是总统任命的。」
「那是自然，可总统是我们任命的，」伊斯特利先生笑道。「日安。盼望在华盛顿再见到您。」
「日安，」范德普尔太太心不在焉地回了一句。

===Original===
After he had gone she walked slowly to Zora’s room and opened the door. For a long time she stood quietly looking in. Zora was curled in a chair with a book. She was in dreamland; in a world of books builded thoughtfully for her by Mrs. Vanderpool, and before that by Miss Smith. Her work took but little of her time and left hours for reading and thinking. In that thought-life, more and more her real living centred.

===Chinese===
他走后，她慢慢踱到佐拉房间，推开门。她静静地往里看了很久。佐拉蜷在椅子上，捧着一本书。她在梦境里；在范德普尔太太——以及更早在史密斯小姐——为她用心构筑的一个书的世界里。她的差事只占去她很少的时间，留下大把的时辰供她读书和思考。在那思想的生活里，她真实的生命越来越凝聚。

===Original===
Hour after hour, day after day, she lay buried, deaf and dumb to all else. Her heart cried, up on the World’s four corners of the Way, and to it came the Vision Splendid. She gossiped with old Herodotus across the earth to the black and blameless Ethiopians; she saw the sculptured glories of Phidias marbled amid the splendor of the swamp; she listened to Demosthenes and walked the Appian Way with Cornelia﻿—while all New York streamed beneath her window.

===Chinese===
一时又一时，一日又一日，她埋首其中，对其他一切又聋又哑。她的心喊出了声，立在这世界之『路』的四角之上，于是那辉煌的异象便降临了。她与年迈的希罗多德隔着大地闲谈，一直谈到大地上那些黑色的、清白无辜的埃塞俄比亚人；她看见菲狄亚斯雕刻的荣光，如大理石般矗立在沼泽的辉煌之中；她聆听德摩斯梯尼，与科涅莉娅一同走过阿庇亚大道——而整个纽约，都在她窗下奔流。

===Original===
She saw the drunken Goths reel upon Rome and heard the careless Negroes yodle as they galloped to Toomsville. Paris, she knew﻿—wonderful, haunting Paris: the Paris of Clovis, and St. Louis; of Louis the Great, and Napoleon III; of Balzac, and her own Dumas. She tasted the mud and comfort of thick old London, and the while wept with Jeremiah and sang with Deborah, Semiramis, and Atala. Mary of Scotland and Joan of Arc held her dark hands in theirs, and Kings lifted up their sceptres.

===Chinese===
她看见醉醺醺的哥特人踉跄着扑向罗马，也听见漫不经心的黑人们策马奔向图姆斯维尔时唱着约德尔调。巴黎，她是认得的——那奇妙的、萦怀不去的巴黎：克洛维的巴黎，圣路易的巴黎；路易大帝的巴黎，拿破仑三世的巴黎；巴尔扎克的巴黎，还有她自己心爱的仲马的巴黎。她尝到了古老浓雾的伦敦的泥泞与惬意，与此同时，又与耶利米一同哭泣，与底波拉、与塞米拉米斯、与阿塔拉一同歌唱。苏格兰的玛丽和贞德握住她那双黝黑的手，列王举起了他们的权杖。

===Original===
She walked on worlds, and worlds of worlds, and heard there in her little room the tread of armies, the paeans of victory, the breaking of hearts, and the music of the spheres.
Mrs. Vanderpool watched her a while.
“Zora,” she presently broke into the girl’s absorption, “how would you like to be Ambassador to France?”

===Chinese===
她行走在一个个世界之上、一个个世界的世界之上，在她那间小屋里，听见了行军的脚步、胜利的凯歌、心碎的声响，和诸天的乐音。
范德普尔太太望了她一会儿。
「佐拉，」她终于打破了姑娘的出神，「要是让你做驻法国大使，你意下如何？」

## XXIV The Education of Alwyn / XXIV 阿尔文的教化

===Original===
Miss Caroline Wynn of Washington had little faith in the world and its people. Nor was this wholly her fault. The world had dealt cruelly with the young dreams and youthful ambitions of the girl; partly with its usual heartlessness, partly with that cynical and deadening reserve fund which it has today for its darker peoples. The girl had bitterly resented her experiences at first: she was brilliant and well-trained; she had a real talent for sculpture, and had studied considerably; she was sprung from at least three generations of respectable mulattoes, who had left a little competence which yielded her three or four hundred dollars a year. Furthermore, while not precisely pretty, she was good-looking and interesting, and she had acquired the marks and insignia of good breeding. Perhaps she wore her manners just a trifle consciously; perhaps she was a little morbid that she would fail of recognition as a lady. Nor was this unnatural: her brown skin invited a different assumption. Despite this almost unconscious mental aggressiveness, she was unusually presentable and always well-groomed and pleasant of speech. Yet she found nearly all careers closed to her. At first it seemed accidental, the luck of life. Then she attributed it to her sex; but at last she was sure that, beyond chance and womanhood, it was the color-line that was hemming her in. Once convinced of this, she let her imagination play and saw the line even where it did not exist.

===Chinese===
华盛顿的卡罗琳·怀恩小姐，对这世界和世上的人没多少信心。这也不全是她的错。这世界对这姑娘年轻的梦和年少抱负下手够狠的；一半用的是它惯常的那副无情，一半用的是它今日为它那些肤色较深的子民备下的、那套愤世而麻木的储备金。姑娘起初对自己的遭遇愤愤不平：她聪颖，训练有素；她颇有雕塑的天分，下过不少功夫钻研；她出身于至少三代体面的穆拉托（mulatto，黑白混血）人家，留下一点薄产，每年能给她三四百元的进项。再者，她虽算不上标致，却好看、耐人寻味，并且已得了良好教养的种种印记和徽章。也许她穿戴上那份仪态时，自觉的意味稍重了那么一分；也许她有些病态地担心，自己不能被认作一位淑女。这也并非不近情理：她那棕色皮肤，招来的本是另一种假定。纵有这近乎无意识的心性上的咄咄逼人，她却出奇地拿得出手，永远仪容整洁、谈吐宜人。然而她发现，几乎所有的人生出路都对她关着门。起初像是偶然，是运气的作弄。后来她归咎于自己的性别；可到末了，她确信，在机缘和身为女人之外，是那条肤色界线把她围了起来。一旦信定了这一点，她便任由想象驰骋，哪怕那界线并不存在，她也能看得见。

===Original===
With her bit of property and brilliant parts she had had many suitors but they had been refused one after another for reasons she could hardly have explained. For years now Tom Teerswell had been her escort. Whether or not Caroline Wynn would ever marry him was a perennial subject of speculation among their friends and it usually ended in the verdict that she could not afford it﻿—that it was financially impossible.

===Chinese===
凭她那一点薄产和出众的才情，她有过不少求婚者，可一个接一个都被她拒了，原因连她自己也难说清。这些年来，汤姆·蒂尔斯韦尔一直是她出入的伴当。卡罗琳·怀恩到底会不会嫁给他，是朋友们中间一个长年话题，而结论通常是她嫁不起——经济上不可能。

===Original===
Nevertheless, the two were usually seen in public together, and although she often showed her quiet mastery of the situation, seldom had she snubbed him so openly as at the Treble Clef concert.
Teerswell was furious and began to plot vengeance; but Miss Wynn was attracted by the personality of Bles Alwyn. Southern country Negroes were rare in her set, but here was a man of intelligence and keenness coupled with an amazing frankness and modesty, and perceptibly shadowed by sorrow. The combination was, so far as she had observed, both rare and temporary and she was disposed to watch it in this case purely as a matter of intellectual curiosity. At the door of her home, therefore, after a walk of unusual interest, she said:
“I’m going to have a few friends in next Tuesday night; won’t you come, Mr. Alwyn?” And Mr. Alwyn said that he would.

===Chinese===
尽管如此，两人在公开场合通常还在一起出入，而虽说她常显露她对局面那份不动声色的掌控，却少有像在高音谱号俱乐部音乐会上那样，当众给他这么大的难堪。
蒂尔斯韦尔怒不可遏，开始盘算报复；可怀恩小姐却被布莱斯·阿尔文这个人吸引住了。南方乡下的黑人在她那个圈子里是稀罕的，可这一位，聪明、敏锐，又配上一种惊人的坦率和谦逊，还明显地被一层愁苦的阴影笼罩着。据她观察，这样的组合既罕见，又是暂时的，她打算就这一例纯然出于心智上的好奇，留心观察一番。于是，在一段意趣不寻常的同行之后，她在自家门口说：
「下星期二晚上我要请几个朋友来；你来吗，阿尔文先生？」阿尔文先生说他来。

===Original===
Next morning Miss Wynn rather repented her hasty invitation, but of course nothing could be done now. Nothing? Well, there was one thing; and she went to the telephone. A suggestion to Bles that he might profitably extend his acquaintance sent him to a certain tailor shop kept by a friend of hers; a word to the tailor guarded against the least suspicion of intrigue entering Bles’s head.

===Chinese===
第二天早上，怀恩小姐对自己那仓促的邀请颇有几分悔意，可如今自然也无可挽回。无可挽回？嗯，倒还有一件事；她走到电话机旁。她向布莱斯提了个建议，说他不妨把交际面再铺得宽些，把他引到一家她朋友开的裁缝铺去；又对那裁缝交代了一句话，好防着布莱斯心里生出哪怕一丝『被做了手脚』的疑心。

===Original===
It turned out quite as Miss Wynn had designed; Mr. Grey, the tailor, gave Bles some points on dressing, and made him, Southern fashion, a frock-coat for dress wear that set off his fine figure. On the night of the gathering at Miss Wynn’s Bles dressed with care, hesitating long over a necktie, but at last choosing one which he had recently purchased and which pleased him particularly. He was prompt to the minute and was consequently the first guest; but Miss Wynn’s greeting was so quietly cordial that his embarrassment soon fled. She looked him over at leisure and sighed at his tie; otherwise he was thoroughly presentable according to the strictest Washington standard.

===Chinese===
事情的走向，正如怀恩小姐所设计；那位裁缝格雷先生，给布莱斯传授了几条穿衣的门道，又依南方款式给他做了一件礼服用的燕尾服，衬得他那副好身段愈发精神。赴怀恩小姐那场聚会的那晚，布莱斯精心打扮，在一条领带上踌躇了好久，终于挑了一条新近买的、他格外中意的。他踩着点准时到，于是成了头一位客人；可怀恩小姐的招呼是那样从容热诚，他的局促很快便消散了。她从容不迫地把他打量了一番，对着他的领带叹了口气；除此之外，依最严苛的华盛顿标准，他是十足地拿得出手。

===Original===
They sat down and talked of generalities. Then an idea occurring to her, she conducted the conversation by devious paths to ties and asked Alwyn if he had heard of the fad of collecting ties. He had not, and she showed him a sofa pillow.
“Your tie quite attracted me,” she said; “it would make just the dash of color I need in my new pillow.”
“You may have it and welcome. I’ll send﻿—”
“Oh, no! A bird in the hand, you know. I’ll trade with you now for another I have.”
“Done!”

===Chinese===
两人坐下，谈了些泛泛之事。后来她心生一计，七拐八弯地把话头引到领带上，问阿尔文可曾听说过收藏领带这种时髦。他没听过，她便指给他看一只沙发靠垫。
「你这条领带可真吸引我，」她说；「它正是我新靠垫上需要的那一抹亮色。」
「你拿去便是，不必客气。我这就送——」
「哦，不！一鸟在手，胜似二鸟在林嘛。我这会儿就拿我另一条跟你换。」
「成交！」

===Original===
The exchange was soon made, Miss Wynn tying the new one herself and sticking a small carved pin in it. Bles slowly sat down again, and after a pause said, “Thank you.”
She looked up quickly, but he seemed quite serious and good-natured.
“You see,” he explained, “in the country we don’t know much about ties.”
The well-balanced Miss Wynn for a moment lost her aplomb, but only for a moment.
“We must all learn,” she replied with penetration, and so their friendship was established.

===Chinese===
交换很快就完成了，怀恩小姐亲手替他系上新的那一条，又插上一枚小小的雕花领带夹。布莱斯慢慢重新坐下，停了停，说：「谢谢你。」
她飞快地抬眼，可他神情一本正经、和和气气。
「你瞧，」他解释，「在乡下，我们对领带懂得不多。」
一向从容的怀恩小姐，这一刻也失了镇定，不过只一瞬。
「我们都得学嘛，」她深有会意地答，于是两人的交情就这样定了下来。

===Original===
The company now began to gather, and soon the double parlor held an assemblage of twenty-five or thirty persons. They formed a picturesque group: conventional but graceful in dress; animated in movement; full of good-natured laughter, but quite un-American in the beautiful modulation of their speaking tones; chiefly noticeable, however, to a stranger, in the vast variety of color in skin, which imparted to the throng a piquant and unusual interest. Every color was here; from the dark brown of Alwyn, who was customarily accounted black, to the pale pink-white of Miss Jones, who could “pass for white” when she would, and found her greatest difficulties when she was trying to “pass” for black. Midway between these two extremes lay the sallow pastor of the church, the creamy Miss Williams, the golden yellow of Mr. Teerswell, the golden brown of Miss Johnson, and the velvet brown of Mr. Grey. The guest themselves did not notice this; they were used to asking one’s color as one asks of height and weight; it was simply an extra dimension in their world whereby to classify men.

===Chinese===
客人们陆续到齐，很快那间双连客厅里就聚了二三十位。他们构成一幅如画的群像：衣着合乎规矩却不失优雅；举止灵动；满是善意的笑声，可说话声那优美的抑扬顿挫，却又很不美国；不过对陌生人而言，最显眼的还是肤色的千差万别，这给这一群人添了一种别致而不寻常的意趣。各色肤色都在此：从阿尔文那种习惯上被算作『黑人』的深棕，到琼斯小姐那种浅粉白——她愿意时能『冒充白人』，而她最大的难处，倒出在她想『冒充』黑人的时候。在这两个极端之间，是那位脸色蜡黄的教堂牧师、奶油色的威廉斯小姐、金黄的蒂尔斯韦尔先生、金棕色的约翰逊小姐，以及天鹅绒般棕色的格雷先生。客人们自己并不在意这一点；他们惯于像问人的身高体重一样问人的肤色；这不过是他们世界里额外的一个维度，用来把人分门别类而已。

===Original===
Beyond this and their hair, there was little to distinguish them from a modern group of men and women. The speech was a softened English, purely and, on the whole, correctly spoken﻿—so much so that it seemed at first almost unfamiliar to Bles, and he experienced again the uncomfortable feeling of being among strangers. Then, too, he missed the loud but hearty good-nature of what he had always called “his people.” To be sure, a more experienced observer might have noted a lively, excitable tropical temperament set and cast in a cold Northern mould, and yet flashing fire now and then in a sudden anomalous out-bursting. But Bles missed this; he seemed to have slipped and lost his bearings, and the characteristics of his simple world were rolling curiously about. Here stood a black man with a white man’s voice, and yonder a white woman with a Negro’s musical cadences; and yet again, a brown girl with exactly Miss Cresswell’s air, and yonder, Miss Williams, with Zora’s wistful willfulness.

===Chinese===
除开这一点和他们的头发，几乎没什么能把他们和一群时新的男女区分开来。他们说的是一种柔和的英语，说得纯正，大体上也正确——以至于起初布莱斯竟觉得几乎陌生，又一次体会到那种身处陌生人之间的不自在。再者，他也想念他一向称作『自己人』的那份虽粗声大嗓却热乎乎的好脾气。诚然，一个更有阅历的观察者，也许能看出一种活泼易热的热带性子，被浇铸进一副冷峻的北方模子里，却又时不时地，在某个突兀的反常爆发里闪出火光。可布莱斯没看出这些；他像是脚下一滑，失了方向，他那简单世界里的种种特征，正在古怪地滚来滚去。这边站着一个黑人，却有一副白人的嗓音；那边一个白人女子，却带着黑人的乐感抑扬；再者，一个棕色皮肤的姑娘，举止活脱脱是克雷斯韦尔小姐的派头，而那边，威廉斯小姐，带着佐拉那种怅惘的任性。

===Original===
Bles was bewildered and silent, and his great undying sorrow sank on his heart with sickening hopeless weight. His hands got in the way and he found no natural nook in all those wide and tastefully furnished rooms. Once he discovered himself standing by a marble statue of a nude woman, and he edged away; then he stumbled over a rug and saved himself only to step on Miss Jones’s silken train. Miss Jones’s smile of pardon was wintry. When he did approach a group and listen, they seemed speaking of things foreign to him﻿—usually of people he did not know, their homes, their doings, their daughters and their fathers. They seemed to know people intimately who lived far away.
“You mean the Smiths of Boston?” asked Miss Jones.
“No, of Cleveland. They’re not related.”
“I heard that McGhee of St. Paul will be in the city next week with his daughter.”
“Yes, and the Bentleys of Chicago.”

===Chinese===
布莱斯手足无措，默不作声，他那巨大而绵绵不绝的哀痛，以一种叫人发木的、无望的分量，压上了他的心。他的两只手无处安放，在那些宽敞、陈设雅致的房间里，竟找不到一个自然而然的栖身角落。有一回他发现自己站在一座裸女大理石像旁，赶紧挪开；接着又被一块地毯绊了一下，好不容易稳住身形，却一脚踩在了琼斯小姐丝绸长裙的拖裾上。琼斯小姐宽恕的微笑，冷得像严冬。他好不容易凑近一群人去听，他们说的又仿佛尽是些与他不相干的事——多半是他不认识的人，他们的家、他们的行止、他们的女儿和父亲。他们似乎与一些住在远方的人相知甚深。
「你说的是波士顿的史密斯一家？」琼斯小姐问。
「不，是克利夫兰的。两家不沾亲。」
「我听说圣保罗的麦吉下周要带女儿进城来。」
「是啊，还有芝加哥的本特利一家。」

===Original===
Bles passed on. He was disappointed. He was full of things to say, of mighty matters to discuss; he felt like stopping these people and crying: “Ho! What of the morning? How goes the great battle for black men’s rights? I have came with messages from the host, to you who guard the mountain tops.”
Apparently they were not discussing or caring about “the Problem.” He grew disgusted and was edging toward the door when he encountered his hostess.
“Is all well with你, Mr. Alwyn?” she asked lightly.
“No, I’m not enjoying myself,” said Bles, truthfully.
“Delicious! And why not?”

===Chinese===
布莱斯走开了。他失望。他一肚子话要说，一肚子要紧事要议；他恨不得拦住这些人，喊：「喂！早晨的战况如何？黑人权利的那场大战打得怎么样了？我是大军的信使，来见你们这些守在山头上的。」
显然他们既不议论，也不关心『那个问题』。他越来越反感，正朝门口蹭去，迎面撞见女主人。
「一切都还好吗，阿尔文先生？」她轻快地问。
「不，我并不尽兴，」布莱斯实话实说。
「妙极了！那是为什么？」

===Original===
He regarded her earnestly.
“There are so many things to talk about,” he said; “earnest things; things of importance. I﻿—I think when our people﻿—” he hesitated. Our?﻿—was *our* right? But he went on: “When our people meet we ought to talk of our situation, and what to do and﻿—”
Miss Wynn continued to smile.
“We’re all talking of it all the time,” she said.
He looked incredulous.
“Yes, we are,” she insisted. “We veil it a little, and laugh as lightly as we can; but there is only one thought in this room, and that’s grave and serious enough to suit even you, and quite your daily topic.”

===Chinese===
他热切地望着她。
「有那么多事可以谈，」他说；「正经的事；要紧的事。我——我想，当我们的人——」他犹豫了。我们的？——『我们的』说得对吗？可他还是接下去：「当我们的人聚在一起，我们该谈谈我们的处境，该做些什么，还有——」
怀恩小姐仍是微笑。
「我们一直都在谈这个，」她说。
他将信将疑。
「是真的，一直在谈，」她坚持。「我们只是把它稍微遮一遮，尽量笑得轻快些；可这屋里只有一桩心事，而那一桩，沉重严肃得足够合你的意，也正合你每日的话题。」

===Original===
“But I don’t understand.”
“Ah, there’s the rub. You haven’t learned our language yet. We don’t just blurt into the Negro Problem; that’s voted bad form. We leave that to our white friends. We saunter to it sideways, touch it delicately because”﻿—her face became a little graver﻿—“because, you see, it hurts.”
Bles stood thoughtful and abashed.
“I﻿—I think I understand,” he gravely said at last.
“Come here,” she said with a sudden turn, and they joined an absorbed group in the midst of a conversation.

===Chinese===
「可我不明白。」
「啊，难就难在这里。你还没学会我们的语言。我们不会一头撞进『黑人问题』里去；那被公认为失礼。那种事留给我们的白人朋友。我们侧面绕着走近它，轻轻地触碰，因为」——她的脸色凝重了些——「因为，你瞧，它会疼。」
布莱斯站着，沉思而赧然。
「我——我想我明白了，」末了他郑重地说。
「来，」她忽然一转身，带他加入一群正谈得起劲的人。

===Original===
“﻿—Thinking of sending Jessie to Bryn Mawr,” Bles heard Miss Jones saying.
“Could she pass?”
“Oh, they might think her Spanish.”
“But it’s a snobbish place and she would have to give up all her friends.”
“Yes, Freddie could scarcely visit﻿—” the rest was lost.
“Which, being interpreted,” whispered Miss Wynn, “means that Bryn Mawr draws the color line while we at times surmount it.”
They moved on to another group.
“﻿—Splendid draughtsman,” a man was saying, “and passed at the head of the crowd; but, of course, he has no chance.”
“Why, it’s civil-service, isn’t it?”
“It is. But what of that? There was Watson﻿—”
Miss Wynn did not pause. She whispered: “This is the tale of Civil Service Reform, and how this mighty government gets rid of black men who know too much.”

===Chinese===
「——正琢磨着把杰西送到布林莫尔去呢，」布莱斯听见琼斯小姐在说。
「她过得去吗？」
「哦，他们也许会当她是个西班牙人。」
「可那是个势利的地方，她得放弃所有的朋友。」
「是啊，弗雷迪就几乎不能去——」后半句听不见了。
「这翻译过来，」怀恩小姐悄声说，「意思是布林莫尔划下了肤色界线，而我们有时能翻过去。」
他们又移到另一群人那里。
「——一个出色的制图员，」一个男人正在说，「考试成绩也名列前茅；可当然了，他没机会。」
「怎么，那不是文官系统吗？」
「是。可那又怎样？沃森不就是——」
怀恩小姐没停步。她悄声道：「这是文官制度改革的故事，说的是这个庞大的政府，怎样摆脱那些知道得太多的黑人。」

===Original===
“But﻿—” Bles tried to protest.
“Hush,” Miss Wynn commanded and they joined the group about the piano. Teerswell, who was speaking, affected not to notice them, and continued:
“﻿—I tell you, it’s got to come. We must act independently and not be bought by a few offices.”
“That’s all well enough for you to talk, Teerswell; you have no wife and babies dependant on you. Why should we who have sacrifice the substance for the shadow?”
“You see, the Judge has got the substance,” laughed Teerswell. “Still I insist: divide and conquer.”
“Nonsense! Unite, and keep.”

===Chinese===
「可是——」布莱斯想抗议。
「嘘，」怀恩小姐一声令下，他们便加入了钢琴边的那一群。正在发言的蒂尔斯韦尔，装作没注意到他们，继续说：
「——我告诉你们，这事迟早要来。我们必须独立行动，不能被几个职位收买。」
「这话你说着轻巧，蒂尔斯韦尔；你没有妻儿靠你养活。凭什么要我们这些有家室的，为个影子丢了实在的？」
「你瞧，实在的都被法官得了去，」蒂尔斯韦尔笑道。「但我还是坚持：分而治之。」
「胡扯！合而守之。」

===Original===
Bles was puzzled.
“They’re talking of the coming campaign,” said Miss Wynn.
“What!” exclaimed Bles aloud. “You don’t mean that anyone can advise a black man to vote the Democratic ticket?”
An elderly man turned to them.
“Thank you, sir,” he said; “that is just my attitude; I fought for my freedom. I know what slavery is; may I forget God when I vote for traitors and slaveholders.”
The discussion waxed warm and Miss Wynn turned away and sought Miss Jones.
“Come, my dear,” she said, “it’s ‘The Problem’ again.” They sauntered away toward a ring of laughter.

===Chinese===
布莱斯一头雾水。
「他们在谈即将到来的大选，」怀恩小姐说。
「什么！」布莱斯脱口而出。「你不是说，居然有人劝黑人投民主党的票？」
一位老者转向他们。
「谢谢你，先生，」他说；「这正是我的态度；我为自己的自由战斗过。我知道奴隶制是什么滋味；倘若我投了叛徒和奴隶主的票，那就让上帝忘了我吧。」
讨论热烈起来，怀恩小姐转身去找琼斯小姐。
「来吧，亲爱的，」她说，「又是『那个问题』了。」她们朝一圈笑声踱去。

===Original===
The discussion thus begun at Miss Wynn’s did not end there. It was on the eve of the great party conventions, and the next night Sam Stillings came around to get some crumbs from this assembly of the inner circle, into which Alwyn had been so unaccountably snatched, and outside of which, despite his endeavors, Stillings lingered and seemed destined to linger. But Stillings was a patient, resolute man beneath his deferential exterior, and he saw in Bles a stepping stone. So he began to drop in at his lodgings and tonight invited him to the Bethel Literary.
“What’s that?” asked Bles.
“A debating club﻿—oldest in the city; the best people all attend.”

===Chinese===
在怀恩小姐家这么起的这场议论，并没有就此打住。正值两大党的全国代表大会前夕，第二天晚上，萨姆·斯蒂林斯便来蹭这个核心圈子的一些余唾——阿尔文被那么莫名其妙地拽进了这圈子，而斯蒂林斯尽管使尽浑身解数，仍徘徊在圈外，似乎命定要徘徊下去。但斯蒂林斯在那副恭顺的外表下，是个耐心而坚决的人，他在布莱斯身上看到了一块垫脚石。于是他开始时不时到布莱斯住处串门，今晚则邀他去伯特利文学会。
「那是什么？」布莱斯问。
「一个辩论俱乐部——城里最老的；最好的人家都去。」

===Original===
Bles hesitated. He had half made up his mind that this was the proper time to call on Miss Wynn. He told Stillings so, and told him also of the evening and the discussion.
“Why, that’s the subject up tonight,” Stillings declared, “and Miss Wynn will be sure to be there. You can make your call later. Perhaps you wouldn’t mind taking me when you call.” Alwyn reached for his hat.
When they arrived, the basement of the great church was filling with a throng of men and women. Soon the officers and the speaker of the evening appeared. The president was a brown woman who spoke easily and well, and introduced the main speaker. He was a tall, thin, hatchet-faced black man, clean shaven and well dressed, a lawyer by profession. His theme was “The Democratic Party and the Negro.” His argument was cool, carefully reasoned, and plausible. He was evidently feeling for the sympathy of his audience, and while they were not enthusiastic, they warmed to him gradually and he certainly was strongly impressing them.

===Chinese===
布莱斯犹豫了。他原本已半打定主意，这会儿正是去拜访怀恩小姐的当口。他跟斯蒂林斯这么说了，也把那晚的聚会和那场讨论讲给他听。
「嘿，那正是今晚的辩题，」斯蒂林斯断言，「怀恩小姐准到。你回头再访也不迟。说不定，你去拜访时，不介意带上我。」阿尔文伸手去拿帽子。
他们到时，那大教堂的地下室正挤满了男男女女。不一会儿，会里的干事和今晚的主讲人到了。主席是个棕色皮肤的女人，说话从容得体，介绍了主讲人。那人又高又瘦，一张刀削脸，是个黑人，刮净了胡须，衣着讲究，本行是律师。他的讲题是『民主党与黑人』。他的论证冷静、推理缜密、颇有说服力。他显然在试探听众的同情，而听众虽不狂热，却渐渐被他暖热，他无疑正强烈地打动着他们。

===Original===
Bles was thinking. He sat in the back of the hall, tense, alert, nervous. As the speaker progressed a white man came in and sat down beside him. He was spectacled, with bushy eyebrows and a sleepy look. But he did not sleep. He was very observant.
“Who’s speaking?” he asked Bles, and Bles told him. Then he inquired about one or two other persons. Bles could not inform him, but Stillings could and did. Stillings seemed willing to devote considerable time to him.
Bles forgot the man. He was almost crouching for a spring, and no sooner had the speaker, with a really fine apostrophe to independence and reason in voting, sat down, than Bles was on his feet, walking forward. His form was commanding, his voice deep and musical, and his earnestness terribly evident. He hardly waited for recognition from the slightly astonished president, but fairly burst into speech.

===Chinese===
布莱斯在思索。他坐在大厅后排，绷紧、警觉、紧张。主讲人讲着讲着，一个白人走了进来，在他身边坐下。那人戴着眼镜，眉毛浓密，一副瞌睡相。可他并不睡。他眼观六路。
「谁在讲？」他问布莱斯，布莱斯告诉了他。接着他又打听另外一两个人。布莱斯答不上来，斯蒂林斯却答得上来，也确实答了。斯蒂林斯似乎乐意在他身上花不少工夫。
布莱斯把这人忘了。他几乎蜷起身子准备一跃，主讲人那段呼吁投票时的独立与理性的精彩感叹话音刚落、屁股刚沾椅，布莱斯便霍然起身，大步走上前去。他身形威严，嗓音深沉而悦耳，那份恳切一目了然得可怕。他几乎没等略带惊讶的主席出声示意，便径直开了口。

===Original===
“I am from Alabama,” he began earnestly, “and I know the Democratic Party.” Then he told of government and conditions in the Black Belt, of the lying, oppression, and helplessness of the sodden black masses; then, turning, he reminded them of the history of slavery. Finally, he pointed to Lincoln’s picture and to Sumner’s and mentioned other white friends.
“And, my brothers, they are not all dead yet. The gentleman spoke of Senator Smith and blamed and ridiculed him. I know Senator Smith but slightly, but I do know his sister well.”

===Chinese===
「我来自阿拉巴马，」他热切地开口，「我了解民主党。」于是他讲起黑土地带的政府和现状，讲起那麻木的黑人群众所遭受的欺骗、压迫和走投无路；接着话锋一转，提醒他们记住奴隶制的历史。最后，他指向林肯的画像，指向萨姆纳的画像，又提了几位白人朋友。
「而，兄弟们，他们并没有全都死绝。方才那位先生提到了史密斯参议员，对他又是责难又是嘲弄。我与史密斯参议员只是泛泛之交，可我确确实实熟知他的妹妹。」

===Original===
Dropping to simple narrative, he told of Miss Smith and of his coming to school; and if his audience felt that great depth of emotion that welled beneath his quiet, almost hesitating, address, it was not simply because of what he did say, but because, too, of the unspoken story that lay too deep for words. He spoke for nearly an hour, and when he stopped, for a moment his hearers sighed and then sprang into a whirlwind of applause. They shouted, clapped, and waved while he sat in blank amazement, and was with difficulty forced to the rostrum to bow again and again. The spectacled white man leaned over to Stillings.
“Who is he?” he asked. Stillings told him. The man noted the name and went quietly out.

===Chinese===
他转而平实地讲起史密斯小姐，讲起自己来上学的事；倘若他的听众感到他那平静、近乎迟疑的讲述下，涌起了一股巨大的情感深流，那不仅仅是因为他说出口的那些话，也因为他那桩深得无法言说的、未出口的故事。他讲了将近一个钟头，他一停，听众先是叹了一口气，随即爆发出一阵旋风般的掌声。他们喊、鼓掌、挥手，而他茫然吃惊地坐在那里，好不容易才被推到讲台上，一次又一次地鞠躬。那个戴眼镜的白人朝斯蒂林斯倾过身。
「他是谁？」他问。斯蒂林斯告诉了他。那人记下了名字，悄然离去。

===Original===
Miss Wynn sat lost in thought, and Teerswell beside her fumed. She was not easily moved, but that speech had moved her. If he could thus stir men and not be himself swayed, she mused, he would be﻿—invincible. But tonight he was moved as greatly as his hearers had been, and that was dangerous. If his intense belief happened to be popular, all right; but if not? She frowned. He was worth watching, she concluded; quite worth watching, and perhaps worth guiding.

===Chinese===
怀恩小姐坐着出了神，一旁的蒂尔斯韦尔怒气冲冲。她不易被打动，可那番演说打动了她。倘若他能这样鼓动众人而自己不为所动，她暗想，他将是——不可战胜的。可今夜他也被打动了，被打动的程度不亚于他的听众，而这就危险了。倘若他那份炽烈的信念恰巧合乎时宜，那好办；倘若不然呢？她皱起眉。他值得观察，她下了断语；很值得观察，也许还值得引导。

===Original===
When Alwyn accompanied her home that night, Miss Wynn set herself to know him better for she suspected that he might be a coming man. The best preliminary to her purpose was, she knew, to speak frankly of herself, and that she did. She told him of her youth and training, her ambitions, her disappointments. Quite unconsciously her cynicism crept to the fore, until in word and tone she had almost scoffed at many things that Alwyn held true and dear. The touch was too light, the meaning too elusive, for Alwyn to grasp always the point of attack; but somehow he got the distant impression that Miss Wynn had little faith in Truth and Goodness and Love. Vaguely shocked he grew so silent that she noticed it and concluded she had said too much. But he pursued the subject.
“Surely there must be many friends of our race willing to stand for the right and sacrifice for it?”
She laughed unpleasantly, almost mockingly.
“Where?”
“Well﻿—there’s Miss Smith.”
“She gets a salary, doesn’t she?”
“A very small one.”
“About as large as she could earn. North, I don’t doubt.”

===Chinese===
那晚阿尔文送她回家，怀恩小姐有心要更深地了解他，因为她怀疑他会是个将要成事的人。要达到这个目的，她知道最好的开场便是坦白地谈谈自己，她也确实这样做了。她跟他讲自己的少年和教养，她的抱负，她的失意。她那愤世之气几乎是不知不觉地浮上来，直到言辞和语调上，她几乎已嘲弄了阿尔文奉为真与宝的许多东西。那笔触太轻，那意味太隐约，布莱斯未必总能抓住被攻讦的要害；可不知怎的，他隐隐得到一个印象：怀恩小姐对真、对善、对爱，没什么信心。他模糊地震惊，沉默下来，沉到她都留意到了，断定自己说过了头。可他却把这个话题追了下去。
「想必，我们族里一定有不少朋友，肯为正义挺身、为正义牺牲吧？」
她不痛快地、近乎嘲弄地笑了。
「在哪儿呢？」
「嗯——有史密斯小姐啊。」
「她领薪水的，不是吗？」
「很薄的一份。」
「大约跟她在北方能挣的差不多，我毫不怀疑。」

===Original===
“But the unselfish work she does﻿—the utter sacrifice?”
“Oh, well, we’ll omit Alabama, and admit the exception.”
“Well, here, in Washington﻿—there’s your friend, the Judge, who has befriended you so, as you admit.”
She laughed again.
“You remember our visit to Senator Smith?”
“Yes.”
“Well, it got the Judge his reappointment to the school board.”
“He deserved it, didn’t he?”
“I deserved it,” she said luxuriously, hugging her knee and smiling; “you see, his appointment meant mine.”

===Chinese===
「可她做的那些无私的奉献——那彻头彻尾的牺牲呢？」
「哦，好吧，我们把阿拉巴马略去不计，承认这是个例外。」
「那好，在这儿，在华盛顿——有你那位朋友，法官，据你自己说，他帮过你不少忙呢。」
她又笑了。
「你还记得我们去找史密斯参议员那次吗？」
「记得。」
「嗯，那次造访，让法官得到了教育委员会的连任。」
「那是他应得的，不是吗？」
「是我应得的，」她惬意地说，抱着膝盖微笑；「你瞧，他的任命意味着我的任命。」

===Original===
“Well, what of it﻿—didn’t﻿—”
“Listen,” she cut in a little sharply. “Once a young brown girl, with boundless faith in white folks, went to a Judge’s office to ask for an appointment which she deserved. There was no one there. The benign old Judge with his saintly face and white hair suggested that she lay aside her wraps and spend the afternoon.”
Bles arose to his feet.
“What﻿—what did you do?” he asked.
“Sit down﻿—there’s a good boy.” I said: “‘Judge, a friend is expecting me at two,’ it was then half-past one, ‘would I not best telephone?’ ”

===Chinese===
「那又怎样——难道不是——」
「听着，」她略带尖锐地打断。「从前有个年轻的棕色皮肤姑娘，对白人有无限的信任，到一个法官的办公室去求一份她本该得到的任命。那里没有旁人。那位慈祥的老法官，一脸圣徒相、一头白发，提议她宽衣，把下午的时光打发在他那儿。」
布莱斯站了起来。
「你——你怎么做的？」他问。
「坐下——乖。」我说：『「法官，两点钟有位朋友在等我，』那时才一点半，『我是不是最好先去打个电话？」』

===Original===
“‘Step right into the booth,’ said the Judge, quite indulgently.” Miss Wynn leaned back, and Bles felt his heart sinking; but he said nothing. “And then,” she continued, “I telephoned the Judge’s wife that he was anxious to see her on a matter of urgent business; namely, my appointment.” She gazed reflectively out of the window. “You should have seen his face when I told him,” she concluded. “I was appointed.”
But Bles asked coldly:
“Why didn’t you have him arrested?”
“For what? And suppose I had?”
Bles threw out his arms helplessly.
“Oh! it isn’t as bad as that all over the world, is it?”
“It’s worse,” affirmed Miss Wynn, quietly positive.

===Chinese===
「『尽管进那电话亭去，』法官说，挺纵容的口气。」怀恩小姐往后一靠，布莱斯感到心在下沉；可他没作声。「然后，」她接着说，「我打电话给法官太太，说法官急着想见她，为着一桩要紧的公事；说的便是，我的任命。」她若有所思地望着窗外。「你真该看看我告诉他时他的脸色，」她总结道。「我被任命了。」
可布莱斯冷冷地问：
「你为什么不告发他？」
「以什么罪名？再说，告了又怎样？」
布莱斯无可奈何地摊开双臂。
「哦！全世界不至于都坏成这样吧？」
「更坏，」怀恩小姐断言，平静而笃定。

===Original===
“And you are still friendly with him?”
“What would you have? I use the world; I did not make it; I did not choose it. He is the world. Through him I earn my bread and butter. I have shown him his place. Shall I try in addition to reform? Shall I make him an enemy? I have neither time nor inclination. Shall I resign and beg, or go tilting at windmills? If he were the only one it would be different; but they’re all alike.” Her face grew hard. “Have I shocked you?” she said as they went toward the door.

===Chinese===
「而你还跟他友好往来？」
「你要怎样？我用这世界；我没造它；我没选它。他就是这世界。靠他我挣我的面包黄油。我已经让他认清了自己的位置。难道我还要外加去改造他？难道我要把他变成仇敌？我既没那工夫，也没那兴致。难道我该辞职去讨饭，或者去跟风车作战？若只有他一个，那又不同；可他们都一个样。」她的脸冷硬起来。「我吓着你了吗？」他们走向门口时她说。

===Original===
“No,” he answered slowly. “But I still﻿—believe in the world.”
“You are young yet, my friend,” she lightly replied. “And besides, that good Miss Smith has gone and grafted a New England conscience on a tropical heart, and﻿—dear me!﻿—but it’s a gorgeous misfit. Goodbye﻿—come again.” She bowed him graciously out, and paused to take the mail from the box. There was, among many others, a letter from Senator Smith.

===Chinese===
「没有，」他慢慢地答。「可我仍然——相信这个世界。」
「你还年轻呢，我的朋友，」她轻快地回。「再者，那位好心的史密斯小姐，把一颗新英格兰的良知，嫁接到了一颗热带的心上，而——天哪！——这错位可真是壮观。再会——再来啊。」她优雅地送他出门，又停下从信箱里取出邮件。在那许多封信里，有一封是史密斯参议员写来的。

## XXV The Campaign / XXV 竞选

===Original===
Mr. Easterly sat in Mrs. Vanderpool’s apartments in the New Willard, Washington, drinking tea. His hostess was saying rather carelessly:
“Do you know, Mr. Vanderpool has developed a quite unaccountable liking for the idea of being Ambassador to France?”
“Dear me!” mildly exclaimed Mr. Easterly, helping himself liberally to cakes. “I do hope the thing can be managed, but﻿—”
“What are the difficulties?” Mrs. Vanderpool interrupted.

===Chinese===
伊斯特利先生坐在华盛顿新威拉德饭店范德普尔太太的寓所里喝茶。女主人正相当漫不经心地说：
「你信不信，范德普尔先生居然对当驻法国大使这个念头，生出了一种说不清的喜爱？」
「哎呀呀！」伊斯特利先生温温和和地叫了一声，毫不客气地大拿糕点。「我真盼望这事能成，不过——」
「难处在哪里？」范德普尔太太打断他。

===Original===
“Well, first and foremost, the difficulty of electing our man.”
“I thought that a foregone conclusion.”
“It was. But do you know that we’re encountering opposition from the most unexpected source?”
The lady was receptive, and the speaker concluded:
“The Negroes.”
“The Negroes!”
“Yes. There are five hundred thousand or more black voters in pivotal Northern States, you know, and they’re in revolt. In a close election the Negroes of New York, Ohio, Indiana, and Illinois choose the President.”

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，头一条，也是头等要紧的一条，难在我们得先把我们的人选上去。」
「我原以为那是板上钉钉的事。」
「本来是。可你知道吗，我们正在遭遇反对，反对来自最出人意料的一个源头。」
这位太太洗耳恭听，说话的人便下了结论：
「黑人。」
「黑人！」
「是的。要知道，在那些举足轻重的北方州里，有五十万乃至更多的黑人选民，而他们正闹着反水。在一场势均力敌的选举里，纽约、俄亥俄、印第安纳、伊利诺伊的黑人，能决定谁当总统。」

===Original===
“What’s the matter?”
“Well, business interests have driven our party to make friends with the South. The South has disfranchised Negroes and lynched a few. The darkies say we’ve deserted them.”
Mrs. Vanderpool laughed.
“What extraordinary penetration,” she cried.
“At any rate,” said Mr. Easterly, drily, “Mr. Vanderpool’s first step toward Paris lies in getting the Northern Negroes to vote the Republican ticket. After that the way is clear.”

===Chinese===
「怎么个事？」
「呃，生意上的利益，逼着咱们党去跟南方套近乎。南方剥夺了黑人的选举权，还私刑处死了几个。黑佬们说咱们把他们抛弃了。」
范德普尔太太笑起来。
「好厉害的眼力，」她叫。
「无论如何，」伊斯特利先生干巴巴地说，「范德普尔先生通往巴黎的头一步，就在于让北方的黑人们投共和党的票。这一关过了，路就通了。」

===Original===
Mrs. Vanderpool mused.
“I don’t suppose you know anyone who is acquainted with any number of these Northern darkies?” continued Mr. Easterly.
“Not on my calling-list,” said Mrs. Vanderpool, and then she added more thoughtfully:
“There’s a young clerk in the Treasury Department named Alwyn who has brains. He’s just from the South, and I happened to read of him this morning﻿—see here.”
Mr. Easterly read an account of the speech at the Bethel Literary.
“We’ll look this young man up,” he decided; “he may help. Of course, Mrs. Vanderpool, we’ll probably win; we can buy these Negroes off with a little money and a few small offices; then if you will use your influence for the part with the Southerners, I can confidently predict from four to eight years’ sojourn in Paris.”

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太沉吟。
「我想，你该不认识什么人，能结交上这批北方黑佬里相当数目的一群吧？」伊斯特利先生接着说。
「那不在我的拜访名单上，」范德普尔太太说，随即又更若有所思地补了一句：
「财政部有个年轻的文书叫阿尔文的，有头脑。他刚从南方来，今早我恰好读到他的消息——你瞧。」
伊斯特利先生读了那段关于伯特利文学会演说的报道。
「这个年轻人我们得见一见，」他定了主意；「也许能帮上忙。当然，范德普尔太太，我们多半会赢；我们可以用一点钱、几个小职位把这些黑人收买过来；之后，若您肯在南方人那一头施加您的影响力，那我就可以满有把握地预言，您能在巴黎住上四到八年。」

===Original===
Mrs. Vanderpool smiled and called her maid as Mr. Easterly went.
“Zora!” She had to call twice, for Zora, with widened eyes, was reading the Washington Post.
Meantime in the office of Senator Smith, toward which Mr. Easterly was making his way, several members of the National Republican campaign committee had been closeted the day before.
“Now, about the niggers,” the chairman had asked; “how much more boodle do they want?”

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太笑了笑，在伊斯特利先生走时唤她的女仆。
「佐拉！」她唤了两声，因为佐拉正瞪大眼睛在读《华盛顿邮报》。
与此同时，伊斯特利先生正朝史密斯参议员办公室走去——前一天，全国共和党竞选委员会的几位委员在那里密议过。
「那么，关于那些黑鬼，」主席曾问；「他们还要多少贿款？」

===Original===
“That’s what’s bothering us,” announced a member; “it isn’t the boodle crowd that’s hollering, but a new set, and I don’t understand them; I don’t know what they represent, nor just how influential they are.”
“What can I do to help you?” asked Senator Smith.
“This. You are here at Washington with these Negro officeholders at your back. Find out for us just what this revolt is, how far it goes, and what good men we can get to swing the darkies into line﻿—see?”
“Very good,” the Senator acquiesced. He called in a spectacled man with bushy eyebrows and a sleepy look.

===Chinese===
「这正是叫我们头疼的地方，」一位委员声言；「嚷嚷的不是那拨要钱的人，而是一班新出来的，我摸不透他们；我不知道他们代表谁，也不知道他们究竟有多大能耐。」
「我能帮上什么忙？」史密斯参议员问。
「这个。您在华盛顿，背后就是这些黑人官吏。替我们摸清楚，这场反水到底是怎么回事、闹到什么程度、我们能找到什么靠得住的人去把黑佬们拉回正轨——明白？」
「很好，」参议员应承。他叫进来一个戴眼镜、眉毛浓密、一副瞌睡相的人。

===Original===
“I want you to work the Negro political situation,” directed the Senator, “and bring me all the data you can get. Personally, I’m at sea. I don’t understand the Negro of today at all; he puzzles me; he doesn’t fit any of my categories, and I suspect that I don’t fit his. See what you can find out.”
The man went out, and the Senator turned to his desk, then paused and smiled. One day, not long since, he had met a colored person who personified his perplexity concerning Negroes; she was a lady, yet she was black﻿—that is, brown; she was educated, even cultured, yet she taught Negroes; she was quiet, astute, quick and diplomatic﻿—everything, in fact, that “Negroes” were not supposed to be; and yet she was a “Negro.” She had given him valuable information which he had sought in vain elsewhere, and the event proved it correct. Suppose he asked Caroline Wynn to help him in this case? It would certainly do no harm and it might elect a Republican president. He wrote a short letter with his own hand and sent it to post.

===Chinese===
「我要你去摸一摸黑人的政治动向，」参议员吩咐，「把你所能弄到的资料都给我拿来。说实在的，我是一头雾水。我完全不懂今天的黑人；他叫我困惑；他对不上我任何一个框框，而我疑心，我也对不上他的。你看看能打听到什么。」
那人出去了，参议员转向书桌，又顿住，笑了笑。前不久的一天，他曾遇见一个有色人种，恰恰集中体现了他对黑人的那种困惑；她是位淑女，可她是黑人——准确说，是棕色皮肤；她受过教育，甚至颇有教养，可她教的是黑人；她沉静、精明、机敏、有手腕——总之，具备『黑人』据说不该有的一切特质；然而她却是个『黑人』。她曾给他提供过宝贵的情报，那情报是他旁处怎么也打听不到的，而事后证明千真万确。这一回，倘若他请卡罗琳·怀恩来帮忙呢？肯定不会坏事，说不定还能选出一个共和党总统。他亲笔写了一封短信，发出去。

===Original===
Miss Wynn read the letter after Alwyn’s departure with a distinct thrill which was something of a luxury for her. Evidently she was coming to her kingdom. The Republican boss was turning to her for confidential information.
“What do the colored people want, and who can best influence them in this campaign?”
She curled up on the ottoman and considered. The first part of the query did not bother her.
“Whatever they want they won’t get,” she said decisively.

===Chinese===
阿尔文走后，怀恩小姐读了这封信，心头掠过一阵明显的激动——这对她算是一种难得的享受。看来，她正走向她的王国。共和党的头号人物，正转而向她求取机密情报。
『有色人种想要什么，这次竞选中谁最能影响他们？』
她蜷在矮榻上琢磨起来。这问题的前半截难不倒她。
『他们想要什么，都不会得到，』她断然说。

===Original===
But as to the man or men who could influence them to believe that they were getting, or about to get, what they wanted﻿—there was a question. One by one she considered the men she knew, and, by a process of elimination, finally arrived at Bles Alwyn.
Why not take this young man in hand and make a Negro leader of him﻿—a protagonist of ten millions? It would not be unpleasant. But could she do it? Would he be amenable to her training and become worldly wise? She flattered herself that he would, and yet﻿—there was a certain steadfast look in the depths of his eyes that might prove to be sheer stubbornness. At any rate, who was better? There was a fellow, Stillings, whom Alwyn had introduced and whom she had heard of. Now he was a politician﻿—but nothing else. She dismissed him. Of course, there was the older set of officeholders and rounders. But she was determined to pick a new man. He was worth trying, at any rate; she knew none other with the same build, the brains, the gifts, the adorable youth. Very good. She wrote two letters, and then curled up to her novel and candy.

===Chinese===
至于那个、或那些能影响他们、使他们相信自己正在得到、或即将得到所求的人——这才是问题所在。她把自己认识的男人一个个过了一遍，经过层层排除，最后落到了布莱斯·阿尔文身上。
何不把这年轻人接过手来，把他塑造成一个黑人领袖——一千万人的领头人呢？这倒不是什么不愉快的事。可她办得到吗？他受不受她的调教，能否变得老于世故？她自忖他能，然而——他眼底有一种沉静的神色，那没准会是彻头彻尾的倔强。无论如何，还有谁更合适呢？倒有个叫斯蒂林斯的，阿尔文引见过，她也听说过。这人是个政客——但也只是个政客。她把他剔除了。当然，还有老一派的官吏和党棍。可她铁了心要挑个新人。无论如何，他值得一试；她再找不出第二个，有他这样的身段、头脑、禀赋，和那叫人欢喜的青春。很好。她写了两封信，便蜷起来看小说、吃糖去了。

===Original===
Next day Senator Smith held Miss Wynn’s letter unopened in his hand when Mr. Easterly entered. They talked of the campaign and various matters, until at last Easterly said:
“Say, there’s a Negro clerk in the Treasury named Alwyn.”
“I know him﻿—I had him appointed.”
“Good. He may help us. Have you seen this?”
The Senator read the clipping.
“I hadn’t noticed it﻿—but here’s my agent.”

===Chinese===
第二天，史密斯参议员手里拿着怀恩小姐尚未拆封的信，伊斯特利先生走了进来。他们谈了竞选和种种事务，末了伊斯特利说：
「嘿，财政部有个黑人文书叫阿尔文的。」
「我认识他——是我安排他进去的。」
「好。他也许能帮上忙。你看过这个没有？」
参议员读了那则剪报。
「我没留意过——不过，我这儿正好有我的探子。」

===Original===
The spectacled man entered with a mass of documents. He had papers, posters, programmes, and letters.
“The situation is this,” he said. “A small group of educated Negroes are trying to induce the rest to punish the Republican Party for not protecting them. These men are not politicians, nor popular leaders, but they have influence and are using it. The old-style Negro politicians are no match for them, and the crowd of officeholders are rather bewildered. Strong measures are needed. Educated men of earnestness and ability might stem the tide. And I believe I know one such man. He spoke at a big meeting last night at the Metropolitan church. His name is Alwyn.”
Senator Smith listened as he opened the letter from Caroline Wynn. Then he started.
“Well!” he ejaculated, looking quickly up at Easterly. “This is positively uncanny. From three separate sources the name of Alwyn pops up. Looks like a mascot. Call up the Treasury. Let’s have him up when the subcommittee meets tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
那个戴眼镜的人捧着一大摞文件走进来。有报纸、海报、节目单、信件。
「情况是这样，」他说。「一小群受过教育的黑人，正设法鼓动其余的人，因共和党不曾保护他们而惩治它一番。这些人不是政客，也不是群众领袖，但他们有影响力，也在运用这影响力。老派的黑人政客根本不是对手，那一帮官吏倒颇为发懵。需要强有力的措施。有热忱又有能力的受教育者，或许能力挽狂澜。而我相信我认得这样一个。昨晚他在大都会教堂一次大型聚会上发了言。他叫阿尔文。」
史密斯参议员一边听，一边拆开卡罗琳·怀恩的信。随即他一怔。
「好家伙！」他叫出来，飞快地抬头看伊斯特利。「这事简直邪门。三个不同的出处，阿尔文这个名字都冒了出来。像个福星了。给财政部打电话。明天小组委员会开会，让他来。」

===Original===
Bles Alwyn hurried up to Senator Smith’s office, hoping to hear something about the school; perhaps even about﻿—but he stopped with a sigh, and sat down in the anteroom. He was kept waiting a few moments while Senator Smith, the chairman, and one other member of the subcommittee had a word.
“Now, I don’t know the young man, mind you,” said the Senator; “but he’s strongly recommended.”
“What shall we offer him?” asked the chairman.
“Try him at twenty-five dollars a speech. If he balks, raise to fifty dollars, but no more.”

===Chinese===
布莱斯·阿尔文急匆匆赶到史密斯参议员办公室，本盼着能听到些学校的消息；说不定还能听到些关于——可他叹了口气，打住了，在前厅坐下。他被让着等了一小会儿，史密斯参议员、主席和小组委员会的另一位成员先凑了几句。
「我先说好，这个年轻人我不认识，」参议员说；「但举荐得很力。」
「咱们给他开什么价？」主席问。
「先试他一场演说二十五块。他要是不干，加到五十，不能再多。」

===Original===
They summoned the young man. The chairman produced cigars.
“I don’t smoke,” said Bles apologetically.
“Well, we haven’t anything to drink,” said the chairman. But Senator Smith broke in, taking up at once the paramount interest.
“Mr. Alwyn, as you know, the Democrats are making an effort to get the Negro vote in this campaign. Now, I know the disadvantages and wrongs which black men in this land are suffering. I believe the Republicans ought to do more to defend them, and I’m satisfied they will; but I doubt if the way to get Negro rights is to vote for those who took them away.”
“I agree with you perfectly,” said Bles.

===Chinese===
他们把年轻人叫了进去。主席拿出雪茄。
「我不抽烟，」布莱斯歉意地说。
「这个嘛，我们这儿也没什么喝的，」主席说。但史密斯参议员插了进来，开门见山切入了那桩头等要紧的事。
「阿尔文先生，你也知道，民主党这次竞选正设法争取黑人的选票。这一国里黑人所受的种种不公与冤屈，我是清楚的。我相信共和党本该多替他们撑腰，我也相信他们终会如此；但是，争取黑人权利的法子，是不是去投那些夺走他们权利的人的票，我倒存疑。」
「我完全赞同您，先生，」布莱斯说。

===Original===
“I understand you do, and that you made an unusually fine speech on the subject the other night.”
“Thank you, sir.” This was a good deal more than Bles had expected, and he was embarrassed.
“Well, now, we think you’re just the man to take the stump during September and October and convince the colored people of their real interests.”
“I doubt if I could, sir; I’m not a speaker. In fact, that was my first public speech.”
“So much the better. Are you willing to try?”
“Why, yes, sir; but I could hardly afford to give up my position.”
“We’ll arrange for a leave of absence.”

===Chinese===
「据我所知你也是这么想的，而且前两天你就这个题目，做了一番格外精彩的演说。」
「谢谢您，先生。」这可比布莱斯预料的多得多，他有点窘。
「那么现在，我们觉得，九月、十月间出来巡回演讲、让有色人种认清他们真正利益所在的，正是你这样的人。」
「我怕是不成，先生；我不是演说家。说实在的，那是我头一回公开演讲。」
「那更好。你愿意试试吗？」
「这个，愿意，先生；可我总不能丢了差事。」
「我们会安排你停薪留职。」

===Original===
“Then I’ll try, sir.”
“What would you expect as pay?”
“I suppose my salary would stop?”
“I mean in addition to that.”
“Oh, nothing, sir; I’d be glad to do the work.”
The chairman nearly choked; sitting back, he eyed the young man. Either they were dealing with a fool, or else a very astute politician. If the former, how far could they trust him; if the latter, what was his game?

===Chinese===
「那我就试试，先生。」
「你想要多少报酬？」
「我想，我的薪水会停吧？」
「我是说，除此之外。」
「哦，不要什么，先生；能做这工作，我很乐意。」
主席差点噎住；他往后一靠，打量着这个年轻人。要么他们面对的是个傻子，要么是个极其精明的政客。若是前者，他们能信他到几分；若是后者，他图的是什么？

===Original===
“Of course, there’ll be considerable travelling,” the chairman ventured, looking reflectively out of the window.
“Yes, sir, I suppose so.”
“We might pay the railroad fare.”
“Thank you, sir. When shall I begin?”
The chairman consulted his calendar.
“Suppose you hold yourself in readiness for one week from today.”
“All right,” and Bles rose. “Good day, gentlemen.”
But the chairman was still puzzled.
“Now, what’s his game?” he asked helplessly.
“He may be honest,” offered Senator Smith, contemplating the door almost wistfully.

===Chinese===
「当然，这要跑不少地方，」主席试探着说，若有所思地望向窗外。
「是的，先生，我想是。」
「车马费我们或许可以出。」
「谢谢您，先生。我什么时候开始？」
主席翻了翻日程。
「就请你从今天起一星期后待命吧。」
「好，」布莱斯站起身。「日安，先生们。」
可主席仍是一头雾水。
「他到底图什么？」他无奈地问。
「说不定他是诚实的，」史密斯参议员接了一句，几乎是怅然地望着那扇门。

===Original===
The campaign progressed. The National Republican Committee said little about the Negro revolt and affected to ignore it. The papers were silent. Underneath this calm, however, the activity was redoubled. The prominent Negroes were carefully catalogued, written to, and put under personal influence. The Negro papers were quietly subsidized, and they began to ridicule and reproach the new leaders.
As the Fall progressed, mass-meetings were held in Washington and the small towns. Larger and larger ones were projected, and more and more Alwyn was pushed to the front. He was developing into a most effective speaker. He had the voice, the presence, the ideas, and above all he was intensely in earnest. There were other colored orators with voice, presence, and eloquence; but their people knew their record and discounted them. Alwyn was new, clear, and sincere, and the black folk hung on his words.

===Chinese===
竞选推进着。全国共和党委员会对黑人的反水几乎不置一词，装出一副不予理会的样子。报纸也噤了声。然而这一片平静之下，暗中活动却有增无减。知名的黑人被仔细造册、写信、施加个人影响。黑人的报纸被悄悄收买，开始嘲弄、非难那些新领袖。
随着秋深，华盛顿和那些小镇纷纷开起群众大会。规模越办越大，阿尔文也越来越多地被推到台前。他正成长为一个极具感染力的演说家。他有嗓音、有气度、有见地，而最重要的是，他无比真诚。也有别的有色人种演说家，有嗓音、有气度、有口才；可他们的人清楚他们的底细，自然要打折扣。阿尔文却是新的、清白的、诚挚的，黑人们屏息听他说话。

===Original===
Large and larger crowds greeted him until he was the central figure in a half dozen great negro mass-meetings in the chief cities of the country, culminating in New York the night before election. Perhaps the secret newspaper work, the personal advice of employers and friends, and the liberal distribution of cash, would have delivered a large part of the Negro vote to the Republican candidate. Perhaps﻿—but there was a doubt. With the work of Alwyn, however, all doubt disappeared, and there was little reason for denying that the new President walked into the White House through the instrumentality of an unknown Georgia Negro, little past his majority. This is what Senator Smith said to Mr. Easterly; what Miss Wynn said to herself; and it was what Mrs. Vanderpool remarked to Zora as Zora was combing her hair on the Wednesday after election.

===Chinese===
迎接他的人群越聚越大，直到他成了全国各大城市六七场黑人群众大会的中心人物，并在选举前夜于纽约达到顶点。也许那些暗中操作的报纸文章、雇主朋友的私人劝告，加上大手大脚撒下的现钞，本就能把大部分黑人选票送到共和党候选人手里。也许——但还存着疑。然而有了阿尔文的奔走，一切疑虑烟消云散，几乎无可否认的是：新总统是踏着一个佐治亚黑人、一个刚刚年满二十一的无名之辈的肩膀走进白宫的。这是史密斯参议员对伊斯特利先生说的；是怀恩小姐对自己说的；也是选举后那个星期三，佐拉给范德普尔太太梳头时，范德普尔太太对佐拉说的。

===Original===
Zora murmured an indistinct response. As already something of the beauty of the world had found question and answer in her soul, and as she began to realize how the world had waxed old in thought and stature, so now in their last days a sense of the power of men, as set over against the immensity and force of their surroundings, became real to her. She had begun to read of the lives and doing of those called great, and in her mind a plan was forming. She saw herself standing dim within the shadows, directing the growing power of a man: a man who would be great as the world counted greatness, rich, high in position, powerful﻿—wonderful because his face was black. He would never see her; never know how she worked and planned, save perhaps at last, in that supreme moment as she passed, her soul would cry to his, “Redeemed!” And he would understand.

===Chinese===
佐拉含混地应了一声。正如这世界的一些美，早已在她灵魂里找到过疑问与回响，正如她开始领会这世界在思想和身量上是何等苍老，如今在这些苍老岁月的末了，一种『人的权能』之感——对着那浩瀚而有力的周遭——在她心里变得真切起来。她开始读那些所谓伟人的生平和行止，一个计划在她脑中成形。她看见自己朦胧地立在阴影里，指引着一个男人日益增长的权势：一个按世俗标准衡量堪称伟大的人，富有、位高、有权——而奇妙之处在于，他的脸是黑的。他永不会看见她；永不会知道她怎样操劳、怎样谋划，也许唯有最后，在她悄然离去的那至高一刻，她的灵魂会向他的灵魂呼喊一声：『救赎了！』而他便会懂。

===Original===
All this she was thinking and weaving; not clearly and definitely, but in great blurred clouds of thought of things as she said slowly:
“He should have a great position for this.”
“Why, certainly,” Mrs. Vanderpool agreed, and then curiously: “What?”
Zora considered. “Negroes,” she said, “have been Registers of the Treasury, and Recorders of Deeds here in Washington, and Douglas was Marshal; but I want Bles﻿—” she paused and started again. “Those are not great enough for Mr. Alwyn; he should have an office so important that Negroes would not think of leaving their party again.”

===Chinese===
这一切她都在想、都在编织；不是清清楚楚、确确切切地，而是在一团团模糊巨大的思绪云雾里——她慢慢地说：
『他该为此得一个了不得的位子。』
『那当然，』范德普尔太太附和，随即好奇道：『什么位子？』
佐拉想了想。『黑人嘛，』她说，『做过财政部注册司长，做过华盛顿的契约登记官，道格拉斯也做过联邦执法官；可我希望布莱斯——』她顿了顿，又重新开口。『这些对阿尔文先生而言都不够大；他该得一个重要到让黑人再也不会动叛党念头的职位。』

===Original===
Mrs. Vanderpool took pains to repeat Zora’s words to Mr. Easterly. He considered the matter.
“In one sense, it’s good advice,” he admitted; “but there’s the South to reckon with. I’ll think it over and speak to the President. Oh, yes; I’m going to mention France at the same time.”
Mrs. Vanderpool smiled and leaned back in her carriage. She noted with considerable interest the young colored woman who was watching her from the sidewalk: a brown, well-appearing young woman of notable self-possession. Caroline Wynn scrutinized Mrs. Vanderpool because she had been speaking with Mr. Easterly, and Mr. Easterly was a figure of political importance. That very morning Miss Wynn had telegraphed Bles Alwyn. Alwyn arrived at Washington just as the morning papers heralded the sweeping Republican victory. All about he met new deference and new friends; strangers greeted him familiarly on the street; Sam Stillings became his shadow; and when he reported for work his chief and fellow clerks took unusual interest in him.

===Chinese===
范德普尔太太特意把佐拉这番话转述给伊斯特利先生。他思量了一番。
『在某种意义上，这是条好建议，』他承认；『不过还得顾及南方。我考虑考虑，去跟总统说一声。哦对了；我打算同时把法国那事提一提。』
范德普尔太太笑了笑，在马车里向后一靠。她颇为有趣地注意到，人行道上有个年轻的有色人种女子正盯着她看：一个棕色皮肤、仪态得体、异常沉着的年轻女子。卡罗琳·怀恩之所以打量范德普尔太太，是因为方才她正与伊斯特利先生交谈，而伊斯特利先生是政界要人。就在那天早上，怀恩小姐给布莱斯·阿尔文发了电报。阿尔文抵达华盛顿时，晨报正宣扬着共和党大获全胜。他四处碰见的，都是新的恭敬和新的朋友；陌生人在街上熟络地跟他打招呼；萨姆·斯蒂林斯成了他的影子；等他去报到上班时，他的上司和同僚文书对他都格外热切。

===Original===
“Have you seen Senator Smith yet?” Miss Wynn asked after a few words of congratulation.
“No. What for?”
“What for?” she answered. “Go to him today; don’t fail. I shall be at home at eight tonight.”
It seemed to Bles an exceedingly silly thing to do﻿—calling on a busy man with no errand; but he went. He decided that he would just thank the Senator for his interest, and get out; or, if the Senator was busy, he would merely send in his card. Evidently the Senator was busy, for his waiting-room was full. Bles handed the card to the secretary with a word of apology, but the secretary detained him.

===Chinese===
『你见过史密斯参议员了吗？』寒暄了几句祝贺之后，怀恩小姐问。
『没有。见他做什么？』
『做什么？』她答。『今天就去见他；别耽搁。我今晚八点在家。』
布莱斯觉得这事蠢透了——无缘无故去打扰一个忙人；可他还是去了。他打定主意，只谢谢参议员的关照就走；要是参议员忙，就只递张名片。参议员显然忙，因为候见室坐满了人。布莱斯把名片递给秘书，道了一声扰，秘书却留住了他。

===Original===
“Ah, Mr. Alwyn,” he said affably; “glad to see you. The Senator will want to see you, I know. Wait just a minute.” And soon Bles was shaking Senator Smith’s hand.
“Well, Mr. Alwyn,” said the Senator heartily, “you delivered the goods.”
“Thank you, sir. I tried to.”
Senator Smith thoughtfully looked him over and drew out the letters.
“Your friends, Mr. Alwyn,” he said, adjusting his glasses, “have a rather high opinion of you. Here now is Stillings, who helped on the campaign. He suggests an eighteen-hundred-dollar clerkship for you.” The Senator glanced up keenly and omitted to state what Stillings suggested for himself. Alwyn was visibly grateful as well as surprised.

===Chinese===
『啊，阿尔文先生，』他和气地说；『见到你很高兴。参议员一定愿意见你，我知道。稍等片刻。』很快，布莱斯便握上了史密斯参议员的手。
『好啦，阿尔文先生，』参议员热忱地说，『你交了货。』
『谢谢您，先生。我尽力了。』
史密斯参议员若有所思地打量他，抽出那几封信。
『你的朋友们，阿尔文先生，』他正了正眼镜说，『对你的评价相当高。这儿是斯蒂林斯，竞选里也出过力。他建议给你一个一千八百元的文书职位。』参议员锐利地抬眼一瞥，没提斯蒂林斯为自己建议了什么。阿尔文既感激又惊讶，溢于言表。

===Original===
“I﻿—I hoped,” he began hesitatingly, “that perhaps I might get a promotion, but I had not thought of a first-class clerkship.”
“H’m.” Senator Smith leaned back and twiddled his thumbs, staring at Alwyn until the hot blood darkened his cheeks. Then Bles sat up and stared politely but steadily back. The Senator’s eyes dropped and he put out his hand for the second note.
“Now, your friend, Miss Wynn”﻿—Alwyn started﻿—“is even more ambitious.” He handed her letter to the young man, and pointed out the words.
“Of course, Senator,” Bles read, “we expect Mr. Alwyn to be the next Register of the Treasury.”

===Chinese===
『我——我曾盼着，』他踌躇着开口，『也许能升一升，可没想过会是一等文书。』
『嗯。』史密斯参议员往后一靠，扳着手指头，盯着阿尔文，直盯得热血涌上年轻人的脸颊。于是布莱斯坐直了身子，礼貌而坚定地回望。参议员移开目光，伸手去拿第二张便笺。
『那么，你的朋友，怀恩小姐——』阿尔文一惊——『更有野心。』他把她的信递给年轻人，点出那几行字。
『当然，参议员，』布莱斯读到，『我们期望阿尔文先生出任下一任财政部注册司长。』

===Original===
Bles looked up in amazement, but the Senator reached for a third letter. The room was very still. At last he found it. “This,” he announced quietly, “is from a man of great power and influence, who has the ear of the new President.” He smoothed out the letter, paused briefly, then read aloud:
“‘It has been suggested to me by’﻿”—the Senator did not read the name; if he had “Mrs. Vanderpool” would have meant little to Alwyn—“‘It has been suggested to me by blank that the future allegiance of the Negro vote to the Republican Party might be insured by giving to some prominent Negro a high political position﻿—for instance, Treasurer of the United States’﻿—salary, six thousand dollars,” interpolated Senator Smith—“‘and that Alwyn would be a popular and safe appointment for that position.’”

===Chinese===
布莱斯惊愕地抬头，参议员却已伸手去拿第三封信。屋里很静。他终于找到了。『这封，』他平静地宣布，『出自一位权势煊赫、能对新总统说上话的人物。』他把信抚平，略一顿，朗声道：
『「有人向我建议——」』参议员没念那名字；即使念了，『范德普尔太太』对阿尔文也意义不大——『「有人向我建议，若要保证日后黑人选票对共和党的忠诚，可授予某位杰出黑人一个高位——譬如，合众国财务长」——年薪六千元，』史密斯参议员插了一句——『「而阿尔文出任此职，将是一个得人心又稳妥的任命。」』

===Original===
The Senator did not read the concluding sentence, which ran: “Think this over; we can’t touch political conditions in the South; perhaps this sop will do.”
For a long time Alwyn sat motionless, while the Senator said nothing. Then the young man rose unsteadily.
“I don’t think I quite grasp all this,” he said as he shook hands. “I’ll think it over,” and he went out.

===Chinese===
参议员没念最后一句，那一句写的是：『斟酌一下；南方的政治状况我们动不得；也许这块骨头能应付过去。』
阿尔文一动不动地坐了很久，参议员也没作声。随后年轻人晃晃悠悠地站起来。
『我想，我还没完全消化这一切，』他一边握手一边说。『我考虑考虑，』便出去了。

===Original===
When Caroline Wynn heard of that extraordinary conversation her amazement knew no bounds. Yet Alwyn ventured to voice doubts:
“I’m not fitted for either of those high offices; there are many others who deserve more, and I don’t somehow like the idea of seeming to have worked hard in the campaign simply for money or fortune. You see, I talked against that very thing.”
Miss Wynn’s eyes widened.
“Well, what else﻿—” she began and then changed. “Mr. Alwyn, the line between virtue and foolishness is dim and wavering, and I should hate to see you lost in that marshy borderland. By a streak of extraordinary luck you have gained the political leadership of Negroes in America. Here’s your chance to lead your people, and here you stand blinking and hesitating. Be a man!”

===Chinese===
卡罗琳·怀恩听说了那场离奇的对话，惊愕得无以复加。可阿尔文竟敢说出自己的疑虑：
『这两个高位我哪个也担不起；还有许多人更有资格，而且我总觉得，让人看着好像我在竞选中卖力，纯粹是为钱为利，这不太……你瞧，我演讲时恰恰反对的就是这个。』
怀恩小姐睁大了眼。
『那又怎——』她开了口，又转了话锋。『阿尔文先生，美德与愚蠢的界线是模糊而游移的，我可不愿眼看你迷失在那片泥泞的边境上。凭着一道异乎寻常的运气，你已得到了美国黑人的政治领袖地位。这正是你引领本族人的良机，你倒站在这儿眨巴着眼、犹豫不决。拿出点男子气来！』

===Original===
Alwyn straightened up and felt his doubts going. The evening passed very pleasantly.
“I’m going to have a little dinner for you,” said Miss Wynn finally, and Alwyn grew hot with pleasure. He turned to her suddenly and said:
“Why, I’m rather﻿—black.” She expressed no surprise but said reflectively:
“You *are* dark.”
“And I’ve been given to understand that Miss Wynn and her set rather﻿—well, preferred the lighter shades of colored folk.”

===Chinese===
阿尔文挺直了身，觉得自己的疑虑正在消散。这个傍晚过得十分愉快。
『我要为你办一次小小的晚宴，』怀恩小姐最后说，阿尔文高兴得发烫。他忽然转向她，说：
『可我……挺——黑的。』她毫无惊讶之色，只若有所思地说：
『你*确实*黑。』
『而且我听说，怀恩小姐和她那个圈子多少——嗯，更偏爱肤色浅些的有色人。』

===Original===
Miss Wynn laughed lightly.
“My parents did,” she said simply. “No dark man ever entered their house; they were simply copying the white world. Now I, as a matter of aesthetic beauty, prefer your brown-velvet color to a jaundiced yellow, or even an uncertain cream; but the world doesn’t.”
“The world?”
“Yes, the world; and especially America. One may be Chinese, Spaniard, even Indian﻿—anything white or dirty white in this land, and demand decent treatment; but to be Negro or darkening toward it unmistakably means perpetual handicap and crucifixion.”

===Chinese===
怀恩小姐轻轻一笑。
『我父母是那样，』她简简单单地说。『深色皮肤的男人从不进他们家的门；他们不过是在学白人世界的样。如今我呢，单论审美之美，我宁愿要你这种天鹅绒般的棕色，胜过病恹恹的黄，甚至不确定的奶油色；可这世界不这样。』
『这世界？』
『对，这世界；尤其是美国。在这一国，一个人可以是华人、西班牙人、甚至印第安人——任何白的、或脏白的人——都可以要求体面的对待；可一旦是黑人、或无可置疑地向着黑人变深，就意味着永恒的劣势与磨难。』

===Original===
“Why not, then, admit that you draw the color-line?”
“Because I don’t; but the world does. I am not prejudiced as my parents were, but I am foresighted. Indeed, it is a deep ethical query, is it not, how far one has the right to bear black children to the world in the Land of the Free and the home of the brave. Is it fair﻿—to the children?”
“Yes, it is!” he cried vehemently. “The more to take up the fight, the surer the victory.”
She laughed at his earnestness.
“You are refreshing,” she said. “Well, we’ll dine next Tuesday, and we’ll have the cream of our world to meet you.”

===Chinese===
『那么，何不承认你也在划肤色界线呢？』
『因为我没有；可这世界有。我不像我父母那样存偏见，但我有远见。说真的，这是一道深刻的伦理难题，不是吗——在这片自由之土、勇者之家里，一个人到底有多大的权利，把黑色的孩子带到这世上来。这对孩子——公道吗？』
『公道的！』他激烈地喊。『多一个投身这场战斗的人，胜利就更有把握。』
她笑他的认真。
『你真叫人耳目一新，』她说。『好，下星期二我们吃饭，我把我们这个圈子里最拔尖的人请来见你。』

===Original===
He knew that this was a great triumph. It flattered his vanity. After all, he was entering this higher dark world whose existence had piqued and puzzled him so long. He glanced at Miss Wynn beside him there in the dimly lighted parlor: she looked so aloof and unapproachable, so handsome and so elegant. He thought how she would complete a house﻿—such a home as his prospective four or six thousand dollars a year could easily purchase. She saw him surveying her, and she smiled at him.
“I find but one fault with you,” she said.

===Chinese===
他知道这是一场了不起的胜利。它满足了他的虚荣。毕竟，他正在进入这个更高层的黑色世界——这个世界存在了这么久，一直撩拨他、困惑他。他瞥了一眼昏暗客厅里身旁的怀恩小姐：她看上去那样超然、不可亲近，那样标致、那样优雅。他想，她会怎样圆满一个家——这样一户人家，凭他那可预期的四千或六千块年薪，轻而易举便能置办得起。她察觉他在打量自己，便朝他一笑。
『我在你身上只挑出一个毛病，』她说。

===Original===
He stammered for a pretty speech, but did not find it before she continued:
“Yes﻿—you are so delightfully primitive; you will not use the world as it is but insist on acting as if it were something else.”
“I am not sure I understand.”
“Well, there is the wife of my Judge: she is a fact in my world; in yours she is a problem to be stated, straightened, and solved. If she had come to you, as she did to me yesterday, with her theory that all that Southern Negroes needed was to learn how to make good servants and lay brick﻿—”
“I should have shown her﻿—” Bles tried to interject.

===Chinese===
他结结巴巴想凑一句漂亮话，可还没凑出来，她又接着说：
『是啊——你这份质朴真叫人欢喜；你偏不肯按这世界本来的样子用它，非要照着它『应当是』的样子行事。』
『我不太明白。』
『这样吧，就拿我那位法官的太太来说：在我的世界里，她是个事实；在你的世界里，她是一道有待陈述、理清、解答的题目。倘若她来找你——就像昨天她来找我那样——带着她那套理论，说南方黑人需要的无非是学会做好仆役、学会砌砖——』
『我就会让她瞧瞧——』布莱斯想插话。

===Original===
“Nothing of the sort. You would have tried to show her and would have failed miserably. She hasn’t learned anything in twenty years.”
“But surely you didn’t join her in advocating that ten million people be menials?”
“Oh, no; I simply listened.”
“Well, there was no harm in that; I believe in silence at times.”
“Ah! but I did not listen like a log, but positively and eloquently; with a nod, a half-formed word, a comment begun, which she finished.”

===Chinese===
『才不会。你会想让她瞧瞧，然后败得一塌糊涂。她二十年里什么也没学会。』
『可你总不会附和她，主张让一千万人都去当家奴吧？』
『哦，不；我只是听着。』
『那倒没什么坏处；有时候我也信奉沉默。』
『啊！可我不是像根木头那样听，我是积极地、雄辩地听；一点头，半个字，一句起了头的议论，由她来接完。』

===Original===
Bles frowned.
“As a result,” continued Miss Wynn, “I have a check for five hundred dollars to finish our cooking-school and buy a cast of Minerva for the assembly-room. More than that, I have now a wealthy friend. She thinks me an unusually clever person who, by a process of thought not unlike her own, has arrived at very similar conclusions.”
“But﻿—but,” objected Bles, “if the time spent cajoling fools were used in convincing the honest and upright, think how much we would gain.”
“Very little. The honest and upright are a sad minority. Most of these white folk﻿—believe me, boy,” she said caressingly﻿—“are fools and knaves: they don’t want truth or progress; they want to keep niggers down.”

===Chinese===
布莱斯皱起眉。
『结果呢，』怀恩小姐接着说，『我得到了一张五百块的支票，用来办完我们的烹饪学校，再给礼堂买一尊密涅瓦的石膏像。不止如此，我还多了一个阔朋友。她觉得我是个格外聪明的人，凭着一番与她颇为相似的心思，得出了与她十分相近的结论。』
『可是——可是，』布莱斯反驳，『要是把哄骗傻子所花的时间，拿去说服那些诚实正直的人，想想我们能赢得多少。』
『微乎其微。诚实正直的人是可悲的少数。这些白人里头大多数——信我吧，孩子，』她爱抚地说——『都是蠢货和恶棍：他们不要真理，不要进步；他们只想把黑鬼踩在脚下。』

===Original===
“I don’t believe it; there are scores, thousands, perhaps millions such, I admit; but the average American loves justice and right, and he is the one to whom I appeal with frankness and truth. Great heavens! don’t you love to be frank and open?”
She narrowed her eyelids.
“Yes, sometimes I do; once I was; but it’s a luxury few of us Negroes can afford. Then, too, I insist that it’s jolly to fool them.”
“Don’t you hate the deception?”
She chuckled and put her head to one side.
“At first I did; but, do you know, now I believe I prefer it.”

===Chinese===
『我不信；这种人几十个、几千个、也许几百万个，我承认；可普通的美国人是热爱正义与公道的，而正是对他们，我才以坦诚和真话相求。老天！难道你就不喜欢坦坦荡荡、敞敞亮亮吗？』
她眯起眼。
『喜欢，有时候喜欢；从前我也是这样的；可这是一种享受，我们黑人里没几个消受得起。再者，我偏要说，把他们都蒙在鼓里，怪有意思的。』
『你不讨厌这种欺骗吗？』
她咯咯一笑，歪了歪头。
『起初讨厌；可你知道吗，如今我倒宁愿这样。』

===Original===
He looked so horrified that she burst out laughing. He laughed too. She was a puzzle to him. He kept thinking what a mistress of a mansion she would make.
“Why do you say these things?” he asked suddenly.
“Because I want you to do well here in Washington.”
“General philanthropy?”
“No, special.” Her eyes were bright with meaning.
“Then you care﻿—for me?”
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
他一副惊骇相，惹得她放声大笑。他也笑了。对他而言，她是个谜。他老在想，她会是一位多么出色的宅邸女主人。
『你为什么说这些？』他忽然问。
『因为我要你在华盛顿过得好。』
『泛爱众生？』
『不，是特殊的。』她眼里闪着意味。
『那么，你在乎——我？』
『是的。』

===Original===
He bent forward and cast the die.
“Enough to marry me?”
She answered very calmly and certainly:
“Yes.”
He leaned toward her. And then between him and her lips a dark and shadowy face; two great storm-swept eyes looked into his out of a world of infinite pain, and he dropped his head in hesitation and shame, and kissed her hand. Miss Wynn thought him delightfully bashful.

===Chinese===
他倾过身，掷下了骰子。
『那么，愿意嫁给我吗？』
她极其平静、极其笃定地答：
『愿意。』
他向她倾身过去。就在这时，横在他和她的双唇之间的，是一张黝黑而幽暗的脸；一双饱经风雨的大眼睛，从一个无尽痛苦的世界里，望进了他的眼。他迟疑地、羞愧地垂下头，吻了她的手。怀恩小姐只觉得他这副腼腆相，可爱极了。

## XXVI Congressman Cresswell / XXVI 克雷斯韦尔众议员

===Original===
The election of Harry Cresswell to Congress was a very simple matter. The Colonel and his son drove to town and consulted the Judge; together they summoned the sheriff and the local member of the State legislature.
“I think it’s about time that we Cresswells asked for a little of the political pie,” the Colonel smilingly opened.
“Well, what do you want?” asked the Judge.
“Harry wants to go to Congress.”
The Judge hesitated. “We’d half promised that to Caldwell,” he objected.

===Chinese===
把哈里·克雷斯韦尔选进国会，是件再简单不过的事。上校和他儿子驱车进城，去找法官商议；他们一道叫来了警长和州议会那名本地议员。
『我想，我们克雷斯韦尔家该来讨一块政治馅饼尝尝了，是时候了，』上校笑吟吟地开了场。
『那么，你们想要什么？』法官问。
『哈里想进国会。』
法官犹豫了。『这事我们已经半个口风许给考德威尔了，』他反对。

===Original===
“It will be a little costly this year, too,” suggested the sheriff, tentatively.
“About how much?” asked the Colonel.
“At least five thousand,” said the Legislator.
The Colonel said nothing. He simply wrote a check and the matter was settled. In the Fall Harry Cresswell was declared elected. There were four hundred and seventy-two votes cast but the sheriff added a cipher. He said it would look better.

===Chinese===
『今年怕是也要费一点钱，』警长试探着提了一句。
『大约要多少？』上校问。
『至少五千。』那位州议员说。
上校没再说什么。他干脆开了一张支票，事情便定了下来。到了秋天，哈里·克雷斯韦尔被宣布当选。实际投出的票是四百七十二张，可警长在后面添了个零。他说这样好看些。

===Original===
Early December found the Cresswells domiciled in a small house in Du Pont Circle, Washington. They had an automobile and four servants, and the house was furnished luxuriously. Mary Taylor Cresswell, standing in her morning room and looking out on the flowers of the square, told herself that few people in the world had cause to be as happy as she. She was tastefully gowned, in a way to set off her blonde beauty and her delicate rounded figure. She was surrounded with wealth, and above all, she was in that atmosphere of aristocracy for which she had always yearned; and already she was acquiring that poise of the head, and a manner of directing the servants, which showed her born to the purple.

===Chinese===
十二月初，克雷斯韦尔一家在华盛顿杜邦圆环的一幢小宅子里安顿下来。他们有一辆汽车、四个仆人，屋里陈设奢靡。玛丽·泰勒·克雷斯韦尔站在她的晨间起居室里，望着广场上的花，暗自说，世上怕是没几个人有她这般快活的理由。她衣着雅致，恰到好处地衬出她金发的美貌和玲珑有致的身段。她被财富簇拥着，而最要紧的是，她置身于她一向向往的那种贵族氛围里；她已经学得了那一份颔首的姿态、那一份支使仆人的做派，显见是天生该穿紫袍的。

===Original===
She had cause to be extremely happy, she told herself this morning, and yet she was puzzled to understand why she was not. Why was she restless and vaguely ill at ease so often these days?
One matter, indeed, did worry her; but that would right itself in time, she was sure. She had always pictured herself as directing her husband’s work. She did not plan to step in and demand a share; she knew from experience with her brother that a woman must prove her usefulness to a man before he will admit it, and even then he may be silent. She intended gradually and tactfully to relieve her husband of care connected with his public life so that, before he realized it, she would be his guiding spirit and his inspiration. She had dreamed the details of doing this so long that it seemed already done, and she could imagine no obstacle to its realization. And yet she found herself today no nearer her goal than when first she married. Not because Mr. Cresswell did not share his work, but because, apparently, he had no work, no duties, no cares.

===Chinese===
她有理由快活得不得了，这天早上她对自己说，可她纳罕，自己为什么并不快活。这些天她为什么时常心神不宁、莫名地不自在呢？
有一件事，确实叫她烦心；但她相信，那迟早会自己归位的。她一向在脑海里描绘自己如何指点丈夫的工作。她没打算横插进去、讨一份差事；她从哥哥身上的经验知道，一个女人得先在一个男人面前证明自己有用，他才肯认账，即便如此，他也未必作声。她打算渐渐地、婉转地，替丈夫分担他公众生活里的操劳，让他不知不觉间，便以她为引路的灵魂、为灵感的源泉。这其中的种种细节她梦了太久，久到仿佛早已做成，她想不出实现它还能有什么障碍。然而今天她发觉，自己离这个目标并不比新婚时更近一步。倒不是因为克雷斯韦尔先生不肯同她分担工作，而是因为——显然——他根本没有工作，没有职责，没有操劳。

===Original===
At first, in the dim glories of the honeymoon, this seemed but part of his delicate courtesy toward her, and it pleased her despite her thrifty New England nature; but now that they were settled in Washington, the election over and Congress in session, it really seemed time for Work and Life to begin in dead earnest, and New England Mary was dreaming mighty dreams and golden futures.
But Harry apparently was as content as ever with doing nothing. He arose at ten, dined at seven, and went to bed between midnight and sunrise. There was some committee meetings and much mail, but Mary was admitted to knowledge of none of these. The obvious step, of course, would be to set him at work; but from this undertaking Mary unconsciously recoiled.

===Chinese===
起初，在蜜月那种朦胧的光辉里，这似乎不过是他对她那份体贴周到的殷勤之一，纵然有违她节俭的新英格兰天性，她还是欢喜；可如今既已在华盛顿安顿下来，选举过了，国会也开议了，实在该是正经『工作与生活』开始的时候了，新英格兰的玛丽正做着宏大的梦、金色的未来。
可哈里显然一如既往地满足于无所事事。他十点起床，七点吃饭，半夜到日出之间上床睡觉。有那么几个委员会会议，还有一大堆邮件，但这些玛丽一个也不得与闻。当然，显而易见的一步，便是催他去干活；可这事玛丽下意识地退避了。

===Original===
She had already recognized that while her tastes and her husband’s were mostly alike, they were also strikingly different in many respects. They agreed in the daintiness of things, the elegance of detail; but they did not agree always as to the things themselves. Given the picture, they would choose the same frame﻿—but they would not choose the same picture. They liked the same voice, but not the same song; the same company, but not the same conversation. Of course, Mary reflected, frowning at the flowers﻿—of course, this must always be so when two human beings are thrown into new and intimate association. In time they would grow to sweet communion; only, she hoped the communion would be on tastes nearer hers than those he sometimes manifested.

===Chinese===
她早已看出，自己的趣味与丈夫的虽大体相投，在许多方面却也判然有别。在事物的精致上、细节的优雅上，他们是一致的；可在事物本身上，他们并不总是一致。给定了画，他们会挑同一个框——可他们不会挑同一幅画。他们喜欢同一种嗓音，却不是同一首歌；喜欢同一群人，却不是同一种话题。当然，玛丽对着花皱眉思忖——当然啦，两个人骤然被抛进一种崭新的、亲密的相处里，向来都是这样的。假以时日，他们终会相知相契；只是她盼望，那份相契会更贴近她的趣味，而不是他有时流露出来的那种。

===Original===
She turned impatiently from the window with a feeling of loneliness. But why lonely? She idly fingered a new book on the table and then put it down sharply. There had been several attempts at reading aloud between them some evenings ago, and this book reminded her of them. She had bought Jane Addams’ “Newer Ideals of Peace,” and he had yawned over it undisguisedly. Then he had brought this novel, and﻿—well, she had balked at the second chapter, and he had kissed her and called her his “little prude.”

===Chinese===
