===Chinese===
直到第二天，两个女人才镇定下来，能理智地商量事情。
『你的打算是什么？』佐拉问。
『把钱存进北方一家储蓄银行，吃三分利；利息的差额，还有办学经费的亏空，从我们的余额里贴；再送你北上募捐。』
佐拉摇头。『这不成，』她反对。『我是个蹩脚的募捐人；我不够懂人情，我也不能照有钱的白人指望我们说话的那种样子，去跟他们说话。』

===Original===
“It wouldn’t be hypocrisy, Zora; you would be serving in a great cause. If you don’t go, I﻿—”
“Wait! You shan’t go. If anyone goes it must be me. But let’s think it out: we pay off the mortgage, we get enough to run the school as it has been run. Then what? There will still be slavery and oppression all around us. The children will be kept in the cotton fields; the men will be cheated, and the women﻿—” Zora paused and her eyes grew hard.

===Chinese===
『那不算虚伪，佐拉；你是在为一桩大事业效力。你不去，那我——』
『等等！你不能去。要有人去，那只能是我。可咱们把这事儿想透：我们还清了抵押，弄到够把钱学校照旧办下去。然后呢？我们四周照样还是奴役和压迫。孩子们还是会被摁在棉花地里；男人们还是会被欺骗，女人们还是——』佐拉顿住，眼神变硬。

===Original===
She began again rapidly: “We must have land﻿—our own farm with our own tenants﻿—to be the beginning of a free community.”
Miss Smith threw up her hands impatiently.
“But sakes alive! Where, Zora? Where can we get land, with Cresswell owning every inch and bound to destroy us?”
Zora sat hugging her knees and staring out the window toward the sombre ramparts of the swamp. In her eyes lay slumbering the madness of long ago; in her brain danced all the dreams and visions of childhood.
“I’m thinking,” she murmured, “of buying the swamp.”

===Chinese===
她又飞快地接下去：『我们必须有地——我们自己的农场、我们自己的佃户——作为一个自由社区的起点。』
史密斯小姐不耐烦地一摊手。
『可老天爷！在哪儿，佐拉？我们上哪儿弄到地？克雷斯韦尔每一寸地都占着，又铁了心要毁掉我们。』
佐拉抱着膝盖坐着，望着窗外沼泽那阴沉的壁垒。她眼里沉睡着久远的狂气；她脑中跳跃着童年所有的梦与幻象。
『我在想，』她低语，『把那片沼泽买下来。』

## XXXIII The Buying of the Swamp / XXXIII 买下沼泽

===Original===
“It’s a shame,” asserted John Taylor with something like real feeling. He was spending Sunday with his father-in-law, and both, over their after-dinner cigars, were gazing thoughtfully at the swamp.
“What’s a shame?” asked Colonel Cresswell.
“To see all that timber and prime cotton-land going to waste. Don’t you remember those fine bales of cotton that came out of there several seasons ago?”
The Colonel smoked placidly. “You can’t get it cleared,” he said.
“But couldn’t you hire some good workers?”
“Niggers won’t work. Now if we had Italians we might do it.”

===Chinese===
『太可惜了，』约翰·泰勒断言，带了几分真切的感触。他这个星期日在岳父家过，两人饭后就着雪茄，都若有所思地望着那片沼泽。
『什么可惜？』克雷斯韦尔上校问。
『眼看着那么多木材和上好的棉田就这么荒着。你不记得几年前从那儿出来的那几包好棉花了吗？』
上校平平静静地抽着烟。『你没法把它开出来，』他说。
『可你难道不能雇些好手？』
『黑鬼不肯干活。我们要是有意大利人，倒还做得成。』

===Original===
“Yes, and in a few years they’d own the country.”
“That’s right; so there we are. There’s only one way to get that swamp cleared.”
“How?”
“Sell it to some fool darkey.”
“Sell it? It’s too valuable to sell.”
“That’s just it. You don’t understand. The only way to get decent work out of some niggers is to let them believe they’re buying land. In nine cases out of ten he works hard a while and then throws up the job. We get back our land and he makes good wages for his work.”

===Chinese===
『是啊，过不了几年，这国家就是他们的了。』
『一点不错；所以就这么回事。要开那片沼泽，只有一个法子。』
『什么法子？』
『卖给哪个傻黑佬。』
『卖？那地太值钱，不能卖。』
『问题就在这儿。你不懂。要从某些黑鬼身上榨出像样的活计，唯一的法子，就是让他们以为自己在买地。十回有九回，他卖力干上一阵，就撂挑子了。地又回到我们手里，而他挣的工钱也不算少。』

===Original===
“But in the tenth case﻿—suppose he should stick to it?”
“Oh,”﻿—easily, “we could get rid of him when we want to. White people rule here.”
John Taylor frowned and looked a little puzzled. He was no moralist, but he had his code and he did not understand Colonel Cresswell. As a matter of fact, Colonel Cresswell was an honest man. In most matters of commerce between men he was punctilious to a degree almost annoying to Taylor. But there was one part of the world which his code of honor did not cover, and he saw no incongruity in the omission. The uninitiated cannot easily picture to himself the mental attitude of a former slaveholder toward property in the hands of a Negro. Such property belonged of right to the master, if the master needed it; and since ridiculous laws safeguarded the property, it was perfectly permissible to circumvent such laws. No Negro starved on the Cresswell place, neither did any accumulate property. Colonel Cresswell saw to both matters.

===Chinese===
『可第十回呢——万一他真死磕下去呢？』
『哦，』他轻描淡写地，『我们想要他走时，自然有法子叫他走。这里说了算的是白人。』
约翰·泰勒皱起眉，有点困惑。他不是个讲道德的人，可他有自己的准则，他弄不懂克雷斯韦尔上校。事实上，克雷斯韦尔上校是个诚实人。在大多数人与人之间的商业往来上，他讲求一丝不苟，几乎到了令泰勒厌烦的地步。但这世上有一块地方，他的荣誉准则是不覆盖的，而他看不见这遗漏有什么不妥。未经点化的人，很难想象一个昔日奴隶主，对黑人之手的财产，抱着怎样的心思。这样的财产，若是主子需要，便理当归主子所有；而既然是些荒唐的法律在保护这财产，那么绕开这些法律，便是完全许可的。克雷斯韦尔的地界上，没有黑人饿死，也没有哪个攒得起家业。这两件事，克雷斯韦尔上校都管到了。

===Original===
As the Colonel and John Taylor were thus conferring, Zora appeared, coming up the walk.
“Who’s that?” asked the Colonel shading his eyes.
“It’s Zora﻿—the girl who went North with Mrs. Vanderpool,” Taylor enlightened him.
“Back, is she? Too trifling to stick to a job, and full of Northern nonsense,” growled the Colonel. “Even got a Northern walk﻿—I thought for a moment she was a lady.”

===Chinese===
正当上校和约翰·泰勒这样商议时，佐拉出现了，顺着小径走来。
『那是谁？』上校举手遮眼问。
『是佐拉——跟范德普尔太太北上的那个姑娘，』泰勒为他点破。
『回来了，是吗？干活熬不住，一肚子北方的歪理，』上校咕噜。『连走路都是北方样——我方才还当她是个淑女呢。』

===Original===
Neither of the gentlemen ever dreamed how long, how hard, how heart-wringing was that walk from the gate up the winding way beneath their careless gaze. It was not the coming of the thoughtless, careless girl of five years ago who had marched a dozen times unthinking before the faces of white men. It was the approach of a woman who knew how the world treated women whom it respected; who knew that no such treatment would be thought of in her case: neither the bow, the lifted hat, nor even the conventional title of decency. Yet she must go on naturally and easily, boldly but circumspectly, and play a daring game with two powerful men.
“Can I speak with you a moment, Colonel?” she asked.
The Colonel did not stir or remove his cigar; he even injected a little gruffness into his tone.
“Well, what is it?”

===Chinese===
两位先生做梦也没想到，从大门开始、在他们漫不经心的目光下、沿着那条蜿蜒小径走上来的这一程，有多么漫长、多么艰难、多么揪心。走来的，不是五年前那个没心没肺、大大咧咧的姑娘——那姑娘曾在白人面前大大咧咧地走过十几回。走来的是一个女子，她深知这世界如何礼待它所尊重的女子；她也深知，轮到她头上，谁也不会想起那样的礼待：不会有一鞠躬、不会有一抬帽，甚至连一个体面的寻常称呼都不会有。然而她必须走得自然、从容，大胆而又审慎，与两个权势人物下一盘大胆的棋。
『我能跟您说句话吗，上校？』她问。
上校动也没动，雪茄也没拿下来；他甚至往嗓音里掺了一分粗鲁。
『什么事？』

===Original===
Of course, she was not asked to sit, but she stood with her hands clasped loosely before her and her eyes half veiled.
“Colonel, I’ve got a thousand dollars.” She did not mention the other nine.
The Colonel sat up.
“Where did you get it?” he asked.
“Mrs. Vanderpool gave it to me to use in helping the colored people.”
“What are you going to do with it?”
“Well, that’s just what I came to see you about. You see, I might give it to the school, but I’ve been thinking that I’d like to buy some land for some of the tenants.”

===Chinese===
她当然没被请坐，可她站着，双手松松地交握在身前，眼睛半垂。
『上校，我有一千块钱。』她没提另外那九千。
上校坐直了身子。
『哪儿来的？』他问。
『范德普尔太太给我的，让我用来帮有色人。』
『你打算怎么用它？』
『嗯，这正是我来找您的事。您瞧，我本可以把它捐给学校，可我一直在想，我想给一些佃户买块地。』

===Original===
“I’ve got no land to sell,” said the Colonel.
“I was thinking you might sell a bit of the swamp.”
Cresswell and Taylor glanced at each other and the Colonel re-lit his cigar.
“How much of it?” he asked finally.
“I don’t know; I thought perhaps two hundred acres.”
“Two hundred acres? Do you expect to buy that land for five dollars an acre?”
“Oh, no, sir. I thought it might cost as much as twenty-five dollars.”
“But you’ve only got a thousand dollars.”

===Chinese===
『我没地卖，』上校说。
『我在想，您也许可以卖一块沼泽。』
克雷斯韦尔和泰勒对视一眼，上校重新点了雪茄。
『多少？』他终于问。
『我也不知道；我想也许两百英亩。』
『两百英亩？你指望五块钱一英亩买下那地？』
『哦，不，先生。我想，也许得贵到二十五块。』
『可你只有一千块。』

===Original===
“Yes, sir; I thought I might pay that down and then pay the rest from the crops.”
“Who’s going to work on the place?”
Zora named a number of the steadiest tenants to whom she had spoken.
“They owe me a lot of money,” said the Colonel.
“We’d try to pay that, too.”
Colonel Cresswell considered. There was absolutely no risk. The cost of the land, the back debts of the tenants﻿—no possible crops could pay for them. Then there was the chance of getting the swamp cleared for almost nothing.
“How’s the school getting on?” he asked suddenly.

===Chinese===
『是的，先生；我想可以先付这笔定钱，然后从收成里付清余款。』
『谁来这块地上干活？』
佐拉报了几个她已说过话的、最稳当的佃户的名字。
『他们欠我一大笔钱呢，』上校说。
『我们也想办法还那笔。』
克雷斯韦尔上校盘算着。毫无风险。地的价、佃户的旧债——再好的收成也还不清。再者，还能几乎不花一文地把沼泽开出来。
『学校怎么样了？』他忽然问。

===Original===
“Very poorly,” answered Zora sadly. “You know it’s mortgaged, and Miss Smith has had to use the mortgage money for yearly expenses.”
The Colonel smiled grimly.
“It will cost you fifty dollars an acre,” he said finally. Zora looked disappointed and figured out the matter slowly.
“That would be one thousand down and nine thousand to pay﻿—”
“With interest,” said Cresswell.
Zora shook her head doubtfully.
“What would the interest be?” she asked.
“Ten percent.”

===Chinese===
『很不好，』佐拉难过地答。『您知道它抵押出去了，史密斯小姐不得不把抵押款拿来贴补每年的开支。』
上校冷酷地笑了笑。
『五十块一英亩，』他终于说。佐拉面露失望，慢慢地算这笔账。
『那就是先付一千、再付九千——』
『还要算利息，』克雷斯韦尔说。
佐拉犹疑地摇摇头。
『利息多少呢？』她问。
『一分。』

===Original===
She stood silent a moment and Colonel Cresswell spoke up:
“It’s the best land about here and about the only land you can buy﻿—I wouldn’t sell it to anybody else.”
She still hesitated.
“The trouble is, you see, Colonel Cresswell, the price is high and the interest heavy. And after all I may not be able to get as many tenants as I’d need. I think though, I’d try it if﻿—if I could be sure you’d treat me fairly, and that I’d get the land if I paid for it.”
Colonel Cresswell reddened a little, and John Taylor looked away.
“Well, if you don’t want to undertake it, all right.”

===Chinese===
她站着静默了一会儿，克雷斯韦尔上校开口：
『这是附近最好的地，也是你差不多唯一能买到的地——换别人我不卖。』
她仍在犹豫。
『难处在于，您瞧，克雷斯韦尔上校，价钱高、利息重。再说，我也未必能凑齐所需数目的佃户。不过，我想我会试一试的，要是——要是我能确定您会公道待我，要是我付了钱就能拿到地。』
克雷斯韦尔上校微微红了脸，约翰·泰勒把目光移开。
『好吧，你不愿意做，就算了。』

===Original===
Zora looked thoughtfully across the field﻿—
“Mr. Maxwell has a bit of land,” she began meditatively.
“Worked out, and not worth five dollars an acre!” snapped the Colonel. But he did not propose to hand Maxwell a thousand dollars. “Now, see here, I’ll treat you as well as anybody, and you know it.”
“I believe so, sir,” acknowledged Zora in a tone that brought a sudden keen glance from Taylor; but her face was a mask. “I reckon I’ll make the bargain.”
“All right. Bring the money and we’ll fix the thing up.”
“The money is here,” said Zora, taking an envelope out of her bosom.

===Chinese===
佐拉若有所思地望过田野——
『马克斯韦尔先生有一小块地，』她出神地开口。
『地力耗尽了，五块钱一英亩都不值！』上校抢白。可他不打算把一千块拱手让给马克斯韦尔。『这样吧，我待你不比待任何人差，这个你是知道的。』
『这个我相信，先生，』佐拉承认，那语调引得泰尔尔忽然投来一道锐利的目光；可她的脸是一副面具。『我想，这买卖我做了。』
『好。把钱带来，咱们就把事办了。』
『钱在这儿，』佐拉说着，从怀里掏出一只信封。

===Original===
“Well, leave it here, and I’ll see to it.”
“But you see, sir, Miss Smith is so methodical; she expects some papers or receipts.”
“Well, it’s too late tonight.”
“Possibly you could sign a sort of receipt and later﻿—”
Cresswell laughed. “Well, write one,” he indulgently assented. And Zora wrote.
When Zora left Colonel Cresswell’s about noon that Sunday she knew her work had just begun, and she walked swiftly along the country roads, calling here and there. Would Uncle Isaac help her build a log home? Would the boys help her some time to clear some swamp land? Would Rob become a tenant when she asked? For this was the idle time of the year. Crops were laid by and planting had not yet begun.

===Chinese===
『嗯，搁在这儿吧，我来办。』
『可您瞧，先生，史密斯小姐做事顶讲究；她要些字据或收据的。』
『这个嘛，今晚太晚了。』
『也许您可以签个收据，回头再——』
克雷斯韦尔笑了。『好吧，你写一个，』他宽容地应承。佐拉便写了起来。
那个星期日，佐拉大约中午离开克雷斯韦尔上校家时，她知道自己的活儿才刚开头，便沿着乡间小路快步走着，这里那里地敲门询问。艾萨克大叔肯帮她盖一间圆木屋吗？小伙子们肯抽空帮她开几块沼泽地吗？她开口相请时，罗布肯来做佃户吗？因为这是一年里最闲的时节。庄稼已搁置不管，下种还没开始。

===Original===
This too was the time of big church meetings. She knew that in her part of the country on that day the black population, man, woman, and child, were gathered in great groups; all day they had been gathering, streaming in snakelike lines along the country roads, in well-brushed, brilliant attire, half fantastic, half crude. Down where the Toomsville-Montgomery highway dipped to the stream that fed the Cresswell swamp squatted a square barn that slept through day and weeks in dull indifference. But on the First Sunday it woke to sudden mighty life. The voices of men and children mingled with the snorting of animals and the cracking of whips. Then came the long drone and singsong of the preacher with its sharp wilder climaxes and the answering “amens” and screams of the worshippers. This was the shrine of the Baptists﻿—shrine and oracle, centre and source of inspiration﻿—and hither Zora hurried.

===Chinese===
这也是大型聚会的时候。她知道，在她这一带，这一天，黑色的男女老少都聚成了庞大的人群；他们聚集了一整天，像蛇一般蜿蜒的队列，沿着乡间道路流动，衣着擦得干干净净、鲜艳夺目，一半奇幻，一半粗朴。在图姆斯维尔—蒙哥马利公路下坡、通往那道滋养克雷斯韦尔沼泽的小溪的地方，蹲伏着一座方方正正的大棚屋，平日里日复一日、周复一周地沉睡着，漠然无所事事。可每月第一个礼拜天，它会猛然惊醒，生出磅礴的生气。男女和孩子的声音，和牲口的喷鼻、鞭子的脆响混在一起。接着是牧师悠长的吟诵和念唱，夹杂着尖锐、更狂乱的高潮，和信徒们应答的『阿门』与尖叫。这是浸信会的圣殿——圣殿与神谕、中心与灵感之源——佐拉便朝这里赶来。

===Original===
The preacher was Jones, a big man, fat, black, and greasy, with little eyes, unctuous voice, and three manners: his white folks manner, soft, humble, wheedling; his black folks manner, voluble, important, condescending; and above all, his pulpit manner, loud, wild, and strong. He was about to don this latter cloak when Zora approached with a request briefly to address the congregation. Remembering some former snubs, his manner was lordly.
“I doesn’t see,” he returned reflectively, wiping his brows, “as how I can rightly spare you any time; the brethren is a-gettin’ mighty onpatient to hear me.” He pulled down his cuffs, regarding her doubtfully.

===Chinese===
布道的是琼斯，个大块头，胖、黑、油光水滑，小眼睛，油滑的嗓音，有三种做派：他待白人的做派，软软的、谦卑的、哄骗的；他待黑人的做派，滔滔不绝、自以为是、居高临下；而最重要的是他讲坛上的做派，响亮、狂野、有力。他正要披上最后这件外衣时，佐拉走上前，请求简短地对会众讲几句话。想起从前几次被晾，他摆出一副老爷派头。
『我倒看不出，』他一面擦额、一面若有所思地回，『我怎么好匀出时间给你呢；弟兄们都急得不行，要听我讲道呢。』他拉了拉袖口，狐疑地打量她。

===Original===
“I might speak after you’re through,” she suggested. But he objected that there was the regular collection and two or three other collections, a baptism, a meeting of the trustees; there was no time, in short; but﻿—he eyed her again.
“Does you want﻿—a collection?” he questioned suspiciously, for he could imagine few other reasons for talking. Then, too, he did not want to be too inflexible, for all of his people knew Zora and liked her.
“Oh, no, I want no collection at all. I only want a little voluntary work on their part.” He looked relieved, frowned through the door at the audience, and looked at his bright gold watch. The whole crowd was not there yet﻿—perhaps﻿—
“You kin say just a word before the sermont,” he finally yielded; “but not long﻿—not long. They’se just a-dying to hear me.”

===Chinese===
『我可以等您讲完再说，』她提议。可他反对，说还有例行募捐和另外两三次募捐、一次洗礼、一次理事会议；简而言之，没工夫；可是——他又拿眼瞟她。
『你莫不是要——募一笔捐？』他疑心地问，因为他想不出几样别的说话理由。再者，他也不想太不通融，毕竟他手下的人都认得佐拉、都喜欢她。
『哦，不，我一点都不募捐。我只想要他们出一点自愿的力气。』他一脸释然，皱着眉从门口望了望会众，又看看他那块亮金表。人还没到齐呢——说不定——
『你可以在讲道前说几句话，』他终于让步；『可不能长——不能长。他们正急着要听我讲呢。』

===Original===
So Zora spoke simply but clearly: of neglect and suffering, of the sins of others that bowed young shoulders, of the great hope of the children’s future. Then she told something of what she had seen and read of the world’s newer ways of helping men and women. She talked of cooperation and refuges and other efforts; she praised their way of adopting children into their own homes; and then finally she told them of the land she was buying for new tenants and the helping hands she needed. The preacher fidgeted and coughed but dared not actually interrupt, for the people were listening breathless to a kind of straightforward talk which they seldom heard and for which they were hungering.

===Chinese===
于是佐拉简朴而清楚地讲起来：讲疏忽与苦难，讲压在年幼肩膀上的他人的罪，讲孩子们未来那巨大的希望。接着她讲了一些她所见所读的、世上帮助男女的较新法子。她谈合作、谈庇护所、谈别的努力；她称赞他们把孩子领养进自己家的做法；末了，她把他们带到正题——她正在为新的佃户买地，她需要援手。那牧师坐立不安、咳嗽连连，却不敢真的打断，因为人们正屏息听着一种他们少有所闻、而又饥渴难耐的直截了当的讲话。

===Original===
And Zora forgot time and occasion. The moments flew; the crowd increased until the wonderful spell of those dark and upturned faces pulsed in her blood. She felt the wild yearning to help them beating in her ears and blinding her eyes.
“Oh, my people!” she almost sobbed. “My own people, I am not asking you to help others; I am pleading with you to help yourselves. Rescue your own flesh and blood﻿—free yourselves﻿—free yourselves!” And from the swaying sobbing hundreds burst a great “Amen!”

===Chinese===
而佐拉忘了时间，也忘了场合。光阴飞逝；人越聚越多，直到那些黝黑仰起的脸所构成的奇妙魔力，在她血里搏动起来。她感到帮助他们那股狂热的渴望，在她耳里擂鼓、在她眼里发盲。
『哦，我的族人！』她几乎是在抽泣。『我自己的族人，我不是在求你们去帮别人；我是在恳求你们帮你们自己。救救你们自己的骨肉——解放你们自己——解放你们自己！』从那摇摆着、抽泣着的几百人里，迸出一声宏大的『阿门！』

===Original===
The minister’s dusky face grew more and more sombre, and the angry sweat started on his brow. He felt himself hoaxed and cheated, and he meant to have his revenge. Two hundred men and women rose and pledged themselves to help Zora; and when she turned with overflowing heart to thank the preacher he had left the platform, and she found him in the yard whispering darkly with two deacons. She realized her mistake, and promised to retrieve it during the week; but the week was full of planning and journeying and talking.
Saturday dawned cool and clear. She had dinner prepared for cooking in the yard: sweet potatoes, hoecake, and buttermilk, and a hog to be barbecued. Everything was ready by eight o’clock in the morning. Emma and two other girl helpers were on the tiptoe of expectancy. Nine o’clock came and no one with it. Ten o’clock came, and eleven. High noon found Zora peering down the highway under her shading hand, but no soul in sight.

===Chinese===
牧师那张黝黑的脸越来越阴沉，愤怒的汗从他额上渗出来。他觉得自己被哄了、被耍了，他要报复。两百男女站起身，立誓帮佐拉；可当她满怀感激地转身要谢那牧师时，他已离开了讲台，她发现他在院子里，正和两个执事阴恻恻地低语。她意识到自己失算了，答应这一周内补救；可这一周满满都是筹划、赶路、谈话。
星期六破晓，清凉晴朗。她备好了在院子里下锅的饭菜：红薯、玉米饼、酪乳，还有一头要烤的猪。早上八点，一切就绪。埃玛和另外两个帮手姑娘，踮着脚尖盼着。九点钟到了，无人上门。十点、十一点到了。正午时分，佐拉仍用手遮着眉，眯眼望着公路尽头，却一个人影也看不见。

===Original===
She tried to think it out: what could have happened? Her people were slow, tardy, but they would not thus forget her and disappoint her without some great cause. She sent the girls home at dusk and then seated herself miserably under the great oak; then at last one half-grown boy hurried by.
“I wanted to come, Miss Zora, but I was afeared. Preacher Jones has been talking everywhere against you. He says that your mother was a voodoo woman and that you don’t believe in God, and the deacons voted that the members mustn’t help you.”
“And do the people believe that?” she asked in consternation.

===Chinese===
她试着想明白：能出什么事？她的人是慢、是拖沓，可不会这样没来由地忘了她、叫她失望，除非有重大的缘由。黄昏时她把姑娘们打发回家，自己凄凄地坐到那棵大橡树下；终于，一个半大的男孩匆匆跑过。
『我是想来的，佐拉小姐，可我怕。琼斯牧师到处说您的坏话。他说您娘是个巫婆，说您不信上帝，执事们还议了，叫会友不许帮您。』
『大家信这话吗？』她惊愕地问。

===Original===
“They just don’t know what to say. They don’t ’zactly believe it, but they has to ’low that you didn’t say much ’bout religion when you talked. You ain’t been near Big Meetin’﻿—and﻿—and﻿—you ain’t saved.” He hurried on.
Zora leaned her head back wearily, watching the laced black branches where the starlight flickered through﻿—as coldly still and immovable as she had watched them from those gnarled roots all her life﻿—and she murmured bitterly the world-old question of despair: “What’s the use?” It seemed to her that every breeze and branch was instinct with sympathy, and murmuring, “What’s the use?” She wondered vaguely why, and as she wondered, she knew.

===Chinese===
『他们也说不清该怎么办。他们倒不全信，可又不得不承认，您讲话时没怎么提宗教。您又没来大聚会——而且——而且——您没得救。』他匆匆走了。
佐拉疲惫地把头往后一靠，望着那交织的黑色枝桠，星光在枝缝里闪烁——她冷冷地、一动不动地望着它们，一如她这辈子都从那些虬结的树根上望着它们——她痛苦地喃喃出那句古老的、绝望的诘问：『有什么用？』她觉得每一阵风、每一根枝，都满怀同情，都低语着：『有什么用？』她恍惚地纳罕为什么，而她一纳罕，便知道了。

===Original===
For yonder where the black earth of the swamp heaved in a formless mound she felt the black arms of Elspeth rising from the sod﻿—gigantic, mighty. They stole toward her with stealthy hands and claw-like talons. They clutched at her skirts. She froze and could not move. Down, down she slipped toward the black slime of the swamp, and the air about was horror﻿—down, down, till the chilly waters stung her knees; and then with one grip she seized the oak, while the great hand of Elspeth twisted and tore her soul. Faint, afar, nearer and nearer and ever mightier, rose a song of mystic melody. She heard its human voice and sought to cry aloud. She strove again and again with that gripping, twisting pain﻿—that awful hand﻿—until the shriek came and she awoke.

===Chinese===
因为在那边，沼泽黑色的土地隆起成一座无形的土丘之处，她感到埃尔斯贝思黑色的双臂正从草皮底下升起——巨大、有力。它们带着蹑手蹑脚的手和爪似的利爪，向她潜来。它们攫住她的裙摆。她僵住了，动弹不得。她向下滑啊、滑啊，滑向沼泽那黑色的污泥，四周的空气是恐怖——滑啊、滑啊，直到冰冷的水刺痛她的膝盖；随即她一把抓住橡树，而埃尔斯贝思的那只巨手扭扯着她的灵魂。隐隐的、远远的，越来越近、越来越雄壮，升起一支神秘的旋律。她听见其中人的嗓音，想要放声大叫。她一次又一次地与那攫住、扭扯的痛——那只可怖的手——搏斗——直到一声尖叫冲出来，她醒了。

===Original===
She lay panting and sweating across the bent and broken roots of the oak. The hand of Elspeth was gone but the song was still there. She rose trembling and listened. It was the singing of the Big Meeting in the church far away. She had forgotten this religious revival in her days of hurried preparation, and the preacher had used her absence and apparent indifference against her and her work. The hand of Elspeth was reaching from the grave to pull her back; but she was no longer dreaming now. Drawing her shawl about her, she hurried down the highway.

===Chinese===
她伏在橡树那弯折断裂的树根间喘着、流着汗。埃尔斯贝思的手不见了，可那歌声还在。她颤巍巍地站起来，听着。那是远处教堂里大聚会的歌声。她在匆匆筹备的日子里，把这宗教复兴忘在了脑后，而那牧师便利用她的缺席和显然的冷淡，来反对她、反对她的事业。埃尔斯贝思的手正从坟墓里伸出来，要拽她回去；可此刻她不再做梦了。她裹紧披肩，急忙下了大路。

===Original===
The meeting had overflowed the church and spread to the edge of the swamp. The tops of young trees had been bent down and interlaced to form a covering and benches twined to their trunks. Thus a low and wide cathedral, all green and silver in the starlight, lay packed with a living mass of black folk. Flaming pine torches burned above the devotees; the rhythm of their stamping, the shout of their voices, and the wild music of their singing shook the night. Four hundred people fell upon their knees when the huge black preacher, uncoated, red-eyed, frenzied, stretched his long arms to heaven.

===Chinese===
聚会已从教堂里溢出来，漫到了沼泽边上。小树梢被弯下来、交织成一片顶盖，长椅缠绕在树干上。于是一座低矮而宽阔的大教堂，在星光下通体碧银，里面挤满了黑压压一片活的人群。燃烧的松枝火把在信徒头顶烧着；他们跺脚的节奏、呼喊的嗓音、和狂野的歌声，震动了夜。当那魁梧的黑人牧师——脱了外衣、两眼发红、如痴如狂——向天伸出长臂时，四百人齐齐跪倒。

===Original===
Zora saw the throng from afar, and hesitated. After all, she knew little of this strange faith of theirs﻿—had little belief in its mummery. She herself had been brought up almost without religion save some few mystic remnants of a half-forgotten heathen cult. The little she had seen of religious observance had not moved her greatly, save once yonder in Washington. There she found God after a searching that had seared her soul; but He had simply pointed the Way, and the way was human.
Humanity was near and real. She loved it. But if she talked again of mere men would these devotees listen? Already the minister had spied her tall form and feared her power. He set his powerful voice and the frenzy of his hearers to crush her.

===Chinese===
佐拉远远地望见那人群，犹豫了。说到底，对他们这陌生的信仰，她所知甚少——对它的装神弄鬼，她也没什么信。她自己几乎是在没有宗教的环境里长大的，只除了某个半被遗忘的异教里几缕神秘的残余。她所见过的那一点宗教仪式，并没怎么打动过她——只有一次，远在华盛顿。在那里，她经历了一场烧灼灵魂的追寻之后找到了上帝；可他只不过指了那条路，而那条路是人间的。
人间是切近的、真实的。她爱它。可她若再只谈人，这些信徒会听吗？那牧师已瞥见了她高挑的身影，忌惮她的力量。他运足了他洪亮的嗓音和听众的狂热，要压垮她。

===Original===
“Who is dis what talks of doing the Lord’s work for Him? What does de good Book say? Take no thought ’bout de morrow. Why is you trying to make dis ole world better? I spits on the world! Come out from it. Seek Jesus. Heaven is my home! Is it yo’s?” “Yes,” groaned the multitude. His arm shot out and he pointed straight at Zora.
“Beware the ebil one!” he shouted, and the multitude moaned. “Beware of dem dat calls ebil good. Beware of dem dat worships debbils; the debbils dat crawl; de debbils what forgits God.”
“Help him, Lord!” cried the multitude.

===Chinese===
『这是谁呀，要替主做主自己的工？善书上怎么说？莫为明日忧虑。你干嘛非要改造这旧世界？我唾这世界！从它里头出来吧。寻求耶稣。天堂才是我的家！是你的吗？』『是，』众人呻吟。他一伸手臂，直直地指向佐拉。
『当心那恶者！』他喊，众人哀鸣。『当心那些把恶叫作善的人。当心那些拜鬼的；那爬行的鬼；那忘了上帝的鬼。』
『主啊，助他！』众人喊。

===Original===
Zora stepped into the circle of light. A hush fell on the throng; the preacher paused a moment, then started boldly forward with upraised hands. Then a curious thing happened. A sharp cry arose far off down toward the swamp and the sound of great footsteps coming, coming as from the end of the world; there swelled a rhythmical chanting, wilder and more primitive than song. On, on it came, until it swung into sight. An old man led the band﻿—tall, massive, with tufted gray hair and wrinkled leathery skin, and his eyes were the eyes of death. He reached the circle of light, and Zora started: once before she had seen that old man. The singing stopped but he came straight on till he reached Zora’s side and then he whirled and spoke.

===Chinese===
佐拉踏进了光圈。人群一片寂静；那牧师顿了一下，随即高举双手，大胆地向前。这时发生了一件怪事。远处朝沼泽那一头，响起一声尖利的呼喊，还有巨大的脚步声——来了，来了，仿佛从世界的尽头而来；一阵有节奏的诵唱高涨起来，比歌更野、更原始。来了，来了，直到它进了视线。一个老人领着这一群——高挑、魁梧，一簇灰发，皱缩的皮革般的皮肤，他一双眼睛，是死神的眼睛。他走到光圈里，佐拉一惊：从前她见过这个老人。歌声停了，可他径直走来，直到走到佐拉身旁，随即一转身，开口说话。

===Original===
The words leaped and flew from his lips as he lashed the throng with bitter fury. He said what Zora wanted to say with two great differences: first, he spoke their religious language and spoke it with absolute confidence and authority; and secondly, he seemed to know each one there personally and intimately so that he spoke to no inchoate throng﻿—he spoke to them individually, and they listened awestruck and fearsome.
“God is done sent me,” he declared in passionate tones, “to preach His acceptable time. Faith without works is dead; who is you that dares to set and wait for the Lord to do your work?” Then in sudden fury, “Ye generation of vipers﻿—who kin save you?” He bent forward and pointed his long finger. “Yes,” he cried, “pray, Sam Collins, you black devil; pray, for the corn you stole Thursday.” The black figure moved. “Moan, Sister Maxwell, for the backbiting you did today. Yell, Jack Tolliver, you sneaking scamp, t’wil the Lord tell Uncle Bill who ruined his daughter. Weep, May Haynes, for that baby﻿—”

===Chinese===
字句从他唇间迸跳飞舞，他用苦毒的狂怒抽打着人群。他说出了佐拉想说的话，但有两点大不同：第一，他说的是他们的宗教语言，说得带着绝对的自信和权威；第二，他似乎认识在场的每一个人、与他们相熟，所以他不是在对一团无定形的人群说话——他对他们一个个地说，他们敬畏而恐惧地听着。
『上帝差我来了，』他以激烈的声调声言，『传他悦纳的禧年。没有行为的信是死的；你是谁，敢坐着干等主替你做工？』随即一阵暴怒，『你们这群毒蛇的种类——谁能救你们？』他俯身向前，长指一指。『对，』他喊，『祷告吧，萨姆·柯林斯，你这黑鬼；祷告吧，为你礼拜四偷的玉米。』那黑色的身影动了。『哀号吧，马克斯韦尔姊妹，为你今天背后咬人。尖叫吧，杰克·托利弗，你这鬼鬼祟祟的无赖，直叫主告诉比尔大叔，是谁毁了他女儿。哭吧，梅·海恩斯，为了那个娃——』

===Original===
But the woman’s shriek drowned his words, and he whirled full on the preacher, stamping his feet and waving his hands. His anger choked him; the fat preacher cowered gray and trembling. The gaunt fanatic towered over him.
“You﻿—you﻿—ornery hound of Hell! God never knowed you and the devil owns your soul!” There leapt from his lips a denunciation so livid, specific, and impassioned that the preacher squatted and bowed, then finally fell upon his face and moaned.
The gaunt speaker turned again to the people. He talked of little children; he pictured their sin and neglect. “God is done sent me to offer you all salvation,” he cried, while the people wept and wailed; “not in praying, but in works. Follow me!” The hour was halfway between midnight and dawn, but nevertheless the people leapt frenziedly to their feet.
“Follow me!” he shouted.

===Chinese===
可那女人的尖叫淹没了他的话，他猛地转身正对那牧师，跺着脚、挥着手。他的怒火噎住了他；那肥胖的牧师缩成一团，灰白着脸、发着抖。这瘦骨嶙峋的狂信者高高耸立在他头顶。
『你——你——地狱里下贱的恶犬！上帝从不认识你，魔鬼才占有你的灵魂！』他唇间跃出一番控诉，那样火辣、那样具体、那样激昂，那牧师先是蹲下、弯腰，末了扑倒在地，呻吟起来。
这瘦削的演说者又转向众人。他谈起小孩子；他描绘他们的罪与被疏忽。『上帝差我来，是要给你们众人救恩，』他喊，众人又哭又号；『不在祷告里，而在行为里。跟从我！』那时辰正当半夜与黎明之间，众人却如痴如狂地跳了起来。
『跟从我！』他喊。

===Original===
And, singing and chanting, the throng poured out upon the black highway, waving their torches. Zora knew his intention. With a half-dozen of younger onlookers she unhitched teams and rode across the land, calling at the cabins. Before sunrise, tools were in the swamp, axes and saws and hammers. The noise of prayer and singing filled the Sabbath dawn. The news of the great revival spread, and men and women came pouring in. Then of a sudden the uproar stopped, and the ringing of axes and grating of saws and tugging of mules was heard. The forest trembled as by some mighty magic, swaying and falling with crash on crash. Huge bonfires blazed and crackled, until at last a wide black scar appeared in the thick south side of the swamp, which widened and widened to full twenty acres.

===Chinese===
唱着、诵着，人群涌上了漆黑的大路，挥动着火把。佐拉明白他的意图。她带着半打年轻的旁观者，解开牲口，骑马穿过田野，挨家挨户地叩门。日出之前，工具已进了沼泽——斧头、锯子、锤子。祷告与歌唱的声响充满了安息日的黎明。大复兴的消息传开，男女源源涌来。随即，那喧嚣戛然而止，响起的是斧头的叮当、锯子的嘎吱、骡子的拉拽。森林像被某种巨大的魔法所震慑，摇晃着，轰然一声接一声地倒下。巨大的篝火熊熊燃烧、噼啪作响，直到沼泽茂密的南面终于现出一道宽宽的黑色伤疤，那伤疤越扩越大，直到足有二十英亩。

===Original===
The sun rose higher and higher till it blazed at high noon. The workers dropped their tools. The aroma of coffee and roasting meat rose in the dim cool shade. With ravenous appetites the dark, half-famished throng fell upon the food, and then in utter weariness stretched themselves and slept: lying along the earth like huge bronze earth-spirits, sitting against trees, curled in dense bushes.
And Zora sat above them on a high rich-scented pile of logs. Her senses slept save her sleepless eyes. Amid a silence she saw in the little grove that still stood, the cabin of Elspeth tremble, sigh, and disappear, and with it flew some spirit of evil.
Then she looked down to the new edge of the swamp, by the old lagoon, and saw Bles Alwyn standing there. It seemed very natural; and closing her eyes, she fell asleep.

===Chinese===
太阳越升越高，直到正午烈烈地燃烧。工人们放下了工具。咖啡与烤肉的香气在幽暗清凉的树荫里升起。那些黝黑、半饥半饱的人群，饿虎扑食般扑向食物，随即在彻底的疲倦里伸展身子、睡去：像一尊尊古铜色的大地精灵似的，沿地面躺着、靠着树坐着、蜷在密灌丛里。
而佐拉坐在他们上方，一摞散发着浓郁香气的高高圆木上。她的感官都睡了，只剩一双不眠的眼。在一片寂静里，她看见那片尚未被伐倒的小树丛中，埃尔斯贝思的小屋颤了颤、叹了口气、消失了，某一种邪灵也随之飞走了。
然后她向下望向沼泽的新边缘，在那旧潟湖边，看见了布莱斯·阿尔文站在那里。这显得再自然不过；她闭上眼，睡了过去。

## XXXIV The Return of Alwyn / XXXIV 阿尔文归来

===Original===
Bles Alwyn stared at Mrs. Harry Cresswell in surprise. He had not seen her since that moment at the ball, and he was startled at the change. Her abundant hair was gone; her face was pale and drawn, and there were little wrinkles below her sunken eyes. In those eyes lurked the tired look of the bewildered and the disappointed. It was in the lofty waiting-room of the Washington station where Alwyn had come to meet a friend. Mrs. Cresswell turned and recognized him with genuine pleasure. He seemed somehow a part of the few things in the world﻿—little and unimportant perhaps﻿—that counted and stood firm, and she shook his hand cordially, not minding the staring of the people about. He took her bag and carried it towards the gate, which made the observers breathe easier, seeing him in servile duty.

===Chinese===
布莱斯·阿尔文惊讶地盯着哈里·克雷斯韦尔太太。自从舞会那一面之后，他没再见过她，她的变化叫他吃惊。她那一头浓密的头发没了；脸苍白、憔悴，凹陷的眼下有了细纹。那双眼里，潜伏着一种迷茫者与失意者才有的疲倦。这是在华盛顿车站高敞的候车大厅里，阿尔文是来接一位朋友的。克雷斯韦尔太太转过身，认出了他，真心地欢喜。他不知怎的，像是这世上寥寥几样——也许微小、也许无关紧要——却算数、却站得稳的事物的一部分，她热忱地握他的手，全不顾周围人盯着的目光。他接过她的提包，朝出口走去——这倒让旁观者们松了口气，因为看见他是在做仆役的差事。不知怎的，她自己也不知怎么的，等她回过神来，已把生命里这场危机讲给了他听；当然不是全部，却讲了很多。这很像是她在跟一个来自另一个世界的人说话——一个局外人；却又是她相识已久的、懂得她的人。从她所述、和她不能述说的那些里，他拼出了事情的本相，这叫他困惑。他原以为白人没有这样的烦恼；然而他转念一想，为什么不呢？他们也是人啊。

===Original===
“I suppose you hear from the school?” he ventured after a pause.
“Why, yes﻿—not directly﻿—but Zora used to speak of it.”
Bles looked up quickly.
“Zora?”
“Yes. Didn’t you see her while she was here? She has gone back now.”
Then the gate opened, the crowd surged through, sweeping them apart, and next moment he was alone.

===Chinese===
『我想，学校的消息你总能听到吧？』停了停，他试探着问。
『哦，是——不是直接听——可佐拉从前常说起。』
布莱斯飞快地抬头。
『佐拉？』
『是。她在的时候，你没见到她吗？她已经回去了。』
这时出口开了，人群涌过，把他们冲散，下一刻他便独自一人了。

===Original===
Alwyn turned slowly away. He forgot the friend he was to meet. He forgot everything but the field of the Silver Fleece. It rose shadowy there in the pale concourse, swaying in ghostly breezes. The purple of its flowers mingled with the silver radiance of tendrils that trembled across the hurrying throng, like threads of mists along low hills. In its midst rose a dark, slim, and quivering form. She had been here﻿—here in Washington! Why had he not known? What was she doing? “She has gone back now”﻿—back to the Sun and the Swamp, back to the Burden.

===Chinese===
阿尔文慢慢地转身。他忘了要接的朋友。他什么都忘了，只记得那片银色羊毛的田。它幽幽地浮现在那苍白的大厅里，在幽灵般的微风中摇曳。它花朵的紫色，糅合着藤蔓银色的光华，那些藤蔓颤动在匆匆人群之上，像低山间一缕缕的雾丝。在它中央，立着一个幽暗、纤细、颤动的身影。她来过这儿——就在华盛顿！他怎么不知道？她在做什么？『她已经回去了』——回到太阳与沼泽，回到那个重担。

===Original===
Why should not he go back, too? He walked on thinking. He had failed. His apparent success had been too sudden, too overwhelming, and when he had faced the crisis his hand had trembled. He had chosen the Right﻿—but the Right was ineffective, impotent, almost ludicrous. It left him shorn, powerless, and in moral revolt. The world had suddenly left him, as the vision of Carrie Wynn had left him, alone, a mere clerk, an insignificant cog in the great grinding wheel of humdrum drudgery. His chance to do and thereby to be had not come.
He thought of Zora again. Why not go back to the South where she had gone? He shuddered as one who sees before him a cold black pool whither his path leads. To face the proscription, the insult, the lawless hate of the South again﻿—never! And yet he went home and sat down and wrote a long letter to Miss Smith.

===Chinese===
他何不也回去呢？他边走边想。他失败了。他表面的成功来得太骤、太铺天盖地，而当他面对那场危机时，他的手抖了。他选择了正义——可正义是无效的、无力的、近乎可笑的。它叫他赤条条、毫无力量，在心里造反。这世界突然抛下了他，正如卡丽·怀恩的幻影抛下了他，留下他孑然一身，一个小小的文书，庸常苦役那巨大的磨轮上一枚无足轻重的齿轮。他有所作为、从而有所成就的机会，并没有来。
他又想起佐拉。为什么不回南方、回她去的地方呢？他打了个寒颤，像一个看见前面有一潭冰冷漆黑的水潭、而他的路正通向那里的人。要再去直面南方的排斥、侮辱、无法无天的仇恨——绝不！可他还是回了家，坐下来给史密斯小姐写了一封长信。

===Original===
The reply that came after some delay was almost curt. It answered few of his questions, argued with none of his doubts, and made no mention of Zora. Yes, there was need of a manager for the new farm and settlement. She was not sure whether Alwyn could do the work or not. The salary was meagre and the work hard. If he wished it, he must decide immediately.
Two weeks later found Alwyn on the train facing Southward in the Jim Crow car. How he had decided to go back South he did not know. In fact, he had not decided. He had sat helpless and inactive in the grip of great and shadowed hands, and the thing was as yet incomprehensible. And so it was that the vision Zora saw in the swamp had been real enough, and Alwyn felt strangely disappointed that she had given no sign of greeting on recognition.

===Chinese===
拖延了一阵才来的回信，几乎是简慢的。它几乎不回答他的问题，不与他任何疑虑辩论，也只字未提佐拉。是的，新农场和定居点正需要一位管理人。她拿不准阿尔文能不能胜任。薪水微薄，活儿又苦。他若想要，就必须立刻决定。
两星期后，阿尔文已在南行的列车上，坐在吉姆·克劳车厢里。他是怎么决定回南方的，他自己也不知道。事实上，他并没有决定。他只是无助地、无所作为地，坐在一双巨大而幽暗的手的掌握里，而事情至今仍不可解。所以，佐拉在沼泽里看见的那一幕幻象，是足够真实的，而阿尔文怪异地感到失望——她认出他时，竟没有半点招呼的表示。

===Original===
In other ways, too, Zora, when he met her, was to him a new creature. She came to him frankly and greeted him, her gladness shining in her eyes, yet looking nothing more than gladness and saying nothing more. Just what he had expected was hard to say; but he had left her on her knees in the dirt with outstretched hands, and somehow he had expected to return to some corresponding mental attitude. The physical change of these three years was marvellous. The girl was a woman, well-rounded and poised, tall, straight, and quick. And with this went mental change: a self-mastery; a veiling of the self even in intimate talk; a subtle air as of one looking from great and unreachable heights down on the dawn of the world.

===Chinese===
在别处也一样，佐拉，当他见到她时，于他是一个新的人。她坦白地走向他、招呼他，欢喜在她眼里闪光，然而那神情不过就是欢喜、那话语不过就是欢喜，再没别的。他原本期待的究竟是什么，很难说；可他离开她时，她是跪在泥土里、双手伸出的，于是他不知怎的，期待回来时能见到某种相应的心境。这三年身体上的变化是惊人的。姑娘成了女人，丰盈而沉稳，高挑、挺拔、敏捷。与此同行的，是心智上的变化：一种自制；一种即使在亲密交谈里也把自我掩覆起来的意味；一种微妙的气度，仿佛一个人站在高不可攀的绝顶之上，俯瞰这世界的黎明。

===Original===
Perhaps no one who had not known the child and the girl as he had would have noted all this; but he saw and realized the transformation with a pang﻿—something had gone; the innocence and wonder of the child, and in their place had grown up something to him incomprehensible and occult.
Miss Smith was not to be easily questioned on the subject. She took no hints and gave no information, and when once he hazarded some pointed questions she turned on him abruptly, observing acidly: “If I were you I’d think less of Zora and more of her work.”

===Chinese===
也许，若不是像他这样曾熟知那孩子和那姑娘的人，是不会注意到这一切的；可他看见了，在一阵刺痛里意识到这番蜕变——有什么东西去了；那孩子的天真和惊奇，取而代之的，是某种他所不能理解、近乎幽秘的东西。
史密斯小姐在这一桩上不容易被问出什么。她不受暗示，也不给消息，有一次他冒险问了几个直截了当的问题，她便猛地转向他，尖酸地说：『我要是你，就会少想些佐拉，多想想她的工作。』

===Original===
Gradually, in his spiritual perplexity, Alwyn turned to Mary Cresswell. She was staying with the Colonel at Cresswell Oaks. Her coming South was supposed to be solely for reasons of health, and her appearance made this excuse plausible. She was lonely and restless, and naturally drawn toward the school. Her intercourse with Miss Smith was only formal, but her interest in Zora’s work grew. Down in the swamp, at the edge of the cleared space, had risen a log cabin; long, low, spacious, overhung with oak and pine. It was Zora’s centre for her settlement-work. There she lived, and with her a half-dozen orphan girls and children too young for the boarding department of the school. Mrs. Cresswell easily fell into the habit of walking by here each day, coming down the avenue of oaks across the road and into the swamp. She saw little of Zora personally but she saw her girls and learned much of her plans.

===Chinese===
渐渐地，在精神的困惑里，阿尔文转向了玛丽·克雷斯韦尔。她正和上校一起住在克雷斯韦尔橡树庄。她南下，据说纯粹是为了养病，而她的形容也让这个借口说得过去。她孤独、不安，自然而然地被学校吸引。她与史密斯小姐的往来仅止于客套，但她对佐拉事业的兴趣与日俱增。在沼泽深处、在那片清出来的空地边上，矗起了一座圆木小屋；长、低、宽敞，橡树和松树在头顶交覆。那是佐拉社区服务工作的中心。她住在那里，和她同住的，有六个孤女和几个年纪太小、还不够上学校寄宿部的孩子。克雷斯韦尔太太很容易地养成了每天来这儿走一趟的习惯，她沿着橡树林荫道下来，穿过大路，走进沼泽。她私底下见佐拉见得不多，但她看见了她的姑娘们，也打听了不少她的计划。

===Original===
The rooms of the cottage were clean and light, supplied with books and pictures, simple toys, and a phonograph. The yard was one wide green and golden playground, and all day the music of children’s glad crooning and the singing of girls went echoing and trembling through the trees, as they played and sewed and washed and worked.
From the Cresswells and the Maxwells and others came loads of clothes for washing and mending. The Tolliver girls had simple dresses made, embroidery was ordered from town, and soon there would be the gardens and cotton fields. Mrs. Cresswell would saunter down of mornings. Sometimes she would talk to the big girls and play with the children; sometimes she would sit hidden in the forest, listening and glimpsing and thinking, thinking, till her head whirled and the world danced red before her eyes, today she rose wearily, for it was near noon, and started home. She saw Alwyn swing along the road to the school dining-room where he had charge of the students at the noonday meal.

===Chinese===
小屋的房间干净、明亮，备有书和画、简单的玩具，还有一台留声机。院子是一片广阔的绿与金的游乐场，整天回荡着孩子们欢快的咿呀和姑娘们的歌声，在树间回响、颤动，他们玩耍、缝纫、洗涤、劳作。
从克雷斯韦尔家、马克斯韦尔家和别人家，一车车的衣服送来洗、送来补。托利弗家的姑娘们在这儿做素净的衣裳，镇上的绣活也来订了，不久之后，还要侍弄菜园和棉花地。克雷斯韦尔太太每每在上午溜达下来。有时她和大姑娘们聊天、陪孩子们玩；有时她坐在树林里躲着，听着、窥着、想着、想着，直想到头发晕、眼前一片红光乱舞——今天她疲惫地站起来，因为快到中午了，便动身回家。她看见阿尔文顺着大路迈步走向学校饭厅，他在那儿负责照管学生用午餐。

===Original===
Alwyn wanted Mrs. Cresswell’s judgment and advice. He was growing doubtful of his own estimate of women. Evidently something about his standards was wrong; consequently he made opportunities to talk with Mrs. Cresswell when she was about, hoping she would bring up the subject of Zora of her own accord. But she did not. She was too full of her own cares and troubles, and she was only too glad of willing and sympathetic ears into which to pour her thoughts. Miss Smith soon began to look on these conversations with some uneasiness. Black men and white women cannot talk together casually in the South and she did not know how far the North had put notions in Alwyn’s head.
Today both met each other almost eagerly.
Mrs. Cresswell had just had a bit of news which only he would fully appreciate.
“Have you heard of the Vanderpools?” she asked.

===Chinese===
阿尔文想要克雷斯韦尔太太的判断和忠告。他越来越怀疑自己对女人的估量。显然，他的标准里有哪里不对；于是他便在她出现时，找机会和她说话，指望她主动提起佐拉。可她没有。她自己的烦忧和苦楚装得太满，她只太乐意有一双愿意听、有同情的耳朵，好把心事倾倒进去。史密斯小姐很快就开始不安地看待这些交谈。在南方，黑人和白人女子不能这样随随便便地交谈，她也不知道北方在阿尔文脑子里灌了多少念头。
今天两人几乎是急切地见到了彼此。
克雷斯韦尔太太刚得到一点消息，只有他才能充分领会。
『你听说范德普尔家的事了吗？』她问。

===Original===
“No﻿—except that he was appointed and confirmed at last.”
“Well, they had only arrived in France when he died of apoplexy. I do not know,” added Mrs. Cresswell, “I may be wrong and﻿—I hope I’m not glad.” Then there leapt to her mind a hypothetical question which had to do with her own curious situation. It was characteristic of her to brood and then restlessly to seek relief in consulting the one person near who knew her story. She started to open the subject again today.
But Alwyn, his own mind full, spoke first and rapidly. He, too, had turned to her as he saw her come from Zora’s home. He must know more about the girl. He could no longer endure this silence. Zora beneath her apparent frankness was impenetrable, and he felt that she carefully avoided him, although she did it so deftly that he felt rather than observed it.

===Chinese===
『没有——只知道他到底被任命、也被确认了。』
『唉，他们刚到法国，他就中风死了。我不知道，』克雷斯韦尔太太补了一句，『也许我想错了，而且——我希望自己不是在幸灾乐祸。』随即她脑里跳出一个假设性的问题，跟她自己那古怪的处境有关。她的特点便是这样沉思，然后不安地去找近处唯一知她故事的人商量，以求解脱。今天她又想把这事打开来讲。
可阿尔文自己满腹心事，抢先飞快地开了口。他看见她从佐拉家出来时，也转向了她。他非多知道这姑娘一些不可。他再也受不了这份沉默了。佐拉在表面的坦白之下是不可看透的，他觉得她在刻意避开他，尽管她避得那样巧妙，他与其说是观察到的，不如说是感觉到的。

===Original===
Miss Smith still systematically snubbed him when he broached the subject of Zora. With others he did not speak; the matter seemed too delicate and sacred, and he always had an awful dread lest sometime, somewhere, a chance and fatal word would be dropped, a breath of evil gossip which would shatter all. He had hated to obtrude his troubles on Mrs. Cresswell, who seemed so torn in soul. But today he must speak, although time pressed.
“Mrs. Cresswell,” he began hurriedly, “there’s a matter﻿—a personal matter of which I have wanted to speak﻿—a long time﻿—I﻿—” The dinner-bell rang, and he stopped, vexed.
“Come up to the house this afternoon,” she said; “Colonel Cresswell will be away﻿—” Then she paused abruptly. A strange startling thought flashed through her brain. Alwyn noticed nothing. He thanked her cordially and hurried toward the dining-hall, meeting Colonel Cresswell on horseback just as he turned into the school gate.

===Chinese===
每当他提起佐拉的话题，史密斯小姐还是照例地训他。对别人他也不说；这事太微妙、太神圣，而他总有一种可怕的恐惧，生怕什么时候、什么地方，会漏出一句致命的闲话，一口恶毒的流言，把一切都打碎。他本不愿把自己的烦恼硬塞给克雷斯韦尔太太——她的灵魂似乎已被撕扯得够受了。可今天他非说不可，纵然时间紧迫。
『克雷斯韦尔太太，』他匆匆开口，『有一件事——一桩私事——我一直想跟您说——很久了——我——』午饭铃响了，他懊恼地停住。
『今天下午到屋子里来吧，』她说；『克雷斯韦尔上校不在家——』随即她戛然顿住。一个古怪而惊人的念头闪过她脑际。阿尔文什么也没留意。他热忱地道了谢，匆匆朝饭厅走去，刚拐进学校门口，就迎面遇见骑在马上的克雷斯韦尔上校。

===Original===
Mary Cresswell walked slowly on, flushing and paling by turns. Could it be that this Negro had dared to misunderstand her﻿—had presumed? She reviewed her conduct. Perhaps she had been indiscreet in thus making a confidant of him in her trouble. She had thought of him as a boy﻿—an old student, a sort of confidential servant; but what had he thought? She remembered Miss Smith’s warning of years before﻿—and he had been North since and acquired Northern notions of freedom and equality. She bit her lip cruelly.
Yet, she mused, she was herself to blame. She had unwittingly made the intimacy and he was but a Negro, looking on every white woman as a goddess and ready to fawn at the slightest encouragement. There had been no one else here to confide in. She could not tell Miss Smith her troubles, although she knew Miss Smith must suspect. Harry Cresswell, apparently, had written nothing home of their quarrel. All the neighbors behaved as if her excuse of ill-health were sufficient to account for her return South to escape the rigors of a Northern winter. Alwyn, and Alwyn alone, really knew. Well, it was her blindness, and she must right it quietly and quickly with hard ruthless plainness. She blushed again at the shame of it; then she began to excuse.

===Chinese===
玛丽·克雷斯韦尔慢慢走着，一阵红、一阵白地变着脸色。难道这个黑人竟敢误解她——竟敢放肆？她回顾自己的言行。也许，她在烦恼中把他当作知己，是有些不谨慎了。她一直把他当作一个孩子——一个从前的学生、一种机要仆役看待；可他是怎么想的呢？她想起多年前史密斯小姐的警告——而他又去过北方，沾染了北方那一套自由平等的念头。她狠狠地咬住嘴唇。
然而，她又想，这得怪她自己。是她不知不觉地结下了这份亲近，而他不过是个黑人，把每一个白人女子都当女神看，只消一点鼓励便巴结上来。这里再没有别人可以倾诉。她不能把自己的苦楚告诉史密斯小姐，尽管她知道史密斯小姐一定在疑心。哈里·克雷斯韦尔，看样子并没有把他们吵架的事写信回家。所有的邻居都表现得好像，她那养病的借口，已足以解释她为何南下躲避北方的严冬。只有阿尔文，唯有阿尔文，是真正知情的。好吧，这是她的盲目，她必须用硬邦邦的、不留情面的直白，悄悄地、迅速地把它纠正过来。她又为这份羞耻红了脸；随即她开始为自己开脱。

===Original===
After all, which was worse﻿—a Cresswell or an Alwyn? It was no sin that Alwyn had done; it was simply ignorant presumption, and she must correct him firmly, but gently, like a child. What a crazy muddle the world was! She thought of Harry Cresswell and the tale he told her in the swamp. She thought of the flitting ghosts that awful night in Washington. She thought of Miss Wynn who had jilted Alwyn and given her herself a very bad quarter of an hour. What a world it was, and after all how far was this black boy wrong? Just then Colonel Cresswell rode up behind and greeted her.
She started almost guiltily, and again a sense of the awkwardness of her position reddened her face and neck. The Colonel dismounted, despite her protest, and walked beside her. They chatted along indifferently, of the crops, her brother’s new baby, the proposed mill.

===Chinese===
说到底，哪一个更糟——一个克雷斯韦尔，还是一个阿尔文？阿尔文并没有犯罪；那不过是无知的放肆，她必须坚决地、却又温和地纠正他，像对一个孩子。这世界真是一团发疯的乱麻！她想起哈里·克雷斯韦尔，和他在沼泽里讲给她的那番话。她想起华盛顿那个可怕的夜里游荡的鬼影。她想起怀恩小姐——她甩了阿尔文，也叫她自己过了非常难堪的一刻钟。这是怎样的一个世界啊，而这个黑小子到底又错到哪儿去了呢？就在这时，克雷斯韦尔上校从后面骑马赶上来，跟她打了招呼。
她几乎是心虚地一惊，对自己处境的尴尬之感，又一次涨红了她脸和脖子。上校不顾她的推辞，下了马，在她身旁步行。他们有一搭没一搭地聊着，聊收成、聊她哥哥新生的婴儿、聊那座拟议中的纱厂。

===Original===
“Mary,” his voice abruptly struck a new note. “I don’t like the way you talk with that Alwyn nigger.”
She was silent.
“Of course,” he continued, “you’re Northern born and you have been a teacher in this school and feel differently from us in some ways; but mark what I say, a nigger will presume on the slightest pretext, and you must keep them in their place. Then, too, you are a Cresswell now﻿—”
She smiled bitterly; he noticed it, but went on:
“You are a Cresswell, even if you have caught Harry up to some of his deviltry,”﻿—she started﻿—“and got miffed about it. It’ll all come out right. You’re a Cresswell, and you must hold yourself too high to ‘Mister’ a nigger or let him dream of any sort of equality.”

===Chinese===
『玛丽，』他的嗓音突然换了个调子。『我不喜欢你跟那个阿尔文黑鬼说话的样子。』
她沉默了。
『当然，』他接着说，『你是北方生的，又在这学校当过老师，有些地方跟我们的感觉不一样；可你记住我的话，黑鬼只要有一点由头就会放肆起来，你必须让他们待在该待的地方。再者，你如今是克雷斯韦尔家的人了——』
她苦笑了一下；他注意到了，可还是接下去：
『你是克雷斯韦尔家的人，哪怕你抓住了哈里一些胡作非为的把柄，』——她一惊——『为这个生了气。都会过去的。你是克雷斯韦尔家的人，你得把自己的身份端得高高的，高到不去对一个黑鬼称『先生』，也不让他做起任何平等的梦。』

===Original===
He spoke pleasantly, but with a certain sharp insistence that struck a note of fear in Mary’s heart. For a moment she thought of writing Alwyn not to call. But, no; a note would be unwise. She and Colonel Cresswell lunched rather silently.
“Well, I must get to town,” he finally announced. “The mill directors meet today. If Maxwell calls by about that lumber tell him I’ll see him in town.” And away he went.
He had scarcely reached the highway and ridden a quarter of a mile or so when he spied Bles Alwyn hurrying across the field toward the Cresswell Oaks. He frowned and rode on. Then reining in his horse, he stopped in the shadow of the trees and watched Alwyn.

===Chinese===
他说得和气，却带着一种尖锐的坚持，在玛丽心里敲出了一声恐惧。有一刻她想写信叫阿尔文别来了。可是，不行；一封信是不智的。她和克雷斯韦尔上校相当沉默地吃了午饭。
『好了，我得进城去，』他终于宣布。『纱厂董事们今天开会。要是马克斯韦尔来谈那批木材的事，告诉他我在城里见他。』说完他就走了。
他刚上了大路、骑出大约四分之一英里，便瞥见布莱斯·阿尔文正匆匆穿过田野，朝克雷斯韦尔橡树庄赶去。他皱起眉，继续骑。随即他勒住马，在树荫里停下，盯着阿尔文。

===Original===
It was here that Zora saw him as she came up from her house. She, too, stopped, and soon saw whom he was watching. She had been planning to see Mr. Cresswell about the cut timber on her land. By legal right it was hers but she knew he would claim half, treating her like a mere tenant. Seeing him watching Alwyn she paused in the shadow and waited, fearing trouble. She, too, had felt that the continued conversations of Alwyn and Mrs. Cresswell were indiscreet, but she hoped that they had attracted no one else’s attention. Now she feared the Colonel was suspicious and her heart sank. Alwyn went straight toward the house and disappeared in the oak avenue. Still Colonel Cresswell waited but Zora waited no longer. Alwyn must be warned. She must reach Cresswell’s mansion before Cresswell did and without him seeing her. This meant a long detour of the swamp to approach the Oaks from the west. She silently gathered up her skirts and walked quickly and carefully away.

===Chinese===
就在这里，佐拉从自家屋里上来时看见了他。她也停下，很快便看清他在盯着谁。她原本打算去找克雷斯韦尔先生，谈谈她地上那批砍下的木材。按法律那是她的，可她知道他会要分一半，把她当个寻常佃户对待。看见他在盯着阿尔文，她便在阴影里停下等着，怕出事。她也觉得，阿尔文和克雷斯韦尔太太这样接连地交谈，是不谨慎的，可她盼着没引起别人注意。如今她怕上校起了疑心，心一沉。阿尔文径直朝屋子走去，消失在橡树荫道里。克雷斯韦尔上校仍在等，可佐拉不再等了。阿尔文必须被警告。她必须赶在克雷斯韦尔之前、且不让他看见，抵达克雷斯韦尔庄。这意味着要绕沼泽一大圈，从西边接近橡树庄。她悄悄地收拢裙摆，小心而迅速地走开了。

===Original===
She was a strong woman, lithe and vigorous, living in the open air and used to walking. Once out of hearing she threw away her hat and bending forward ran through the swamp. For a while she ran easily and swiftly. Then for a moment she grew dizzy and it seemed as though she was standing still and the swamp in solemn grandeur marching past﻿—in solemn mocking grandeur. She loosened her dress at the neck and flew on.
She sped at last through the oaks, up the terraces, and slowing down to an unsteady walk, staggered into the house. No one would wonder at her being there. She came up now and then and sorted the linen and piled the baskets for her girls. She entered a side door and listened. The Colonel’s voice sounded impatiently in the front hall.
“Mary! Mary?”

===Chinese===
她是个强健的女人，柔韧而有力，长年住在户外、惯于步行。一走出听得见的范围，她便甩掉帽子，俯身穿过沼泽跑了起来。起初她跑得轻松而飞快。后来有一阵她发晕，仿佛自己是站着不动，而沼泽正以一种庄严的、嘲弄的壮丽，从她身旁列队而过。她把领口的衣扣松开，飞奔下去。
她终于穿过橡树、登上阶地，放缓成跌跌撞撞的步子，踉跄着进了屋。谁也不会奇怪她在那里。她时不常来一趟，分拣亚麻衣物、给她的姑娘们装满篮子。她从一扇侧门进去，侧耳听。上校的嗓音在前厅里不耐烦地响着。
『玛丽！玛丽？』

===Original===
A pause, then an answer:
“Yes, father!”
He started up the front stairway and Zora hurried up the narrow back stairs, almost overturning a servant.
“I’m after the clothes,” she explained. She reached the back landing just in time to see Colonel Cresswell’s head rising up the front staircase. With a quick bound she almost fell into the first room at the top of the stairs.
Bles Alwyn had hurried through his dinner duties and hastened to the Oaks. The questions, the doubts, the uncertainty within him were clamoring for utterance. How much had Mrs. Cresswell ever known of Zora? What kind of a woman was Zora now? Mrs. Cresswell had seen her and had talked to her and watched her. What did she think? Thus he formulated his questions as he went, half timid, and fearful in putting them and yet determined to know.

===Chinese===
一顿，然后一个回答：
『在，爸！』
他开始从前楼梯上来，佐拉便匆匆从狭窄的后楼梯上去，差点撞翻一个仆人。
『我是来拿衣服的，』她解释。她赶到后面楼梯口时，正好看见克雷斯韦尔上校的头从前楼梯升上来。她一个箭步，几乎跌进楼梯顶头第一间房。
布莱斯·阿尔文匆匆做完午饭的差事，便赶往橡树庄。他心里的那些疑问、那些犹疑、那些不确定，正喧腾着要冲出口。克雷斯韦尔太太到底知道佐拉多少？佐拉如今是怎样的一个女人？克雷斯韦尔太太见过她、跟她谈过话、观察过她。她怎么看？他一边走一边这样组织着自己的问题，半是胆怯，怕问出口，却又铁了心要知道。

===Original===
Mrs. Cresswell, waiting for him, was almost panic-stricken. Probably he would beat round the bush seeking further encouragement; but at the slightest indication she must crush him ruthlessly and at the same time point the path of duty. He ought to marry some good girl﻿—not Zora, but someone. Somehow Zora seemed too unusual and strange for him﻿—too inhuman, as Mary Cresswell judged humanity. She glanced out from her seat on the upper veranda over the front porch and saw Alwyn coming. Where should she receive him? On the porch and have Mr. Maxwell ride up? In the parlor and have the servants astounded and talking? If she took him up to her own sitting-room the servants would think he was doing some work or fetching something for the school. She greeted him briefly and asked him in.
“Good afternoon, Bles”﻿—using his first name to show him his place, and then inwardly recoiling at its note of familiarity. She preceded him upstairs to the sitting-room, where, leaving the door ajar, she seated herself on the opposite side of the room and waited.

===Chinese===
等着他的克雷斯韦尔太太，几乎是惊惶失措。多半他会拐弯抹角地找进一步的鼓励；可只要有一点苗头，她就必须无情地粉碎他，同时给他指明本分的路。他该娶个好姑娘——不是佐拉，而是某个人。不知怎的，佐拉于他显得太不寻常、太怪异——太不像人了，在玛丽·克雷斯韦尔判断人性的尺度里。她从楼上阳台的座位上、越过前廊望出去，看见阿尔文来了。她在哪儿接待他？在廊下，让马克斯韦尔先生骑马撞见？在客厅里，让仆人们目瞪口呆、窃窃私语？要是她把他带上楼自己的起居室，仆人们会以为他在做什么活儿、或替学校来取什么东西。她简短地招呼他，请他进来。
『下午好，布莱斯』——她直呼他的名，好让他明白自己的位置，随即又为这一声熟稔在心里退缩了一下。她领他上楼进起居室，把门虚掩着，在屋子另一头坐下，等着。

===Original===
He fidgeted, then spoke rapidly.
“Mrs. Cresswell﻿—this is a personal affair.” She reddened angrily. “A love affair”﻿—she paled with something like fear﻿—“and I”﻿—she started to speak, but could not﻿—“I want to know what you think about Zora?”
“About Zora!” she gasped weakly. The sudden reaction, the revulsion of her agitated feelings, left her breathless.
“About Zora. You know I loved her dearly as a boy﻿—how dearly I have only just begun to realize: I’ve been wondering if I understood﻿—if I wasn’t﻿—”
Mrs. Cresswell got angrily to her feet.
“You have come here to speak to me of that﻿—that﻿—” she choked, and Bles thought his worst fears realized.

===Chinese===
他坐立不安，随即飞快地说起来。
『克雷斯韦尔太太——这是一桩私事。』她恼怒地涨红了脸。『一桩情事』——她吓得发白——『而我——』她想说话，却说不出——『我想知道您怎么看佐拉？』
『佐拉！』她虚弱地喘息。这骤然的反转、她激荡情绪的剧变，叫她喘不过气来。
『关于佐拉。您知道，我小时候是那样爱她——爱到什么程度，我直到最近才开始明白：我一直在想，我是不是懂了——我是不是没有——』
克雷斯韦尔太太气冲冲地站起来。
『你到这儿来，就是要跟我说那个——那个——』她哽住，布莱斯以为自己最可怕的担忧成真了。

===Original===
“Mary, Mary!” Colonel Cresswell’s voice broke suddenly in upon them. With a start of fear Mrs. Cresswell rushed out into the hall and closed the door.
“Mary, has that Alwyn nigger been here this afternoon?” Mr. Cresswell was coming upstairs, carrying his riding whip.
“Why, no!” she answered, lying instinctively before she quite realized what her lie meant. She hesitated. “That is, I haven’t seen him. I must have nodded over my book,”﻿—looking toward the little veranda at the front of the upper hall, where her easy chair stood with her book. Then with an awful flash of enlightenment she realized what her lie might mean, and her heart paused.

===Chinese===
『玛丽，玛丽！』克雷斯韦尔上校的嗓音突然闯了进来。克雷斯韦尔太太惊恐地一跳，冲进走廊，关上门。
『玛丽，那个阿尔文黑鬼今天下午来过吗？』克雷斯韦尔先生正一手提着马鞭上楼来。
『咦，没有！』她答，还来不及完全意识到这句谎意味着什么，便本能地撒了谎。她迟疑了一下。『我是说，我没看见他。我准是看书看打盹了，』——她望向楼上走廊前头那个小阳台，她的安乐椅和书还摆在那里。随即一道可怕的灵光闪过，她意识到自己的谎可能意味着什么，她的心跳停了一拍。

===Original===
Cresswell strode up.
“I saw him come up﻿—he must have entered. He’s nowhere downstairs,” he wavered and scowled. “Have you been in your sitting-room?” And then, not waiting for a reply, he strode to the door.
“But the damned scoundrel wouldn’t dare!”
He deliberately placed his hand in his right-hand hip-pocket and threw open the door.
Mary Cresswell stood frozen. The full horror of the thing burst upon her. Her own silly misapprehension, the infatuation of Alwyn for Zora, her thoughtless﻿—no, vindictive﻿—betrayal of him to something worse than death. She listened for the crack of doom. She heard a bird singing far down in the swamp; she heard the soft raising of a window and the closing of a door. And then﻿—great God in heaven! must she live forever in this agony?﻿—and then, she heard the door bang and Mr. Cresswell’s gruff voice﻿—
“Well, where is he?﻿—he isn’t in there!”

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔大步走上来。
『我看见他上来了——他一定进去了。他不在楼下，』他动摇着、皱着眉。『你在起居室里待过吗？』随即不等回答，他便大步走到门前。
『可这该死的混账谅他也不敢！』
他故意把手伸进右侧后裤兜，一把推开了门。
玛丽·克雷斯韦尔冻住了。事情全部的恐怖向她扑来。她自己愚蠢的误解、阿尔文对佐拉的痴情、她那轻率的——不，报复性的——把他出卖到比死更糟的境地。她侧耳等着末日的崩裂。她听见远处沼泽里一只鸟在唱；她听见一扇窗子被轻轻推开、一扇门被关上。然后——老天在上！难道她要永远活在这煎熬里吗？——然后，她听见门砰地一声、和克雷斯韦尔先生粗哑的嗓音——
『咦，他在哪儿？——他不在里头！』

===Original===
Mary Cresswell felt that something was giving way within. She swayed and would have crashed to the bottom of the staircase if just then she had not seen at the opposite end of the hall, near the back stairs, Zora and Alwyn emerge calmly from a room, carrying a basket full of clothes. Colonel Cresswell stared at them, and Zora instinctively put up her hand and fastened her dress at the throat. The Colonel scowled, for it was all clear to him now.
“Look here,” he angrily opened upon them, “if you niggers want to meet around keep out of this house; hereafter I’ll send the clothes down. By God, if you want to make love go to the swamp!” He stamped down the stairs while an ashy paleness stole beneath the dark-red bronze of Zora’s face.

===Chinese===
玛丽·克雷斯韦尔感到体内有什么东西正在崩塌。她摇晃着，眼看要栽到楼梯底下——就在这时，她看见走廊另一头、后楼梯边，佐拉和阿尔文正平静地从一间屋里走出来，抬着一篮满满的衣服。克雷斯韦尔上校瞪着他们，佐拉本能地抬手扣紧了喉头的衣扣。上校沉下脸，因为此刻他全明白了。
『听着，』他冲他们怒气冲冲地开口，『你们这些黑鬼要幽会，就别进这屋子；以后我把衣服送下去。天哪，你们要谈情说爱，上沼泽里去！』他跺着脚下了楼，而一层灰白的惨色，悄悄爬上佐拉那张暗红古铜色的脸。

===Original===
They walked silently down the road together﻿—the old familiar road. Alwyn was staring moodily ahead.
“We must get married﻿—before Christmas, Zora,” he presently avowed, not looking at her. He felt the basket pause and he glanced up. Her dark eyes were full upon him and he saw something in their depths that brought him to himself and made him realize his blunder.
“Zora!” he stammered, “forgive me! Will you marry me?”
She looked at him calmly with infinite compassion. But her reply was uttered unhesitantly; distinct, direct.
“No, Bles.”

===Chinese===
他们默默地沿着大路一同走下去——那条旧的、熟稔的路。阿尔文阴郁地瞪着前方。
『我们得结婚——圣诞节前，佐拉，』过了一会儿他声言，没看她。他感到那篮子一顿，便抬眼望去。她那双幽黑的眼正满含在他身上，他看见那眼底有什么东西，把他拉回了自己，让他意识到自己犯了多大的错。
『佐拉！』他结巴，『原谅我！你愿意嫁给我吗？』
她平静地看着他，带着无尽的怜悯。可她的回答毫不犹豫地说出口；清晰，直接。
『不，布莱斯。』

## XXXV The Cotton Mill / XXXV 棉纺厂

===Original===
The people of Toomsville started in their beds and listened. A new song was rising on the air: a harsh, low, murmuring croon that shook the village ranged around its old square of dilapadated stores. It was not a song of joy; it was not a song of sorrow; it was not a song at all, perhaps, but a confused whizzing and murmuring, as of a thousand ill-tuned, busy voices. Some of the listeners wondered; but most of the town cried joyfully, “It’s the new cotton-mill!”
John Taylor’s head teemed with new schemes. The mill trust of the North was at last a fact. The small mills had not been able to buy cotton when it was low because Cresswell was cornering it in the name of the Farmers’ League; now that it was high they could not afford to, and many surrendered to the trust.

===Chinese===
图姆斯维尔的居民在床上惊起，侧耳倾听。空中正升起一支新的歌：一种粗粝、低沉、嘤嘤嗡嗡的吟唱，震动了那围着一圈破旧店铺的老广场排列着的村镇。那不是欢乐之歌；也不是悲苦之歌；也许根本算不上是歌，而是一片混乱的呼啸与嘤嗡，像一千个走调的、忙碌的嗓子。听的人里有些纳罕；可镇上大多数人欢喜地喊：『是新棉纺厂！』
约翰·泰勒的脑瓜里塞满了新盘算。北方的纱厂托拉斯终于成了现实。那些小厂在棉花低价时买不起，因为克雷斯韦尔正以农民同盟的名义囤积；如今棉花涨了，它们更买不起，许多家便归顺了托拉斯。

===Original===
“Next thing,” wrote Taylor to Easterly, “is to reduce cost of production. Too much goes in wages. Gradually transfer mills South.”
Easterly argued that the labor was too unskilled in the South and that to send Northern spinners down would spread labor troubles. Taylor replied briefly: “Never fear; we’ll scare them with a vision of niggers in the mills!”
Colonel Cresswell was not so easily won over to the new scheme. In the first place he was angry because the school, which he had come to regard as on its last legs, somehow still continued to flourish. The ten-thousand-dollar mortgage had but three more years, and that would end all; but he had hoped for a crash even earlier.

===Chinese===
『下一步，』泰勒写信给伊斯特利，『是降低生产成本。花在工资上的太多了。把纱厂逐步南迁。』
伊斯特利争辩说，南方的劳动力太不熟练，把北方的纺工派下去，会扩散劳资纠纷。泰勒简短地回：『别怕；我们拿纱厂里要进黑鬼的幻影吓唬他们就行了！』
克雷斯韦尔上校不那么容易被说服接受这个新方案。其一，他正恼火，因为他本以为那学校已奄奄一息，可它不知怎的还在兴旺。一万元的抵押只剩三年，到时候就一切了结；可他原本盼着它更早崩溃。

===Original===
Instead of this, Miss Smith was cheerfully expanding the work, hiring new teachers, and especially she had brought to help her two young Negroes whom he suspected. Colonel Cresswell had prevented the Tolliver land sale, only to be inveigled himself into Zora’s scheme which now began to worry him. He must evict Zora’s tenants as soon as the crops were planted and harvested. There was nothing unjust about such a course, he argued, for Negroes anyway were too lazy and shiftless to buy the land. They would not, they could not, work without driving. All this he imparted to John Taylor, to which that gentleman listened carefully.

===Chinese===
非但没有崩溃，史密斯小姐还在兴高采烈地扩展事业，聘请新教师，尤其是她带回来两个年轻的黑人帮她，这两人令他生疑。克雷斯韦尔上校阻挠了托利弗那块地的出售，自己反倒被套进了佐拉的方案，如今这方案开始叫他发愁。他必须在庄稼种下、收完之后，立刻把佐拉的佃户赶走。他说，这样的做法并无不公，因为黑人本来就太懒、太没出息，买不起地。不打不骂，他们不愿、也不能干活。这些他都讲给了约翰·泰勒，那位先生仔细地听着。

===Original===
“H’m, I see,” he owned. “And I know the way out.”
“How?”
“A cotton mill in Toomsville.”
“What’s that got to do with it?”
“Bring in whites.”
“But I don’t want poor white trash; I’d sooner have niggers.”
“Now, see here,” argued Taylor, “you can’t have everything you want﻿—day’s gone by for aristocracy of old kind. You must have neighbors: choose, then, white or black. I say white.”

===Chinese===
『嗯，我明白了，』他承认。『而且我知道出路。』
『什么出路？』
『在图姆斯维尔建一座棉纺厂。』
『那跟这事有什么关系？』
『把白人引进来。』
『可我不要那些穷白人渣滓；我宁可要黑鬼。』
『这样吧，』泰勒论证道，『你不可能什么都要——老派贵族的好日子过去了。你必须有邻居：那么，白人还是黑人，你挑。我说是白人。』

===Original===
“But they’ll rule us﻿—outvote us﻿—marry our daughters,” warmly objected the Colonel.
“Some of them may﻿—most of them won’t. A few of them with brains will help us rule the rest with money. We’ll plant cotton mills beside the cotton fields, use whites to keep niggers in their place, and the fear of niggers to keep the poorer whites in theirs.”
The Colonel looked thoughtful.
“There’s something in that,” he confessed after a while; “but it’s a mighty big experiment, and it may go awry.”
“Not with brains and money to guide it. And at any rate, we’ve got to try it; it’s the next logical step, and we must take it.”

===Chinese===
『可他们会统治我们——在选票上压过我们——娶我们的女儿，』上校激烈地反对。
『也许有几个会——大多数不会。少数有脑子的，会帮我们用钱统治其余的人。我们把棉纺厂建在棉田边，用白人把黑鬼摁在他们该在的位置，再用对黑鬼的恐惧，把更穷的白人摁在他们的位置。』
上校显出若有所思的样子。
『这里头有点道理，』过了一会儿他承认；『可这是个大得不得了的实验，说不定会出岔子。』
『有脑子和钱来掌舵，就不会。无论如何，我们总得试一试；这是顺理成章的下一步，我们必须走。』

===Original===
“But in the meantime, I’m not going to give up good old methods; I’m going to set the sheriff behind these lazy niggers,” said the Colonel; “and I’m going to stop that school putting notions into their heads.”
In three short months the mill at Toomsville was open and its wheels whizzing to the boundless pride of the citizens.
“Our enterprise, sir!” they said to the strangers on the strength of the five thousand dollars locally invested.

===Chinese===
『可在那之前，我不会丢掉好的老法子；我要让警长去盯紧这些懒黑鬼，』上校说；『我还要叫那学校别再往他们脑子里灌歪理。』
短短三个月，图姆斯维尔的纱厂便开了张，轮子呼呼地转，市民们自豪得无边无际。
『我们的企业，先生！』他们凭着本地投资的那五千块钱，对陌生人们说。

===Original===
Once it had vigor to sing, the song of the mill knew no resting; morning and evening, day and night it crooned its rhythmic tune; only during the daylight Sundays did its murmur die to a sibilant hiss. All the week its doors were filled with the coming and going of men and women and children: many men, more women, and greater and greater throngs of children. It seemed to devour children, sitting with its myriad eyes gleaming and its black maw open, drawing in the pale white mites, sucking their blood and spewing them out paler and ever paler. The face of the town began to change, showing a ragged tuberculous looking side with dingy homes in short and homely rows.

===Chinese===
这歌一旦有了力气唱起来，便不知停歇；朝朝暮暮、日日夜夜，它哼着有节奏的调子；只有在白天的礼拜天，它的嘤嗡才低成一个嘶嘶的气音。整一周，它的大门里进进出出的都是男男女女和老老小小：男人很多，女人更多，而孩子是一群比一群多。它仿佛在吞噬孩子，坐在那里，千万只眼睛闪着光，黑黢黢的大口张着，把那些苍白的白色小虫吸进去，吮他们的血，再把他们吐出来，一个比一个更苍白。小镇的脸开始变了，露出一边褴褛的、肺痨似的模样，一排排灰扑扑的、又矮又寒碜的屋子。

===Original===
There came gradually a new consciousness to the town. Hitherto town and country had been ruled by a few great landlords but at the very first election, Colton, an unknown outsider, had beaten the regular candidate for sheriff by such a majority that the big property owners dared not count him out. They had, however, an earnest consultation with John Taylor.
“It’s just as I said,” growled Colonel Cresswell, “if you don’t watch out our whole plantation system will be ruined and we’ll be governed by this white trash from the hills.”
“There’s only one way,” sighed Caldwell, the merchant; “we’ve got to vote the niggers.”

===Chinese===
镇上渐渐地生出了一种新的意识。从前，城乡都由几个大地主统治，可在头一次选举里，一个名不见经传的外乡人科尔顿，竟以压倒性多数击败了正规的警长候选人，大产业主们不敢把他的票做掉。然而，他们认真地找约翰·泰勒商议了一番。
『正像我说的一样，』克雷斯韦尔上校咆哮，『你们不当心，咱们整个种植园体制就要毁了，咱们就要被山里来的这帮白人渣滓统治。』
『只有一个法子，』商人考德威尔叹道；『咱们得拉黑鬼的票。』

===Original===
John Taylor laughed. “Nonsense!” he spurned the suggestion. “You’re old-fashioned. Let the mill-hands have the offices. What good will it do?”
“What good! Why, they’ll do as they please with us.”
“Bosh! Don’t we own the mill? Can’t we keep wages where we like by threatening to bring in nigger labor?”
“No, you can’t, permanently,” Maxwell disputed, “for they sometime will call your bluff.”
“Let ’em call,” said Taylor, “and we’ll put niggers in the mills.”
“What!” ejaculated the landlords in chorus. Only Maxwell was silent. “And kill the plantation system?”

===Chinese===
约翰·泰勒笑了。『胡扯！』他驳回这个提议。『你们老古板了。让厂里的工人去当官吧。那有什么好处？』
『有什么好处！怎么没好处，他们要怎么摆布我们就怎么摆布。』
『废话！厂子不是咱们的吗？咱们拿引进黑鬼劳工来威胁，还怕把工资压不在咱们想要的位置？』
『不，长久不了，』马克斯韦尔反驳，『他们迟早会戳穿你的虚张声势。』
『让他们戳去，』泰勒说，『咱们就把黑鬼塞进厂里。』
『什么！』地主们齐声惊呼。只有马克斯韦尔沉默。『那不就毁了种植园体制？』

===Original===
“Oh, maybe some time, of course. But not for years; not until you’ve made your pile. You don’t really expect to keep the darkies down forever, do you?”
“No, I don’t,” Maxwell slowly admitted. “This system can’t last always﻿—sometimes I think it can’t last long. It’s wrong, through and through. It’s built on ignorance, theft, and force, and I wish to God we had courage enough to overthrow it and take the consequences. I wish it was possible to be a Southerner and a Christian and an honest man, to treat niggers and dagoes and white trash like men, and be big enough to say, ‘To Hell with consequences!’ ”

===Chinese===
『哦，当然，也许终归有那么一天。但不是这几年内；在你们赚够之前不会。你们不至于真指望把黑佬们永远踩下去吧？』
『不，我不指望，』马克斯韦尔慢慢地承认。『这体制不可能永远撑下去——有时候我觉得它撑不了多久了。它彻头彻尾是错的。它建立在无知、偷盗和暴力之上，我但愿上帝给我们足够的勇气，推翻它、承担后果。我但愿做一个南方人、同时又做一个基督徒、一个诚实人是可能的，把黑鬼、外国佬和白人渣滓都当人对待，并且有足够的胸襟说一句：『让后果见鬼去吧！』』

===Original===
Colonel Cresswell stared at his neighbor, speechless with bewilderment and outraged traditions. Such unbelievable heresy from a Northerner or a Negro would have been natural; but from a Southerner whose father had owned five hundred slaves﻿—it was incredible! The other landlords scarcely listened; they were dogged and impatient and they could suggest no remedy. They could only blame the mill for their troubles.
John Taylor left the conference blithely. “No,” he said to the committee from the new mill-workers’ union. “Can’t raise wages, gentlemen, and can’t lessen hours. Mill is just started and not yet paying expenses. You’re getting better wages than you ever got. If you don’t want to work, quit. There are plenty of others, white and black, who want your jobs.”

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔上校瞪着他的邻居，被这惊世骇俗之论和被冒犯的传统惊得说不出话。这种叫人难以置信的异端邪说，出自北方人或黑人之口尚属自然；可出自一个父亲曾拥有五百名奴隶的南方人之口——简直不可思议！其他地主几乎没在听；他们又顽固又焦躁，提不出任何补救。他们只能把自己的麻烦归咎于纱厂。
约翰·泰勒轻快地离开了这场会议。『不行，』他对新成立的纱厂工会的委员会说。『涨不了工资，先生们，也减不了工时。厂子刚开工，还没收回成本。你们拿的工钱已比以往任何时候都多。不愿干，就别干。要你们位子的人，白人黑人，多得是。』

===Original===
The mention of black people as competitors for wages was like a red rag to a bull. The laborers got together and at the next election they made a clean sweep, judge, sheriff, two members of the legislature, and the registrars of votes. Undoubtedly the following year they would capture Harry Cresswell’s seat in Congress.
The result was curious. From two sides, from landlord and white laborer, came renewed oppression of black men. The laborers found that their political power gave them little economic advantage as long as the threatening cloud of Negro competition loomed ahead. There was some talk of a strike, but Colton, the new sheriff, discouraged it.

===Chinese===
把黑人当作争工钱的对手一提，就像对牛甩红布。工人们纠集起来，在下一次选举里来了个大清扫——法官、警长、两名州议会议员、还有选民登记官，统统换人。毫无疑问，第二年他们还会夺走哈里·克雷斯韦尔的国会席位。
结果很古怪。从两边——从地主和白人劳工——都涌来了对黑人新一波的压迫。劳工们发现，只要黑人竞争的乌云还压在前头，他们的政治权力就没给他们带来多少经济好处。有人谈起了罢工，可新警长科尔顿不赞成。

===Original===
“I tell you, boys, where the trouble lies: it’s the niggers. They live on nothing and take any kind of treatment, and they keep wages down. If you strike, they’ll get your jobs, sure. We’ll just have to grin and bear it a while, but get back at the darkies whenever you can. I’ll stick ’em into the chain-gang every chance I get.”
On the other hand, inspired by fright, the grip of the landlords on the black serfs closed with steadily increasing firmness. They saw one class rising from beneath them to power, and they tightened the chains on the other. Matters simmered on in this way, and the only party wholly satisfied with conditions was John Taylor and the few young Southerners who saw through his eyes. He was making money.

===Chinese===
『我告诉你们，弟兄们，麻烦在哪儿：就在那些黑鬼。他们什么都不花也活得下去，什么样的待遇都受得了，他们把工资压得低低的。你们一罢工，他们的确会把你们的位子抢了去。咱们只好咬着牙忍一阵，但只要有机会，就报复那些黑佬。我一有机会，就把他们塞进苦役队。』
另一方面，地主管束黑奴的手，因恐惧而越攥越紧。他们看见一个阶级从他们脚下崛起掌权，便把另一个阶级身上的链子勒得更紧。事情就这样酝酿着，唯一对现状完全满意的，是约翰·泰勒，和那几个跟他一条心的年轻南方人。他在赚钱。

===Original===
The landlords, on the contrary, were losing power and prestige, and their farm labor, despite strenuous efforts, was drifting to town attracted by new and incidental work and higher wages. The mill-hands were more and more overworked and underpaid, and hated the Negroes for it in accordance with their leaders’ directions.
At the same time the oppressed blacks and scowling mill-hands could not help recurring again and again to the same inarticulate thought which no one was brave enough to voice. Once, however, it came out flatly. It was when Zora, crowding into the village courthouse to see if she could not help Aunt Rachel’s accused boy, found herself beside a gaunt, overworked white woman. The woman was struggling with a crippled child and Zora, turning, lifted him carefully for the weak mother, who thanked her half timidly. “That mill’s about killed him,” she said.

===Chinese===
地主们则相反，正在丧失权势和威望，而他们的农工，尽管他们百般挽留，仍被镇上新出的零活和更高的工钱吸引，纷纷外流。厂里的工人则越来越劳累、越来越被克扣，并按他们领袖们的指引，为此恨上了黑人。
与此同时，受压迫的黑人和怒目而视的厂工，都忍不住一遍又一遍地回到同一个说不出口的念头——谁也没胆子把它说出口。然而有一回，它径直说出来了。那是佐拉挤进镇上的法院，看她能不能帮蕾切尔大婶那个被告的男孩，发现自己旁边站着一个瘦削的、劳累过度的白人女子。那女人正吃力地抱着一个残疾的孩子，佐拉转过身，小心地替这位虚弱的母亲把孩子抱起来，那女人半怯生生地谢她。『那厂子差点把他弄死，』她说。

===Original===
At this juncture the manacled boy was led into court, and the woman suddenly turned again to Zora.
“Durned if I don’t think these white slaves and black slaves had ought ter git together,” she declared.
“I think so, too,” Zora agreed.
Colonel Cresswell himself caught the conversation and it struck him with a certain dismay. Suppose such a conjunction should come to pass? He edged over to John Taylor and spoke to him; but Taylor, who had just successfully stopped a suit for damages to the injured boy, merely shrugged his shoulders.

===Chinese===
就在这时，那戴着手铐的男孩被押进法庭，那女人忽然又转向佐拉。
『我倒真觉得，这些白奴和黑奴，就该联合起来，』她声言。
『我也这么想，』佐拉附和。
克雷斯韦尔上校亲耳听到了这番话，心里掠过一阵沮丧。万一这种联合竟成真了呢？他挪到约翰·泰勒身边，跟他说了；可泰勒刚刚成功地拦下了一桩为那受伤男孩索赔的官司，只耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“What’s this nigger charged with?” demanded the Judge when the first black boy was brought up before him.
“Breaking his labor contract.”
“Any witnesses?”
“I have the contract here,” announced the sheriff. “He refuses to work.”
“A year, or one hundred dollars.”
Colonel Cresswell paid his fine, and took him in charge.
“What’s the charge here?” said the Judge, pointing to Aunt Rachel’s boy.
“Attempt to kill a white man.”

===Chinese===
『这黑鬼犯了什么罪？』第一个黑男孩被带到法官面前时，法官问。
『违反用工合同。』
『有证人吗？』
『合同在这儿，』警长宣布。『他拒绝干活。』
『一年苦役，或一百块罚款。』
克雷斯韦尔上校替他交了罚款，把他收归自己手下。
『这一桩是什么罪？』法官指着蕾切尔大婶的男孩说。
『企图杀害白人。』

===Original===
“Any witnesses?”
“None except the victim.”
“And I,” said Zora, coming forward.
Both the sheriff and Colonel Cresswell stared at her. Of course, she was simply a black girl but she was an educated woman, who knew things about the Cresswell plantations that it was unnecessary to air in court. The newly elected Judge had not yet taken his seat, and Cresswell’s word was still law in the court. He whispered to the Judge.
“Case postponed,” said the Court.

===Chinese===
『有证人吗？』
『除了受害者，没有。』
『还有我，』佐拉说着，走上前来。
警长和克雷斯韦尔上校都瞪着她。当然，她不过是个黑人姑娘，可她是个受过教育的女子，她知道一些关于克雷斯韦尔种植园的事，那是没必要在法庭上抖出来的。新当选的法官尚未就职，克雷斯韦尔的话在庭上仍是法律。他向法官耳语。
『本案延期，』法庭宣布。

===Original===
The sheriff scowled.
“Wait till Jim gets on the bench,” he growled.
The white bystanders, however, did not seem enthusiastic and one man﻿—he was a Northern spinner﻿—spoke out plainly.
“It’s none o’ my business, of course. I’ve been fired and I’m damned glad of it. But see here: if you mutts think you’re going to beat these big blokes at their own game of cheating niggers you’re daffy. You take this from me: get together with the niggers and hold up this whole capitalist gang. If you don’t get the niggers first, they’ll use ’em as a club to throw you down. You hear me,” and he departed for the train.

===Chinese===
警长沉下脸。
『等吉姆上了法官席再说吧，』他咕噜。
然而，围观的白人似乎并不起劲，其中一个——他是个北方的纺工——直截了当地说了出来。
『当然，这不关我的事。我刚被解雇了，而且我他妈高兴得很。可你听好：要是你们这帮呆子以为，在人家骗黑鬼的那套把戏上能赢过这些大款，那你们是疯了。我这话你们记住：跟黑鬼联合起来，制住这一整帮资本家。你要不先把黑鬼拉过来，他们就会拿黑鬼当棍子，把你们打翻。听见没有，』说完他便动身去火车站了。

===Original===
Colton was suspicious. The sentiment of joining with the Negroes did not seem to arouse the bitter resentment he expected. There even came whispers to his ears that he had sold out to the landlords, and there was enough truth in the report to scare him. Thus to both parties came the uncomfortable spectre of the black men, and both sides went to work to lay the ghost.
Particularly was Colonel Cresswell stirred to action. He realized that in Bles and Zora he was dealing with a younger class of educated black folk, who were learning to fight with new weapons. They were, he was sure, as dissolute and weak as their parents, but they were shrewder and more aspiring. They must be crushed, and crushed quickly. To this end he had recourse to two sources of help﻿—Johnson and the whites in town.

===Chinese===
科尔顿起了疑心。跟黑人联合这种情绪，似乎并没有激起他预料中的那种激烈反感。甚至有风声传到他耳里，说他已向地主们卖了人情，而这传闻里有足够的实情，足以吓着他。于是，对双方来说，黑人都成了一个叫人不快的幽灵，双方都着手去驱这个鬼。
克雷斯韦尔上校尤其被激起了行动。他意识到，在布莱斯和佐拉身上，他面对的是较年轻的一类受过教育的黑人，他们正学着用新的武器战斗。他确信，他们和他们的父辈一样堕落、一样软弱，但他们更精明、也更有野心。必须压碎他们，而且要快。为此，他求助于两个来源——约翰逊，和镇上的白人。

===Original===
Johnson was what Colonel Cresswell repeatedly called “a faithful nigger.” He was one of those constitutionally timid creatures into whom the servility of his fathers had sunk so deep that it had become second-nature. To him a white man was an archangel, while the Cresswells, his father’s masters, stood for God. He served them with doglike faith, asking no reward, and for what he gave in reverence to them, he took back in contempt for his fellows﻿—“niggers!” He applied the epithet with more contempt than the Colonel himself could express. To the Negroes he was a “white folk’s nigger,” to be despised and feared.
To him Colonel Cresswell gave a few pregnant directions. Then he rode to town, and told Taylor again of his fears of a labor movement which would include whites and blacks. Taylor could not see any great danger.

===Chinese===
约翰逊正是克雷斯韦尔上校反复称作『一个忠心的黑鬼』的那种人。他是那种天生怯懦的人，父辈的奴性在他身上沉得那样深，已成了第二本能。在他看来，白人是大天使，而克雷斯韦尔家——他父亲的主子——则代表上帝。他以狗一般的忠心侍奉他们，不求回报，而他从敬奉他们那里所收回的，是对同类的蔑视——『黑鬼！』他用这个蔑称时，比上校本人所能表达的还要鄙夷。在黑人眼里，他是『白人的黑鬼』，为人所鄙、也为人所惧。
克雷斯韦尔上校向他交代了几句意味深长的话。然后他骑马进城，又把自己对一场黑白联合的劳工运动的恐惧，告诉了泰勒。泰勒看不出有什么大危险。

===Original===
“Of course,” he conceded, “they’ll eventually get together; their interests are identical. I’ll admit it’s our game to delay this as long possible.”
“It must be delayed forever, sir.”
“Can’t be,” was the terse response. “But even if they do ally themselves, our way is easy: separate the leaders, the talented, the pushers, of both races from their masses, and through them rule the rest by money.”
But Colonel Cresswell shook his head. “It’s precisely these leaders of the Negroes that we mush crush,” he insisted. Taylor looked puzzled.
“I thought it was the lazy, shiftless, and criminal Negroes, you feared?”
“Hang it, no! We can deal with them; we’ve got whips, chain-gangs, and﻿—mobs, if need be﻿—no, it’s the Negro who wants to climb up that we’ve got to beat to his knees.”

===Chinese===
『当然，』他让步，『他们终归会联合起来；他们的利益是一致的。我承认，尽量拖延这一刻，正是我们的戏。』
『必须永远拖延下去，先生。』
『做不到，』是简短的回答。『可就算他们真的结了盟，咱们的路也好走：把两个种族里的领袖、有才干的、爱钻营的，从他们的大众里分出来，通过他们用钱统治其余的人。』
可克雷斯韦尔上校摇头。『恰恰是黑人的这些领袖，我们必须压碎，』他坚持。泰勒显出困惑。
『我还以为你怕的是那些又懒、又没出息、又犯罪的黑人呢？』
『见鬼，不是！那些人我们对付得了；我们有鞭子、苦役队，还有——必要时——暴民——不，我们要打趴下的，是那个想往上爬的黑人。』

===Original===
Taylor could not follow this reasoning. He believed in an aristocracy of talent alone, and secretly despised Colonel Cresswell’s pretensions of birth. If a man had ability and push Taylor was willing and anxious to open the way for him, even though he were black. The caste way of thinking in the South, both as applied to poor whites and to Negroes, he simply could not understand. The weak and the ignorant of all races he despised and had no patience with them. “But others﻿—a man’s a man, isn’t he?” he persisted. But Colonel Cresswell replied:
“No, never, if he’s black, and not always when he’s white,” and he stalked away.

===Chinese===
泰勒跟不上这套逻辑。他只信凭才干的贵族，私底下瞧不起克雷斯韦尔上校那套出身论。一个人只要有本事、有冲劲，哪怕他是黑人，泰勒也愿意、也急于为他开路。南方那套等级观念，无论用在穷白人还是黑人身上，他简直无法理解。所有种族里的弱者和无知者，他都鄙视，对他们没有耐心。『可别的人——人就是人，不是吗？』他坚持。可克雷斯韦尔上校回：
『不，永远不是，要是他是黑人的话；也不总是，哪怕他是白人，』说完他大步走开了。

===Original===
Zora sensed fully the situation. She did not anticipate any immediate understanding with the laboring whites, but she knew that eventually it would be inevitable. Meantime the Negro must strengthen himself and bring to the alliance as much independent economic strength as possible. For the development of her plans she needed Bles Alwyn’s constant cooperation. He was business manager of the school and was doing well, but she wanted to point out to him the larger field. So long as she was uncertain of his attitude toward her, it was difficult to act; but now, since the flash of the imminent tragedy at Cresswell Oaks had cleared the air, with all its hurt a frank understanding had been made possible. The very next day Zora chose to show Bles over her new home and grounds, and to speak frankly to him. They looked at the land, examined the proposed farm sites, and viewed the living-room and dormitory in the house.

===Chinese===
佐拉充分意识到了局势。她不指望眼下就和白人劳工达成什么谅解，但她知道那终归不可避免。眼下，黑人必须壮大自己，尽可能多地为这场联盟带去独立的经济力量。为推进她的计划，她需要布莱斯·阿尔文持续的合作。他是学校的商务主管，干得不错，可她想向他指出那片更大的天地。只要她拿不准他对她的态度，便难以行动；可如今，橡树庄那场险些酿成的悲剧一闪、廓清了空气，尽管带着伤痛，一份坦白的谅解已成为可能。第二天，佐拉便特意领布莱斯参观她的新家和园子，坦白地跟他谈。他们看了地，察看了拟议中的农场地块，又看了屋里的起居室和宿舍。

===Original===
“You haven’t seen my den,” said Zora.
“No.”
“Miss Smith is in there now; she often hides there. Come.”
He went into the large central house and into the living-room, then out on the porch, beyond which lay the kitchen. But to the left, and at the end of the porch, was a small building. It was ceiled in dark yellow pine, with figured denim on the walls. A straight desk of rough hewn wood stood in the corner by the white-curtained window, and a couch and two large easy-chairs faced a tall narrow fireplace of uneven stone. A thick green rag-carpet covered the floor; a few pictures were on the walls﻿—a Madonna, a scene of mad careering horses, and some sad baby faces.

===Chinese===
『你还没见过我的小窝呢，』佐拉说。
『没有。』
『史密斯小姐这会儿在里面；她常躲到那儿去。来。』
他进了那座居中的大屋，进了起居室，又出到游廊上，游廊外头便是厨房。可在左边、游廊尽头，有一座小建筑。它的顶棚和四壁镶着深黄色的松木，墙上覆着印花粗斜棉布。一张粗凿的直腿书桌立在挂着白窗帘的窗边角落，一张躺椅和两把大安乐椅，面朝一座又高又窄、用参差石块砌成的壁炉。地上铺着厚厚的绿色碎布地毯；墙上有几幅画——一幅圣母像、一幅奔马狂驰的图景，还有几张忧伤的婴儿面孔。

===Original===
The room was a unity; things fitted together as if they belonged together. It was restful and beautiful, from the cheerful pine blaze before which Miss Smith was sitting, to the square-paned window that let in the crimson rays of gathering night. All round the room, stopping only at the fireplace, ran low shelves of the same yellow pine, filled with books and magazines. He scanned curiously Plato’s Republic, Gorky’s “Comrades,” a Cyclopædia of Agriculture, Balzac’s novels, Spencer’s “First Principles,” Tennyson’s Poems.
“This is my university,” Zora explained, smiling at his interested survey. They went out again and wandered down near the old lagoon.

===Chinese===
这间屋子是一个整体；物件与物件契合，仿佛本就该在一起。它宁静而美，从史密斯小姐坐着的那蓬欢快的松木炉火，一直到那方格窗——窗外透进渐浓的夜的绯红光线。屋子四面、只让壁炉断开处除外，排着一圈低低的、同样黄松木的书架，堆满了书和杂志。他好奇地扫过柏拉图的《理想国》、高尔基的《同志们》、一部《农业百科全书》、巴尔扎克的小说、斯宾塞的《第一原理》、丁尼生的诗集。
『这是我的大学，』佐拉见他看得入神，笑着解释。他们又出来，溜达到旧潟湖边上。

===Original===
