“Now, Bles,” she began, “since we understand each other, can we not work together as good friends?” She spoke simply and frankly, without apparent effort, and talked on at length of her work and vision.
Somehow he could not understand. His mental attitude toward Zora had always been one of guidance, guardianship, and instruction. He had been judging and weighing her from on high, looking down upon her with thoughts of uplift and development. Always he had been holding her dark little hands to lead her out of the swamp of life, and always, when in senseless anger he had half forgotten and deserted her, this vision of elder brotherhood had still remained.

===Chinese===
『那么，布莱斯，』她开口，『既然我们彼此理解了，能不能像好朋友那样，一同做事呢？』她说得简单而坦白，不显一点刻意，又接着讲了许久她的工作和她的愿景。
他不知怎的，听不进去。他对佐拉的心智态度，一向是引导、监护、训导。他一直高高在上地评判她、权衡她，俯视着她，怀着提携和造就她的念头。他总以为自己是牵着那双黝黑的小手、领她走出人生的沼泽，而即使他在无谓的愤怒里把她半遗忘、半抛弃时，这份兄长般的情怀也始终留着。

===Original===
Now this attitude was being revolutionized. She was proposing to him a plan of wide scope﻿—a bold regeneration of the land. It was a plan carefully studied out, long thought of and read about. He was asked to be co-worker﻿—nay, in a sense to be a follower, for he was ignorant of much.
He hesitated. Then all at once a sense of his utter unworthiness overwhelmed him. Who was he to stand and judge this unselfish woman? Who was he to falter when she called? A sense of his smallness and narrowness, of his priggish blindness, rose like a mockery in his soul.

===Chinese===
如今，这态度被彻底翻转了。她正向他提出一个宏大规模的计划——一场大胆的土地复兴。这是她深思熟虑、长久研读、反复琢磨出的计划。他被邀请做合作者——不，在某种意义上是做追随者，因为许多事他都不懂。
他犹豫了。随即，一种自己完全不配的感觉压倒了他。他是谁，竟站着评判这个无私的女子？他是谁，竟在她召唤时畏缩不前？一种自己渺小与狭隘之感、一种自命不凡的盲目之感，像嘲弄一样在他灵魂里升起。

===Original===
One thing alone held him back: he was not unwilling to be simply human, a learner and a follower; but would he as such ever command the love and respect of this new and inexplicable woman? Would not comradeship on the basis of the new friendship which she insisted on, be the death of love and thoughts of love?
Thus he hesitated, knowing that his duty lay clear. In her direst need he had deserted her. He had left her to go to destruction and expected that she would. By a superhuman miracle she had risen and seated herself above him. She was working; here was work to be done. He was asked to help; he would help. If it killed his old and newborn dream of love, well and good; it was his punishment.

===Chinese===
只有一件事拽住他：他并不介意只做一个普通人、一个学习者、一个追随者；可他若这样做了，还能赢得这个全新的、莫测的女子的爱与敬重吗？在她所坚持的新友谊基础上建立同志之情，难道不会扼杀爱情、和一切关于爱的念想？
他就这样犹豫着，明知自己的本分清清楚楚。在她最危急的时刻，他抛弃了她。他把她丢下去毁灭，并且料定她会毁灭。凭着超乎人力的奇迹，她站了起来，并坐在了他之上。她在工作；这里有活儿要做。他被请求帮忙；他会帮。如果这扼杀了他旧日的和新生的爱的梦，那也好；这是他的惩罚。

===Original===
Yet the sacrifice, the readjustment was hard; he grew to it gradually, inwardly revolting, feeling always a great longing to take this woman and make her nestle in his arms as she used to; catching himself again and again on the point of speaking to her and urging, yet ever again holding himself back and bowing in silent respect to the dignity of her life. Only now and then, when their eyes met suddenly or unthinkingly, a great kindling flash of flame seemed struggling behind showers of tears, until in a moment she smiled or spoke, and then the dropping veil left only the frank open glance, unwavering, soft, kind, but nothing more. Then Alwyn would go wearily away, vexed or disappointed, or merely sad, and both would turn to their work again.

===Chinese===
然而这牺牲、这重新调适是艰难的；他是一点点地长进去的，内心在反叛，总感到一种强烈的渴望，想把这个女人揽进怀里、让她像从前那样偎在他怀中；他一次次地发觉自己差点要开口恳求她、劝说她，又一次次地克制住自己，默默地向她生命的尊严致敬。只是偶尔，当两人的目光猝然或无意地相遇，一团巨大的、点燃的火焰似乎在泪雨之后挣扎，直到她忽而一笑、或说上一句话，那垂下的帷幕便只留下坦荡的目光——稳定、温柔、和善，却再无别的。于是阿尔文便疲惫地走开，或恼、或失望、或只是悲伤，而两人又各自回到自己的工作里。

## XXXVI The Land / XXXVI 土地

===Original===
Colonel Cresswell started all the more grimly to overthrow the new work at the school because somewhere down beneath his heart a pity and a wonder were stirring; pity at the perfectly useless struggle to raise the unraisable, a wonder at certain signs of rising. But it was impossible﻿—and unthinkable, even if possible. So he squared his jaw and cheated Zora deliberately in the matter of the cut timber. He placed every obstacle in the way of getting tenants for the school land. Here Johnson, the “faithful nigger,” was of incalculable assistance. He was among the first to hear the call for prospective tenants.

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔上校越发铁了心要搞垮学校那摊新事业，因为在他心底深处，有一种怜悯和一种惊奇正翻涌着；怜悯那想把扶不起的人扶起来、纯属徒劳的努力，惊奇某些确在抬头的迹象。但这是不可能的——即便可能，也是不可想象的。于是他绷紧下巴，在那批砍下的木材上故意坑了佐拉。他为学校那块地招佃户一事设置重重障碍。在这里，那个『忠心的黑鬼』约翰逊，帮了大忙。他是头一批听见招佃风声的人之一。

===Original===
The meeting was in the big room of Zora’s house, and Aunt Rachel came early with her cheery voice and smile which faded so quickly to lines of sorrow and despair, and then twinkled back again. After her hobbled old Sykes. Fully a half-hour later Rob hurried in.
“Johnson,” he informed the others, “has sneaked over to Cresswell’s to tell of this meeting. We ought to beat that nigger up.” But Zora asked him about the new baby, and he was soon deep in child-lore. Higgins and Sanders came together﻿—dirty, apologetic, and furtive. Then came Johnson.

===Chinese===
会开在佐拉家的大屋。蕾切尔大婶来得早，带着她那欢快的嗓音和笑，那笑那样快就褪成悲伤与绝望的纹路，随即又闪烁回来。她身后一瘸一拐地跟着老赛克斯。足足半小时后罗布才匆匆赶到。
『约翰逊，』他告诉大家，『溜到克雷斯韦尔家去告发这次聚会了。我们该把这黑鬼揍一顿。』可佐拉问起他的新生儿，他便很快一头扎进了育儿经。希金斯和桑德斯一同来了——脏兮兮的、赔着笑脸、鬼鬼祟祟。然后约翰逊来了。

===Original===
“How do, Miss Zora﻿—Mr. Alwyn, I sure is glad to see you, sir. Well, if there ain’t Aunt Rachel! looking as young as ever. And Higgins, you scamp﻿—Ah, Mr. Sanders﻿—well, gentlemen and ladies, this sure is gwine to be a good cotton season. I remember﻿—” And he ran on endlessly, now to this one, now to that, now to all, his little eyes all the while dancing insinuatingly here and there. About nine o’clock a buggy drove up and Carter and Simpson came in﻿—Carter, a silent, strong-faced, brown laborer, who listened and looked, and Simpson, a worried nervous man, who sat still with difficulty and commenced many sentences but did not finish them.

===Chinese===
『您好，佐拉小姐——阿尔文先生，能见到您我真高兴，先生。嘿，这不是蕾切尔大婶吗！看着还是那么年轻。还有希金斯，你小子——啊，桑德斯先生——好吧，先生们女士们，今年准是个棉花好年成。我记得——』他滔滔不绝地说下去，一会儿对这个、一会儿对那个、一会儿对所有人，他那双小眼睛始终在这里那里讨好地转个不停。约莫九点钟，一辆轻便马车驶来，卡特和辛普森进来——卡特是个沉默、脸庞坚毅的棕色皮肤劳工，只听只看；辛普森是个忧心忡忡、神经质的人，坐不住，开了许多个头却没一个说完。

===Original===
Alwyn looked at his watch and at Zora, but she gave no sign until they heard a rollicking song outside and Tylor burst into the room. He was nearly seven feet high and broad-shouldered, yellow, with curling hair and laughing brown eyes. He was chewing an enormous quid of tobacco, the juice of which he distributed generously, and had had just liquor enough to make him jolly. His entrance was a breeze and a roar.
Alwyn then undertook to explain the land scheme.
“It is the best land in the county﻿—”
“When it’s cl’ared,” interrupted Johnson, and Simpson looked alarmed.

===Chinese===
阿尔文看看表，又看看佐拉，可她毫无表示，直到他们听见外头一阵喧闹的歌声，泰勒一头闯了进来。他足有七尺高、膀阔肩圆，黄皮肤、鬈头发、一双笑眯眯的棕眼睛。他嘴里嚼着好大一口烟叶，烟汁慷慨地四处乱吐，喝的酒恰到好处，正好叫他快活。他这一进来，就是一阵风、一声吼。
随后阿尔文着手讲解土地方案。
『这是全县最好的地——』
『那是清出来之后，』约翰逊插嘴，辛普森面露惊慌。

===Original===
“It is partially cleared,” continued Alwyn, “and our plan is to sell off small twenty-acre farms﻿—”
“You can’t do nothing on twenty acres﻿—” began Johnson, but Tylor laid his huge hand right over his mouth and said briefly:
“Shut up!”
Alwyn started again: “We shall sell a few twenty-acre farms but keep one central plantation of one hundred acres for the school. Here Miss Zora will carry on her work and the school will run a model farm with your help. We want to centre here agencies to make life better. We want all sorts of industries; we want a little hospital with a resident physician and two or three nurses; we want a cooperative store for buying supplies; we want a cotton-gin and sawmill, and in the future other things. This land here, as I have said, is the richest around. We want to keep this hundred acres for the public good, and not sell it. We are going to deed it to a board of trustees, and those trustees are to be chosen from the ones who buy the small farms.”

===Chinese===
『有一部分已经清出来了，』阿尔文接着说，『我们的方案是，分售二十英亩一块的小农场——』
『二十英亩你能干出个啥——』约翰逊开口，可泰勒一只大手直接捂上了他的嘴，简短地说：
『闭嘴！』
阿尔文重新开始：『我们将分售几块二十英亩的农场，但保留一百英亩的中心种植园给学校。在这里，佐拉小姐将开展她的工作，学校将在你们的帮助下经营一个示范农场。我们要把改善生活的各种机构集中在这里。我们要各行各业；我们要一间小医院，配一位驻院医生和两三个护士；我们要一个买物资的合作商店；我们要一座轧花机和锯木厂，将来还要别的。这里的地，正如我方才所说，是附近最肥的。这一百英亩我们要留作公益，不卖。我们打算把它立契交给一个理事会，而那些理事，从购买小农场的人里选出。』

===Original===
“Who’s going to get what’s made on this land?” asked Sanders.
“All of us. It is going first to pay for the land, then to support the Home and the School, and then to furnish capital for industries.”
Johnson snickered. “You mean youse gwine to git yo’ livin’ off it?”
“Yes,” answered Alwyn; “but I’m going to work for it.”
“Who’s gwine﻿—” began Simpson, but stopped helplessly.
“Who’s going to tend this land?” asked the practical Carter.

===Chinese===
『这块地上出产的东西，归谁？』桑德斯问。
『归我们大家。它先用来付地价，再用来养家舍和学校，然后为各业提供本钱。』
约翰逊窃笑。『你是说，你们打算靠它讨生活？』
『是的，』阿尔文答；『不过我是要干活的。』
『谁要——』辛普森开口，又无助地停住。
『这块地谁来种？』务实的卡特问。

===Original===
“All of us. Each man is going to promise us so many days’ work a year, and we’re going to ask others to help﻿—the women and girls and school children﻿—they will all help.”
“Can you put trust in that sort of help?”
“We can when once the community learns that it pays.”
“Does you own the land?” asked Johnson suddenly.
“No; we’re buying it, and it’s part paid for already.”
The discussion became general. Zora moved about among the men whispering and explaining; while Johnson moved, too, objecting and hinting. At last he arose.

===Chinese===
『我们大家。每个人向我们保证一年干多少天的活，我们还会请别人帮忙——妇女、姑娘和学校里的孩子——他们都会来帮忙。』
『这样的帮工，靠得住吗？』
『等这个社区明白这是划算的，就靠得住了。』
『这地是你们的吗？』约翰逊忽然问。
『不是；我们正在买，已经付了一部分了。』
讨论变得普遍起来。佐拉在众人中间走来走去，低声解释；约翰逊也在动，反对着、暗示着。终于他站了起来。

===Original===
“Brethren,” he began, “the plan’s good enough for talkin’ but you can’t work it; who ever heer’d tell of such a thing? First place, the land ain’t yours; second place, you can’t get it worked; third place, white folks won’t ’low it. Who ever heer’d of such working land on shares?”
“You do it for white folks each day, why not for yourselves,” Alwyn pointed out.
“’Cause we ain’t white, and we can’t do nothin’ like that.”
Tylor was asleep and snoring and the others looked doubtfully at each other. It was a proposal a little too daring for them, a bit too far beyond their experience. One consideration alone kept them from shrinking away and that was Zora’s influence. Not a man was there whom she had not helped and encouraged nor who had not perfect faith in her; in her impetuous hope, her deep enthusiasm, and her strong will. Even her defects﻿—the hard-held temper, the deeply rooted dislikes﻿—caught their imagination.

===Chinese===
『弟兄们，』他开口，『这计划说说是好的，可你们做不成；谁听说过这样的事？头一件，地不是你们的；第二件，你们弄不到人种；第三件，白人不准。谁听说过这样合伙种地的？』
『你们天天替白人这么干，怎么不替自己干，』阿尔文指出。
『因为我们不是白人，我们干不了那样的事。』
泰勒已经睡着了打呼噜，其余的人面面相觑、半信半疑。这提议对他们来说太大胆了一点，离他们的经验太远了一点。只有一件事让他们没有退缩，那就是佐拉的影响力。在场的人，没有一个没受过她的帮助和鼓励，也没有一个不对她有十足的信心；信她那股冲劲勃勃的希望、她那深沉的热忱、她那坚强的意志。连她的缺点——那紧绷着的脾气、那根深蒂固的好恶——都激起了他们的想象。

===Original===
Finally, after several other meetings five men took courage﻿—three of the best and two of the weakest. During the Spring long negotiations were entered into by Miss Smith to “buy” the five men. Colonel Cresswell and Mr. Tolliver had them all charged with large sums of indebtedness and these sums had to be assumed by the school. As Colonel Cresswell counted over two thousand dollars of school notes and deposited them beside the mortgage he smiled grimly for he saw the end. Yet, even then his hand trembled and that curious doubt came creeping back. He put it aside angrily and glanced up.
“Nigger wants to talk with you,” announced his clerk.
The Colonel sauntered out and found Bles Alwyn waiting.

===Chinese===
最后，又开过几次会后，有五个人鼓起了勇气——三个最好的、两个最弱的。整个春天，史密斯小姐为『买下』这五人进行了漫长的谈判。克雷斯韦尔上校和托利弗先生给他们每个人都挂上了一大笔债务，而这些债都得由学校来承担。克雷斯韦尔上校点着两千多块钱的学校欠条、把它们放在抵押文件旁边时，冷酷地笑了，因为他看见了尽头。然而即便那时，他的手也在抖，那古怪的疑虑又悄悄爬了回来。他恼怒地把它甩开，抬起头。
『有个黑鬼要见您，』他的伙计通报。
上校踱出去，看见布莱斯·阿尔文在等。

===Original===
“Colonel Cresswell,” he said, “I have charge of the buying for the school and our tenants this year and I naturally want to do the best possible. I thought I’d come over and see about getting my supplies at your store.”
“That’s all right; you can get anything you want,” said Colonel Cresswell cheerily, for this to his mind was evidence of sense on the part of the Negroes. Bles showed his list of needed supplies﻿—seeds, meat, cornmeal, coffee, sugar, etc. The Colonel glanced over it carelessly, then moved away.
“All right. Come and get what you want﻿—any time,” he called back.
“But about the prices,” said Alwyn, following him.
“Oh, they’ll be all right.”
“Of course. But what I want is an estimate of your lowest cash prices.”
“Cash?”
“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
『克雷斯韦尔上校，』他说，『今年学校和佃户们的采购归我管，我自然想把事情办得尽量好。我想过来谈谈，在您铺子里进我们的货。』
『没问题；你想要什么就拿什么，』克雷斯韦尔上校乐呵呵地说，因为在他看来，这是黑人有脑子的证据。布莱斯亮出他需要的物资清单——种子、肉、玉米面、咖啡、糖等等。上校漫不经心地扫了一眼，便走开了。
『行。要什么尽管来拿——什么时候都行，』他回头喊。
『可价钱呢，』阿尔文跟着他。
『哦，价钱没问题。』
『当然。可我想要的是，您最低的现钱价的估算。』
『现钱？』
『是的，先生。』

===Original===
Cresswell thought a while; such a businesslike proposition from Negroes surprised him.
“Well, I’ll let you know,” he said.
It was nearly a week later before Alwyn approached him again.
“Now, see here,” said Colonel Cresswell, “there’s practically no difference between cash and time prices. We buy our stock on time and you can just as well take advantage of this as not. I have figured out about what these things will cost. The best thing for you to do is to make a deposit here and get things when you want them. If you make a good deposit I’ll throw off ten percent, which is all of my profit.”
“Thank you,” said Alwyn, but he looked over the account and found the whole bill at least twice as large as he expected. Without further parley, he made some excuse and started to town while Mr. Cresswell went to the telephone.

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔想了一会儿；黑人们竟提出这样一笔正经生意，叫他吃惊。
『好吧，我回头告诉你，』他说。
将近一星期后，阿尔文才再找上他。
『这样，』克雷斯韦尔上校说，『现钱价和赊账价其实没什么差别。我们的货是赊来的，你尽可以沾这个光。我已经算出这些东西大概要多少钱。你最好是存一笔押金在这儿，要用什么就拿。你若存得够多，我让一成，那也就是我全部的利了。』
『谢谢，』阿尔文说，可他一过账，发现整笔账至少比他预想的大一倍。他不再多话，找个借口便动身进城，而克雷斯韦尔先生则走向了电话。

===Original===
In town Alwyn went to all the chief merchants one after another and received to his great surprise practically the same estimate. He could not understand it. He had estimated the current market prices according to the Montgomery paper, yet the prices in Toomsville were fifty to a hundred and fifty percent higher. The merchant to whom he went last, laughed.
“Don’t you know we’re not going to interfere with Colonel Cresswell’s tenants?” He stated the dealers’ attitude, and Alwyn saw light. He went home and told Zora, and she listened without surprise.
“Now to business,” she said briskly. “Miss Smith,” turning to the teacher, “as I told you, they’re combined against us in town and we must buy in Montgomery. I was sure it was coming, but I wanted to give Colonel Cresswell every chance. Bles starts for Montgomery﻿—”

===Chinese===
在镇上，阿尔文挨家挨户地走访所有大商家，大为惊讶地发现，每家给的报价几乎一模一样。他闹不明白。他本是按蒙哥马利报纸上的行情估的价，可图姆斯维尔的价高出五成到一倍半。他最后去的那家商人笑了。
『你不知道吗？我们不会插手克雷斯韦尔上校的佃户。』他点破了商人们的立场，阿尔文恍然大悟。他回家告诉佐拉，她听着，并不惊讶。
『言归正传，』她利落地说。『史密斯小姐，』她转向那位教师，『正如我跟您说的，镇上的人联手对付我们，我们必须去蒙哥马利进货。我料定会有这一天，可我想给克雷斯韦尔上校每一个机会。布莱斯这就动身去蒙哥马利——』

===Original===
Alwyn looked up. “Does he?” he asked, smiling.
“Yes,” said Zora, smiling in turn. “We must lose no further time.”
“But there’s no train from Toomsville tonight.”
“But there’s one from Barton in the morning and Barton is only twenty miles away.”
“It is a long walk.” Alwyn thought a while, silently. Then he rose. “I’m going,” he said. “Goodbye.”
In less than a week the storehouse was full, and tenants were at work. The twenty acres of cleared swamp land, attended to by the voluntary labor of all the tenants, was soon bearing a magnificent crop. Colonel Cresswell inspected all the crops daily with a proprietary air that would have been natural had these folk been simply tenants, and as such he persisted in regarding them.

===Chinese===
阿尔文抬起头。『是吗？』他笑着问。
『是的，』佐拉也笑着说。『不能再耽搁了。』
『可今晚图姆斯维尔没火车。』
『可早上巴顿有一班，而巴顿只有二十英里远。』
『那可是很长一段路。』阿尔文默默想了一会儿。然后他站起来。『我去了，』他说。『再见。』
不到一星期，仓库满了，佃户们下了地。那二十英亩清出来的沼泽地，靠所有佃户的义务劳作照料，很快长起了喜人的庄稼。克雷斯韦尔上校每天以一种主人的派头巡视所有庄稼——倘若这些人只是寻常佃户，这派头倒也自然，而他也就执意把他们当作寻常佃户看待。

===Original===
The cotton now growing was perhaps not so uniformly fine as the first acre of Silver Fleece, but it was of unusual height and thickness.
“At least a bale to the acre,” Alwyn estimated, and the Colonel mentally determined to take two-thirds of the crop. After that he decided that he would evict Zora immediately; since sufficient land was cleared already for his purposes and moreover, he had seen with consternation a herd of cattle grazing in one field on some early green stuff, and heard a drove of hogs in the swamp. Such an example before the tenants of the Black Belt would be fatal. He must wait a few weeks for them to pick the cotton﻿—then, the end. He was fighting the battle of his color and caste.

===Chinese===
眼下长着的棉花，也许不如头一亩银色羊毛那样齐整地好，却长得格外高、格外密。
『至少一英亩一包，』阿尔文估。上校心里盘算着要拿走三分之二的收成。之后，他决定立刻把佐拉赶走；因为为他所需，开出来的地已经够了，再者，他骇然看见一群牛在一片地里啃早青，又听见沼泽里有一群猪。这样的榜样摆在黑土地带的佃户面前，将是致命的。他得等几个礼拜，等他们把棉花摘了——然后，就是尽头。他在为他这一肤色、这一等级而战。

===Original===
The children sang merrily in the brown-white field. The wide baskets, poised aloft, foamed on the erect and swaying bodies of the dark carriers. The crop throughout the land was short that year, for prices had ruled low last season in accordance with the policy of the Combine. This year they started high again. Would they fall? Many thought so and hastened to sell.
Zora and Alwyn gathered their tenants’ crops, ginned them at the Cresswells’ gin, and carried their cotton to town, where it was deposited in the warehouse of the Farmers’ League.
“Now,” said Alwyn, “we would best sell while prices are high.”
Zora laughed at him frankly.
“We can’t,” she said. “Don’t you know that Colonel Cresswell will attach our cotton for rent as soon as it touches the warehouse?”

===Chinese===
孩子们在棕白色的田里欢快地唱着歌。宽大的篓子高高顶在头上，在那些黑色搬运工笔挺摇曳的身子上泡沫般堆涌。那年全境收成都不好，因为上一季联合体压低价钱的策略。今年又开了高价。会跌吗？许多人这么想，急着脱手。
佐拉和阿尔文收齐了佃户们的棉花，在克雷斯韦尔家的轧花机里轧了，运进镇上，存进了农民同盟的仓库。
『现在，』阿尔文说，『趁价高，最好卖了。』
佐拉坦率地笑他。
『不能卖，』她说。『你不知道吗？棉花一进仓库，克雷斯韦尔上校就会扣押它抵租？』

===Original===
“But it’s ours.”
“Nothing is ours. No black man ordinarily can sell his crop without a white creditor’s consent.”
Alwyn fumed.
“The best way,” he declared, “is to go to Montgomery and get a first-class lawyer and just fight the thing through. The land is legally ours, and he has no right to our cotton.”
“Yes, but you must remember that no man like Colonel Cresswell regards a business bargain with a colored man as binding. No white man under ordinary circumstances will help enforce such a bargain against prevailing public opinion.”

===Chinese===
『可它是我们的。』
『没有什么是我们的。黑人通常没有白人债主同意，卖不了自己的庄稼。』
阿尔文直冒火。
『最好的办法，』他声言，『就是去蒙哥马利，请一个一流的律师，把这事打到底。地在法律上是我们的，他对我们的棉花没有权利。』
『是的，可你得记住，像克雷斯韦尔上校这样的人，不认为跟有色人做的买卖有约束力。在通常情形下，没有哪个白人会帮着去执行这样一桩违背主流民意的买卖。』

===Original===
“But if we cannot trust to the justice of the case, and if you knew we couldn’t, why did you try?”
“Because I had to try; and moreover the circumstances are not altogether ordinary: the men in power in Toomsville now are not the landlords of this county; they are poor whites. The Judge and sheriff were both elected by mill-hands who hate Cresswell and Taylor. Then there’s a new young lawyer who wants Harry Cresswell’s seat in Congress; he don’t know much law, I’m afraid; but what he don’t know of this case I think I do. I’ll get his advice and then﻿—I mean to conduct the case myself,” Zora calmly concluded.
“Without a lawyer!” Bles Alwyn stared his amazement.
“Without a lawyer in court.”
“Zora! That would be foolish!”
“Is it? Let’s think. For over a year now I’ve been studying the law of the case,” and she pointed to her law books; “I know the law and most of the decisions. Moreover, as a black woman fighting a hopeless battle with landlords, I’ll gain the one thing lacking.”
“What’s that?”
“The sympathy of the court and the bystanders.”

===Chinese===
『可要是我们不能指望这案子的公道，而你也知道不能，那你为什么还要试？』
『因为我非试不可；再者，眼下的情形并不全然寻常：如今图姆斯维尔掌权的，不是本县的地主，而是穷白人。法官和警长都是厂工选上去的，他们恨克雷斯韦尔和泰勒。还有一个年轻的律师，想夺哈里·克雷斯韦尔的国会席位；他懂的法律恐怕不多，可这桩案子他不懂的地方，我想我懂。我去请教他，然后——我打算自己来主理这桩案子，』佐拉平静地下了结论。
『没律师！』布莱斯·阿尔文瞪着眼，难掩惊讶。
『庭上没律师。』
『佐拉！那太蠢了！』
『是吗？咱们想想。这一年来，我一直在研读这案子的法律，』她指着她的法律书；『我懂这些法律，也懂大多数判例。再者，作为一个黑人女子，与地主们打一场无望的官司，我会赢得一样目前还缺的东西。』
『缺什么？』
『法庭和旁听者的同情。』

===Original===
“Pshaw! From these Southerners?”
“Yes, from them. They are very human, these men, especially the laborers. Their prejudices are cruel enough, but there are joints in their armor. They are used to seeing us either scared or blindly angry, and they understand how to handle us then, but at other times it is hard for them to do anything but meet us in a human way.”
“But, Zora, think of the contact of the court, the humiliation, the coarse talk﻿—”
Zora put up her hand and lightly touched his arm. Looking at him, she said:
“Mud doesn’t hurt much. This is my duty. Let me do it.”
His eyes fell before the shadow of a deeper rebuke. He arose heavily.
“Very well,” he acquiesced as he passed slowly out.

===Chinese===
『呸！从这些南方人那里？』
『是的，从他们那里。这些人非常有人情味，尤其是那些劳工。他们的偏见够残酷，可他们的盔甲也有缝隙。他们看惯了我们不是吓破了胆、就是瞎发火，那时候他们懂得怎么对付我们，可别的时候，他们除了以人情相待，也很难做到别的。』
『可是，佐拉，你想想法庭上的接触、那份屈辱、那些粗话——』
佐拉抬起手，轻轻触了触他的手臂。她望着他说：
『泥巴伤不了人多少。这是我的本分。让我去做。』
在一重更深责备的阴影前，他垂下了眼。他沉重地站起来。
『好吧，』他慢慢走出去时，应承道。

===Original===
The young lawyer started to refuse to touch the case until he saw﻿—or did Zora adroitly make him see?﻿—a chance for eventual political capital. They went over the matter carefully, and the lawyer acquired a respect for the young woman’s knowledge.
“First,” he said, “get an injunction on the cotton﻿—then go to court.” And to insure the matter he slipped over and saw the Judge.
Colonel Cresswell next day stalked angrily into his lawyers’ office.
“See here,” he thundered, handing the lawyer the notice of the injunction.
“See the Judge,” began the lawyer, and then remembered, as he was often forced to do these days, who was Judge.
He inquired carefully into the case and examined the papers. Then he said:
“Colonel Cresswell, who drew this contract of sale?”
“The black girl did.”
“Impossible!”
“She certainly did﻿—wrote it in my presence.”
“Well, it’s mighty well done.”
“You mean it will stand in law?”
“It certainly will. There’s but one way to break it, and that’s to allege misunderstanding on your part.”

===Chinese===
那位年轻律师起初不肯接这案子，直到他看见——或者是佐拉巧妙地让他看见？——日后的一点政治资本。他们仔细地把事情过了一遍，律师对这年轻女子的学识生出几分敬意。
『头一步，』他说，『对那批棉花申请一道禁令——然后上庭。』为确保万无一失，他悄悄去见了法官。
第二天，克雷斯韦尔上校怒气冲冲地大步闯进他律师的办公室。
『你瞧，』他雷鸣般地吼，把禁令通知递给律师。
『去找法官，』律师开口，随即又想起来——这些天他常不得不想起——如今法官是谁。
他仔细问了案情、审查了文件。然后他说：
『克雷斯韦尔上校，这份买卖合同是谁起草的？』
『那个黑人丫头。』
『不可能！』
『确实是她——当着我的面写的。』
『嗯，写得很漂亮。』
『你是说它在法律上站得住？』
『当然站得住。要推翻它只有一个法子，就是声称你方面存在误解。』

===Original===
Cresswell winced. It was not pleasant to go into open court and acknowledge himself overreached by a Negro; but several thousand dollars in cotton and land were at stake.
“Go ahead,” he concurred.
“You can depend on Taylor, of course?” added the lawyer.
“Of course,” answered Cresswell. “But why prolong the thing?”
“You see, she’s got your cotton tied by injunction.”
“I don’t see how she did it.”
“Easy enough: this Judge is the poor white you opposed in the last primary.”
Within a week the case was called, and they filed into the courtroom. Cresswell’s lawyer saw only this black woman﻿—no other lawyer or sign of one appeared to represent her. The place soon filled with a lazy, tobacco-chewing throng of white men. A few blacks whispered in one corner. The dirty stove was glowing with pinewood and the Judge sat at a desk.

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔缩了一下。在公开法庭上承认自己被一个黑人占了上风，并不愉快；但几千块钱的棉花和地都系于此。
『去办吧，』他同意。
『泰勒当然靠得住吧？』律师补了一句。
『当然，』克雷斯韦尔答。『可为什么要拖？』
『你瞧，她用禁令把你的棉花扣住了。』
『我不懂她怎么做到的。』
『容易得很：这位法官，就是你在上次初选里反对的那个穷白人。』
不出一星期，案子开庭，他们鱼贯走进法庭。克雷斯韦尔的律师只看见这个黑人女子——没有别的律师、也没有任何律师的影子出现来替她代理。法庭很快挤满了一群懒洋洋的、嚼着烟叶的白人男子。几个黑人在一个角落里低语。肮脏的炉子里松木烧得通红，法官坐在桌前。

===Original===
“Where’s your lawyer?” he asked sharply of Zora.
“I have none,” returned Zora, rising.
There came a silence in the court. Her voice was low, and the men leaned forward to listen. The Judge felt impelled to be over-gruff.
“Get a lawyer,” he ordered.
“Your honor, my case is simple, and with your honor’s permission I wish to conduct it myself. I cannot afford a lawyer, and I do not think I need one.”
Cresswell’s lawyer smiled and leaned back. It was going to be easier than he supposed. Evidently the woman believed she had no case, and was weakening.

===Chinese===
『你的律师呢？』他尖锐地问佐拉。
『我没有律师，』佐拉起身答。
法庭里一片静默。她的嗓音低，人们都倾身去听。法官觉得自己非得格外粗声粗气不可。
『请个律师，』他命令。
『阁下，我的案子很简单，蒙阁下恩准，我想自己主理。我请不起律师，也觉得不必。』
克雷斯韦尔的律师微微一笑，往后一靠。这比他想的还要容易。显然，这女人自知理亏，正在泄气。

===Original===
The trial proceeded, and Zora stated her contention. She told how long her mother and grandmother had served the Cresswells and showed her receipt for rent paid.
“A friend sent me some money. I went to Mr. Cresswell and asked him to sell me two hundred acres of land. He consented to do so and signed this contract in the presence of his son-in-law.”
Just then John Taylor came into the court, and Cresswell beckoned to him.
“I want you to help me out, John.”
“All right,” whispered Taylor. “What can I do?”
“Swear that Cresswell didn’t mean to sign this,” said the lawyer quickly, as he arose to address the court.

===Chinese===
审讯进行下去，佐拉陈述了她的主张。她讲了她母亲和祖母在克雷斯韦尔家服役了多少代，并出示了她交租的收据。
『一位朋友寄给我一笔钱。我去找克雷斯韦尔先生，请他卖给我两百英亩地。他同意了，并当着他女婿的面签了这份合同。』
就在这时，约翰·泰勒走进法庭，克雷斯韦尔朝他招手。
『约翰，我要你帮我个忙。』
『行，』泰勒悄声道。『我能做什么？』
『发誓说克雷斯韦尔签字时不是那个意思，』律师飞快地说，一边起身准备向法庭陈述。

===Original===
Taylor looked at the paper blankly and then at Cresswell and some inkling of the irreconcilable difference in the two natures leapt in both their hearts. Cresswell might gamble and drink and lie “like a gentleman,” but he would never willingly cheat or take advantage of a white man’s financial necessities. Taylor, on the other hand, had a horror of a lie, never drank nor played games of chance, but his whole life was speculation and in the business game he was utterly ruthless and respected no one. Such men could never thoroughly understand each other. To Cresswell a man who had cheated the whole South out of millions by a series of misrepresentations ought to regard this little falsehood as nothing.

===Chinese===
泰勒茫然地看着那张纸，又看了看克雷斯韦尔，两人心里同时一闪，意识到彼此天性间那不可调和的鸿沟。克雷斯韦尔也许会『像个绅士那样』赌钱、喝酒、撒谎，但他绝不会蓄意欺骗、或趁白人手紧时占便宜。泰勒则相反，他对谎言深恶痛绝，从不喝酒、从不赌博，可他整个生命就是投机，而在生意这场游戏里他冷酷无情、谁也不放在眼里。这样的人永远无法彻底相互理解。在克雷斯韦尔看来，一个靠一连串欺诈骗走整个南方几百万的人，本该把这小小的谎话当作无物。

===Original===
Meantime Colonel Cresswell’s lawyer was on his feet, and he adopted his most irritating and contemptuous manner.
“This nigger wench wrote out some illegible stuff and Colonel Cresswell signed it to get rid of her. We are not going to question the legality of the form﻿—that’s neither here nor there. The point is, Mr. Cresswell never intended﻿—never dreamed of selling this wench land right in front of his door. He meant to rent her the land and sign a receipt for rent paid in advance. I will not worry your honor by a long argument to prove this, but just call one of the witnesses well known to you﻿—Mr. John Taylor of the Toomsville mills.”
Taylor looked toward the door and then slowly took the stand.
“Mr. Taylor,” said the lawyer carelessly, “were you present at this transaction?”
“Yes.”
“Did you see Colonel Cresswell sign this paper?”
“Yes.”
“Well, did he intend so far as you know to sign such a paper?”
“I do not know his intentions.”
“Did he say he meant to sign such a contract?”
Taylor hesitated.
“Yes,” he finally answered. Colonel Cresswell looked up in amazement and the lawyer dropped his glasses.

===Chinese===
与此同时，克雷斯韦尔上校的律师已站了起来，他拿出了最叫人窝火、最轻蔑的做派。
『这个黑鬼丫头写了些鬼画符的东西，克雷斯韦尔上校为了打发她才签了字。我们不打算质疑这格式的合法性——那无关紧要。要点是，克雷斯韦尔先生从来没有这个意思——做梦也没想过——把家门前的地卖给这丫头。他是要租给她地、签一张预付租金的收据。我不想用一通长篇大论来劳烦阁下证明这一点，只传唤一位诸位熟知的证人——图姆斯维尔纱厂的约翰·泰勒先生。』
泰勒望向门口，然后慢慢走上证人席。
『泰勒先生，』律师漫不经心地说，『这笔交易在场吗？』
『在。』
『您看见克雷斯韦尔上校签这张纸了吗？』
『看见了。』
『那么，就您所知，他是有意签这样一张纸的吗？』
『我不知道他的用意。』
『他说过他有意签这样一份合同吗？』
泰勒犹豫了。
『说过，』他终于答。克雷斯韦尔上校惊讶地抬起头，律师的眼镜也掉了。

===Original===
“I﻿—I don’t think you perhaps understood me, Mr. Taylor,” he gasped. “I﻿—er﻿—meant to ask if Colonel Cresswell, in signing this paper, meant to sign a contract to sell this wench two hundred acres of land?”
“He said he did,” reiterated Taylor. “Although I ought to add that he did not think the girl would ever be able to pay. If he had thought she would pay, I don’t think he would have signed the paper.”
Colonel Cresswell went red, then pale, and leaning forward before the whole court, he hurled:
“You damned scoundrel!”
The Judge rapped for order and fidgeted in his seat. There was some confusion and snickering in the courtroom. Finally the Judge plucked up courage:
“The defendant is ordered to deliver this cotton to Zora Cresswell,” he directed.

===Chinese===
『我——我想您也许没听懂我的意思，泰勒先生，』他倒吸一口气。『我——呃——是想问，克雷斯韦尔上校签这张纸时，是不是有意签一份把这丫头两百英亩地卖掉的合同？』
『他说他是的，』泰勒重申。『虽然我应当补充一句，他当时并不认为这丫头付得起钱。要是他认为她付得起，我想他就不会签这张纸了。』
克雷斯韦尔上校先涨红了脸，又白了脸，当着整个法庭倾身向前，吼道：
『你这该死的无赖！』
法官敲槌维持秩序，在座位上不安地动来动去。法庭里有些骚动和窃笑。终于法官鼓起勇气：
『被告被令将这批棉花交付给佐拉·克雷斯韦尔，』他裁示。

===Original===
The raging of Colonel Cresswell’s anger now turned against John Taylor as well as the Negroes. Wind of the estrangement flew over town quickly. The poor whites saw a chance to win Taylor’s influence and the sheriff approached him cautiously. Taylor paid him slight courtesy. He was irritated with this devilish Negro problem; he was making money; his wife and babies were enjoying life, and here was this fool trial to upset matters. But the sheriff talked.
“The thing I’m afraid of,” he said, “is that Cresswell and his gang will swing in the niggers on us.”
“How do you mean?”
“Let ’em vote.”
“But they’d have to read and write.”
“Sure!”
“Well, then,” said Taylor, “it might be a good thing.”

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔上校的怒火，如今既烧向黑人、也烧向约翰·泰勒。两人失和的风声很快传遍全镇。穷白人们看见了拉拢泰勒影响力的机会，警长小心翼翼地接近他。泰勒对他不怎么客气。他被这该死的黑人问题弄得心烦；他在赚钱；妻儿正享受生活，偏偏这场蠢官司来搅局。可警长还是说下去。
『我怕的是，』他说，『克雷斯韦尔和他那一伙会拿黑鬼来对付我们。』
『怎么讲？』
『让他们投票。』
『可他们得识字才行。』
『那当然！』
『那么，』泰勒说，『这也许是件好事。』

===Original===
Colton eyed him suspiciously.
“You’d let a nigger vote?”
“Why, yes, if he had sense enough.”
“There ain’t no nigger got sense.”
“Oh, pshaw!” Taylor ejaculated, walking away.
The sheriff was angry and mistrustful. He believed he had discovered a deep-laid scheme of the aristocrats to cultivate friendliness between whites and blacks, and then use black voters to crush the whites. Such a course was, in Colton’s mind, dangerous, monstrous, and unnatural; it must be stopped at all hazards. He began to whisper among his friends. One or two meetings were held, and the flame of racial prejudice was studiously fanned.

===Chinese===
科尔顿狐疑地打量他。
『你会让黑鬼投票？』
『这个，会啊，只要他有脑子。』
『没有哪个黑鬼有脑子。』
『哦，去你的！』泰勒脱口而出，走开了。
警长又气又疑。他相信自己识破了贵族们的一个深谋：先培植黑白友好，再用黑人选票压垮白人。在科尔顿看来，这样的做法危险、怪诞、不自然；必须不惜一切代价阻止。他开始在朋友间散布风声。开了一两次会，种族偏见的火焰被有意地扇动着。

===Original===
The atmosphere of the town and country quickly began to change. Whatever little beginnings of friendship and understanding had arisen now quickly disappeared. The town of a Saturday no longer belonged to a happy, careless crowd of black peasants, but the black folk found themselves elbowed to the gutter, while ugly quarrels flashed here and there with a quick arrest of the Negroes.
Colonel Cresswell made a sudden resolve. He sent for the sheriff and received him at the Oaks, in his most respectable style, filling him with good food, and warming him with good liquor.
“Colton,” he asked, “are you sending any of your white children to the nigger school yet?”
“What!” yelled Colton.

===Chinese===
小镇和乡间的气氛迅速开始变化。从前那一点点友谊与谅解的萌芽，如今很快消散。镇上的星期六，不再属于一群快快活活、大大咧咧的黑人农民，黑人们发现自己被挤到了排水沟边，而丑陋的争吵此起彼伏，伴随着黑人迅速被捕。
克雷斯韦尔上校做了一个突兀的决定。他派人去请警长，在橡树庄以最体面的排场接待他，用好菜填饱他、用好酒暖热他。
『科尔顿，』他问，『你把你的白人孩子送进黑人学校了吗？』
『什么！』科尔顿大叫。

===Original===
The Colonel laughed, frankly telling Colton John Taylor’s philosophy on the race problem﻿—his willingness to let Negroes vote; his threat to let blacks and whites work together; his contempt for the officials elected by the people.
“Candidly, Colton,” he concluded, “I believe in aristocracy. I can’t think it right or wise to replace the old aristocracy by new and untried blood.” And in a sudden outburst﻿—“But, by God, sir! I’m a white man, and I place the lowest white man ever created above the highest darkey ever thought of. This Yankee, Taylor, is a nigger-lover. He’s secretly encouraging and helping them. You saw what he did to me, and I’m warning you in time.”

===Chinese===
上校笑了，坦白地把约翰·泰勒那一套种族问题的哲学告诉科尔顿——他乐意让黑人投票；他威胁要让黑人和白人一起做工；他瞧不起人民选出来的官员。
『坦白说，科尔顿，』他总结道，『我信贵族政治。我无法认为，用未经考验的新血液取代旧贵族是对头、是明智的。』随即一阵爆发——『可是，老天在上，先生！我是个白人，我把世间最低贱的白人，置于人们所能想到的最高贵的黑佬之上。这个扬基佬泰勒，是个黑鬼同情者。他在暗中怂恿他们、帮助他们。你亲眼看见他对我做了什么，我及时警告你。』

===Original===
Colton’s glass dropped.
“I thought it was you that was corralling the niggers against us,” he exclaimed.
The Colonel reddened. “I don’t count all white men my equals, I admit,” he returned with dignity, “but I know the difference between a white man and a nigger.”
Colton stretched out his massive hand. “Put it there, sir,” said he; “I misjudged you, Colonel Cresswell. I’m a Southerner, and I honor the old aristocracy you represent. I’m going to join with you to crush this Yankee and put the niggers in their places. They are getting impudent around here; they need a lesson and, by gad! they’ll get one they’ll remember.”
“Now, see here, Colton﻿—nothing rash,” the Colonel charged him, warningly. “Don’t stir up needless trouble; but﻿—well, things must change.”

===Chinese===
科尔顿的杯子掉了。
『我还以为是你，在收拢黑鬼来对付我们呢，』他叫。
上校红了脸。『我承认，我不把所有白人都算作与我平等，』他庄重地回，『可我分得清白人和黑鬼的区别。』
科尔顿伸出他那只粗大的手。『握个手，先生，』他说；『我错看您了，克雷斯韦尔上校。我是个南方人，我敬重您所代表的老贵族。我要跟您联手，压垮这个扬基佬，让黑鬼们各归其位。他们在这附近越来越放肆了；他们需要个教训，老天！他们会得一个忘不了的教训。』
『好了，听我说，科尔顿——别莽撞，』上校警告地嘱咐他。『别惹不必要的乱子；可是——嗯，事情总得变一变。』

===Original===
Colton rose and shook his head.
“The niggers need a lesson,” he muttered as he unsteadily bade his host goodbye. Cresswell watched him uncomfortably as he rode away, and again a feeling of doubt stirred within him. What new force was he loosening against his black folk﻿—his own black folk, who had lived about him and his fathers nigh three hundred years? He saw the huge form of the sheriff loom like an evil spirit a moment on the rise of the road and sink into the night. He turned slowly to his cheerless house shuddering as he entered the uninviting portals.

===Chinese===
科尔顿站起身，摇了摇头。
『黑鬼们需要个教训，』他嘟囔着，步履不稳地向主人告辞。克雷斯韦尔看着他骑马离去，心里很不自在，那种疑虑之感又一次在他心里翻动。他在对自家黑人——世世代代在他和他父辈身边生活了将近三百年的自家黑人——释放出怎样的新力量？他看见警长魁梧的身影，像一道邪灵，在路的高坡上隐现片刻，便沉入夜色。他慢慢转身，回到他那冷清的宅子，进门时，在那阴森的门廊里打了个寒颤。

## XXXVII The Mob / XXXVII 暴民

===Original===
When Emma, Bertie’s child, came home after a two years’ course of study, she had passed from girlhood to young womanhood. She was white, and sandy-haired. She was not beautiful, and she appeared to be fragile; but she also looked sweet and good, with that peculiar innocence which peers out upon the world with calm, round eyes and sees no evil, but does methodically its simple, everyday work. Zora mothered her, Miss Smith found her plenty to do, and Bles thought her a good girl. But Mrs. Cresswell found her perfect, and began to scheme to marry her off. For Mary Cresswell, with the restlessness and unhappiness of an unemployed woman, was trying to atone for her former blunders.

===Chinese===
伯蒂的孩子埃玛，两年学业后回家时，已从少女长成了青年女子。她是白人，沙色头发。她算不上美，看着还显得纤弱；可她也显得甜美、善良，带着那种特有的天真——用一双沉静的圆眼睛望着这世界，看不见邪恶，只本本分分地做着简单日常的活计。佐拉像母亲一样照管她，史密斯小姐给她找了许多事做，布莱斯觉得她是个好姑娘。可克雷斯韦尔太太觉得她完美，开始盘算着把她嫁出去。因为玛丽·克雷斯韦尔，带着一个无所事事的女人的那种烦躁和不快，正设法为自己从前的过失赎罪。

===Original===
Her humiliation after the episode at Cresswell Oaks had been complete. It seemed to her that the original cause of her whole life punishment lay in her persistent misunderstanding of the black people and their problem. Zora appeared to her in a new and glorified light﻿—a vigorous, self-sacrificing woman. She knew that Zora had refused to marry Bles, and this again seemed fitting. Zora was not meant for marrying; she was a born leader, wedded to a great cause; she had long outgrown the boy and girl affection. She was the sort of woman she herself might have been if she had not married.

===Chinese===
橡树庄那一桩之后，她的屈辱可谓到了极点。在她看来，她一生受罚的根源，就在于她一贯地误解了黑人及其问题。佐拉在她眼里显现出一种新的、升华了的光彩——一个生气勃勃、自我牺牲的女子。她知道佐拉拒绝了布莱斯的求婚，而这似乎也恰如其分。佐拉不是为婚姻而生的；她天生是领袖，与一桩大事业结了婚；她早已长过了少男少女的那份情。她正是那种女人——倘若她自己不曾结婚，本可以成为的那种女人。

===Original===
Alwyn, on the other hand, needed a wife; he was a great, virile boy, requiring a simple, affectionate mate. No sooner did she see Emma than she was sure that this was the ideal wife. She compared herself with Helen Cresswell. Helen was a contented wife and mother because she was fitted for the position, and happy in it; while she who had aimed so high had fallen piteously. From such a fate she would save Zora and Bles.
Emma’s course in nurse-training had been simple and short and there was no resident physician; but Emma, in her unemotional way, was a born nurse and did much good among the sick in the neighborhood. Zora had a small log hospital erected with four white beds, a private room, and an office which was also Emma’s bedroom. The new white physician in town, just fresh from school in Atlanta, became interested and helped with advice and suggestions.

===Chinese===
而阿尔文，需要一个妻子；他是个高大、健壮的男子，需要一个简单、深情的伴侣。她一见埃玛，便断定这就是理想的妻子。她拿自己与海伦·克雷斯韦尔相比。海伦是个知足的妻子和母亲，因为她适合那个位置，并在其中得到快乐；而她，目标那样高，却凄惨地跌了下来。这样的命运，她要替佐拉和布莱斯免去。
埃玛受的护士训练简单而短暂，这里也没有驻院医生；可埃玛，以她那不动声色的方式，是个天生的护士，在四邻的病人中做了许多好事。佐拉盖了一座小小的圆木医院，有四张白床、一间单人房、一间办公室——这办公室也是埃玛的卧室。镇上新来的白人医生，刚从亚特兰大的学校毕业，起了兴趣，常常来出主意、提建议。

===Original===
Meantime John Taylor’s troubles began to increase. Under the old political regime it had been an easy matter to avoid serious damage-suits for the accidents in the mill. Much child labor and the lack of protective devices made accidents painfully frequent. Taylor insisted that the chief cause was carelessness, while the mill hands alleged criminal neglect on his part. When the new labor officials took charge of the court and the break occurred between Colonel Cresswell and his son-in-law, Taylor found that several damage-suits were likely to cost him a considerable sum.

===Chinese===
与此同时，约翰·泰勒的麻烦开始增多。在旧的政治体制下，要躲开厂里事故的巨额索赔，本是件容易事。大量童工，加上缺少防护装置，使事故频繁得令人痛心。泰勒坚持说，主要原因是工人不小心，而厂工们则指控他犯了刑事疏忽。当新上台的劳工官员掌管了法庭、克雷斯韦尔上校又与女婿决裂之后，泰勒发现，好几桩索赔官司很可能要叫他破一大笔财。

===Original===
He determined not to let the bad feelings go too far, and when a particularly distressing accident to a little girl took place, he showed more than his usual interest and offered to care for her. The new young physician recommended Zora’s infirmary as the only near place that offered a chance for the child’s recovery.
“Take her out,” Taylor promptly directed.
Zora was troubled when the child came. She knew the suspicious temper of the town whites. The very next day Taylor sent out a second case, a child who had been hurt some time before and was not recovering as she should.

===Chinese===
他决心不让这种恶感闹得太过，因此当一个小女孩出了尤其凄惨的事故时，他表现出超过往常的关切，主动提出要照料她。那位年轻的新医生推荐了佐拉的小医院，说那是附近唯一能让这孩子有康复机会的地方。
『把她送过去，』泰勒当即吩咐。
孩子送来时，佐拉心里不安。她深知镇上白人多疑的脾气。就在第二天，泰勒又送来了第二个病例，一个早先受了伤、却迟迟不见好的孩子。

===Original===
Under the care of the little hospital and the gentle nurse the children improved rapidly, and in two weeks were outdoors, playing with the little black children and even creeping into classrooms and listening. The grateful mothers came out twice a week at least; at first with suspicious aloofness, but gradually melting under Zora’s tact until they sat and talked with her and told their troubles and struggles. Zora realized how human they were, and how like their problems were to hers. They and their children grew to love this busy, thoughtful woman, and Zora’s fears were quieted.

===Chinese===
在小医院和温柔护士的照料下，孩子们好得很快，两周后已能到户外，和黑人小孩们一起玩耍，甚至溜进教室听课。感恩的母亲们至少每周来两次；起初带着多疑的疏离，但在佐拉的圆通之下渐渐融化，直到坐下来跟她聊天，诉说自己的烦恼和挣扎。佐拉意识到她们多么有人情味，她们的难题又与自己的何其相似。她们和她们的孩子，渐渐爱上了这个忙碌、周到的女子，佐拉的忧虑也平息了。

===Original===
The catastrophe came suddenly. The sheriff rode by, scowling and hunting for some poor black runaway, when he saw white children in the Negro school and white women, whom he knew were mill-hands, looking on. He was black with anger; turning he galloped back to town. A few hours later the young physician arrived hastily in a cab to take the women and children to town. He said something in a low tone to Zora and drove away, frowning.
Zora came quickly to the school and asked for Alwyn. He was in the barn and she hurried there.
“Bles,” she said quietly, “it is reported that a Toomsville mob will burn the school tonight.”

===Chinese===
灾难来得突然。警长骑马经过，皱着眉、正搜捕哪个可怜的黑人逃奴，这时他看见黑人学校里有白人孩子，还有他认得是厂工的白人女子在旁观看。他气得脸发黑；一转身，纵马奔回镇上。几小时后，那位年轻医生匆匆乘马车赶来，把那几个女子和孩子接回镇上。他低声对佐拉说了句什么，皱着眉，驾车走了。
佐拉快步赶到学校，找阿尔文。他在谷仓里，她急忙赶过去。
『布莱斯，』她平静地说，『有消息说，图姆斯维尔的暴民今夜要来烧学校。』

===Original===
Bles stood motionless.
“I’ve been fearing it. The sheriff has been stirring up the worst elements in the town lately and the mills pay off tonight.”
“Well,” she said quietly, “we must prepare.”
He looked at her, his face aglow with admiration.
“You wonder-woman!” he exclaimed softly.
A moment they regarded each other. She saw the love in his eyes, and he saw rising in hers something that made his heart bound. But she turned quickly away.
“You must hurry, Bles; lives are at stake.” And in another moment he thundered out of the barn on the black mare.

===Chinese===
布莱斯一动不动地站着。
『我一直担心这个。警长这些日子一直在煽动镇上最坏的那拨人，而今晚厂里发工钱。』
『那么，』她平静地说，『我们得做准备。』
他看着她，满脸敬佩的光彩。
『你这奇女子！』他轻声叫。
他们对视了片刻。她看见他眼里的爱，而他看见她眼里升起某种东西，令他的心猛跳了一下。但她很快转过身。
『你得赶快，布莱斯；人命关天。』转眼间，他便策马冲出谷仓，骑的是那匹黑马母马。

===Original===
Along the pike he flew and up the plantation roads. Across broad fields and back again, over to the Barton pike and along the swamp. At every cabin he whispered a word, and left behind him grey faces and whispering children.
His horse was reeking with sweat as he staggered again into the schoolyard; but already the people were gathering, with frightened, anxious, desperate faces. Women with bundles and children, men with guns, tottering old folks, wide-eyed boys and girls. Up from the swamp land came the children crying and moaning. The sun was setting. The women and children hurried into the school building, closing the doors and windows.

===Chinese===
他沿着大路飞驰，奔上种植园的小径。穿过宽阔的田野又折回来，越过巴顿大路，沿着沼泽。每一座小屋前他都低语一句，留在他身后的是一张张灰白的脸和窃窃低语的孩子。
他的马汗出如浆、摇摇晃晃地又冲进校园时，人们已开始聚拢，一张张惊恐、焦灼、绝望的脸。抱着包袱和孩子的女人，扛枪的男人，颤巍巍的老人，瞪大眼的少男少女。从沼泽地那边上来的是哭号的孩子。太阳正在落下。妇女和孩子急忙涌进校舍，关上门窗。

===Original===
A moment Alwyn stood without and looked back. The world was peaceful. He could hear the whistle of birds and the sobbing of the breeze in the shadowing oaks. The sky was flashing to dull and purplish blue, and over all lay the twilight hush as though God did not care.
He threw back his head and clenched his hands. His soul groaned within him. “Heavenly Father, was man ever before set to such a task?” Fight? God! if he could but fight! If he could but let go the elemental passions that were leaping and gathering and burning in the eyes of yonder caged and desperate black men.

===Chinese===
阿尔文在外面站了一会儿，回头望去。世界一片宁静。他听得见鸟的啼鸣、微风在投下阴影的橡树间的呜咽。天空闪着一种暗淡的、发紫的蓝，暮色的寂静笼罩万物，仿佛上帝并不在乎。
他仰起头，攥紧双拳。他的灵魂在体内呻吟。『天父，人可曾有过被置于这般境地的吗？』战斗？老天！要是能战斗就好了！要是能放开那些原始的激情就好了——它们正在那边那些被困的、绝望的黑人眼中跳跃、聚拢、燃烧。

===Original===
But his hands were tied﻿—manacled. One desperate struggle, a whirl of blood, and the whole world would rise to crush him and his people. The white operator in yonder town had but to flash the news, “Negroes killing whites,” to bring all the country, all the State, all the nation, to red vengeance. It mattered not what the provocation, what the desperate cause.
The door suddenly opened behind him and he wheeled around.
“Zora!” he whispered.
“Bles,” she answered softly, and they went silently in to their people.

===Chinese===
可他的手是被缚住的——上了镣铐。一次拼死的挣扎、一片血的旋涡，整个世界便会起来碾碎他和他的人。镇上那个白人接线员，只需拍出一则电讯：『黑人在杀白人』，便能引来举国、全州、全国的血色复仇。无论挑衅是什么、无论那绝望的缘由是什么，都无关紧要。
他身后的门忽然开了，他猛地转身。
『佐拉！』他低语。
『布莱斯，』她轻声答，他们默默地走进去，回到他们的人中间。

===Original===
All at once, from floor to roof, the whole schoolhouse was lighted up, save a dark window here and there. Then someone slipped out into the darkness and soon watch-fire after watch-fire flickered and flamed in the night, and then burned vividly, sending up sparks and black smoke. Thus ringed with flaming silence, the school lay at the edge of the great, black swamp and waited. Owls hooted in the forest. Afar the shriek of the Montgomery train was heard across the night, mingling with the wail of a wakeful babe; and then redoubled silence.

===Chinese===
忽然，从地到顶，整座校舍都亮了起来，只有这里那里一扇暗窗。随即有人溜进黑暗里，很快，一堆接一堆的守望之火在夜里闪烁、燃起，继而熊熊燃烧，腾起火星和黑烟。就这样，被一圈燃烧的寂静环绕着，学校卧在巨大的黑色沼泽边上，等待着。猫头鹰在林间啼叫。远处传来蒙哥马利火车的汽笛，划过夜空，和一个醒着的婴儿的啼哭交织在一起；随后是加倍的寂静。

===Original===
The men became restless, and Johnson began to edge away toward the lower hall. Alwyn was watching him when a faint noise came to him on the eastern breeze﻿—a low, rumbling murmur. It died away, and rose again; then a distant gunshot woke the echoes.
“They’re coming!” he cried. Standing back in the shadow of a front window, he waited. Slowly, intermittently, the murmuring swelled, till it grew distinguishable as yelling, cursing, and singing, intermingled with the crash of pistol-shots. Far away a flame, as of a burning cabin, arose, and a wilder, louder yell greeted it.

===Chinese===
男人们坐立不安，约翰逊开始朝下面的厅挪动，想溜。阿尔文正盯着他，这时一阵隐约的声响随东风传来——一阵低低的、隆隆的嘤嗡。它消逝了，又升起；接着一声远处的枪响，唤醒了回声。
『他们来了！』他喊。他退到一扇前窗的阴影里，等着。那嘤嗡慢慢地、断断续续地高涨，直到能辨出是叫喊、咒骂和歌唱，夹杂着手枪的砰砰声。远处升起一道火焰，像是一间燃烧的小屋，一阵更狂、更响的嚎叫迎接了它。

===Original===
Now the tramp of footsteps could be heard, and clearer and thicker the grating and booming of voices, until suddenly, far up the pike, a black moving mass, with glitter and shout, swept into view. They came headlong, guided by pine-torches, which threw their white and haggard faces into wild distortion. Then as bonfire after bonfire met their gaze, they moved slowly and more slowly, and at last sent a volley of bullets at the fires. One bullet flew high and sang through a lighted window. Without a word, Uncle Isaac sank upon the floor and lay still.

===Chinese===
如今脚步声已能听见，嗓音的摩擦和轰鸣越来越清晰、越来越浓，直到突然，在大路远处，一团黑色的、移动的、带着闪光和呼喊的群体，涌进视线。他们气势汹汹地冲来，由松脂火把引路，火光把他们苍白、憔悴的脸照得扭曲变形。随即，当一堆又一堆篝火映入他们眼帘，他们走得越来越慢，终于朝火堆射出一排子弹。一颗子弹飞高了，嗖地穿过一扇亮着的窗。艾萨克大叔一声不吭地栽倒在地板上，不动了。

===Original===
Silence and renewed murmuring ensued, and the sound of high voices in dispute. Then the mass divided into two wings and slowly encircled the fence of fire; starting noisily and confidently, and then going more slowly, quietly, warily, as the silence of the flame began to tell on their heated nerves.
Strained whispers arose.
“Careful there!”
“Go on, damn ye!”
“There’s someone by yon fire.”
“No, there ain’t.”
“See the bushes move.”
*Bang! bang! bang!*
“Who’s that?”
“It’s me.”

===Chinese===
随之是沉默、又起的嘤嗡，和高声争执的嗓音。随即那团人群分成两翼，缓缓地包围了那道火篱；起初吵吵嚷嚷、信心十足，随后越走越慢、越走越静、越走越小心，因为那火焰的沉默，开始在他们发热的神经上起了作用。
紧绷的低语升起。
『当心！』
『上啊，妈的！』
『那堆火边上有人。』
『不，没有。』
『瞧那灌木丛在动。』
*砰！砰！砰！*
『谁？』
『是我。』

===Original===
“Let’s rush through and fire the house.”
“And leave a pa’cel of niggers behind to shoot your lights out? Not me.”
“What the hell are you going to do?”
“I don’t know yet.”
“I wish I could see a nigger.”
“*Hark!*”
Stealthy steps were approaching, a glint of steel flashed behind the fire lights. Each band mistook the other for the armed Negroes, and the leaders yelled in vain; human power can not stay the dashing torrent of fear-inspired human panic.

===Chinese===
『咱们冲过去，把房子点了。』
『把一帮黑鬼留在后头，把你们的灯都打灭？我才不干。』
『你他妈打算怎么办？』
『我还不知道。』
『我恨不得看见个黑鬼。』
『*听！*』
蹑手蹑脚的脚步正在逼近，一道钢铁的闪光在火光后一闪。每一伙人都把对方误作武装的黑人，头目们徒劳地喊叫；人力挡不住那股由恐惧激起的人潮洪流。

===Original===
Whirling, the mob fled till it struck the road in two confused, surging masses. Then in quick frenzy, shots flew; three men threw up their hands and tumbled limply in the dust, while the main body rushed pellmell toward town.
At early dawn, when the men relaxed from the strain of the night’s vigil, Alwyn briefly counselled them: “Hide your guns.”
“Why?” blustered Rob. “Haven’t I a right to have a gun?”
“Yes, you have, Rob; but don’t be foolish﻿—hide it. We’ve not heard the last of this.”
But Rob tossed his head belligerently.

===Chinese===
暴民转身逃跑，直到撞上大路，分成两团混乱汹涌的人潮。随即在一阵急促的狂乱中，枪弹横飞；三个人扬起手、软绵绵地栽进尘土里，而主力人群狼狈不堪地朝镇上逃去。
天蒙蒙亮时，男人们从一夜守夜的紧张里松弛下来，阿尔文简短地劝他们：『把枪藏起来。』
『凭什么？』罗布叫嚣。『我没权有杆枪吗？』
『有，你有权，罗布；可别犯傻——藏起来。这事没完。』
可罗布好斗地把头一甩。

===Original===
In town, rumor spread like wildfire. A body of peaceful whites passing through the black settlement had been fired on from ambush, and six killed﻿—no, three killed﻿—no, one killed and two severely wounded.
“The thing mustn’t stop here,” shouted Sheriff Colton; “these niggers must have a lesson.” And before nine next morning fully half the grown members of the same mob, now sworn in as deputies, rode with him to search the settlement. They tramped insolently through the school grounds, but there was no shred of evidence until they came to Rob’s cabin and found his gun. They tied his hands behind him and marched him toward town.

===Chinese===
在镇上，谣言像野火一样传开。一队和平的白人经过黑人定居点时，遭到埋伏开枪袭击，死了六个——不，死了三个——不，死了一个、重伤两个。
『这事不能就这么算了，』警长科尔顿喊；『这些黑鬼得有个教训。』第二天早上九点前，昨夜那伙暴民里整整一半成年汉子，如今宣誓成了副警长，随他一同去搜查定居点。他们傲慢地踏过校园，却找不到半点证据，直到他们来到罗布的小屋，找到了他的枪。他们把他的手反绑在身后，押往镇上。

===Original===
But before the mob arrived the night before, Johnson feeling that his safety lay in informing the white folks, had crawled with his gun into the swamp. In the morning he peered out as the cavalcade approached, and not knowing what had happened, he recognized Colton, the sheriff, and signalled to him cautiously. In a moment a dozen men were on him, and he appealed and explained in vain﻿—the gun was damning evidence. The voices of Rob’s wife and children could be heard behind the two men as they were hurried along at a dog trot.
The town poured out to greet them﻿—“The murderers! the murderers! Kill the niggers!” and they came on with a rush. The sheriff turned and disappeared in the rear. There was a great cloud of dust, a cry and a wild scramble, as the white and angry faces of men and boys gleamed a moment and faded.

===Chinese===
可就在昨夜暴民到达之前，约翰逊觉得自己的安全在于向白人告密，便带着枪爬进了沼泽。早上，他见那一行人马走近，便探出头来张望，不知出了什么事，认出了警长科尔顿，便小心地向他打了个暗号。转眼间，十几个人就扑到他身上，他百般求告、百般解释也无用——那杆枪就是铁证。两人被押着小跑赶路时，背后听得见罗布妻儿的哭喊声。
全镇人都涌出来迎他们——『杀人犯！杀人犯！杀了这些黑鬼！』他们一拥而上。警长转身，消失在后头。腾起一大团尘土，一声喊叫，一阵疯狂的拉扯，男人和男孩们苍白愤怒的脸一闪一闪，随即消逝。

===Original===
A hundred or more shots rang out; then slowly and silently, the mass of women and men were sucked into the streets of the town, leaving but black eddies on the corners to throw backward glances toward the bare, towering pine where swung two red and awful things. The pale boy-face of one, with soft brown eyes glared up sightless to the sun; the dead, leathered bronze of the other was carved in piteous terror.

===Chinese===
一百多声枪响响起；随后慢慢地、默默地，那一大群男女被吸进了镇上的街道，只在街角留下一个个黑色的漩涡，回头望向那棵光秃秃的、高耸的松树——树上，晃荡着两样红红的、可怖的东西。其中一张苍白的男孩脸，一双柔和的棕色眼睛向上瞪着，对太阳已毫无所见；另一具死了的、皮革般古铜色的身躯，凝固着一种凄惨的恐惧。

## XXXVIII Atonement / XXXVIII 赎罪

===Original===
Three months had flown. It was Spring again, and Zora sat in the transformed swamp﻿—now a swamp in name only﻿—beneath the great oak, dreaming. And what she dreamed there in the golden day she dared not formulate even to her own soul. She rose with a start, for there was work to do. Aunt Rachel was ill, and Emma went daily to attend her; today, as she came back, she brought news that Colonel Cresswell, who had been unwell for several days, was worse. She must send Emma up to help, and as she started toward the school she glanced toward the Cresswell Oaks and saw the armchair of its master on the pillared porch.

===Chinese===
三个月飞逝而过。又是春天，佐拉坐在变了样的沼泽里——如今它只是徒有其名的沼泽了——坐在那棵大橡树下，做着梦。在那个金色的白日里她梦见了什么，她连对自己的灵魂都不敢形诸言辞。她一惊而起，因为还有事要做。蕾切尔大婶病了，埃玛每天去照料她；今天她回来时，带来了消息：克雷斯韦尔上校病了几天，如今更重了。她得派埃玛上去帮忙，她朝学校走去时，朝克雷斯韦尔橡树庄望了一眼，看见那廊柱游廊上摆着主人的扶手椅。

===Original===
Colonel Cresswell sat in his chair on the porch, alone. As far as he could see, there was no human soul. His eyes were bloodshot, his cheeks sunken, and his breath came in painful gasps. A sort of terror shook him until he heard the distant songs of black folk in the fields. He sighed, and lying back, closed his eyes and the breath came easier. When he opened them again a white figure was coming up the avenue of the Oaks. He watched it greedily. It was Mary Cresswell, and she started when she saw him.
“You are worse, father?” she asked.
“Worse and better,” he replied, smiling cynically. Then suddenly he announced: “I’ve made my will.”

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔上校独自坐在游廊的椅子上。目之所及，没有一个人影。他两眼充血，双颊凹陷，呼吸痛苦地一阵阵喘。一种恐怖攫着他，直到他听见远处田里黑人唱歌的声音。他叹了口气，往后一靠，闭上眼，呼吸顺畅了些。他再睁开眼时，一个白色的身影正沿橡树荫道走来。他贪婪地盯着她。是玛丽·克雷斯韦尔，她看见他时，吃了一惊。
『你更重了，爸？』她问。
『更重，也更好，』他讥诮地笑着答。随即他忽然宣布：『我立了遗嘱。』

===Original===
“Why﻿—why﻿—” she stammered.
“Why?” sharply. “Because I’m going to die.”
She said nothing. He smiled and continued:
“I’ve got it all fixed. Harry was in a tight place﻿—gambling as usual﻿—and I gave him a lump sum in lieu of all claims. Then I gave John Taylor﻿—you needn’t look. I sent for him. He’s a damned scoundrel; but he won’t lie, and I needed him. I willed his children all the rest except two or three legacies. One was one hundred thousand dollars for you﻿—”

===Chinese===
『怎么——怎么——』她结巴。
『怎么？』他尖锐地。『因为我要死了。』
她没作声。他笑了笑，接着说：
『我都安排妥了。哈里手头紧——照旧赌钱——我给了他一笔整款，买断他一切的索取权。然后我给约翰·泰勒——你不必找了。我派人去叫了他。他是个该死的无赖；可他不撒谎，而我需要他。除了两三笔遗赠，其余的我全遗给了他的孩子。其中一笔是十万块，给你的——』

===Original===
“Oh, father!” she cried. “I don’t deserve it.”
“I reckon two years with Harry was worth about that much,” he returned grimly. “Then there’s another gift of two hundred thousand dollars and this house and plantation. Whom do you think that’s for?”
“Helen?”
“Helen!” he raised his hand in threatening anger. “I might rot here for all she cares. No﻿—no﻿—but then﻿—I’ll not tell you﻿—I﻿—ah﻿—” A spasm of pain shot across his face, and he lay back white and still. Abruptly he sat up again and peered down the oaks. “Hush!” he gasped. “Who’s that?”

===Chinese===
『哦，爸！』她叫。『我不配。』
『我想，跟哈里过的那两年，大约就值这么多，』他冷酷地回。『另外还有一笔，二十万块，连同这宅子和种植园。你猜是给谁的？』
『海伦？』
『海伦！』他威胁而愤怒地举起手。『我烂在这儿她也不在乎。不——不——可是——我不告诉你——我——啊——』一阵痛苦的痉挛掠过他的脸，他往后一靠，苍白、一动不动。忽然他又坐起来，顺着橡树荫道向下望去。『嘘！』他喘息。『那是谁？』

===Original===
“I don’t know﻿—it’s a girl﻿—I﻿—”
He gripped her till she winced.
“My God﻿—it walks﻿—like my wife﻿—I tell you﻿—she held her head so﻿—who is it?” He half rose.
“Oh, father, it’s nobody but Emma﻿—little Emma﻿—Bertie’s child﻿—the mulatto girl. She’s a nurse now, and I asked to have her come and attend you.”
“Oh,” he said, “oh﻿—” He looked at the girl curiously. “Come here.” He peered into her white young face. “Do you know me?”

===Chinese===
『我不知道——是个姑娘——我——』
他攥住她，攥得她直皱眉。
『我的上帝——她走路——像我的妻子——我跟你说——她就是这样昂着头的——那是谁？』他半站起身。
『哦，爸，那不是别人，是埃玛——小埃玛——伯蒂的孩子——那个穆拉托姑娘。她如今是护士了，我请她来照料您。』
『哦，』他说，『哦——』他好奇地打量那姑娘。『过来。』他凑近端详她那张白皙年轻的脸。『你认得我吗？』

===Original===
The girl shrank away from him.
“Yes, sir.”
“What do you do?”
“I teach and nurse at the school.”
“Good! Well, I’m going to give you some money﻿—do you know why?”
A flash of self-consciousness passed over the girl’s face; she looked at him with her wide blue eyes.
“Yes, Grandfather,” she faltered.

===Chinese===
那姑娘从他身边缩开。
『认得，先生。』
『你做什么事？』
『我在学校里教书、当护士。』
『好！嗯，我要给你一笔钱——你知道为什么吗？』
一抹自识的神色掠过姑娘的脸；她睁大那双蓝眼睛望着他。
『知道，外公，』她迟疑地说。

===Original===
Mrs. Cresswell rose to her feet; but the old man slowly dropped the girl’s hand and lay back in his chair, with lips half smiling. “Grandfather,” he repeated softly. He closed his eyes a space and then opened them. A tremor shivered in his limbs as he stared darkly at the swamp.
“Hark!” he cried harshly. “Do you hear the bodies creaking on the limbs? It’s Rob and Johnson. I did it﻿—I﻿—”

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔太太站了起来；可老人慢慢放开姑娘的手，靠回椅子里，嘴唇半带笑意。『外公，』他轻轻重复。他闭了一会儿眼，又睁开。他阴沉地盯着沼泽，四肢一阵颤抖。
『听！』他粗厉地喊。『你听见那些尸体在树枝上嘎吱作响吗？那是罗布和约翰逊。是我干的——我——』

===Original===
Suddenly he rose and stood erect and his wild eyes stricken with death stared full upon Emma. Slowly and thickly he spoke, working his trembling hands.
“Nell﻿—Nell! Is it you, little wife, come back to accuse me? Ah, Nell, don’t shrink! I know﻿—I have sinned against the light and the blood of your poor black people is red on these old hands. No, don’t put your clean white hands upon me, Nell, till I wash mine. I’ll do it, Nell; I’ll atone. I’m a Cresswell yet, Nell, a Cresswell and a gen﻿—” He swayed. Vainly he struggled for the word. The shudder of death shook his soul, and he passed.

===Chinese===
忽然他站起身，笔直地立着，那双被死亡击中的狂乱眼睛，直直地盯住埃玛。他缓慢而滞重地说着，颤抖的双手不停地动着。
『内尔——内尔！是你吗，小妻子，回来控诉我吗？啊，内尔，别躲！我知道——我得罪了那光，你那可怜的黑人族人的血，红红地沾在这双老手上。不，别把你那干净的白手放在我身上，内尔，等我洗了我的。我会的，内尔；我会赎罪。我到底还是个克雷斯韦尔，内尔，一个克雷斯韦尔，一个绅——』他晃了晃。他徒劳地挣扎着想说那个字。死亡的战栗震颤了他的灵魂，他去了。

===Original===
A week after the funeral of Colonel Cresswell, John Taylor drove out to the school and was closeted with Miss Smith. His sister, installed once again for a few days in her old room at the school, understood that he was conferring about Emma’s legacy, and she was glad. She was more and more convinced that the marriage of Emma and Bles was the best possible solution of many difficulties. She had asked Emma once if she liked Bles, and Emma had replied in her innocent way,
“Oh, so much.”

===Chinese===
克雷斯韦尔上校葬礼后一星期，约翰·泰勒驱车来到学校，与史密斯小姐密谈。他的妹妹——这几天又住回学校她从前的房间——明白他是在商谈埃玛的那笔遗赠，她很高兴。她越来越相信，埃玛和布莱斯的婚事，是许多难题的最佳解法。她曾问过埃玛一次，喜不喜欢布莱斯，埃玛以她那无邪的样子答：
『哦，很喜欢。』

===Original===
As for Bles, he was often saying what a dear child Emma was. Neither perhaps realized yet that this was love, but it needed, Mrs. Cresswell was sure, only the lightning-flash, and they would know. And who could furnish that illumination better than Zora, the calm, methodical Zora, who knew them so well?
As for herself, once she had accomplished the marriage and paid the mortgage on the school out of her legacy, she would go abroad and in travel seek forgetfulness and healing. There had been no formal divorce, and so far as she was concerned there never would be; but the separation from her husband and America would be forever.

===Chinese===
至于布莱斯，他也常说埃玛是个多好的孩子。也许两人自己还没意识到这就是爱，但克雷斯韦尔太太确信，只需一道闪电，他们便会知晓。而谁能比佐拉——那沉静、有条不紊、对他们了如指掌的佐拉——更能提供那道照亮的光呢？
至于她自己，等她促成这桩婚事、又用她那笔遗赠还清了学校的抵押，她就要出国，在旅行里寻求忘却与疗愈。并没有正式离婚，就她而言，也永远不会有；但她与丈夫、与美国的分离，将是永远。

===Original===
Her brother came out of the office, nodded casually, for they had little intercourse these days, and rode away. She rushed in to Miss Smith and found her sitting there﻿—straight, upright, composed in all save that the tears were streaming down her face and she was making no effort to stop them.
“Why﻿—Miss Smith!” she faltered.
Miss Smith pointed to a paper. Mrs. Cresswell picked it up curiously. It was an official notification to the trustees of the Smith School of a legacy of two hundred thousand dollars together with the Cresswell house and plantation. Mrs. Gresswell sat down in open-mouthed astonishment. Twice she tried to speak, but there were so many things to say that she could not choose.
“Tell Zora,” Miss Smith at last managed to say.

===Chinese===
她哥哥从办公室出来，随意地点了点头——这些天他们很少来往——便骑马走了。她冲进去找史密斯小姐，见她坐在那里——挺直、端坐、处处从容，只除了泪水正顺着她的脸往下淌，而她一点也不去擦。
『怎么——史密斯小姐！』她支吾。
史密斯小姐指着一纸文书。克雷斯韦尔太太好奇地拿起来。这是一份发给史密斯学校理事会的正式通知：一笔二十万元的遗赠，连同克雷斯韦尔宅邸和种植园。克雷斯韦尔太太张着嘴、惊得坐下。她两次想说话，可要说的话太多，她不知从何挑起。
『去告诉佐拉，』史密斯小姐总算说出了口。

===Original===
Zora was dreaming again. Somehow, the old dream-life, with its glorious fantasies, had come silently back, richer and sweeter than ever. There was no tangible reason why, and yet today she had shut herself in her den. Searching down in the depths of her trunk, she drew forth that filmy cloud of white﻿—silk-bordered and half finished to a gown. Why were her eyes wet today and her mind on the Silver Fleece? It was an anniversary, and perhaps she still remembered that moment, that supreme moment before the mob. She half slipped on, half wound about her, the white cloud of cloth, standing with parted lips, looking into the long mirror and gleaming in the fading day like midnight gowned in mists and stars. Abruptly there came a peremptory knocking at the door.

===Chinese===
佐拉又在做梦了。不知怎的，那旧日的梦的生活，带着它辉煌的幻想，又悄悄地回来了，比从前更丰盈、更甜美。没有什么说得清的理由，可今天她把自己关进了小窝。她在箱子深处翻寻，掏出那片轻纱般的白云——镶着丝边、半成一件长袍。为什么今天她眼里含泪、心思全在银色羊毛上？这是个周年纪念日，也许她仍记得那一刻，暴民来临之前那至高无上的一刻。她半披半缠地把那片白色的云雾裹在身上，唇微启，望着那面长镜，在渐暗的天光里闪着光，像子夜披着雾与星。忽然，门上传来不容分说的叩门声。

===Original===
“Zora! Zora!” sounded Mrs. Cresswell’s voice. Forgetting her informal attire, she opened the door, fearing some mishap. Mrs. Cresswell poured out the news. Zora received it in such motionless silence that Mary wondered at her want of feeling. At last, however, she said happily to Zora:
“Well, the battle’s over, isn’t it?”
“No, it’s just begun.”
“Just begun?” echoed Mary in amazement.
“Think of the servile black folk, the half awakened restless whites, the fat land waiting for the harvest, the masses panting to know﻿—why, the battle is scarcely even begun.”

===Chinese===
『佐拉！佐拉！』是克雷斯韦尔太太的嗓音。她忘了自己这一身装束，怕出了什么事，开了门。克雷斯韦尔太太倾倒般地说出消息。佐拉那样一动不动、默然无语地接收了它，以至于玛丽诧异她怎么这样无动于衷。终于，她快活地对佐拉说：
『好了，战斗结束了，不是吗？』
『不，才刚刚开始。』
『才刚刚开始？』玛丽惊愕地重复。
『想想那些奴性的黑人，半醒的、不安的白人，等着丰收的沃土，喘息着渴望知晓的芸芸众生——嘿，这场战斗几乎还没开始呢。』

===Original===
“Yes, I guess that’s so,” Mary began to comprehend. “We’ll thank God it has begun, though.”
“Thank God!” Zora reverently repeated.
“Come, let’s go back to poor, dear Miss Smith,” suggested Mary.
“I can’t come just now﻿—but pretty soon.”
“Why? Oh, I see; you’re trying on something﻿—how pretty and becoming! Well, hurry.”
As they stood together, the white woman deemed the moment opportune; she slipped her arm about the black woman’s waist and began:
“Zora, I’ve had something on my mind for a long time, and I shouldn’t wonder if you had thought of the same thing.”

===Chinese===
『是的，我想是这样，』玛丽开始明白。『不过，我们要感谢上帝，它已经开始了。』
『感谢上帝！』佐拉虔敬地重复。
『来，咱们回去看可怜的、亲爱的史密斯小姐吧，』玛丽提议。
『我这会儿还不能去——不过很快就来。』
『为什么？哦，我明白了；你在试衣服——多漂亮、多衬你！好吧，快点。』
两人并肩站着时，白人女子觉得时机正好；她悄悄地把胳膊搂上黑人女子的腰，开口道：
『佐拉，我心里有一件事挂了很久，要是你也想过同一件事，我不会意外。』

===Original===
“What is it?”
“Bles and Emma.”
“What of them?”
“Their liking for each other.”
Zora bent a moment and caught up the folds of the Fleece.
“I hadn’t noticed it,” she said in a low voice.
“Well, you’re busy, you see. They’ve been very much together﻿—his taking her to her charges, bringing her back, and all that. I know they love each other; yet something holds them apart, afraid to show their love. Do you know﻿—I’ve wondered if﻿—quite unconciously, it is you? You know Bles used to imagine himself in love with you, just as he did afterward with Miss Wynn.”

===Chinese===
『什么事？』
『布莱斯和埃玛。』
『他们怎么了？』
『他们彼此喜欢。』
佐拉弯下腰，拾起羊毛的褶裥。
『我没留意到，』她低声说。
『这个嘛，你看，你忙。他们常常在一起——他送她去她照看的人家、又接她回来，如此种种。我知道他们相爱；可有一样东西把他们隔开，不敢表露他们的爱。你知道吗——我一直在想，是不是——完全无意识地——是因为你？你知道的，布莱斯从前以为他爱的是你，就像他后来以为爱的是怀恩小姐一样。』

===Original===
“Miss﻿—Wynn?”
“Yes, the Washington girl. But he got over that and you straightened him out finally. Still, Emma probably thinks yours is the prior claim, knowing, of course, nothing of facts. And Bles knows she thinks of him and you, and I’m convinced if you say the word, they’d love and marry.”
Zora walked silently with her to the door, where, looking out, she saw Bles and Emma coming from Aunt Rachel’s. He was helping her from the carriage with smiling eyes, and her innocent blue eyes were fastened on him.
Zora looked long and searchingly.
“Please run and tell them of the legacy,” she begged. “I﻿—I will come﻿—in a moment.” And Mrs. Cresswell hurried out.

===Chinese===
『怀恩——小姐？』
『是的，华盛顿那个姑娘。可他过了那一关，最后还是你把他理顺了。不过，埃玛多半以为你的优先权在先，当然，她对内情一无所知。而布莱斯知道她把他和你联在一起想，我深信，只要你一句话，他们就会相爱、成婚。』
佐拉默默地陪她走到门口，从那里望出去，她看见布莱斯和埃玛正从蕾切尔大婶家出来。他含笑送她下车，她那无邪的蓝眼睛定定地望着他。
佐拉久久地、探究地望着。
『麻烦你跑去把遗赠的事告诉他们，』她恳求。『我——我一会儿就来。』克雷斯韦尔太太匆匆出去了。

===Original===
Zora turned back steadily to her room, and locked herself in. After all, why shouldn’t it be? Why had it not occurred to her before in her blindness? If she had wanted him﻿—and ah, God! was not all her life simply the want of him?﻿—why had she not bound him to her when he had offered himself? Why had she not bound him to her? She knew as she asked﻿—because she had wanted all, not a part﻿—everything, love, respect and perfect faith﻿—not one thing could she spare then﻿—not one thing. And now, oh, God! she had dreamed that it was all hers, since that night of death and circling flame when they looked at each other soul to soul. But he had not meant anything. It was pity she had seen there, not love; and she rose and walked the room slowly, fast and faster.

===Chinese===
佐拉稳稳地转身回房，把自己锁在里面。说到底，为什么不应该是这样呢？她从前怎么瞎了眼，竟没想到这一层？如果她要过他——啊，上帝！她这一辈子，难道不就只是对他的渴盼吗？——那他献上自己的时候，她为什么不把他绑在身边？她为什么不把他绑在身边？她一边问，一边就知道答案——因为她要的是全部，不是一部分——一切：爱、敬重、和十足的信任——那时她一样也不能少——一样也不能。而如今，哦，上帝！她一直梦想着那全是她的，自从那个死亡与环形火焰之夜，他们灵魂对着灵魂相望。可他并没有那个意思。她在那里看到的是怜悯，不是爱；她站起来，在屋里慢慢踱步，越来越快。

===Original===
With trembling hands she drew the Silver Fleece round her. Her head swam again and the blood flashed in her eyes. She heard a calling in the swamp, and the shadow of Elspeth seemed to hover over her, claiming her for her own, dragging her down, down.﻿… She rushed through the swamp. The lagoon lay there before her presently, gleaming in the darkness﻿—cold and still, and in it swam an awful shape.
She held her burning head﻿—was not everything plain? Was not everything clear? This was Sacrifice! This was the Atonement for the unforgiven sin. Emma’s was the pure soul which she must offer up to God; for it was God, a cold and mighty God, who had given it to Bles﻿—her Bles. It was well; God willed it. But could she live? Must she live? Did God ask that, too?

===Chinese===
她用颤抖的双手把银色羊毛裹在身上。她的头又晕眩起来，血在她眼里闪光。她听见沼泽里一声呼唤，埃尔斯贝思的阴影仿佛盘桓在她头顶，把她认作自己人，把她往下、往下拽。……她冲过沼泽。潟湖很快横在她面前，在黑暗中闪烁——又冷又静，里面游着一个可怖的形体。
她捧住燃烧的头——一切还不明白吗？一切还不清楚吗？这就是牺牲！这就是那不可宽恕之罪的赎罪祭。埃玛那纯洁的灵魂，是她必须献给上帝的；因为是上帝——一个冷峻而大能的上帝——把她赐给了布莱斯——她的布莱斯。这是好的；上帝如此定意。可她能活下去吗？她必须活下去吗？这个，上帝也要吗？

===Original===
All at once she stood straight; her whole body grew tense, alert. She heard no sound behind her, but knew he was there, and braced herself. She must be true. She must be just. She must pay the uttermost farthing.
“Bles,” she called faintly, but did not turn her head.
“Zora!”
“Bles,” she choked, but her voice came stronger, “I know﻿—all. Emma is a good girl. I helped bring her up myself and did all I could for her and she﻿—she is pure; marry her.”

===Chinese===
忽然她挺直了身子；她整个身体绷紧、警觉。她没听见身后有声音，却知道他在那里，便稳住了自己。她必须真实。她必须公道。她必须付出最后一个铜板。
『布莱斯，』她微弱地唤，却没回头。
『佐拉！』
『布莱斯，』她哽咽，但嗓音更有力了，『我知道——一切。埃玛是个好姑娘。我亲手帮着带大她的，为她尽了力，而她——她是纯洁的；娶她吧。』

===Original===
His voice came slow and firm:
“Emma? But I don’t love Emma. I love﻿—someone else.”
Her heart bounded and again was still. It was that Washington girl then. She answered dully, groping for words, for she was tired:
“Who is it?”
“The best woman in all the world, Zora.”
“And is”﻿—she struggled at the word madly﻿—“is she pure?”
“She is more than pure.”
“Then you must marry her, Bles.”
“I am not worthy of her,” he answered, sinking before her.

===Chinese===
他的嗓音缓慢而坚定地传来：
『埃玛？可我不爱埃玛。我爱——另一个人。』
她的心猛跳了一下，又静止了。原来还是华盛顿那个姑娘。她木然地答，摸索着字眼，因为她累了：
『是谁？』
『世上最好的女子，佐拉。』
『那么她——』她疯狂地挣扎着那个字——『她是纯洁的吗？』
『她比纯洁更甚。』
『那么你必须娶她，布莱斯。』
『我不配她，』他说着，在她面前低下了头。

===Original===
Then at last illumination dawned upon her blindness. She stood very still and lifted up her eyes. The swamp was living, vibrant, tremulous. There where the first long note of night lay shot with burning crimson, burst in sudden radiance the wide beauty of the moon. There pulsed a glory in the air. Her little hands groped and wandered over his close-curled hair, and she sobbed, deep voiced:
“Will you﻿—marry me, Bles?”

===Chinese===
于是，光明终于照进了她的盲目。她静静地站着，抬起眼。沼泽是活的、震颤的、悸动的。在夜的第一声长音被燃烧的绯红穿透之处，月亮的浩瀚之美骤然绽放出光芒。空气里搏动着一种荣耀。她那双小手在他那紧鬈的头发上摸索、游走，她用低沉的嗓音哽咽道：
『你愿意——娶我吗，布莱斯？』

===Original===
L’Envoi
Lend me thine ears, O God the Reader, whose Fathers aforetime sent mine down into the land of Egypt, into this House of Bondage. Lay not these words aside for a moment’s fantasy, but lift up thine eyes upon the Horror in this land;﻿—the maiming and mocking and murdering of my people, and the prisonment of their souls. Let my people go, O Infinite One, lest the world shudder at **The End**

===Chinese===
跋
请垂听我言，读者上帝啊——您的先人，昔时将我的先人遣下埃及之地、遣入这奴役之屋。莫将这些话当作一时兴起而搁置一旁，请抬起您的眼，看看这国土上的恐怖吧——对我族人的残害、嘲弄与屠戮，以及对他们灵魂的囚禁。放我的族人走吧，无穷的主啊，免得这世界为**终局**而颤栗。
